\chapter{From CY Categories to Chiral Algebras}
\label{ch:cy-to-chiral}

\begin{theorem}[Two-stage factorisation of \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_d$}{Phi-d}; headline]
\label{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation-headline}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
For a specialisation datum $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$, and at $d=3$ for a
witnessed admissible datum in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:witnessed-admissible-specialisation-datum}, the
Calabi--Yau-to-chiral construction is the two-stage assignment
\[
 \Phi_d^{(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)} \;=\; \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1},\, C} \circ \PhiFA_d
 \colon \CY_d\text{-}\Cat
 \xrightarrow{\;\PhiFA_d\;} \EdHolFA(X)
 \xrightarrow{\;\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}\;} E_{n(d)}\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(C),
 \qquad
 n(d)=
 \begin{cases}
 \infty, & d=1,\\
 2, & d=2,\\
 1, & d\geq3.
 \end{cases}
\]
On the verified loci, after fixing the relevant $E_d$-formality /
associator datum and, at $d=3$, the chain-level framing,
anomaly-cancellation, and completion witnesses, the residual Stage~$1$
obstruction space is contractible on the connective/unit-connected
strictified locus: the $E_d$-holomorphic factorisation algebra
$\PhiFA_d(\cC)$ on a CY$_d$ target $X$ is assembled from the
Gerstenhaber bracket of degree $1 - d$ on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ by the
conjunction of Kontsevich
$E_d$-formality~\cite{Kontsevich1999}, Tamarkin
deformation-quantisation~\cite{Tamarkin2007}, and
Costello--Gwilliam--Li holomorphic locality on the
target~\cite{CostelloGwilliam2017,CostelloLi2016,CostelloLi2020};
at $d=3$, before this choice is fixed, the formality ambiguity is the
standard $\GRT_1(\Q)$-torsor. The \emph{formal locus} -- the sub-stratum
on which Stage~$1$ is unconditional -- stratifies as follows: at $d = 1$
every smooth elliptic curve $E$ is formal \textup{(}Kontsevich $L_\infty$
rank-zero degenerate bracket\textup{);} at $d = 2$ every smooth K3 and
every abelian surface is formal generically
\textup{(}Kapranov~\cite{Kapranov1999} identifies the Atiyah class
as the $L_\infty$-bracket structure constant; on a CY surface
$\wedge^2 \Omega^1_X = K_X$ has $h^{2,0} = 1$ but the
Rozansky--Witten cubic $\wedge^3 \Omega^1_X = 0$ vanishes identically
for dimension reasons, and Dolgushev--Tamarkin--Tsygan
\cite{DolgushevTamarkinTsygan2010} supplies the chain-level
homotopy-calculus formality\textup{);}
at $d = 3$ the product $K3 \times E$ is formal through the Künneth
reduction $\mathrm{At}(T_{K3 \times E}) = \pi_{K3}^*\,\mathrm{At}(T_{K3})
\oplus \pi_E^*\,\mathrm{At}(T_E)$ with $\mathrm{At}(T_E) = 0$
\textup{(}$T_E \simeq \cO_E$ is holomorphically trivial, so the class
in $H^1(E, \Omega^1_E \otimes \mathrm{End}(T_E))$ vanishes\textup{):}
every cubic cup of $\mathrm{At}(T_X)$ on $X = K3 \times E$ factors
through the $K3$-component alone, and on $K3$ the triple self-cup of
$\mathrm{At}(T_{K3})$ lands in
$H^3(K3, \wedge^3 \Omega^1_{K3} \otimes T_{K3}^{\otimes 3})$ whose
factor $\wedge^3 \Omega^1_{K3}$ vanishes identically because
$\dim_\C K3 = 2 < 3$. The Rozansky--Witten cubic, the concrete
Kuranishi obstruction to the $L_\infty$-structure on $T_X[-1]$
carrying the Hochschild polyvector bracket, accordingly vanishes on
$K3 \times E$ \textup{(}Kapranov~\cite{Kapranov1999} Formula~1.2
identifies $\mathrm{At}(T_X)$ as the $L_\infty$-bracket structure
constant, expresses the Rozansky--Witten cubic obstruction as the
cyclic triple Atiyah-class cup in
$H^3(X, \wedge^3 \Omega^1_X \otimes T_X^{\otimes 3})$, and computes it
in terms of Hodge-theoretic data on $X$; Dolgushev--Tamarkin--Tsygan
\cite{DolgushevTamarkinTsygan2010} supplies the chain-level
homotopy-calculus implementation on the Hochschild cochain complex
that forwards the vanishing to strict $E_3$-formality\textup{);}
compact CY$_3$ targets with
$\mathrm{At}(T_X) \neq 0$ \textup{(}the quintic $X_5 \subset \P^4$
has $\mathrm{At}(T_{X_5}) \in H^1(X_5, \Omega^1_{X_5} \otimes
\mathrm{End}(T_{X_5}))$ non-zero -- the Atiyah class as an $L_\infty$
structure-constant does not reduce to its trace $c_1(T_{X_5})$,
which vanishes on any CY, and the Euler sequence
$0 \to T_{X_5} \to T_{\P^4}|_{X_5} \to \cO_{X_5}(5) \to 0$ computes
$\mathrm{At}(T_{X_5})$ via restriction of $\mathrm{At}(T_{\P^4})$
with the normal-bundle correction $[\mathrm{At}(\cO_{X_5}(5))] =
5 h|_{X_5}$ non-zero in $H^1(X_5, \Omega^1)$\textup{)} carry a genuine
Kuranishi obstruction to strict $E_3$-formality of
$\CC^\bullet(\cC, \cC)$, and Stage~$1$ requires the chain-level
framing homotopy and Costello--Li completion witnesses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cya3-existence-rigidity} and
Chapter~\ref{ch:m3-b2-obstruction}; at $d \geq 4$ the
$E_d$-formality of Hochschild cochains is open even on explicit
targets \textup{(}Fresse--Willwacher~\cite{Willwacher2015}
intrinsic formality is operad-level; category-level formality at
$d \geq 4$ is the frontier\textup{).}
Stage~$2$ is factorisation homology over the specialisation cycle
$\Sigma_{d - 1}$ restricted to the reference curve $C$; at $d=3$ the
exact pushforward/kernel formula uses the Beck--Chevalley,
Fubini/envelope, proper-support, Tor-independence, compact-support, and
completion witnesses of
Definition~\ref{def:witnessed-admissible-specialisation-datum}
(Francis--Gaitsgory~\cite{FrancisGaitsgory2012}). Two specialisation
data $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C) \neq (\Sigma'_{d-1}, C')$ on the same CY$_d$
input produce two \emph{different} $E_{n(d)}$-chiral shadows of a
single Stage~$1$ output; the multiplicity of chiral images attached to
$\cC$ is the multiplicity of specialisation cycles, not a multiplicity
of $\Phi_d$-applications. Functoriality on arbitrary CY morphisms and
general compact non-formal CY$_3$ chain strictification are deferred to
Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality} and the compact CY$_3$
strictification programme.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[PTVV shift law for the native operadic level]
\label{prop:ptvv-shift-law}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
Under hypotheses \textup{(H1)--(H4)} of Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-fa-three-step-assembly}, the native operadic level of the Stage-$1$ output $\PhiFA_d(\cC)$ is forced by the Gerstenhaber-bracket degree $1 - d$ on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ together with Dunn--Lurie additivity; equivalently, by the Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi $(2-d)$-shifted symplectic form on the derived moduli stack $\cM(\cC)$~\cite{PTVV2013}. The two shifts are related by Koszul: a $k$-shifted Poisson structure gives functions an $E_{1-k}$-algebra structure~\cite{CPTVV2017}, so with $k = 2-d$ on $\cM(\cC)$ the functions acquire a $P_{d-1}$-Poisson structure, while the Hochschild cochain complex $\CC^\bullet(\cC, \cC)$ carries directly the $E_d$-algebra structure from Kontsevich $E_d$-formality. The table
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c|c}
 d & \textup{Serre shift on } \cC & \textup{PTVV shift on } \cM(\cC) & E_n \textup{ on } \CC^\bullet(\cC,\cC) & n_{\mathrm{native}}(d) \\\hline
 1 & \textup{[1]} & +1 & \Einf & \infty \\
 2 & \textup{[2]} & \phantom{-}0 & E_2 & 2 \\
 3 & \textup{[3]} & -1 & E_3 & 1 \\
 4 & \textup{[4]} & -2 & E_4 & 1 \\
 5 & \textup{[5]} & -3 & E_5 & 1
\end{array}
\]
records the forced assignment. The PTVV shift column is $2 - d$ uniformly; the Hochschild $E_d$-structure sits in $\CC^\bullet(\cC, \cC)$ above the moduli-functions $P_{d-1}$-Poisson structure, with the two related by CPTVV Koszul duality. Stage-$2$ reads out the residual operadic level on the reference curve $C$ as follows. At $d = 1$ the target $X$ is the curve itself \textup{(}CY$_1$ = elliptic curve\textup{);} the bracket vanishes on rank grounds and $A_\cC$ is $\Einf$-commutative \textup{(}lattice-VOA output\textup{).} At $d = 2$ the target $X$ is a surface, the reference curve $C \subset X$ is a complex-$1$-dimensional slice, and the $E_2$-holomorphic factorisation structure on $X$ restricts to the $E_2$-chiral structure on $C$ without drop in operadic level: the little-$2$-disks operad on $X$ pulls back to the little-$2$-disks on an embedded Riemann surface. At $d \geq 3$ the target $X$ has complex dimension $d$, the specialisation cycle $\Sigma_{d-1}$ is a complex $(d-1)$-dimensional submanifold of $X$, and holomorphic factorisation homology over $\Sigma_{d-1}$ in the sense of Costello--Gwilliam~\cite{CostelloGwilliam2017} \S$6.2$, Francis--Gaitsgory~\cite{FrancisGaitsgory2012}, and Ayala--Francis~\cite{AyalaFrancis2015} transferred to the holomorphic setting gives
\[
 \int_{\Sigma_{d-1}}^{\mathrm{hol}} \colon E_d\text{-HolFA}(X) \longrightarrow E_{d - (d-1)}\text{-HolFA}(C) = E_1\text{-HolFA}(C)
\]
upon subsequent holomorphic restriction to $C$: the complex-operadic level drops by $\dim_\C \Sigma_{d-1} = d - 1$ complex levels, landing at complex $E_1 = \Eone$-ordered. At $d = 3$ the canonical choice is $\Sigma_2 = K3$-fibre with reference curve $C = E$ \textup{(}the elliptic base\textup{);} the residual output $A_\cC$ on $C$ is $\Eone$-ordered. The braided $\Etwo$-structure at $d \geq 3$ lives on $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC))$, not on $A_\cC$ itself \textup{(}Remark~\ref{rem:e2-on-drinfeld-centre}; $(\infty,1)$-categorical statement at Theorem~\ref{prop:e2-enhancement-drinfeld}\textup{).} No choice of $\bS^d$-framing can raise $n_{\mathrm{native}}$ above the degree-forced value: the PTVV shift on $\cM(\cC)$ and Ayala--Francis on the specialisation datum together control the operadic level unconditionally.
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[CY-A\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} object-level existence-and-\texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1}-rigidity]
\label{thm:cya3-existence-rigidity}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
Let $\cC$ be a smooth proper CY$_3$ category on the witnessed
object-level locus: hypotheses \textup{(H1)--(H3)} of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}, a connective/unit-connected
strictified Hochschild model when Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}
is invoked, a chosen $E_3$-formality point, a chain-level $\bS^3$-framing
homotopy, a Costello--Li anomaly-cancellation and analytic-completion
witness, and a witnessed admissible specialisation datum $(\Sigma_2,C)$
in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:witnessed-admissible-specialisation-datum}
whenever the exact Stage-$2$ kernel formula is invoked. Three
vanishings, each independent and each load-bearing, remove the remaining
obstruction to the constructed Stage~$1$ object:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{$E_1$-Hochschild vanishing.} $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(\cC) = 0$ on the connective, unit-connected strictified model by $\HH^0(\cC) = k$ together with bar-filtration collapse \textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}, steps \textup{(i)--(iii)}\textup{).}
 \item \emph{Topological $\bS^3$-framing vanishing.} The structure-group reduction $\mathrm{GL}(n, \C) \to \Sp(2m)$ forced by the antisymmetric Euler form of CY$_3$ Serre duality places the framing class in $\pi_3(B\Sp(2m))$. By Bott periodicity $\pi_3(B\Sp(2m)) = 0$ for every $m \geq 1$, this topological obstruction vanishes on the symplectic-reduction locus \textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}\textup{).}
 \item \emph{Contractibility of residual lifts.} After the formality point, chain-level framing homotopy, and anomaly-completion witness are fixed, the space of compatible residual $\Eone$-structures on $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ is contractible \textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}, step \textup{(iv)}\textup{).}
\end{enumerate}
Consequently, $\Phi_3$ exists on this witnessed object-level locus, is
$\Eone$-rigid \textup{(}any two $\Eone$-lifts are canonically
quasi-isomorphic\textup{),} and is determined on objects up to a
contractible space of residual choices after the non-contractible
formality torsor and the chain-level witnesses have been fixed. The
statement is an
\emph{existence-and-rigidity} theorem, not an equivalence: no inverse
functor from $\Eone$-chiral algebras back to CY$_3$ categories is
asserted, no functoriality on arbitrary morphisms is asserted, and no
global quantum group $G(\cC)$ is produced. The proof is three vanishings
of three distinct obstruction groups, each with its own primary source
\textup{(}\cite{KS2008,CostelloGwilliam2017,CostelloLi2020}, together
with Bott periodicity $\pi_3(B\Sp(2m)) = 0$\textup{);} the chain-level
$\Ainf$-compatibility is supplied on the verified loci by the Costello
open-closed TCFT of Chapter~\ref{ch:m3-b2-obstruction}. The three
vanishings do not by themselves construct the formality point, the
framing homotopy, the anomaly trivialisation, or the Stage-$2$ kernel
datum; those are part of the witnessed input.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Object-level chain-level \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_d$}{Phi-d} versus \texorpdfstring{$(\infty,1)$}{(inf,1)}-categorical \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_d$}{Phi-d}-as-functor]
\label{rem:phi-d-chain-vs-inf1-functor}
The symbol $\Phi_d$ names two statements about different categorical structures, each load-bearing, neither reducible to the other:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{Object-level chain-level $\Phi_d$.} An assignment $\cC \mapsto \PhiFA_d(\cC) \mapsto \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}(\PhiFA_d(\cC))$ at the level of explicit factorisation algebras, with witnessing homotopies supplied by Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality, Costello--Gwilliam--Li locality, and; at $d = 3$; the chain-level framing, anomaly-completion, and specialisation data whose residual obstruction is killed by the three vanishings of Theorem~\ref{thm:cya3-existence-rigidity}. The chain-level object $\PhiFA_d(\cC)$ has explicit witnessing data: the degree-$(1-d)$ $\lambda$-bracket on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$, the $E_d$-formality $L_\infty$-morphisms, the Costello--Li holomorphic twist of 6D hCS, the $5$d-hCS BV realisation of Proposition~\ref{prop:5d-hcs-bv-factorisation} via $\Obs^{5\mathrm{d\,hCS}}(X \times \R_t, \frakg) \simeq \Obs^{1\mathrm{d\,top}}(\R_t) \boxtimes \Obs^{4\mathrm{d\,hol}}(X, \frakg)$ on its hypotheses, and; at $d = 2$ unconditionally and at $d = 3$ only on the witnessed K3-fibre/Borcherds comparison locus; the explicit denominator formula of the $\SpCh$-output (e.g.\ $\Delta_5(Z)^{-2}$ for $K3 \times E$ by Oberdieck--Pixton after Mukai--Heisenberg reduction and Borcherds pushforward).
 \item \emph{$(\infty,1)$-categorical $\Phi_d$-as-functor.} A functor on $(\infty,1)$-categories of CY$_d$ data, sending morphisms of CY categories to morphisms of $E_{n(d)}$-chiral algebras. Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality} is this statement; it is open in general and tested at $d = 2$ on the Mukai transform. The $(\infty,1)$-categorical statement requires preservation of CY-morphisms (cyclic $\Ainf$-functors compatible with $\bS^d$-framing) and is a separate theorem with a separate proof horizon.
\end{enumerate}
Statement \textup{(a)} at $d = 3$ is Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}; statement \textup{(b)} at every $d$ is the per-$d$ functoriality Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality}. Neither subsumes the other. Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product} \textup{(}K\"unneth factorisation\textup{)} is chain-level at the Stage-$1$ level, statement~\textup{(a)}. The Ben-Zvi--Francis--Nadler identification of Remark~\ref{rem:e2-on-drinfeld-centre} is $(\infty,1)$-categorical, statement~\textup{(b)}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The CY-A\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} conjecture-upgrade stratification S1--S5]
\label{rem:cya3-conjecture-stratification-S1-S5}
Between Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-platonic} and Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} lies a fault line: a statement is a theorem when its proof at $d = 3$ is an instance of Theorem~\ref{thm:cya3-existence-rigidity}; it is a conjecture when the chain-level $\bS^3$-framing hypothesis~\ref{hyp:framing} has not been witnessed on the specific CY$_3$ category at hand. The $\infty$-categorical resolution of Theorem~\ref{thm:cya3-existence-rigidity} partitions the conjectural CY-A$_3$ corollaries into five strata:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{S\arabic*.}]
 \item \emph{$\infty$-resolved, verified on explicit object-level loci.} The tagged assignment $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ exists on the loci covered by Theorem~\ref{thm:cya3-existence-rigidity}; the chain-level $\bS^3$-framing is a witnessed datum, and the three vanishings of that theorem show that no further derived obstruction remains after the Costello TCFT cancellation of Chapter~\ref{ch:m3-b2-obstruction}. Sample explicit local instances are the toric framed outputs attached to $D^b(\Coh(\C^3))$, the conifold, and local~$\bP^2$.
 \item \emph{Compact CY$_3$ with K3-fibration.} The tagged object $\Phi_3^{(K3,E)}(D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E)))$ lies in the verified object-level scope when the stated K3-fibre/elliptic-base datum is fixed; the comparison with the Borcherds--Gritsenko shadow is the typed route
 $\SpCh_{K3,E}(\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E))) \to \mathcal H_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{\mathrm{Heis}} \xrightarrow{\mathrm{Bor}} U_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$
 on the principal locus of Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}, with $\kBKM = 5$ by the Borcherds weight identity. Enriques $\times E$ belongs instead to the torsion-canonical/orbifold extension and is conditional on that extension.
 \item \emph{Compact CY$_3$ without K3-fibration.} For the quintic and bicubic, object-level existence belongs to the verified scope only when the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:cya3-existence-rigidity} and an admissible $(\Sigma_2,C)$ are witnessed; explicit $\SpCh$ outputs are separate quiver-chart or Gepner-critical-CoHA problems, not automatic Borcherds lifts \textup{(}the Mukai lattice fails even unimodularity: $b_3 = 204$ for the quintic\textup{).} See Remark~\ref{rem:phi3-quintic-local-p2-preview}.
 \item \emph{Functoriality on morphisms.} Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality} at $d = 3$: the $(\infty,1)$-categorical statement of Remark~\ref{rem:phi-d-chain-vs-inf1-functor}\textup{(b)}. Open at all $d \geq 2$; tested at $d = 2$ on the Mukai transform.
 \item \emph{Global quantum vertex group $G(\cC)$.} Assembly of local $Y^+(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ into a global quantum group via the Drinfeld double is obstructed by non-commutation of doubles with hocolims \textup{(}Proposition~\ref{prop:center-hocolim}\textup{);} Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} does not produce $G(\cC)$ in general, and the CY-C programme is open.
\end{enumerate}
A CY-A$_3$ corollary belongs in S1 \textup{(}proved-here scope\textup{)}
when its ingredients are \textup{(i)}--\textup{(iii)} of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cya3-existence-rigidity}; in S4
\textup{(}conjectural\textup{)} when it requires
$(\infty,1)$-functoriality on morphisms; and in S5
\textup{(}conjectural\textup{)} when it invokes $G(\cC)$. The present
chapter maintains this stratification: every status tag records which
stratum witnesses the claim, and no statement labelled S4 or S5 carries
a proved-here badge.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Equivalence with the chapter's \texorpdfstring{$\CoHA(\C^3) \mapsto Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$}{CoHA(C-3) |-> Y-+(widehatfgl-1)} construction]
\label{rem:two-stage-equals-coha-yangian}
The local $\C^3$ construction carried out in this chapter is the
Hall-side model for the CY$_3$ bridge, not an application of $\Phi_3$ to
the associative algebra $\CoHA(\C^3)$. The input geometry is the toric
chart/Jordan quiver with cubic potential; its critical Hall algebra is
\[
 \CoHA(\C^3)\cong Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)
\]
by Kontsevich--Soibelman and Schiffmann--Vasserot. The missing
comparison with Stage~$1$ is the map
$\Theta_{\hCS\to\Hall}$ of Problem~\ref{op:cy3-hcs-hall-comparison}.
The positive half $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$; not the full
$\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$; is the direct algebraic Hall output.
The evaluation-chain disambiguation
$Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1) \hookrightarrow
Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1) \xrightarrow{\rho_\lambda}
\mathrm{End}(\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]\textup{-vacuum})$ exhibits
$\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$ as a Fock/vacuum representation target for
the full Yangian, obtained from $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ only after
Drinfeld-centre doubling of property~\eqref{phi:U3}.
\end{remark}

Section~\ref{sec:two-stage-organising-frame} develops the factorisation in full (Definition~\ref{def:phi-fa-and-sp}, Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation}); the reader seeking its positive-geometry grammar is directed to the preface, \S\ref{wn:sec:platonic-preface-positive-geometry-grammar}.

A Calabi--Yau category has a trace. A chiral algebra has an OPE. The trace has a Hochschild shadow $\HH_d(\cC)\to\C$, but the CY datum used by the framing is the negative-cyclic class $[\sigma_\cC]\in HC^-_d(\cC)$ with its Connes-closedness. The OPE is a distribution-valued product on sections over a punctured disc. No formal argument connects them: the trace is finite-dimensional cyclic Hochschild data, the OPE is an infinite-dimensional vertex structure on a curve. Volumes~I and~II accept a chiral algebra as given and extract its invariants: bar-cobar adjunction, the modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$, the shadow tower, the five theorems A--D+H. The geometric source of that algebra is left unspecified. Affine Kac--Moody algebras arise from flat connections, Virasoro from reparametrizations; every other chiral algebra in the standard landscape (conifold, Hilbert schemes of K3, resolved $A_n$ singularities, local threefolds with potential) must be supplied by hand. The question is: what functor connects CY categories to chiral algebras, and what structure must it preserve?

The answer requires four constructions, each with a precise structural role. A CY category $\cC$ of dimension $d$ carries a cyclic $\Ainf$-structure, a Connes $B$-operator, and an $\bS^d$-framing on Hochschild homology. None of these are chiral algebras. The Gerstenhaber bracket on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ produces a Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}_\cC$; the factorization envelope on a curve $X$ promotes $\mathfrak{L}_\cC$ to a factorization algebra; the $\bS^d$-framing provides the higher operadic enhancement; and the CY trace determines the quantization. The desired general functor is the central object of this volume. The proved functorial case is
\[
 \Phi \colon \CY_2\text{-}\Cat \longrightarrow \Etwo\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg},
\]
while the $d = 3$ construction is the framed object-level assignment, for fixed witnessed admissible $(\Sigma_2,C)$,
\[
 \Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(\cC)
 :=
 \SpCh_{\Sigma_2,C}(\PhiFA_3(\cC))
 \in \Eone\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(C)
\]
under hypotheses \textup{H1--H4} of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}. It must preserve three things: the Hochschild data (the bar complex of the output recovers the cyclic bar complex of $\cC$ on the constructed locus), the modular characteristic (at $d = 2$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_2(\cC))$ equals the CY Euler characteristic $\chi^{\CY}(\cC)$; see Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2} for the precise hypothesis and the abelian-surface counterexample), and the operadic level (the framing determines the correct $E_n$-structure, not higher).

At $d = 2$, the correspondence $\Phi_2$ is unconditional: the $\bS^2$-framing of Kontsevich--Vlassopoulos enhances $\Fact_X(\mathfrak{L}_\cC)$ to an $\Etwo$-chiral algebra, and $\Phi_2$ is constructed end-to-end. At $d = 3$, a fundamental obstruction intervenes. The native $E_3$-structure on $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ restricts to \emph{symmetric} braiding under Dunn additivity; the nonsymmetric quantum group braiding that physics demands is constructed via the Drinfeld center $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC))$, where the half-braiding $\sigma_{V_u}(V_v)$ on evaluation modules \emph{is} the $R$-matrix (Construction~\ref{constr:rmatrix-from-center}). This chapter verifies the Hall/representation-side $d = 3$ chain for $\C^3$: $\CoHA(\C^3) \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ on the CY Hall side \textup{(}Schiffmann--Vasserot; not $\cW_{1+\infty}$ directly, and emphatically not an equivalence of $Y^+$ with $\cW_{1+\infty}$\textup{),} and the evaluation-chain disambiguation $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1) \hookrightarrow Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1) \xrightarrow{\mathrm{ev}_\lambda} \mathrm{End}(\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]\text{-vacuum})$ exhibits $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$ \textup{(}Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak\textup{)} as the image of $Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ acting on the vacuum module. The hCS-to-Hall comparison preceding this chain is the typed problem isolated in Chapter~\ref{ch:cy3-chain-level-bridge}; the first-principles normal form of the complete bridge is Theorem~\ref{thm:seven-rigidification-normal-form}, and its witnessed simplex-by-simplex realisation is Theorem~\ref{thm:cy3-witnessed-package-realises-seven}.

% ============================================================
\section{The two-stage factorisation \texorpdfstring{$\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}\circ\PhiFA_d$}{SpCh-Sigma-d-1-C o PhiFA-d}}
\label{sec:two-stage-organising-frame}
% ============================================================

On the verified loci, and after the relevant specialisation datum has
been fixed, the CY-to-chiral construction is the composite
\[
 \Phi_d^{(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)} \;\colon\;
 \CY_d\text{-}\Cat
 \xrightarrow{\;\PhiFA_d\;}
 \EdHolFA(X)
 \xrightarrow{\;\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}\;}
 \EnHolFA(C),
\]
with stage~$1$ the native holomorphic factorisation algebra on the
CY$_d$ variety $X$; assembled from the Gerstenhaber bracket of degree
$1 - d$ on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ by the conjunction of Kontsevich
$E_d$-formality~\cite{Kontsevich1999}, Tamarkin
deformation-quantisation~\cite{Tamarkin2007}, and
Costello--Gwilliam--Li holomorphic
locality~\cite{CostelloGwilliam2017,CostelloLi2016,CostelloLi2020} --
and stage~$2$ the chiral specialisation $\int_{\Sigma_{d - 1}}(-)|_C$
along an admissible $(d - 1)$-dimensional cycle
$\Sigma_{d - 1} \subset X$ and a reference curve $C \subset X$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation};
	Definition~\ref{def:phi-fa-and-sp}). After the relevant
$E_d$-formality / associator datum is fixed, the residual choices in
Stage~$1$ are contractible on the verified loci; before that datum is
fixed, the $d=3$ ambiguity is the standard $\GRT_1(\Q)$-torsor.
Stage~$2$ is determined by the geometric datum $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$.

The four-step cyclic-to-chiral passage constructed in this chapter --
cyclic $\Ainf \to$ Lie conformal algebra $\to$ factorisation envelope
$\to$ quantisation; is the computational incarnation of
$\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}$ composed with the explicit realisation of
$\PhiFA_d$. The Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}_\cC$ is the
one-dimensional shadow of the $E_d$-holomorphic factorisation algebra
$\PhiFA_d(\cC)$ on $X$ under pushforward along a codimension-one cycle;
the factorisation envelope $\Fact_C(\mathfrak{L}_\cC)$ restores the
chiral structure on the reference curve. Native operadic level on $C$ is
$n_{\mathrm{native}}(d) = \infty, 2, 1$ at $d = 1, 2, d \geq 3$
respectively (Proposition~\ref{prop:native-en-level}); the braided
$\Etwo$-enhancement at $d \geq 3$ lives on the derived chiral centre of
$\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC)$, not on $A_\cC$ itself
(Remark~\ref{rem:e2-on-drinfeld-centre}). A compact CY$_d$ admitting
several admissible $\Sigma_{d-1}$-fibrations generates a family of
$E_{n(d)}$-chiral shadows of the single native $\PhiFA_d(\cC)$
(Remark~\ref{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations}); the six routes to
$G(K3 \times E)$ are six distinct constructions/specialisations of this
kind, not six applications of $\Phi_3$ to one object.

\subsection{The two-stage factorisation \texorpdfstring{$\PhiFA_d$}{PhiFA-d} and \texorpdfstring{$\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}$}{SpCh-Sigma-d-1, C}}
\label{subsec:phi-fa-spch-framework}

The CY-to-chiral construction $\Phi_d$ of Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral}
factors, on the verified loci, through a native stage that lands in
holomorphic factorisation algebras on the CY$_d$ variety, followed by a
specialisation stage that descends to $E_n$-chiral algebras on a
reference curve. The native stage is pinned only after the relevant
formality / associator datum is fixed; before that datum is chosen its
ambiguity is the corresponding formality torsor, not a canonical
identification. The specialisation stage depends on the geometric
datum of an admissible $(d-1)$-dimensional cycle and a reference curve;
at $d=3$, the exact kernel and functoriality statements use the
witnessed admissible datum of
Definition~\ref{def:witnessed-admissible-specialisation-datum}.

\begin{definition}[Native holomorphic factorisation algebra; specialisation assignment]
\label{def:phi-fa-and-sp}
Let $\cC$ be a CY$_d$ category and $X$ a smooth CY$_d$ variety over $\C$ (including $X = \C^d$ in the non-compact setting).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The \emph{native holomorphic factorisation algebra} of $\cC$ on $X$ is the $E_d$-factorisation algebra
 \[
 \PhiFA_d(\cC) \in \EdHolFA(X)
 \]
 assembled from the Gerstenhaber bracket of degree $1 - d$ on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ by the conjunction of Kontsevich $E_d$-formality~\cite{Kontsevich1999} and Tamarkin deformation-quantisation~\cite{Tamarkin2007}, followed by Costello--Gwilliam--Li holomorphic locality~\cite{CostelloGwilliam2017,CostelloLi2016,CostelloLi2020} on $X$. It satisfies factorisation over $d$-dimensional opens: $\PhiFA_d(\cC)(U \sqcup V) \simeq \PhiFA_d(\cC)(U) \otimes \PhiFA_d(\cC)(V)$ for disjoint opens $U, V \subset X$. For \(d\geq4\) this definition names the conditional stage-$1$ template until the corresponding holomorphic-twist, framing, and completion witnesses are constructed.
 \item For an admissible $(d - 1)$-dimensional cycle $\Sigma_{d - 1} \subset X$ and a reference curve $C \subset X$, the \emph{chiral specialisation functor}
 \[
 \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C} \colon \EdHolFA(X) \longrightarrow \EnHolFA(C)
 \]
 sends an $E_d$-hFA $\cF$ to its restriction-after-pushforward along $\Sigma_{d-1}$ and the inclusion $C \hookrightarrow X$:
 \[
 \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}(\cF) \;=\; \Bigl(\textstyle\int_{\Sigma_{d-1}} \cF\Bigr)\Big|_C.
 \]
 The pushforward $\int_{\Sigma_{d - 1}}$ is factorisation homology (Lurie, Francis--Gaitsgory); the residual operadic level on $C$ is $n_{\mathrm{native}}(d)$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:native-en-level} below.
 At $d=3$, exactness, functoriality, and the pushforward/kernel formula
 are the proof-grade statements of
 Proposition~\ref{prop:spch-infty1-kernel}; they require a witnessed
 admissible datum, not only the notation of a cycle and a curve.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Two-stage factorisation of \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_d$}{Phi-d}]
\label{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{Formal two-stage factorisation on constructed loci; the stage-$1$ construction for \(d\geq4\) remains conditional.}
For every $d \geq 1$, on the verified loci and for a specialisation
datum $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$ \textup{(}witnessed admissible at $d=3$ in
the sense of
Definition~\ref{def:witnessed-admissible-specialisation-datum}\textup{),}
the CY-to-chiral construction
factors as
\[
 \Phi_d^{(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)} \;\colon\;
 \CY_d\text{-}\Cat
 \xrightarrow{\;\PhiFA_d\;}
 \EdHolFA(X)
 \xrightarrow{\;\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}\;}
 \EnHolFA(C).
\]
After fixing the relevant $E_d$-formality / associator datum, Stage~1
is the corresponding torsor-pointed construction on those loci; it is
assembled from the Gerstenhaber bracket of degree $1 - d$ on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ together with the
conjunction of Kontsevich $E_d$-formality~\cite{Kontsevich1999},
Tamarkin deformation-quantisation~\cite{Tamarkin2007}, and
Costello--Gwilliam--Li holomorphic
locality~\cite{CostelloGwilliam2017,CostelloLi2016,CostelloLi2020} on
the CY$_d$ target; at $d=3$, before this choice is fixed, the
formality ambiguity is the standard $\GRT_1(\Q)$-torsor. Stage~2 is
determined by the specialisation datum
$(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$; on morphisms of witnessed specialisation data,
where the properness, Tor-independence, Beck--Chevalley,
Fubini/envelope, compact-support, holomorphic-propagator, and
completion cells are supplied, the assignment
$(\Sigma_{d-1}, C) \mapsto \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}(\PhiFA_d(\cC))$ is
functorial, symmetric monoidal under disjoint union of witnessed cycles,
and Künneth-compatible under products of CY$_d$ varieties on that
witnessed locus. Beyond it, pushforward/envelope commutation remains a
separate proof obligation.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Native operadic level on the curve]
\label{prop:native-en-level}
Let $\cC$ be a CY$_d$ category and $A_\cC = \Phi_d(\cC) = \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}(\PhiFA_d(\cC))$ its chiral-algebra shadow on a reference curve~$C$. The native operadic level of $A_\cC$ is
\[
 n_{\mathrm{native}}(d) \;=\; \begin{cases} \infty, & d = 1, \\ 2, & d = 2, \\ 1, & d \geq 3. \end{cases}
\]
The Gerstenhaber bracket on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ has degree $1 - d$: at $d = 1$ the bracket vanishes on rank grounds and $A_\cC$ is $\Einf$-commutative; at $d = 2$ the bracket is the degree-$(-1)$ Schouten--Nijenhuis bracket and $A_\cC$ is $\Etwo$-braided; at $d \geq 3$ Serre duality antisymmetrises the Euler form and $A_\cC$ is $\Eone$-ordered.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Three-step decomposition of stage~1 \texorpdfstring{$\PhiFA_d$}{PhiFA-d}]
\label{prop:phi-fa-three-step-assembly}
\ClaimStatusConditional{\textup{(}on $d\leq2$ and on the verified $d=3$ chain-level locus\textup{)}}
Fix $d \in \{1,2,3\}$. Under hypotheses \textup{(H1)} $\cC$ smooth proper over a field of characteristic zero, \textup{(H2)} $\HH^0(\cC) = k$ \textup{(}connected unit\textup{),} \textup{(H3)} non-degenerate cyclic pairing in degree $-d$, and \textup{(H4)} the target $X$ a smooth complex CY$_d$ variety with a chosen Calabi--Yau form $\Omega_X \in H^0(X, K_X)$, with the $d=3$ chain-level framing and Costello--Li witness of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} imposed when $d=3$, stage~$1$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation} factors as
\[
 \cC
 \xrightarrow{\;(\mathrm{a})\;}
 E_d\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}(\HH^\bullet(\cC))
 \xrightarrow{\;(\mathrm{b})\;}
 \mathrm{FactAlg}_{E_d}^{\mathrm{top}}(X)
 \xrightarrow{\;(\mathrm{c})\;}
 \EdHolFA(X).
\]
Step~\textup{(a)} is Kontsevich $E_d$-formality~\cite{Kontsevich1999} together with Tamarkin deformation-quantisation~\cite{Tamarkin2007}: under \textup{(H1)--(H3)} the Gerstenhaber bracket of degree $1 - d$ on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ lifts, after choosing the relevant formality / associator datum, to an $E_d$-algebra structure on the Hochschild cochain complex. Step~\textup{(b)} is Costello--Gwilliam locality on the topological space underlying $X$~\cite{CostelloGwilliam2017}: the $E_d$-algebra of step~\textup{(a)} assembles into a topological $E_d$-factorisation algebra on $X$ by the factorisation-envelope construction. Step~\textup{(c)} is the Costello--Li holomorphic twist~\cite{CostelloLi2016,CostelloLi2020} together with the Calabi--Yau form $\Omega_X$: the BV data determined by $\Omega_X$ refine the topological $E_d$-FA of step~\textup{(b)} to an $E_d$-holomorphic FA on $X$. Steps~\textup{(a)} and~\textup{(b)} are source-theorem inputs under \textup{(H1)--(H4)}. Step~\textup{(c)} is unconditional at $d \leq 2$; at $d=3$ the added chain-level framing and Costello--Li witness turn the holomorphic-twist obstruction into the verified Stage-$1$ construction of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} and Chapter~\ref{ch:m3-b2-obstruction}. The same three-step diagram beyond these verified loci is the template governed by Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality}, not a weakening of the proved $d\leq3$ statement.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Three-step slippage made precise: \texorpdfstring{$E_d$}{E-d}-algebra vs.\ topological \texorpdfstring{$E_d$}{E-d}-FA vs.\ \texorpdfstring{$E_d$}{E-d}-holomorphic FA]
\label{rem:three-step-slippage-precise}
The phrase ``Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality and Costello--Gwilliam--Li locality'' in Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation} names a composite of three operations whose distinctness matters at the chain level. The $E_d$-algebra output of step~\textup{(a)} lives on a single point (the Hochschild cochain complex of $\cC$); the $E_d$-factorisation-algebra output of step~\textup{(b)} lives on the topological space underlying $X$ and depends on disc-configuration combinatorics, not on complex structure; the $E_d$-holomorphic factorisation algebra output of step~\textup{(c)} requires the Dolbeault operator of $X$ and the Calabi--Yau form $\Omega_X$, and is a chain-level refinement that uses the CY$_d$ pairing in degree $-d$ to rigidify the BV data. At $d = 1$ all three steps are trivial (the bracket vanishes). At $d = 2$ step~\textup{(c)} is the Kontsevich--Vlassopoulos $\bS^2$-framing and proceeds unconditionally. At $d \geq 3$ step~\textup{(c)} is the $\bS^d$-framing question and the chain-level content of the $[m_3, B^{(2)}]$ obstruction of Chapter~\ref{ch:m3-b2-obstruction}.
\end{remark}

% ============================================================
\subsubsection{Kontsevich--Tamarkin $E_3$-formality over $\Q$ and the $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-torsor on Stage-$1$ of $\PhiFA_3$}
\label{subsubsec:kt-e3-formality-grt1-torsor-cfg2026}
% ============================================================

Step~(a) of Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-fa-three-step-assembly} at $d = 3$ promotes the Gerstenhaber bracket of degree $-2$ on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ to an $E_3$-algebra structure on the Hochschild cochain complex. The lift is not unique; the space of such lifts is a torsor under a pro-unipotent $\Q$-algebraic group. The present subsection constructs the torsor from the configuration-space ground up, names its structure group, proves invariance of the four $\kappa_\bullet$-invariants, and places the Costello--Francis--Gwilliam $2026$ topological Chern--Simons $E_3$-structure in the same universal torsor.

\begin{proposition}[$E_3$-formality of the little $3$-disks operad over $\Q$]
\label{prop:cfg-e3-formality-rational}
\ClaimStatusProvedElsewhere
The singular chain operad $C_\bullet(D_3; \Q)$ of the little $3$-disks operad is rationally formal: there exists a zig-zag of operadic quasi-isomorphisms
\[
 C_\bullet(D_3; \Q) \;\xleftarrow{\;\sim\;}\; \mathrm{Graphs}_3 \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\; \mathrm{Ger}_3
\]
in $\mathrm{Op}(\mathrm{Ch}(\Q))$, with $\mathrm{Graphs}_3$ the Kontsevich graph operad weighted by compactified-configuration-space integrals (Kontsevich~\cite{Kontsevich1999}, Tamarkin~\cite{Tamarkin2007}), and $\mathrm{Ger}_3$ the Gerstenhaber operad with commutative product of degree $0$ and graded Lie bracket $\{-, -\}$ of degree $-2$ (F.~Cohen $1976$, \textit{LNM}~$533$: $H^\bullet(F(k, \R^n); \Q) = \mathrm{Ger}_n$). The extension to $n \geq 3$ is Fresse--Willwacher, \emph{The intrinsic formality of $E_n$-operads}, \textit{J.~Eur.~Math.~Soc.}~$22$ ($2020$), no.~$7$, $2165$--$2238$, arXiv:$1503.08699$, Theorem~$A$: the $E_n$-operad is intrinsically formal over $\Q$ for every $n \geq 3$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[Formality space at $d = 3$ is a $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-torsor]
\label{thm:cfg-form3-grt1-torsor}
\ClaimStatusProvedElsewhere
The set $\mathrm{Form}_3(\Q)$ of $E_3$-formality quasi-isomorphisms $F \colon C_\bullet(D_3; \Q) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathrm{Ger}_3$ in $\mathrm{Op}(\mathrm{Ch}(\Q))$, modulo operadic homotopy, is a free and transitive torsor under the pro-unipotent Grothendieck--Teichm\"uller group
\[
 \mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q) \;=\; \mathrm{Spec}\bigl(H^0(\mathrm{GC}_2; \Q)^\vee\bigr),
\]
the pro-unipotent $\Q$-algebraic group spectrum of the Kontsevich graph complex $\mathrm{GC}_2$ in two directions (Willwacher~\cite{Willwacher2015} Theorem~$1.1$, extended to $n = 3$ by Fresse--Willwacher, \textit{J.~Eur.~Math.~Soc.}~$22$ ($2020$), $2165$--$2238$, arXiv:$1503.08699$, Theorem~$B$). The torsor is non-trivial; every two formality quasi-isomorphisms differ by a unique $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-element.
\end{theorem}

\begin{definition}[Space of Drinfeld $\Q$-associators]
\label{def:drinfeld-q-associator-cfg}
The set $\mathrm{Assoc}(\Q)$ of Drinfeld $\Q$-associators consists of group-like elements $\Phi \in \hat{U}(\mathfrak{t}_3)_{\geq 2}$ in the completed universal enveloping algebra of the infinitesimal-pure-braid Lie algebra $\mathfrak{t}_3 = \langle t_{12}, t_{13}, t_{23} \mid [t_{ij}, t_{ik} + t_{jk}] = 0,\; [t_{ij}, t_{kl}] = 0 \text{ for disjoint pairs} \rangle$ satisfying the pentagon and the two hexagon relations. Drinfeld~\cite{Drinfeld1990}, \textit{Leningrad Math.~J.}~$2$ ($1991$), $829$--$860$ \emph{On quasitriangular quasi-Hopf algebras and a group closely connected with $\mathrm{Gal}(\overline{\Q}/\Q)$}, Theorem~$A'$: $\mathrm{Assoc}(\Q)$ is non-empty, constructed iteratively from degree data, and the $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-action on it is free and transitive. The Knizhnik--Zamolodchikov associator $\Phi_{\mathrm{KZ}} \in \mathrm{Assoc}(\C)$ has coefficients multiple zeta values.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Stage-$1$ pinning on $\PhiFA_3$ via formality torsor]
\label{thm:cfg-e3-formality-stage-1-phiFA3-torsor}
\ClaimStatusConditional{} \textup{(torsor pinning on the verified Stage-$1$ holomorphic locus, after fixing the formality and holomorphic-twist witnesses)}
Let $\cC \in \CY_3\text{-}\Cat^{\mathrm{dg}}$ and $X$ a smooth complex CY$_3$ with $\Omega_X \in H^0(X, K_X)$. Step~\textup{(a)} of Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-fa-three-step-assembly} is implemented by choosing $F \in \mathrm{Form}_3(\Q)$; this choice produces an $E_3$-algebra structure
\[
 \CC^\bullet(\cC, \cC) \;\in\; E_3\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}(\mathrm{Ch}(\Q))
\]
whose underlying commutative product is the Gerstenhaber cup $\cup$ and whose underlying degree-$(-2)$ Lie bracket is the Gerstenhaber bracket $\{-, -\}_G$. Different choices $F, F' \in \mathrm{Form}_3(\Q)$ related by $F' = g \cdot F$ with $g \in \mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$ produce the same cohomological $\mathrm{Ger}_3$-structure on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ and differ by the standard formality-torsor action at the Hochschild $E_3$ level. Hence Stage-$1$ of $\PhiFA_3$ is pinned only after fixing the torsor point: on the verified holomorphic locus the corresponding objects $\PhiFA_3(\cC)_F, \PhiFA_3(\cC)_{F'} \in \EdHolFA(X)^{(\infty,1)}$ are related by the transported $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$ action, not by a canonical equality before that datum is fixed.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof]
Theorem~\ref{thm:cfg-form3-grt1-torsor} and Proposition~\ref{prop:cfg-e3-formality-rational} supply the formality torsor; Dolgushev--Tamarkin--Tsygan~\cite{DolgushevTamarkinTsygan2010} establishes the homotopy-calculus formality of Hochschild cochains that realises $\CC^\bullet(\cC, \cC)$ as an $E_3$-algebra once $F$ is fixed, the $\mathrm{GRT}_1$-action on $F$ being Tamarkin $2003$~\cite{Tamarkin2007} Corollary~$4.2$. Costello--Gwilliam~\cite{CostelloGwilliam2017} assembly of $E_3$-algebras into topological factorisation algebras is $(\infty,1)$-functorial. On the Stage-$1$ verified holomorphic locus, the Costello--Li twist~\cite{CostelloLi2016,CostelloLi2020} transports this torsor action to the corresponding holomorphic factorisation algebra; outside that locus this is part of the CY$_3$ strictification problem, not an automatic consequence of the formality torsor.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[CY$_3$ self-duality does not rigidify the $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-torsor]
\label{prop:cfg-cy3-self-duality-torsor-not-rigidified}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
The CY$_3$ self-duality $\omega_X \cong \cO_X$ furnished by $\Omega_X$ provides a symmetric pairing on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ in cohomological degree $-3$ compatible with the $\mathrm{Ger}_3$-structure, but does not select a point in $\mathrm{Form}_3(\Q)$. The pairing-preserving subtorsor of $\mathrm{Form}_3(\Q)$ is the full $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-torsor.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The cyclic structure on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ is the Serre-duality trace $\mathrm{tr} \colon \HH_3(\cC) \to k$, a cohomological datum fixed by $\Omega_X$. The $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-action on $\mathrm{Form}_3(\Q)$ modifies the higher-arity chain-level operations of $F$ but fixes the pairing-degree of the operad: the Kontsevich graph cocycles generating $\mathrm{GRT}_1$ have arity $\geq 3$ and internal-valence $\geq 2$, sitting above the pairing layer. Cyclic $L_\infty$-formality (Kontsevich--Soibelman $2008$, \emph{Notes on $A_\infty$-algebras, $A_\infty$-categories and non-commutative geometry I}, arXiv:math/$0606241$, Theorem~$11.2.1$) defines the $\mathrm{GRT}_1^{\mathrm{cyc}}$-torsor, naturally isomorphic to $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$ at every even CY dimension. The cyclic constraint preserves the full torsor structure: the CY pairing is a parallel-transport datum on the torsor, not a reduction of structure group.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[$\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-invariance of the four $\kappa_\bullet$-invariants]
\label{cor:cfg-grt1-invariance-kappa}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For every $\cC \in \CY_3\text{-}\Cat^{\mathrm{dg}}$ and every specialisation datum $(\Sigma_2, C)$ on which the following quantities are defined \textup{(}witnessed admissible whenever the $\Phi_3$ Stage-$2$ specialisation is used\textup{)}, the four CY$_3$ invariants
\[
 \kch\bigl(\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)}(\cC)\bigr),
 \quad
 \kcat(\cC) = \chi(\cO_X),
 \quad
 \kBKM(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2,
 \quad
 \kappa_{\mathrm{fibre}}(\cC)
\]
are $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-invariant: each depends on the $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-orbit of $F \in \mathrm{Form}_3(\Q)$, not on the individual torsor point. The term $\kBKM(\Phi_N)=c_N(0)/2$ is included only after a Borcherds input has been chosen.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
$\kch$ is the Heisenberg--Cartan generator count on $\HH^{\bullet + 1}(\cC)$, a cohomological invariant preserved by the formality map. $\kcat(\cC) = \chi(\cO_X) = \sum_q (-1)^q h^{0, q}(X)$ is the Hodge supertrace, a topological invariant of $X$; the graph-complex action does not touch Hodge numbers. $\kBKM(\Phi_N) = c_N(0) / 2$ is the Fourier coefficient of a Borcherds-lifted automorphic form, read from a modular-arithmetic datum external to the chain-level formality. $\kappa_{\mathrm{fibre}} = \mathrm{rank}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{Muk}})$ is the Mukai-lattice rank, a derived-categorical invariant. All four factor through the $\mathrm{GRT}_1$-orbit.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Side-by-side with Costello--Francis--Gwilliam $2026$ topological Chern--Simons]
\label{rem:cfg2026-topological-CS-side-by-side-e3-torsor}
The Costello--Francis--Gwilliam~$2026$ result~\cite{CFG2026} establishes, for ordinary real $3$d Chern--Simons theory, a locally constant filtered $E_3$ factorisation algebra on $3$-balls and computes its factorisation-homology traces. It is the topological analogue and test oracle for the Stage-$1$ grammar of $\PhiFA_3$, not the source theorem for the CY$_3$ Dolbeault object. The chain-level comparison is:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{ll}
\toprule
Costello--Francis--Gwilliam~$2026$ & CY$_3$ Stage-$1$ $\PhiFA_3$ \\
\midrule
Topological $3$-manifold $M^3$ & Complex CY$_3$ variety $X$ \\
Reductive gauge $\frakg$ & Polyvector dg-Lie $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ \\
$I_{\mathrm{CS}} = \int_{M^3} \cA d\cA + \tfrac{1}{3}\cA^3$ & $I_{\hCS} = \int_X \Omega_X \wedge (\tfrac{1}{2}\langle \alpha, \dbar\alpha\rangle + \tfrac{1}{6}\langle \alpha, [\alpha, \alpha]\rangle)$ \\
Topological observables $\Obs^{\mathrm{CS}}_{\mathrm{top}}(M^3, \frakg)$ & Holomorphic observables $\Obs^{\hCS}(X, \HH^\bullet(\cC))$ \\
$E_3$-algebra structure on $\Obs^{\mathrm{CS}}_{\mathrm{top}}$ & $E_3$-holomorphic FA structure on $\PhiFA_3(\cC)$ \\
$\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-torsor of formalities & $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-torsor of formalities \\
$\int_{M^3}$ factorisation homology & $\SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C}$ specialisation \\
Topological target $\mathrm{Ch}(\Q)$ & Holomorphic target $\mathrm{ShvFact}^{\mathrm{hol}}(X)$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The two constructions use the same operadic grammar of little $3$-discs
after a formality / associator datum is fixed, but this does not identify
their local observable objects. CFG's ordinary CS model reaches
$C^\bullet(\frakg)$ by local constancy on real balls. The CY$_3$ object
keeps the Dolbeault differential, holomorphic jets, polydisc residues, and
the local model of Definition~\ref{def:cy3-many-variable-chiral-ce}. The
conditional map $\Theta_{\hCS \to \Hall}$ of
Problem~\ref{op:cy3-hcs-hall-comparison} realises the further passage from
$\PhiFA_3$ to the Hall/CoHA side; it is a separate oriented comparison
datum, not a consequence of CFG formality.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Non-rigidifications: what the torsor does not do]
\label{rem:cfg-grt1-torsor-non-rigidifications-d3}
The $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-torsor on Stage-$1$ of $\PhiFA_3$ does not supply a canonical associator, does not rigidify the chain-level $\bS^3$-framing obstruction of Chapter~\ref{ch:m3-b2-obstruction} (a separate homotopy datum), and does not commute with Stage-$2$ specialisation (the restriction $E_3 \to E_1$ destroys the higher-arity operations visible to $\mathrm{GRT}_1$). The torsor is compatible with Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}'s framing hypothesis~\textup{(H4)} but does not imply it. Over $\C$ the Knizhnik--Zamolodchikov associator $\Phi_{\mathrm{KZ}}$ is distinguished by multiple zeta values (Drinfeld~\cite{Drinfeld1990}; Brown $2012$ \textit{Ann.~Math.}~$175$: MZVs generate the motivic fundamental-group Lie algebra of $\mathbb{P}^1 \setminus \{0, 1, \infty\}$); over $\Q$ no single associator is distinguished.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[$\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-torsor at $d = 2$ and $d = 3$: the super-refinement]
\label{rem:cfg-grt1-d2-vs-d3}
At $d = 2$ the Gerstenhaber bracket on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ has degree $-1$ (odd integer), so the super-signed refinement $\mathrm{GRT}_1^{\mathrm{super}}(\Q)$ enters the $E_2$-formality torsor at the level of the Etingof--Kazhdan quantisation data (Remark~\ref{rem:cycat-ce-motivic-fundamental-group}; \texttt{quantum\_groups\_foundations.tex} Theorem~\ref{thm:qgf-grt1-bkm-transitivity}). At $d = 3$ the bracket has degree $-2$ (even integer), and the Koszul sign shift induced by odd-degree operators is trivial: the formality torsor is the non-super $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$. The super-decoration at $d = 2$ affects the representation of the torsor on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$, not the underlying pro-unipotent group, which is $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$ in both dimensions.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Per-\texorpdfstring{$d$}{d} canonical specialisation cycle]
\label{rem:per-d-canonical-cycle}
The two-stage factorisation $\Phi_d = \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C} \circ \PhiFA_d$ determines the specialisation datum $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$ only up to homotopy of $(d-1)$-cycles in $X$; canonical choices exist per CY class but not uniformly across the CY$_d$ landscape.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item $d = 1$. $\Sigma_0 \subset X$ is a $0$-cycle; for an elliptic curve $X = E$ the canonical choice is $(\Sigma_0, C) = (\{\mathrm{pt}\}, E)$, factorisation homology over a point is the identity, and $\PhiFA_1$ evaluated at the point recovers the rank-$2$ Heisenberg generated by $H^0(E) \oplus H^1(E)$.
 \item $d = 2$. $\Sigma_1 \subset X$ is a real $1$-cycle realised as a genuine curve in the CY$_2$ target, \emph{not} a topological circle in a configuration space. For $X$ a K3 surface the canonical cycle is $\Sigma_1^{\mathrm{Nakajima}}$, the Nakajima cycle realising the K3 Hilbert scheme as factorisation homology (Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}). For $X$ an abelian surface $A = E_1 \times E_2$ the canonical cycle is $\Sigma_1 = \{\mathrm{pt}\} \times E_2 \sqcup E_1 \times \{\mathrm{pt}\}$; the two summands feed the two Heisenberg factors that appear in the $h^{1,0} \neq 0$ correction of Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}. The naive choice $\Sigma_1 = S^1$ embedded as a topological circle is \emph{not} canonical in this sense: the specialisation datum is a $1$-cycle in the CY variety, not a cycle in the configuration space of disc centres.
 \item $d = 3$. $\Sigma_2 \subset X$ is a complex surface
 specialisation datum, singled out per CY$_3$ class: for
 $X = K3 \times E$ the canonical datum is the K3 fibre
 $\Sigma_2 = p_E^{-1}(\mathrm{pt}) \simeq K3$ over the elliptic base
 $C=E$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}); for $X = A \times E$ the
 canonical datum is the abelian-surface fibre $A$; for the quintic
 $X = Q_5 \subset \bP^4$ no canonical $\Sigma_2$ is singled out and
 $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(Q_5)$ requires an explicit fixing of the
 specialisation datum in its statement.
 \item $d \geq 4$. No canonical cycle exists in general: a compact CY$_4$ admits a lattice of effective divisor classes, and different divisor classes produce different $\Phi_4$-shadows (Remark~\ref{rem:phi-d-parameter-tagging}, last clause). The $n_{\mathrm{native}}(d) = 1$ statement of Proposition~\ref{prop:native-en-level} is the residual operadic level surviving the specialisation, uniform in the cycle choice; the actual residual level at $d \geq 4$ depends on the homotopy type of $\Sigma_{d-1}$ and on whether the specialisation stabilises to an $\Eone$-ordered chiral output or to a higher level when $\Sigma_{d-1}$ is a product of closed odd-dimensional manifolds.
\end{enumerate}
In every abbreviation $\Phi_d(\cC)$ without an explicit $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$-tag, the implicit cycle is the canonical one fixed for the CY class at hand, or the statement uniformly quantifies over cycle choices.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Location of the braided \texorpdfstring{$\Etwo$}{E2} at \texorpdfstring{$d \geq 3$}{d >= 3}]
\label{rem:e2-on-drinfeld-centre}
At $d \geq 3$ the braided $\Etwo$-structure attached to $A_\cC$ lives on the derived chiral centre of its $\Eone$-monoidal representation category:
\[
 \cZ\bigl(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC)\bigr) \;\simeq\; \Rep^{\Etwo}\bigl(\Zder(A_\cC)\bigr)
\]
(Ben-Zvi--Francis--Nadler, Theorem~\ref{prop:e2-enhancement-drinfeld}). The $\bS^3$-framing at $d = 3$ supplies an $E_3$-structure on $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$; its Dunn restriction to $\Etwo$ is symmetric because $\pi_1(\mathrm{Conf}_2(\R^3)) = 0$, and the non-symmetric quantum-group $R$-matrix on $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC))$ records the half-braiding datum of the Drinfeld centre.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Multiple specialisations of one canonical \texorpdfstring{$\PhiFA_d$}{PhiFA-d}]
\label{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations}
A compact CY$_d$ $X$ admitting several $\Sigma_{d-1}$-fibrations carries a family of $\Eone$-chiral shadows
\(
 \{\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}(\PhiFA_d(\cC))\}_{\Sigma_{d-1}}
\)
on a fixed reference curve~$C$. At $d = 3$, the $K3$-fibration on $K3 \times E$ gives the witnessed Stage-$2$ Heisenberg shadow, and its Borcherds--$\Delta_5$ comparison is obtained only after the Mukai--Heisenberg reduction and Borcherds pushforward of Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}; the $T^4$-fibration on $A \times E$ gives the Igusa--$\chi_{10}$ shadow on its analogous hypotheses; Enriques-$\times E$ and K3-orbifold fibrations give the Gritsenko--Clery paramodular shadows only after their orbifold comparison data are constructed. Each constructed case is a genuine $\Eone$-chiral algebra on $C$, and each Borcherds-side weight is read from the chosen automorphic input by $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}=c(0)/2$, not from a bare Euler characteristic of the specialisation cycle. The multiplicity of BKM images attached to the chiral side is the multiplicity of constructed specialisation data $\Sigma_{d-1}$ through which the single canonical $\PhiFA_d(\cC)$ can be evaluated; it is not a multiplicity of $\Phi_d$-applications.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Parameter-tagged notation \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_d^{(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)}$}{Phi-d-(Sigma-d-1, C)} and reading conventions]
\label{rem:phi-d-parameter-tagging}
The two-stage factorisation of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation} makes the chiral-algebra output a function of two arguments: the CY datum $\cC$ (consumed by Stage~$1$) and the specialisation datum $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C) \subset X \times X$ (consumed by Stage~$2$). Three reading conventions are used throughout Vol~III.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Fully tagged:} $\Phi_d^{(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)}(\cC) := \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}(\PhiFA_d(\cC))$. Used whenever two distinct specialisation cycles must be compared on a single CY $X$, or when the chosen cycle is non-canonical (Corollary~\ref{cor:sibling-BKMs-from-one-phiFA}; Remark~\ref{rem:four-kappa-stage-assignment}).
 \item \emph{Canonically tagged:} at $d = 3$ on $X = K3 \times E$ with the distinguished K3-fibration $\Sigma_2 = p_E^{-1}(\mathrm{pt})$ and the elliptic reference curve $C = E$, the unqualified symbol $\Phi_3(D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E)))$ abbreviates $\Phi_3^{(K3, E)}(D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E)))$. The same abbreviation applies at $d = 2$: $\Phi(D^b(\Coh(K3)))$ abbreviates $\Phi_2^{(\Sigma_1^{\mathrm{Nakajima}}, C)}(D^b(\Coh(K3)))$ for the Nakajima cycle and reference curve of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}. In every such abbreviation the implicit $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$ is the canonical cycle fixed for that CY class.
 \item \emph{Stage-$1$ only:} $\PhiFA_d(\cC) \in \EdHolFA(X)$ names the canonical $E_d$-factorisation algebra on $X$ before any specialisation, without an implicit $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$. Used when the statement concerns properties shared by all specialisation outputs (Stage-$1$ invariants $\kcat$, and $\kappa_{\mathrm{fibre}}$ on the ambient) or when HMS / Künneth identifications act at the factorisation-algebra level (Chapter~\ref{ch:derived-cy}, Section~\ref{sec:db-coh-cy}; Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product}).
\end{enumerate}
On higher-dimensional hyperkähler inputs no canonical $\Sigma_{d-1}$ is singled out: the fibration class, the Lagrangian-section class, and the Beauville--Bogomolov isotropic class yield distinct $\Phi_d^{(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)}$-shadows. Any appearance of $\Phi_d$ at such an input must fix the cycle explicitly in its statement, or carry a scope clause indicating the statement holds uniformly in $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$. The Künneth proposition (Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product}) is a Stage-$1$-only statement: it lives in $\PhiFA_d$ and is agnostic to the specialisation datum, so that two different $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$-choices descending from $\PhiFA_{d_1} \boxtimes \PhiFA_{d_2}$ produce two different $\Phi_{d_1 + d_2}^{(\Sigma, C)}$-shadows that agree at the Stage-$1$ level.
\end{remark}

% ============================================================
\subsection{Derived-categorical rigour of \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_d$}{Phi-d}: source, target, kernel}
\label{subsec:phi-d-derived-rigour}
% ============================================================

The two-stage factorisation of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation} is ambiguous unless the source $\CY_d\text{-}\Cat$ is equipped with a specific sheaf of rings, the target $\EnHolFA(C)$ with an $(\infty,1)$-stable structure, and the functors $\PhiFA_d$, $\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}$ with their respective Fourier--Mukai-type kernels. This subsection supplies the data in the precise form required.

\begin{definition}[Source \texorpdfstring{$(\infty,1)$}{(inf,1)}-category \texorpdfstring{$\CY_d\text{-}\Cat^{\mathrm{dg}}$}{CY-d-Cat-dg}]
\label{def:cy-d-cat-dg}
The source of $\Phi_d$ is the full $(\infty,1)$-subcategory
\[
 \CY_d\text{-}\Cat^{\mathrm{dg}} \;\subset\; \mathrm{dgCat}^{\mathrm{sm,prop}}_k
\]
of smooth proper dg-categories over $k = \C$, closed under shifts, whose objects $\cC$ satisfy \textup{(H1)} smooth proper, \textup{(H2)} $\HH^0(\cC) = k$, \textup{(H3)} a negative-cyclic Calabi--Yau class $[\sigma_\cC]\in HC^-_d(\cC)$ whose Hochschild shadow is a non-degenerate cyclic pairing $\tau_\cC \colon \HH_d(\cC) \to k$ of degree $-d$, witnessed by a quasi-isomorphism $\cC \xrightarrow{\sim} \cC^\vee[d]$ of $\cC$-bimodules (Kontsevich--Soibelman~$2006$; Ginzburg~$2006$ arXiv:\texttt{math/0612139}). Morphisms are cyclic $\Ainf$-functors compatible with $[\sigma_\cC]$ and $\tau_\cC$. The canonical functor
\[
 D^b(\Coh(X)) \;\in\; \CY_d\text{-}\Cat^{\mathrm{dg}} \quad \text{(for $X$ a smooth projective CY$_d$ variety)}
\]
is represented by its unique dg-enhancement $\mathrm{Perf}^{\mathrm{dg}}(X)$ (To\"en $2007$, \textit{Inventiones}~$167$ Theorem~$8.15$; Lunts--Orlov~$2010$, \textit{J.~Amer.~Math.~Soc.}~$23$ Corollary~$6.11$: uniqueness of the dg-enhancement of $D^b(\Coh(X))$ for smooth projective $X$). On compact strict-stratum inputs $D^b(\Coh(X))$ arises as the homotopy category $H^0(\mathrm{Perf}^{\mathrm{dg}}(X))$. For local quiver charts, the source is the $3$CY Ginzburg/perfect or matrix-factorisation category attached to $(Q,W)$; the critical CoHA is the Hall-side associative algebra extracted from its moduli, not an object of $\CY_d\text{-}\Cat^{\mathrm{dg}}$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Target \texorpdfstring{$(\infty,1)$}{(inf,1)}-stable category \texorpdfstring{$\EnHolFA(C)^{(\infty,1)}$}{EnHolFA(C)-(inf,1)}]
\label{def:en-hol-fa-infty}
For a smooth complex curve $C$ and $n \in \{1, 2, \infty\}$, the $(\infty,1)$-stable category of $E_n$-holomorphic factorisation algebras on $C$ is
\[
 \EnHolFA(C)^{(\infty,1)} \;=\; \mathrm{Alg}_{E_n}\bigl(\mathrm{ShvFact}^{\mathrm{hol}}(C, \mathrm{Ch}(k))\bigr),
\]
the $(\infty,1)$-category of $E_n$-algebras in the $(\infty,1)$-category of holomorphic factorisation cosheaves on $\Ran(C)$ valued in chain complexes over $k$ (Francis--Gaitsgory~$2012$~\cite{FrancisGaitsgory2012} Theorem~$1.2.4$ for the chiral-Koszul equivalence $\mathrm{Lie}\text{-alg}^{\mathrm{ch}} \simeq \mathrm{Com}\text{-coalg}^{\mathrm{ch,fact}}$ on $\Ran X$; Costello--Gwilliam~\cite{CostelloGwilliam} Vol.~I Chapter~$6$ for the Weiss-cosheaf local-to-global axiom; Lurie \textit{Higher Algebra}~$5.5.4$ for factorizable cosheaves). It is presentable and stable: shifts $[1]$, cofibre sequences, and filtered colimits exist. Dunn additivity (Lurie, \textit{HA}~Theorem~$5.1.2.2$) supplies the restriction functor
\[
 \mathrm{res}_{E_n \to E_{n-1}} \colon \EnHolFA(C)^{(\infty,1)} \longrightarrow E_{n-1}\text{-}\HolFA(C)^{(\infty,1)}
\]
and the chiral-bar functor $B^{\mathrm{ord}}$ of Vol~I is the right adjoint in the Vol~I bar-cobar adjunction
\[
 \Omega^{\mathrm{ch}} \;\dashv\; B^{\mathrm{ord}} \colon \Eone\text{-}\HolFA(C)^{(\infty,1)} \;\longrightarrow\; \mathrm{ChirCoAlg}^{\mathrm{conil}}(C)^{(\infty,1)}
\]
of Vol~I Theorem~A.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Intermediate \texorpdfstring{$(\infty,1)$}{(inf,1)}-stable category \texorpdfstring{$\EdHolFA(X)^{(\infty,1)}$}{EdHolFA(X)-(inf,1)}]
\label{def:ed-hol-fa-infty}
For $X$ a smooth complex CY$_d$ variety the intermediate category is
\[
 \EdHolFA(X)^{(\infty,1)} \;=\; \mathrm{Alg}_{E_d}\bigl(\mathrm{ShvFact}^{\mathrm{hol}}(X, \mathrm{Ch}(k))\bigr),
\]
the $(\infty,1)$-category of $E_d$-algebras in holomorphic factorisation cosheaves on $X$ (Costello--Li~\cite{CostelloLi2016,CostelloLi2020}, holomorphic twist of the Costello--Gwilliam~\cite{CostelloGwilliam2017} topological factorisation framework). It is presentable and stable; the unit $\mathbf{1}$ is the constant hFA $\cO_X$; disjoint union gives the symmetric-monoidal structure.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Witnessed admissible specialisation datum]
\label{def:witnessed-admissible-specialisation-datum}
\ClaimStatusDefinitional{}
Let $X$ be a smooth CY$_3$ target and let
$\cF\in\Ethree\HolFA(X)^{(\infty,1)}$ be an already constructed
Stage-$1$ holomorphic factorisation algebra. A witnessed admissible
specialisation datum from $X$ to a smooth curve $C_0$ is the tuple
\[
 \mathfrak s=(\Sigma_2,i_{C_0},\nu_{\Sigma},\cO_{\Sigma_2\times C_0},
 \mathrm{BC}_{\mathfrak s},\mathrm{Fub}_{\mathfrak s})
\]
consisting of:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item a compact or properly supported holomorphic surface
$\Sigma_2\subset X$ with oriented tubular normal datum
$\nu_{\Sigma}$ and a fixed compact-support convention;
\item a holomorphic embedding $i_{C_0}\colon C_0\hookrightarrow X$;
\item an incidence kernel $\cO_{\Sigma_2\times C_0}$ on
$X\times C_0$ whose projections are proper on support and
Tor-independent for the factorisation-cosheaf tensor product;
\item a Beck--Chevalley $2$-cell $\mathrm{BC}_{\mathfrak s}$ identifying
pushforward along $\Sigma_2$ followed by restriction to $C_0$ with the
kernel formula
$(\pi_{C_0})_*(\pi_X^*(-)\otimes^{\bL}\cO_{\Sigma_2\times C_0})$;
\item a Fubini/envelope $2$-cell $\mathrm{Fub}_{\mathfrak s}$ recording
the compatibility of this pushforward with the chosen
$E_3$-factorisation products, compact supports, and holomorphic
propagator conventions.
\end{enumerate}
If a Hall, stable-envelope, or Borcherds comparison is invoked, the
datum is enlarged by the corresponding orientation local system,
vanishing-cycle normalisation, chamber/polarisation/slope data, and
banding-pullback compatibility. A morphism of witnessed data is a
cyclic Fourier--Mukai or $\Ainf$ kernel together with coherent
isomorphisms preserving every item above, including the formality point,
negative-cyclic CY class, $\bS^3$-framing homotopy, anomaly-cancellation
witness, and completion.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$\PhiFA_d$}{PhiFA-d} assembly on verified loci; Fourier--Mukai-type kernel]
\label{prop:phifa-infty1-kernel}
\ClaimStatusConditional{} \textup{(object-level Stage-$1$ assembly on verified loci; arbitrary morphism functoriality is separated in Proposition~\ref{prop:phifa-infty1-morphism-kernel-conditional})}
The stage-$1$ assembly of Definition~\ref{def:phi-fa-and-sp} has a proved object-level $(\infty,1)$-categorical kernel on the loci where the holomorphic-twist and framing data are constructed:
\[
 \cC \;\longmapsto\; \PhiFA_d(\cC) \in \EdHolFA(X)^{(\infty,1)},
\]
represented by a \emph{Fourier--Mukai-type integral kernel}: on geometric inputs $\cC = \mathrm{Perf}^{\mathrm{dg}}(X)$ satisfying those hypotheses, $\PhiFA_d(\mathrm{Perf}^{\mathrm{dg}}(X))$ is the $E_d$-holomorphic factorisation algebra of observables of $(2d)$-real-dimensional holomorphic Chern--Simons on $X$ with gauge dg-Lie $\HH^\bullet(\mathrm{Perf}^{\mathrm{dg}}(X)) \cong \bigoplus_{p+q=\bullet} H^q(X, \wedge^p T_X)$, and its integral-representation kernel is the Costello--Li holomorphic-twist BV kernel
\[
 \cK_{\PhiFA_d}(X) \;\in\; \mathrm{PreFA}^{\mathrm{hol}}_{E_d}\!\bigl(X \times X^\vee\bigr)
\]
with $X^\vee \cong X$ under Serre duality \textup{(}CY form
$\Omega_X$ trivialises the canonical bundle\textup{)}. No arbitrary
morphism functoriality is asserted in this proposition.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Step~(a) of Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-fa-three-step-assembly} (Kontsevich $E_d$-formality plus Tamarkin deformation-quantisation) upgrades $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ to an $E_d$-algebra; at $d=3$ the formality point is the $\GRT_1(\Q)$-torsor datum of Theorem~\ref{thm:cfg-e3-formality-stage-1-phiFA3-torsor}. Step~(b) (Costello--Gwilliam locality) assembles the $E_d$-algebra into a topological factorisation algebra on the underlying space of $X$; this assembly is the factorisation-envelope construction of Theorem~\ref{thm:cfg-factorization-envelope-stage-one}. Step~(c) (Costello--Li holomorphic twist with CY form $\Omega_X$) refines the constructed topological envelope to a holomorphic factorisation algebra on the verified locus. The integral-kernel representation is the standard BV-factorisation dictionary: observables of holomorphic Chern--Simons at $X \times X$ support pair with observables on $X$ via Serre-duality pushforward. This proves the object-level Stage-$1$ kernel.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Conditional morphism kernel for \texorpdfstring{$\PhiFA_d$}{PhiFA-d}]
\label{prop:phifa-infty1-morphism-kernel-conditional}
\ClaimStatusConditional{Conditional on the per-$d$ functoriality datum of Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality}.}
When the per-$d$ functoriality hypothesis of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality} is available, a cyclic
$\Ainf$-functor $f \colon \cC \to \cC'$ represented by a
Fourier--Mukai kernel $K_f \in D^b(\Coh(X \times X'))$ with flat support
over both factors induces
\[
 \PhiFA_d(f) \;=\; (\pi_{X'})_* \bigl(\pi_{X \times X'}^* \cK_{\PhiFA_d}(X) \otimes^{\bL} K_f\bigr) \in \EdHolFA(X')^{(\infty,1)},
\]
with $\pi_{X'}, \pi_{X \times X'}$ the projections from
$X \times X \times X'$. This is the Fourier--Mukai convolution of the
native kernel with $K_f$ in the sense of Bondal--Orlov~$1995$ and
Mukai~$1981$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
For morphisms represented by Fourier--Mukai kernels, the displayed
formula is a conditional implication from per-$d$ functoriality:
Ben-Zvi--Francis--Nadler~$2010$ \textit{J.~Amer.~Math.~Soc.}~$23$,
Proposition~$2.3$, gives the convolution compatibility once the
cyclic/factorisation morphism datum of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality} is supplied.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}$}{SpCh-Sigma-d-1, C} as an \texorpdfstring{$(\infty,1)$}{(inf,1)}-stable functor; factorisation-homology kernel]
\label{prop:spch-infty1-kernel}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For an already constructed $E_d$-holomorphic factorisation algebra and a witnessed admissible specialisation datum in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:witnessed-admissible-specialisation-datum}, the stage-$2$ specialisation of Definition~\ref{def:phi-fa-and-sp} is an exact $(\infty,1)$-functor
\[
 \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C} \;\colon\; \EdHolFA(X)^{(\infty,1)} \;\longrightarrow\; \EnHolFA(C)^{(\infty,1)},
 \qquad n = n_{\mathrm{native}}(d),
\]
represented on this locus by the factorisation-homology kernel $\cO_{\Sigma_{d-1} \times C}$ on $X \times C$: for $\cF \in \EdHolFA(X)^{(\infty,1)}$,
\[
 \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}(\cF) \;=\; \bigl((\pi_C)_*\bigl(\pi_X^* \cF \otimes^{\bL} \cO_{\Sigma_{d-1} \times C}\bigr)\bigr)^{E_n\text{-}\mathrm{structure}}
\]
in $\EnHolFA(C)^{(\infty,1)}$, with $\pi_X, \pi_C$ the projections from $X \times C$ and the $E_n$-structure supplied by the Dunn restriction of the $\Sigma_{d-1}$-parallel direction (at $d \geq 3$: $E_d \to E_{d - (d-1)} = E_1$), enhanced to $E_2$ at $d = 2$ by the Kontsevich--Vlassopoulos $\bS^2$-framing, and to $E_\infty$ at $d = 1$ by the vanishing of the Gerstenhaber bracket. This is factorisation homology over $\Sigma_{d-1}$ followed by holomorphic restriction to $C$ in the sense of Lurie, \textit{HA}~$5.5.3$ and Francis--Gaitsgory~$2012$~\cite{FrancisGaitsgory2012} Proposition~$3.4.2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Factorisation homology $\int_{\Sigma_{d-1}}(-) \colon \EdHolFA(X) \to E_{d - (d-1)}\text{-}\HolFA(X \setminus \Sigma_{d-1})$ is an $(\infty,1)$-functor by Lurie \textit{HA}~$5.5.3.6$ on the witnessed support condition; restriction to a reference curve $C \subset X$ is exact by holomorphic pullback. The Beck--Chevalley cell in Definition~\ref{def:witnessed-admissible-specialisation-datum} identifies the composite with the pushforward-pullback
$(\pi_C)_*(\pi_X^* - \otimes^{\bL} \cO_{\Sigma_{d-1} \times C})$, and the stated properness and Tor-independence give the projection formula for factorisation cosheaves (Francis--Gaitsgory~$2012$ Lemma~$3.3.4$). The Fubini/envelope cell records that this pushforward is compatible with the $E_d$-factorisation products. The $E_n$-structure on the output is then Dunn-inherited from $E_d$ on the input by the calculation of native operadic level (Proposition~\ref{prop:native-en-level}).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Two-stage assignment of \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_d$}{Phi-d} after specialisation]
\label{thm:phi-two-stage-derived}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
For a witnessed admissible specialisation datum $(\Sigma_{d-1},C)$ and on the verified Stage-$1$ locus, the CY-to-chiral assignment is the composite
\[
 \Phi_d^{(\Sigma_{d-1},C)} \;=\; \CY_d\text{-}\Cat^{\mathrm{dg}}
 \xrightarrow{\;\PhiFA_d\;}
 \EdHolFA(X)^{(\infty,1)}
 \xrightarrow{\;\SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C}\;}
 \EnHolFA(C)^{(\infty,1)}
\]
of the Stage-$1$ assembly and the Stage-$2$ specialisation, with Propositions~\ref{prop:phifa-infty1-kernel} and~\ref{prop:spch-infty1-kernel} supplying the respective kernels on their stated scopes. The composite kernel
\[
 \cK_{\Phi_d}^{(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)} \;=\; \cK_{\PhiFA_d}(X) \;\circ\; \cO_{\Sigma_{d-1} \times C} \;\in\; \mathrm{PreFA}^{\mathrm{hol}}_{E_n}\!\bigl(X \times X^\vee \times C\bigr)
\]
represents $\Phi_d^{(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)}$ as an integral transform. At $d \leq 2$ this is the proved functorial construction. At $d = 3$ it is the framed object-level assignment of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}; arbitrary CY$_3$ morphism functoriality, homotopy-colimit preservation, and global exactness remain part of Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality} unless the relevant hypotheses are separately verified.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
Compose Propositions~\ref{prop:phifa-infty1-kernel} and~\ref{prop:spch-infty1-kernel}. Strict commutativity is the definition of $\Phi_d^{(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)}$ as the composite. On the witnessed locus, exactness of Stage~$2$ is Proposition~\ref{prop:spch-infty1-kernel}; exactness or homotopy-colimit preservation of the full $d=3$ assignment on arbitrary morphisms requires the extra cyclic-functoriality, Costello--Li naturality, Fubini, and Beck--Chevalley data included in a morphism of witnessed data. Monoidality under disjoint union of witnessed cycles is the disjoint-union multiplicativity established separately in Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[\texorpdfstring{$\Phi_3$}{Phi-3}-admissible CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} kernel datum]
\label{def:phi3-admissible-kernel-datum}
\ClaimStatusDefinitional{}
Fix a reference curve $C_0$ \textup{(}or a chosen equivalence between
the reference curves on source and target\textup{).} A
\emph{$\Phi_3$-admissible object} is a tuple
\[
 \mathfrak x=(\cC,X,\Omega_X,F_3,[\sigma_\cC],
 h_{\fr},h_{\mathrm{an}},\mathfrak s,\widehat{\mathcal O}_{\mathrm{OPE}})
\]
where $\cC$ satisfies hypotheses \textup{\ref{hyp:cy3-smooth}--\ref{hyp:framing}}
of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}, $X$ is the CY$_3$ target,
$F_3$ is the chosen Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality / associator point,
$[\sigma_\cC]\in HC^-_3(\cC)$ is the negative-cyclic CY class,
$h_{\fr}$ is the chain-level $\bS^3$-framing homotopy,
$h_{\mathrm{an}}$ is the Costello--Li anomaly-cancellation and
analytic-completion witness, $\mathfrak s$ is a witnessed admissible
specialisation datum of Definition~\ref{def:witnessed-admissible-specialisation-datum},
and $\widehat{\mathcal O}_{\mathrm{OPE}}$ records the chosen completion in which the OPE
series is defined. Its image is
\[
 A_{\mathfrak x}
 :=\Phi_3^{\mathfrak s}(\cC)
 =\SpCh_{\mathfrak s}\bigl(\PhiFA_3(\cC)\bigr)
 \in \Eone\text{-}\HolFA(C_0)^{(\infty,1)} .
\]

A \emph{$\Phi_3$-admissible kernel datum}
$\mathfrak k\colon\mathfrak x\to\mathfrak y$ consists of:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(K\arabic*)}]
 \item a cyclic $\Ainf$-functor, equivalently on geometric inputs a
 perfect Fourier--Mukai kernel
 $K\in\Perf(X\times Y)$ of finite Tor-amplitude, proper on the support
 over both factors, with convolution against composable kernels again
 perfect and proper;
 \item an orientation-line and CY-coherence isomorphism identifying the
 determinant line of $K$ with the transported CY orientation and carrying
 $[\sigma_\cC]$ to $[\sigma_\cD]$ in negative cyclic homology, up to a
 specified Connes-closed homotopy;
 \item a cyclic bar-transfer homotopy
 $\mathrm{CC}_\bullet(\cC)\to \mathrm{CC}_\bullet(\cD)$ commuting with
 $b$, $B$, and the $B^{(2)}$-operator used by the $\bS^3$-framing;
 \item a formality/framing compatibility cell: the chosen $F_3$-points
 differ by the specified $\GRT_1$-gauge and the kernel transports
 $h_{\fr}^{\mathfrak x}$ to $h_{\fr}^{\mathfrak y}$;
 \item a Costello--Li naturality cell producing a morphism of
 holomorphic factorisation algebras
 $\PhiFA_3(\mathfrak k)\colon \PhiFA_3(\cC)\to\PhiFA_3(\cD)$ and
 identifying the anomaly-cancellation and completion witnesses;
 \item a morphism of witnessed specialisation data
 $\mathfrak s_{\mathfrak x}\to\mathfrak s_{\mathfrak y}$ whose
 Beck--Chevalley and Fubini cells identify Stage~$2$ pushforward with
 convolution by the incidence kernels on $X\times C_0$ and $Y\times C_0$;
 \item coherent associativity and unit $2$-cells for convolution, so
 that $(K_2\circ K_1)\circ K_0$ and $K_2\circ(K_1\circ K_0)$ induce the
 same Stage-$1$ and Stage-$2$ maps up to the prescribed homotopy.
\end{enumerate}
These data define the $(\infty,1)$-category
$\CY_3\text{-}\Cat_{\Phi_3}^{\mathrm{wit}}$ of witnessed CY$_3$
objects and $\Phi_3$-admissible kernels.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$\Phi_3$}{Phi-3} functoriality on witnessed CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} kernels]
\label{thm:phi3-witnessed-kernel-functoriality}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
The framed object-level assignment of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}
extends to an $(\infty,1)$-functor on the witnessed kernel category:
\[
 \Phi_3^{\mathrm{wit}}\colon
 \CY_3\text{-}\Cat_{\Phi_3}^{\mathrm{wit}}
 \longrightarrow
 \Eone\text{-}\HolFA(C_0)^{(\infty,1)},\qquad
 \mathfrak x\longmapsto A_{\mathfrak x}.
\]
For a $\Phi_3$-admissible kernel datum
$\mathfrak k\colon\mathfrak x\to\mathfrak y$, the morphism
\[
 \Phi_3^{\mathrm{wit}}(\mathfrak k)
 \colon A_{\mathfrak x}\longrightarrow A_{\mathfrak y}
\]
is the Stage-$2$ specialisation of the Stage-$1$ factorisation-algebra
map supplied in Definition~\ref{def:phi3-admissible-kernel-datum}\textup{(K5)}
and transported through the Beck--Chevalley/Fubini cells of
\textup{(K6)}. It preserves identities and composition up to the
coherent natural quasi-isomorphisms in \textup{(K7)}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The object part is exactly Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}: an
object $\mathfrak x$ contains the hypotheses \textup{\ref{hyp:cy3-smooth}--\ref{hyp:framing}},
the formality point, analytic completion, and the witnessed
specialisation datum needed to form
$A_{\mathfrak x}=\SpCh_{\mathfrak s}(\PhiFA_3(\cC))$.

Let $\mathfrak k\colon\mathfrak x\to\mathfrak y$ be a morphism. The
cyclic kernel and orientation data \textup{(K1)--(K3)} give a morphism
of negative-cyclic bar complexes preserving the CY trace and the
$B^{(2)}$-homotopy used in the $\bS^3$-framing. The formality and
framing compatibility \textup{(K4)} transports this bar-level morphism
through the chosen $E_3$-formality point without changing the
Gerstenhaber degree $-2$. The Costello--Li naturality cell
\textup{(K5)} is precisely the missing chain-level datum needed to make
the holomorphic twist functorial at $d=3$; it produces a morphism
\[
 \PhiFA_3(\mathfrak k)\colon
 \PhiFA_3(\cC)\longrightarrow\PhiFA_3(\cD)
\]
in $\Ethree\HolFA^{(\infty,1)}$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:spch-infty1-kernel} applies to the witnessed
specialisation data on source and target. The Beck--Chevalley and
Fubini cells in \textup{(K6)} identify specialisation after
$\PhiFA_3(\mathfrak k)$ with the pushforward-pullback kernel formula on
the reference curve, hence define the required
$\Eone$-chiral-algebra morphism.

The identity kernel carries the identity orientation, cyclic transfer,
framing, Costello--Li, Beck--Chevalley, and Fubini cells, so it maps to
the identity of $A_{\mathfrak x}$. For composable kernels,
Fourier--Mukai convolution is associative only after choosing the usual
derived projection-formula and base-change coherences. These are
included in \textup{(K7)}; applying Stage~$1$ and then Stage~$2$ sends
those coherences to natural quasi-isomorphisms between
$\Phi_3^{\mathrm{wit}}(\mathfrak k_2\circ\mathfrak k_1)$ and
$\Phi_3^{\mathrm{wit}}(\mathfrak k_2)\circ
\Phi_3^{\mathrm{wit}}(\mathfrak k_1)$. The higher coherences are the
higher cells of \textup{(K7)}, so the assignment is an
$(\infty,1)$-functor.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Obstruction criterion for arbitrary CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} morphisms]
\label{prop:phi3-arbitrary-morphism-obstruction-criterion}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
Let $f\colon\cC\to\cD$ be a cyclic $\Ainf$-functor, or on geometric
inputs a Fourier--Mukai kernel $K\in\Perf(X\times Y)$, between
CY$_3$ objects satisfying the object-level hypotheses of
