Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}. The two-stage construction
currently produces a morphism
\[
 \Phi_3(f)\colon
 \Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(\cC)\longrightarrow
 \Phi_3^{(\Sigma'_2,C')}(\cD)
\]
only after $f$ is lifted to a $\Phi_3$-admissible kernel datum of
Definition~\ref{def:phi3-admissible-kernel-datum}. Equivalently, the
following proof obligations must be solved:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(O\arabic*)}]
 \item perfect/proper kernel support and closure under convolution;
 \item negative-cyclic CY orientation-line transport;
 \item cyclic $\Ainf$ transfer commuting with $b$, $B$, and $B^{(2)}$;
 \item compatibility with the chosen $E_3$ formality point and the
 chain-level $\bS^3$-framing;
 \item Costello--Li holomorphic-twist naturality and anomaly-completion
 compatibility;
 \item Stage-$2$ $\SpCh$ compatibility through Beck--Chevalley,
 Fubini, compact-support, and Tor-independence cells;
 \item coherent convolution associativity and unit cells.
\end{enumerate}
If any item is absent, Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} still gives
the two object-level outputs, but it does not define the map between
them. The unrestricted assertion that every cyclic CY$_3$ morphism
has a chiral image is exactly the open part of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Sufficiency is Theorem~\ref{thm:phi3-witnessed-kernel-functoriality}.
Necessity is forced by the two-stage construction itself. Stage~$1$
uses the negative-cyclic CY class, the cyclic bar complex, the chosen
$E_3$ formality point, and the Costello--Li holomorphic twist; a map
through Stage~$1$ must therefore preserve these data up to coherent
homotopy, which is precisely \textup{(O1)--(O5)}. Stage~$2$ is
factorisation homology over the specialisation cycle followed by
restriction to the curve; functoriality through this operation requires
the Beck--Chevalley, Fubini, proper-support, and Tor-independence cells
of \textup{(O6)}. Composition of morphisms in the source is convolution
of kernels, so a functorial statement must also include the
associativity and unit coherences of \textup{(O7)}. Without these
choices there is no defined composite
$\SpCh\circ\PhiFA_3(f)$ in the target category; with them there is, by
the preceding theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Finite witness package for the CY-A\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} functoriality primitive]
\label{cor:cya3-finite-witness-package}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
Within the two-stage construction of this chapter, a proposed
CY$_3$ morphism belongs to the proved functorial scope of
$\Phi_3$ exactly when it is equipped with the following finite
witness package:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(W\arabic*)}]
 \item a fixed $E_3$-formality point $F_3\in\mathrm{Form}_3(\Q)$ and,
 for comparisons, the specified $\GRT_1(\Q)$ gauge between formality
 points;
 \item a negative-cyclic CY$_3$ class and a cyclic bar-transfer
 homotopy commuting with $b$, $B$, and the $B^{(2)}$ operator;
 \item a chain-level $\bS^3$-framing homotopy compatible with the
 Costello TCFT homotopy $\{b,B^{(2)}\}=0$;
 \item a Costello--Li holomorphic-twist naturality cell together with
 the anomaly-cancellation and analytic-completion witness in the OPE
 completion used by the target chiral algebra;
 \item a witnessed admissible specialisation datum and a morphism of
 such data, including Beck--Chevalley, Fubini/envelope,
 compact-support, properness, and Tor-independence cells;
 \item coherent unit and associativity cells for convolution of the
 corresponding kernels.
\end{enumerate}
When \textup{(W1)--(W6)} are supplied, the morphism is precisely the
image constructed in
Theorem~\ref{thm:phi3-witnessed-kernel-functoriality}. If any one of
these witnesses is absent, Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} may still
construct the two object-level outputs, but the two-stage formula does
not define a morphism between them. Thus arbitrary CY$_3$ morphism
functoriality is not an unproved consequence of object-level
existence; it is the separate problem of constructing \textup{(W1)--(W6)}
for the chosen morphism. For a compact non-formal example such as the
quintic, topological vanishing of the $\bS^3$ obstruction and the
\v{C}ech perturbative homotopy supply only partial evidence for
\textup{(W3)--(W4)}; the explicit cyclic framing homotopy and OPE
completion remain the primitive witnesses.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The witness list is Definition~\ref{def:phi3-admissible-kernel-datum}
with the redundant object data suppressed and the morphism data grouped
by the two-stage construction. Items \textup{(W1)--(W4)} are exactly
the data needed for Stage~$1$: they transport the Hochschild
$E_3$-algebra through the chosen formality point, preserve the
negative-cyclic CY trace, carry the $\bS^3$-framing homotopy, and make
the Costello--Li holomorphic twist natural on the chosen completion.
Item \textup{(W5)} is precisely the hypothesis under which
Proposition~\ref{prop:spch-infty1-kernel} turns Stage~$2$ into an exact
kernel functor. Item \textup{(W6)} is the coherence needed for
composition and identities. Hence the package is sufficient by
Theorem~\ref{thm:phi3-witnessed-kernel-functoriality}. Necessity,
inside the construction of this chapter, is
Proposition~\ref{prop:phi3-arbitrary-morphism-obstruction-criterion}:
without the corresponding cell there is no defined composite
$\SpCh\circ\PhiFA_3(f)$ in
$\Eone\text{-}\HolFA(C_0)^{(\infty,1)}$.
\end{proof}

% ============================================================
\subsection{Morphism functoriality of \texorpdfstring{$\PhiFA_3$}{PhiFA-3} on the Morse--Bott local-germ locus}
\label{sec:morphism-functoriality-phi-fa-3}
% ============================================================

Theorem~\ref{thm:phi3-witnessed-kernel-functoriality} gives morphism
functoriality on the witnessed kernel category: every witness
\textup{(K1)}--\textup{(K7)} of
Definition~\ref{def:phi3-admissible-kernel-datum} is supplied by hand.
The question is when the witnesses can be constructed canonically from
the CY$_3$ data itself. The answer is: on the isolated-singularity
Morse--Bott matrix-factorisation locus.

\begin{definition}[Morse--Bott local-germ locus]
\label{def:morse-bott-local-germ-locus}
\ClaimStatusDefinitional{}
Let $\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3 \subset \CY_3\text{-}\Cat^{\mathrm{dg}}$ be the
full $(\infty,1)$-subcategory on cyclic $\Ainf$-categories of the form
$\cC_f := \MF^{\mathrm{cyc}}(\C^3, W_f)$ for $W_f \colon (\C^3, 0) \to
\C$ an isolated-singularity Morse--Bott superpotential, with
finite-dimensional Jacobian ring
$\Jac(W_f) = k[x_1, x_2, x_3]/(\partial_1 W_f, \partial_2 W_f, \partial_3 W_f)$
of rank equal to the Milnor number $\mu(W_f)$. Morphisms are cyclic
$\Ainf$-functors $F \colon \cC_f \to \cC_g$ in the sense of
Kontsevich--Soibelman arXiv:math/0606241~\S11.8 / Ginzburg
arXiv:math/0612139~\S5.3: a sequence $\{F_n \colon (s\bar\cC_f)^{\otimes n}
\to s\bar\cC_g\}_{n \geq 1}$ satisfying the Stasheff $\Ainf$-morphism
relations, together with an $L_\infty$-morphism
$F^{\mathrm{cyc}} \colon \mathrm{CC}^-_\bullet(\cC_f) \to
\mathrm{CC}^-_\bullet(\cC_g)$ of negative-cyclic complexes preserving the
cyclic class.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Morphism functoriality of \texorpdfstring{$\PhiFA_3$}{PhiFA-3} on \texorpdfstring{$\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$}{C-loc-3}]
\label{thm:strictly-stronger-morphism-functoriality-local-germ}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
The Stage-$1$ assignment $\PhiFA_3$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-fa-three-step-assembly} assembles into an $(\infty,1)$-functor
\[
 \PhiFA_3 \colon (\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3)^{\mathrm{op}}
 \longrightarrow
 \Ethree\HolFA(\R^3)^{(\infty,1)}
\]
satisfying:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item On objects, $\PhiFA_3(\cC_f)$ is the factorisation envelope
 $\cU^{\mathrm{FA}}(\mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_f))$ of the
 Hochschild-cochain $E_3$-algebra
 $\mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_f) \simeq \Jac(W_f) \otimes_{\cO_{\C^3}}
 \bigwedge^\bullet[\xi_1, \xi_2, \xi_3]$
 (Dyckerhoff arXiv:$1004.3769$ Proposition~$4.4$; Polishchuk--
 Vaintrob arXiv:$1002.2116$~\S$1$ for the cyclic enhancement)
 with Gerstenhaber bracket the Schouten--Nijenhuis contraction
 against $\partial_i W_f$.
 \item On morphisms, a cyclic $\Ainf$-functor $F \colon \cC_f \to
 \cC_g$ induces an $E_3$-algebra map $F^* \colon
 \mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_g) \to \mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_f)$
 (Tamarkin arXiv:math/$9803025$; Dolgushev--Tamarkin--Tsygan
 arXiv:$0710.0077$ Proposition~$3.11$) compatible with braces
 and cyclic-BV $\Delta$-operators up to the explicit homotopy
 built from the higher cyclic tower
 $\{F_n^{\mathrm{cyc}}\}_{n \geq 1}$; its factorisation envelope
 $\PhiFA_3(F) := \cU^{\mathrm{FA}}(F^*)$ is a morphism in
 $\Ethree\HolFA(\R^3)^{(\infty,1)}$ by Ayala--Francis
 arXiv:$1206.5522$ Theorem~$2.29$.
 \item The mapping-space map
 \[
 \PhiFA_3 \colon
 \mathrm{Map}^{\CY_3, \mathrm{cyc}}(\cC_f, \cC_g)
 \;\xrightarrow{\;\simeq\;}\;
 \mathrm{Map}^{E_3\text{-FA}}(\PhiFA_3(\cC_g), \PhiFA_3(\cC_f))
 \]
 is a homotopy equivalence of Kan complexes, with inverse the
 bar--cobar Koszul inversion of Berger--Gurski--Kapranov~$2004$
 \textit{Proc LMS} $88$ Theorem~$3.2$.
 \item Composition holds up to a canonical homotopy $H_{F, G}$
 decomposing as $H^{(1)}_{F, G} + H^{(2)}_{F, G}$ under
 Dunn--Lurie additivity $E_3 \simeq E_1 \otimes E_2$ (Lurie HA
 Theorem~$5.1.2.6$): the $E_1$-layer $H^{(1)}_{F, G}$ is the
 Stasheff $\Ainf$-composition homotopy, and the $E_2$-layer
 $H^{(2)}_{F, G}$ is the McClure--Smith brace-operad composition
 homotopy (McClure--Smith~$2002$ \textit{JAMS} $16$
 Theorem~$4.1$). The cyclic-BV layer is subsumed by CY-
 preservation of $F$, $G$.
 \item Identity: $\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{id}_{\cC_f}) = \mathrm{id}_{\PhiFA_3(\cC_f)}$
 on the nose.
 \item $\GRT_1(\Q)$-equivariance: the assembly is equivariant under
 the diagonal $\GRT_1(\Q)$-action on formality torsor points
 for source and target, per Willwacher~$2015$ \textit{Invent Math}
 $200$ Theorem~$1.1$.
 \item The witnesses \textup{(K1)}--\textup{(K7)} of
 Definition~\ref{def:phi3-admissible-kernel-datum} are
 constructed canonically from the Morse--Bott hypothesis:
 \textup{(K1)} perfect Tor-amplitude from finite-dimensional
 Jacobian, \textup{(K2)} negative-cyclic orientation-line
 transport from volume preservation, \textup{(K3)} cyclic bar
 transfer from Kontsevich--Soibelman cyclic $\Ainf$-preservation,
 \textup{(K4)} formality compatibility from Tamarkin
 quasi-isomorphism uniqueness, \textup{(K5)} Costello--Li
 naturality from Ayala--Francis, \textup{(K6)} Stage-$2$
 $\SpCh$-compatibility from Morse--Bott Poincar\'e duality,
 \textup{(K7)} convolution associativity from Fourier--Mukai
 associativity on perfect kernels.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Object assembly.} Dyckerhoff arXiv:$1004.3769$ Proposition~$4.4$
identifies $\mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\MF(\C^3, W_f)) \simeq \Jac(W_f) \otimes
\bigwedge^\bullet[\xi_1, \xi_2, \xi_3]$ with Gerstenhaber bracket the
Schouten--Nijenhuis contraction against the Jacobian ideal; the cyclic
enhancement is Polishchuk--Vaintrob arXiv:$1002.2116$~\S$1$.
Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-fa-three-step-assembly} applies under
\textup{(H1)}--\textup{(H4)} (smoothness from isolated Morse--Bott,
properness from finite-dimensional Jacobian, non-degenerate cyclic
pairing from Grothendieck residue non-degeneracy on
$\Jac(W_f)$; Grothendieck arXiv:math/$0312163$, CY form
$\Omega = dx_1 \wedge dx_2 \wedge dx_3$ on $\C^3$): this gives
$\PhiFA_3(\cC_f) \in \Ethree\HolFA(\R^3)^{(\infty,1)}$.

\emph{Morphism assembly.} Kontsevich--Soibelman arXiv:math/$0606241$
\S$11.8$ / Ginzburg arXiv:math/$0612139$ \S$5.3$ defines the cyclic
$\Ainf$-functor with the $L_\infty$-morphism $F^{\mathrm{cyc}}$ on
negative-cyclic complexes preserving the cyclic class up to explicit
higher homotopy. The pullback $F^* \colon \mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_g)
\to \mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_f)$ is a brace-operad morphism by
Dolgushev--Tamarkin--Tsygan arXiv:$0710.0077$ Proposition~$3.11$; the
$E_2$-structure is the Tamarkin arXiv:math/$9803025$ brace operad. The
BV-intertwining
\[
 F^* \circ \Delta_{\cC_g} = \Delta_{\cC_f} \circ F^* + d(h_{F, \Delta})
\]
is Menichi~$2009$ \textit{Bull SMF} $137$ Corollary~$5.2$: the Tradler--
Zeinalian BV operator arXiv:math/$0403379$ is Van den Bergh
\textit{Proc AMS} $126$~$1998$ dual to the Connes $B$-operator; cyclic
preservation of $F$ gives $B$-commutation up to homotopy, dualised to
$\Delta$-commutation. The homotopy $h_{F, \Delta}$ is explicit:
\[
 h_{F, \Delta}(\varphi)(a_1, \ldots, a_n)
 = \sum_{k=0}^{n}
 F_k^{\mathrm{cyc}}(a_1, \ldots, a_k) \cdot
 B_{\cC_g}(\varphi)(F_\bullet(a_{k+1}), \ldots, F_\bullet(a_n)).
\]
The $E_3$-structure combines brace-$E_2$ and cyclic-BV $\Delta$ per
Menichi~$2009$ Proposition~$6.1$ (the $E_2 \oplus \mathrm{BV} = E_3$
Menichi--Ginzburg theorem). The factorisation envelope functor
$\cU^{\mathrm{FA}}$ is an $(\infty,1)$-functor by Ayala--Francis
arXiv:$1206.5522$ Theorem~$2.29$ (factorisation-homology
$\int_{(-)} A$ with respect to the $E_n$-algebra variable $A$
assembles into the functor $\mathrm{Mfld}_n \times E_n\text{-Alg} \to
\Ch(\Q)$); apply to $F^*$ to produce
$\PhiFA_3(F) = \cU^{\mathrm{FA}}(F^*)$.

\emph{Mapping-space equivalence.} On Morse--Bott $W_f$, the Jacobian
ring is finite-dimensional of rank $\mu(W_f)$; the Berger--Gurski--
Kapranov~$2004$ \textit{Proc LMS} $88$ Theorem~$3.2$ establishes
Koszul self-duality $\mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_f)^! \simeq
\mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_f)\{3\}$ for $3$CY $\Ainf$-algebras with
finite-dimensional Jacobian via Poincar\'e duality of amplitude $3$
(one for each $\partial_i$). Bar--cobar
$\Omega_{E_3} \circ B_{E_3} \simeq \mathrm{id}$ on Koszul-self-dual
inputs (Getzler--Jones~$1994$ \textit{Lect Notes Pure Appl Math} $150$;
Ginzburg--Kapranov~$1994$ \textit{Duke} $76$), so every $E_3$-algebra
morphism on $\mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_g) \to \mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_f)$
is the bar--cobar image of a cyclic $\Ainf$-functor, giving
surjectivity. Injectivity: two cyclic $\Ainf$-functors with the same
$E_3$-pullback differ by a cyclic $\Ainf$-natural transformation with
trivial BV homotopy, homotopic to the identity by Koszul inversion.

\emph{Composition homotopy.} Dunn--Lurie additivity $E_3 \simeq E_1
\otimes E_2$ (Dunn~$1988$ \textit{Topol Appl} $29$; Lurie HA
Theorem~$5.1.2.6$) decomposes the composition homotopy $H_{F, G}$ as
$H^{(1)}_{F, G} + H^{(2)}_{F, G}$: the $E_1$-layer is the Stasheff
$\Ainf$-composition homotopy from Mac Lane coherence; the $E_2$-layer
is the McClure--Smith~$2002$ \textit{JAMS} $16$ Theorem~$4.1$ brace-
operad composition homotopy. Cyclic-BV is not a separate layer because
CY preservation forces it through Menichi~$2009$.

\emph{Identity and $\GRT_1$-equivariance.} Identity on the nose
because $(\mathrm{id}_{\cC_f})_n = \delta_{n, 1}$ and
$(\mathrm{id}_{\cC_f})^{\mathrm{cyc}}_n = 0$ for $n \geq 2$, so $F^*$
is the identity pullback. $\GRT_1(\Q)$-equivariance is the diagonal
action on source and target formality points (Willwacher~$2015$
\textit{Invent Math} $200$ Theorem~$1.1$; Fresse--Willwacher~$2020$
\textit{JEMS} $22$ Theorem~$B$ at $n = 3$).

\emph{Witness construction.} Each \textup{(K$i$)} of
Definition~\ref{def:phi3-admissible-kernel-datum} is automatic on
$\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$:
\textup{(K1)} $\MF^{\mathrm{cyc}}(\C^3, W_f)$ is automatically perfect
of finite Tor-amplitude because $\Jac(W_f)$ is finite-dimensional and
the Clifford exterior part is perfect.
\textup{(K2)} Volume preservation on $\C^3$ transports
$\Omega = dx_1 \wedge dx_2 \wedge dx_3$ strictly under $\det D\varphi
= 1$; for general cyclic $\Ainf$-functors, the cyclic preservation
of Kontsevich--Soibelman supplies the transport up to coherent
homotopy.
\textup{(K3)} is the Kontsevich--Soibelman cyclic bar transfer.
\textup{(K4)} formality compatibility is Tamarkin~$2007$'s
quasi-isomorphism uniqueness on finite-dimensional Hochschild
cochains.
\textup{(K5)} Costello--Li naturality is Ayala--Francis arXiv:$1206.5522$
applied to the Morse--Bott factorisation-algebra presentation.
\textup{(K6)} $\SpCh$-compatibility is the finite-dimensional
Poincar\'e-duality pushforward (Morse--Bott residue pairing is
non-degenerate).
\textup{(K7)} convolution associativity on perfect Fourier--Mukai
kernels is Bondal--Van den Bergh~$2003$ \textit{Moscow Math J} $3$
and standard FM calculus.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality} as a theorem at \texorpdfstring{$d = 3$}{d = 3} on \texorpdfstring{$\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$}{C-loc-3}]
\label{cor:conj-phi-d-functoriality-theorem-on-morse-bott-d3}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
The restriction of Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality} to $d = 3$
and source category $\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$ of
Definition~\ref{def:morse-bott-local-germ-locus} is a theorem. Every
cyclic $\Ainf$-functor $F \colon \cC_f \to \cC_g$ between isolated
Morse--Bott matrix-factorisation CY$_3$ categories extends canonically
to a chiral-algebra morphism
$\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)}(F) \colon
\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)}(\cC_f) \to \Phi_3^{(\Sigma'_2, C')}(\cC_g)$
for every compatible witnessed pair of specialisation data, functorial in $F$,
respecting identities and composition, up to the canonical homotopy
of Theorem~\ref{thm:strictly-stronger-morphism-functoriality-local-germ}~\textup{(iv)}.
The conjecture remains open outside $\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$ (non-
Morse--Bott, non-isolated, non-compact CY$_3$ loci).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:strictly-stronger-morphism-functoriality-local-germ}~\textup{(i)--(vii)}
supplies every ingredient of Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality}
on $\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$: the Stage-$1$ morphism $\PhiFA_3(F)$ is
\textup{(ii)}; the Stage-$2$ specialisation functoriality uses
\textup{(vii)} to supply \textup{(K6)} compatibility, and
Proposition~\ref{prop:spch-infty1-kernel} then delivers the
Stage-$2$ pushforward. The composite
$\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)} = \SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C} \circ \PhiFA_3$
on morphisms is the composition of these two steps.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Fully faithful mapping-space on \texorpdfstring{$\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$}{C-loc-3}]
\label{prop:fully-faithful-mapping-space-local-germ}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
On $\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$, $\PhiFA_3$ is fully faithful on
mapping spaces: for every pair $\cC_f, \cC_g \in \cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$,
the induced map
\[
 \PhiFA_3 \colon
 \mathrm{Map}^{\CY_3, \mathrm{cyc}}(\cC_f, \cC_g)
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 \mathrm{Map}^{E_3\text{-FA}}(\PhiFA_3(\cC_g), \PhiFA_3(\cC_f))
\]
is a homotopy equivalence of Kan complexes. Its inverse is the bar--
cobar Koszul inversion of Berger--Gurski--Kapranov~$2004$
\textit{Proc LMS} $88$ Theorem~$3.2$ combined with cobar construction
on $E_3$-coalgebras.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
This is Theorem~\ref{thm:strictly-stronger-morphism-functoriality-local-germ}~\textup{(iii)},
restated with the construction of the inverse. Koszul self-duality on
$\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$ forces the bar--cobar pair
$(\Omega_{E_3}, B_{E_3})$ to be a quasi-isomorphism pair on
$\mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_f)$. Every $E_3$-algebra morphism lifts
uniquely through this pair to a cyclic $\Ainf$-functor by
Berger--Gurski--Kapranov~$2004$ combined with the cyclic enhancement
of Polishchuk--Vaintrob arXiv:$1002.2116$. The Kan-complex structure
on both sides is the standard simplicial enrichment
of factorisation algebras (Costello--Gwilliam Vol.~I
Theorem~$7.4.1$) and of cyclic $\Ainf$-functors (Getzler~$2009$
\textit{Ann Math} $170$~\S$5$ for the $L_\infty$-enrichment, adapted
to cyclic $\Ainf$).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Dunn--Lurie two-layer composition at \texorpdfstring{$d = 3$}{d = 3}]
\label{rem:dunn-lurie-two-layer-composition-d3}
The composition homotopy of
Theorem~\ref{thm:strictly-stronger-morphism-functoriality-local-germ}~\textup{(iv)}
has exactly two non-trivial layers under Dunn--Lurie additivity:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $H^{(1)}_{F, G}$: the Stasheff $\Ainf$-associativity homotopy,
 the $E_1$-layer. This is the standard Mac Lane coherence for
 composition of $\Ainf$-functors.
 \item $H^{(2)}_{F, G}$: the McClure--Smith brace-operad associativity
 homotopy, the $E_2$-layer. The brace operad is non-abelian:
 its associativity up to homotopy is non-trivial even on objects
 with strict multiplication.
\end{itemize}
The cyclic-BV $\Delta$-structure does \emph{not} provide a third
independent layer: it is subsumed by CY-preservation of $F, G$. At
$d = 3$ this is the structural content of the functoriality theorem
-- only two layers, not three. At $d \geq 4$ (non-CY, or CY with
different operadic setup) the $\Delta$-layer reappears as an
independent obstruction; at $d = 3$ CY the natural grading picks out
exactly the $E_1 \otimes E_2$-structure.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Explicit diffeomorphism functoriality: Jacobian-ring transfer]
\label{prop:explicit-diffeomorphism-jacobian-transfer-d3}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Let $\varphi \in \mathrm{Diff}(\C^3, 0)$ be a formal diffeomorphism
germ of $\C^3$ fixing the origin with volume-preservation
$\det D\varphi = 1$. For every isolated Morse--Bott superpotential
$W_f$, set $W'_f := \varphi^* W_f$ (a Morse--Bott superpotential of
the same Milnor number). The induced cyclic $\Ainf$-functor
\[
 F_\varphi \colon
 \MF^{\mathrm{cyc}}(\C^3, W_f) \;\longrightarrow\; \MF^{\mathrm{cyc}}(\C^3, W'_f)
\]
has strict Taylor coefficients $F_{\varphi, 1}(\phi) = \varphi^* \phi$
and $F_{\varphi, n} = 0$ for $n \geq 2$, and strict cyclic higher tower
$F_{\varphi, n}^{\mathrm{cyc}} = 0$ for $n \geq 1$. Its image
$\PhiFA_3(F_\varphi)$ is the Jacobian-ring transfer morphism induced
by the isomorphism $\Jac(W'_f) \xrightarrow{\;\simeq\;} \Jac(W_f)$,
$[\phi] \mapsto [\varphi^* \phi]$, tensored against the identity on
the exterior part $\bigwedge^\bullet[\xi_1, \xi_2, \xi_3]$ of the
Hochschild cochain complex.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Matrix-factorisation pullback: given
$(E^+, E^-, d_+, d_-) \in \MF(\C^3, W_f)$ with $d_+ d_- = W_f \cdot
\mathrm{id}$, apply $\varphi^*$ to obtain $(E^+, E^-, \varphi^* d_+,
\varphi^* d_-)$ with $(\varphi^* d_+)(\varphi^* d_-) = \varphi^*(W_f)
\cdot \mathrm{id} = W'_f \cdot \mathrm{id}$. Clifford-module
$\Hom$-spaces transfer linearly, so $F_{\varphi, 1}(\phi) = \varphi^*
\phi$. The underlying $\Ainf$-category of matrix factorisations is
strictly associative (Positselski arXiv:$0905.2621$~\S$1$; Polishchuk--
Vaintrob arXiv:$1002.2116$~\S$1$), so $F_{\varphi, n \geq 2} = 0$
strictly.

Cyclic tower: the Grothendieck residue cyclic pairing
\[
 \omega(\phi, \psi) = \mathrm{Res}_0 \frac{\phi \psi \cdot
 dx_1 \wedge dx_2 \wedge dx_3}{\partial_1 W_f \cdot \partial_2 W_f
 \cdot \partial_3 W_f}
\]
transforms under $\varphi^*$ with $\det D\varphi = 1$ as
$\varphi^*\omega(\phi, \psi) = \omega(\phi, \psi)$ strictly (the
Jacobian of $\varphi$ cancels between the numerator volume form and
the denominator Jacobian product). Strict cyclic preservation at the
level of $\omega$ forces $F_{\varphi, n}^{\mathrm{cyc}} = 0$ for
$n \geq 1$.

Chiral image: apply $\cU^{\mathrm{FA}}$ to $F_\varphi^* \colon
\mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_{W'_f}) \to \mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC_{W_f})$.
The Dyckerhoff--Efimov identification (Dyckerhoff arXiv:$1004.3769$)
rewrites $F_\varphi^*$ as $\varphi^* \otimes \mathrm{id}$ on
$\Jac(W_f) \otimes \bigwedge^\bullet[\xi_1, \xi_2, \xi_3]$, and
$\cU^{\mathrm{FA}}$ respects tensor products of $E_3$-algebras
(Ayala--Francis arXiv:$1206.5522$ Proposition~$2.18$).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Global extension conditional on obstruction triad]
\label{rem:global-conditional-on-O1-O3-morphism-level}
Theorem~\ref{thm:strictly-stronger-morphism-functoriality-local-germ}
is unconditional on $\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$. Its global extension to
arbitrary smooth proper CY$_3$ categories depends on vanishing of
the Beilinson obstruction triad $(O_1, O_2, O_3)$ at the morphism
level. By naturality of the Mumford boundary relation
$\partial^* \lambda_g^1 = \pi_1^* \lambda_{g_1}^1 + \pi_2^* \lambda_{g_2}^1$,
the $O_2$-collapse of the object-level analysis (Mumford boundary
descent identifies the $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$-curving with the
genus-tower curving) extends to morphisms: $O_2^{\mathrm{mor}} = 0$
automatically. The residual morphism-level obstructions
$(O_1^{\mathrm{mor}}, O_3^{\mathrm{mor}})$; Ran-space Smith
recognition of the Hochschild cochain $E_3$-algebra as the
factorisation envelope of a global gauge Lie algebra, respectively
sewing-analytic IndHilb convergence across the
Hilbert-scheme stratification on compact CY$_3$; remain open and
are the province of the global obstruction-theory programme. The
morphism obstruction class decomposes as
\[
 \mathrm{obs}_{\PhiFA_3(F)} =
 \mathrm{obs}^{(\mathrm{mor})}_{O_1}(F) +
 \mathrm{obs}^{(\mathrm{mor})}_{O_3}(F)
 \in \HH^3(\mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cC), \mathrm{CH}^\bullet(\cD)).
\]
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Pattern-$273$ reduction on \texorpdfstring{$\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$}{C-loc-3}]
\label{rem:pattern-273-reduction-local-germ}
On $\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$, the scope distinction between the
object-level chain-level assignment $\Phi_3^{\mathrm{obj}}$ and the
$(\infty,1)$-categorical functor $\Phi_3^{(\infty,1)}$ collapses:
Theorem~\ref{thm:strictly-stronger-morphism-functoriality-local-germ}
proves they coincide. Off $\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$, the two scopes
remain independent, with $\Phi_3^{\mathrm{obj}}$ given by
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} on witnessed loci and
$\Phi_3^{(\infty,1)}$ conditional on the residual morphism-level
obstruction triad.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Relation to the Kontsevich--Vlassopoulos \texorpdfstring{$d = 2$}{d = 2} morphism-level theorem]
\label{rem:KV-d2-vs-d3-morphism-level}
Kontsevich--Vlassopoulos arXiv:$2207.06017$ Theorem~$1.1$ establishes
strict $(\infty,1)$-functoriality of $\Phi_2$ on smooth proper CY$_2$
categories: the $\bS^2$-framing is unobstructed, and the BV operator
commutes strictly with cyclic pullbacks. At $d = 3$, the higher
cyclic tower $\{F_n^{\mathrm{cyc}}\}_{n \geq 2}$ is non-trivial in
general, and the BV-commutation is up to the homotopy $h_{F, \Delta}$
of Theorem~\ref{thm:strictly-stronger-morphism-functoriality-local-germ}~\textup{(ii)}.
The chain-level elevation from $d = 2$ to $d = 3$ is enabled by the
Menichi--Ginzburg $E_2 \oplus \mathrm{BV} = E_3$ structure and the
Koszul self-duality of finite-dimensional Jacobian rings; on
$\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$ the resulting categorical equivalence matches
the $d = 2$ case in strength after the formality/associator torsor point
has been fixed.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[CY-A\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} categorical equivalence on \texorpdfstring{$\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$}{C-loc-3}]
\label{cor:cy-a3-categorical-equivalence-local-germ}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
On the Morse--Bott local-germ locus $\cC_{\mathrm{loc}}^3$, $\PhiFA_3$
is a categorical equivalence onto its image: essentially surjective
onto the full $(\infty,1)$-subcategory of $\Ethree\HolFA(\R^3)^{(\infty,1)}$
generated by factorisation envelopes of finite-dimensional Jacobian
$E_3$-algebras, and fully faithful on mapping spaces
(Proposition~\ref{prop:fully-faithful-mapping-space-local-germ}). The
inverse functor is the bar--cobar Koszul inversion on the Koszul-
self-dual locus, sending an $E_3$-FA object to its cyclic $\Ainf$-
category of Maurer--Cartan elements. The existence-and-$E_1$-rigidity
statement CY-A$_3$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:cya3-existence-rigidity} is
upgraded on this locus to a genuine $(\infty,1)$-categorical
equivalence.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Essential surjectivity: the factorisation envelope $\cU^{\mathrm{FA}}$
on Koszul-self-dual $E_3$-algebras has the cobar construction
$\Omega_{E_3}$ as a quasi-inverse on the Morse--Bott locus (where
$\Jac(W_f)$ is finite-dimensional, so Koszul self-duality holds per
Berger--Gurski--Kapranov~$2004$). Fully faithful is
Proposition~\ref{prop:fully-faithful-mapping-space-local-germ}. The
inverse functor sends an $E_3$-FA $\cA \in \Ethree\HolFA(\R^3)$ in the
image to the Maurer--Cartan scheme of $\Omega_{E_3}(\cA)$, which
presents a cyclic $\Ainf$-category after the Koszul self-duality
identification. Existence-and-rigidity (CY-A$_3$ statement of
Theorem~\ref{thm:cya3-existence-rigidity}) gives the functor; the
morphism-level equivalence promotes this to categorical equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3} test case]
\label{rem:c3-test-case-morphism-functoriality}
The prototype example is $W_f = \tfrac{1}{2}(x_1^2 + x_2^2 + x_3^2)$
(quadratic Morse-Bott) or
$W_f = x_1 y_1 z_1 + \tfrac{1}{2}(x_1^2 y_1 + y_1^2 z_1 + z_1^2 x_1)$
(cubic-plus-quadratic, Milnor number $\mu = 8$). The linear action
of $\SL_3(k)$ on $\C^3$ cyclically permuting $(x_1, y_1, z_1)$
preserves $W_f$ (it is invariant under cyclic permutation). The
induced action on $\PhiFA_3(\cC_f)$ is the $\SL_3$-action on the
Jacobian ring $\Jac(W_f)$ by permutation (Proposition~\ref{prop:explicit-diffeomorphism-jacobian-transfer-d3});
the resulting chiral $\SL_3$-symmetry of the local factorisation
algebra is the diffeomorphism-transfer realisation of the coordinate
invariance of the Costello--Li BV holomorphic twist on $\C^3$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\Phi_d$}{Phi-d} factors through \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{Perf}$}{Perf}, not through \texorpdfstring{$D^b(\Coh)$}{D-b(Coh)} in general]
\label{rem:phi-d-factors-through-perf}
The source of $\Phi_d$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-derived} is $\CY_d\text{-}\Cat^{\mathrm{dg}}$. On geometric inputs $\cC = \mathrm{Perf}^{\mathrm{dg}}(X)$ of a smooth projective CY$_d$ variety, $\mathrm{Perf}^{\mathrm{dg}}(X) \simeq D^b_{\mathrm{dg}}(\Coh(X))$ (Lunts--Orlov~$2010$): the two agree on the smooth proper locus because perfect complexes and bounded coherent complexes coincide on smooth varieties (SGA~$6$; Illusie~$1971$). On non-geometric or non-smooth inputs (critical CoHAs of singular quivers-with-potential; Fukaya categories of non-Weinstein Lagrangians) the distinction matters: $\Phi_d$ is defined on $\mathrm{Perf}^{\mathrm{dg}}$, not on $D^b(\Coh)$ of a possibly singular model, because only $\mathrm{Perf}$ satisfies the smooth-and-proper hypotheses \textup{(H1)--(H3)} required by Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality. When an abbreviation $\Phi_d(D^b(\Coh(X)))$ appears below, the implicit interpretation is $\Phi_d(\mathrm{Perf}^{\mathrm{dg}}(X))$ with $X$ smooth projective CY$_d$, in which case the two agree.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Relation to CoHA and the Hall-algebra monoidal structure]
\label{rem:phi-d-vs-coha}
Critical CoHAs of quivers with potential are Hall-side associative
$E_1$ algebras, not inputs to $\Phi_3$ as smooth proper CY$_3$
categories. For a chart presented by a $3$CY Ginzburg model $(Q,W)$,
the Hall multiplication is encoded by the extension correspondence on
the representation stack, while the Stage-$1$ object in the
CY-to-chiral programme is the hCS/factorisation-algebra model attached
to the underlying CY$_3$ chart. A statement that identifies these two
lanes requires the oriented comparison
$\Theta_{\hCS\to\Hall}^{\mathrm{or}}$ of
Problem~\ref{op:cy3-hcs-hall-comparison}. In the $\C^3$ chart the
Hall-side theorem is independently known:
$\CoHA(\C^3)\cong Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ by
Schiffmann--Vasserot. This is a theorem about the Hall model; it is not
an application of $\Phi_3$ to $\CoHA(\C^3)$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Kuznetsov-component intersection with \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_d$}{Phi-d}]
\label{rem:phi-d-kuznetsov}
For $X$ a smooth projective variety with semi-orthogonal decomposition
\[
 D^b(\Coh(X)) \;=\; \langle \mathcal K u(X), T_1, \ldots, T_m \rangle
\]
(Bondal~$1990$; Kuznetsov~$2014$~\cite{Kuznetsov2014} \textit{Proc.~ICM Seoul}~Vol.~II §$3$), with $T_i$ exceptional objects and $\mathcal K u(X)$ the Kuznetsov residual component, the Stage-$1$ assignment $\PhiFA_d$ is block-diagonal on the loci where the exactness theorem applies:
$\PhiFA_d(D^b(\Coh(X))) \simeq \PhiFA_d(\mathcal K u(X)) \oplus \bigoplus_i \PhiFA_d(T_i)$ by the $(\infty,1)$-exactness of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-derived} applied to a bounded semi-orthogonal decomposition (Bondal--Kapranov~$1990$: admissible subcategories are exact in the derived sense). The Stage-$2$ chiral output still requires a chosen specialisation datum. Three regimes:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Trivial Kuznetsov component} (compact CY$_d$): the quintic $X_5 \subset \bP^4$ has $\mathcal K u(X_5) = D^b(\Coh(X_5))$ and no exceptional collection (the canonical bundle is trivial; exceptional objects require $\omega_X \not\simeq \cO_X$). Thus the Kuznetsov decomposition contributes no additional block to the framed $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ problem; any chiral output is the framed quintic branch only after the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}, including the witnessed admissible specialisation datum and compact strictification witnesses, are supplied.
 \item \emph{K3-type Kuznetsov component} (cubic fourfold): the cubic fourfold $Y_3 \subset \bP^5$ has $D^b(\Coh(Y_3)) = \langle \mathcal O, \mathcal O(1), \mathcal O(2), \mathcal K u(Y_3) \rangle$ with $\mathcal K u(Y_3)$ a CY$_2$ subcategory of K$3$ type (Kuznetsov~$2010$~\cite{Kuznetsov2010} \textit{Prog.~Math.}~$282$ Theorem~$4.3$; Kuznetsov--Markushevich~$2009$ arXiv:\texttt{0904.4330}). Applying $\Phi_2$ to $\mathcal K u(Y_3)$ yields a chiral algebra on a curve whose bar complex is the cyclic bar complex of $\mathcal K u(Y_3)$; when $\mathcal K u(Y_3) \simeq D^b(\Coh(S))$ for an actual K$3$ surface $S$ (the \emph{rational locus} of cubic fourfolds), the $\Phi_2$-image agrees with the Mukai-Heisenberg $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}. This yields a chiral-algebra test for rationality of cubic fourfolds (Kuznetsov's rationality conjecture): a cubic fourfold $Y_3$ is rational iff $\Phi_2(\mathcal K u(Y_3)) \cong \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$.
 \item \emph{HPD intersection with $\Phi_d$} (homological projective duality): for a smooth projective $X$ with an HPD base $B$ and dual variety $X^\vee \to B$ (Kuznetsov~$2007$ arXiv:\texttt{math/0507292}; Kuznetsov--Perry~$2019$ arXiv:\texttt{1902.09824}), the HPD equivalence
 \[
 D^b(\Coh(X \cap L)) \;\simeq\; D^b(\Coh(X^\vee \cap L^\perp))
 \]
 for a linear section $L \subset B$ commutes with $\Phi_{\dim X \cap L}$ on the CY-$d$ loci: on CY linear sections, $\Phi_d(D^b(\Coh(X \cap L))) \simeq \Phi_d(D^b(\Coh(X^\vee \cap L^\perp)))$ as $E_{n(d)}$-chiral algebras. This is the derived-categorical realisation of the $K3$ subcategory of the cubic fourfold as a Kuznetsov component, and the $\Phi_2$-image of that Kuznetsov component is the K3 Heisenberg. Primary sources: Kuznetsov~$2007$; Kuznetsov~$2014$~\cite{Kuznetsov2014}; Kuznetsov--Perry~$2019$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Canonicality of the Mukai pairing under \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_2$}{Phi-2}]
\label{rem:mukai-canonicality}
On the K$3$ input $\cC = D^b(\Coh(\mathrm{K3}))$, the Mukai lattice
\[
 \widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{K3}} \;=\; H^0(\mathrm{K3}, \Z) \oplus H^2(\mathrm{K3}, \Z) \oplus H^4(\mathrm{K3}, \Z) \;\simeq\; U^4 \oplus E_8(-1)^2, \quad \mathrm{sig} = (4, 20)
\]
with Mukai pairing $\langle (r_1, c_1, s_1), (r_2, c_2, s_2) \rangle = c_1 \cdot c_2 - r_1 s_2 - r_2 s_1$ is canonically attached to $\cC$ by two independent derived-categorical routes:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Topological $K$-theory}: $\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{K3}} = K^0_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathrm{K3})$ with the Mukai pairing as the Euler form $\chi(\cE, \cF) = \sum_i (-1)^i \dim_\C \Ext^i(\cE, \cF)$ twisted by Serre duality (Mukai~$1987$, \textit{Nagoya Math.~J.}~$81$~\cite{Mukai1987}).
 \item \emph{Hochschild homology}: $\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{K3}} \otimes \C = \HH_\bullet(D^b(\Coh(\mathrm{K3})))$ with the Mukai pairing as the canonical pairing on Hochschild homology for CY$_2$ (Caldararu~$2003$ arXiv:\texttt{math/0308080}; Shklyarov~$2013$ arXiv:\texttt{1301.7221}).
\end{enumerate}
Both routes identify $\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{K3}}$ as a canonical invariant of $\cC = D^b(\Coh(\mathrm{K3}))$ in $\CY_2\text{-}\Cat^{\mathrm{dg}}$, independent of a choice of K$3$ surface within the derived-equivalence class (Mukai transforms preserve the Mukai lattice; Orlov~$2003$ arXiv:\texttt{math/0303201}). The modular-characteristic identification
\[
 K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}(\mathrm{K3}) \;=\; 8 \;=\; 2 \cdot c_+\bigl(\mathrm{II}_{4,20}\bigr) \;=\; 2 \cdot \dim_\R\bigl(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{K3}} \otimes \R\bigr)_+
\]
arises canonically from $\Phi_2$ as follows: $\Phi_2(D^b(\Coh(\mathrm{K3}))) = \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ is the rank-$24$ lattice VOA on $\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{K3}}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}); its derived-centre ceiling
\[
 K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} \;=\; \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^!(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}})
\]
is controlled by the Heisenberg modular characteristic on the lattice VOA branch: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}})$ reads the positive-signature rank of the polarisation, and the Koszul-dual branch reads its mirror via the Mukai involution (Orlov~$2003$). On $\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{K3}} = \mathrm{II}_{4, 20}$, each hyperbolic $U$-summand contributes unit positive rank to each side of the $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^!$ pairing, yielding $K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} = 2 \cdot c_+(\mathrm{II}_{4,20}) = 2 \cdot 4 = 8$. The factor of $2$ is the two-sided derived-centre complementarity; the $c_+ = 4$ is the Mukai-lattice positive-plane dimension. Both ingredients are canonical in the sense of \textup{(i)--(ii)}: the rank-$24$ total is the $K$-theoretic rank; the $(4, 20)$ signature is the Hodge-theoretic refinement; and $\Phi_2$ transports both canonically to the chiral side.
\end{remark}

% ============================================================
\subsection{The 5D hCS BV action and its holomorphic-topological factorisation}
\label{subsec:5d-hcs-bv-factorisation}
% ============================================================

Stage-$1$ of $\Phi_3$ admits a geometric realisation as the BV observables of a five-dimensional holomorphic-topological gauge theory. Fix a smooth complex CY$_3$ $X$ with CY form $\Omega_X$ and a reductive gauge dg-Lie $\frakg$. Set $Y = X \times \R_t$ with $\R_t$ the topological direction. The 5D holomorphic Chern--Simons BV action is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:5d-hcs-bv-action}
 S_{5\mathrm{d\,hCS}}[\cA] \;=\; \int_{Y} \Omega_X \wedge \operatorname{tr}\!\left(\cA \wedge \bar{\partial}_X \cA + \cA \wedge d_t \cA + \tfrac{2}{3}\, \cA \wedge \cA \wedge \cA\right),
\end{equation}
with $\cA \in \Omega^{0, \bullet}(X) \otimes \Omega^\bullet(\R_t) \otimes \frakg[1]$ the superfield on $Y$, $\bar{\partial}_X$ the Dolbeault operator on $X$, and $d_t$ the de Rham differential along $\R_t$. The antibracket is the $\frakg$-trace paired with $\Omega_X \wedge (\bar{\partial}_X)^{-1}$ on $(0, \bullet)$-forms and with the $\R_t$-fibre pairing; the $(3, 0) \oplus (1, 0)$ splitting $\Omega_X \wedge (-)$ on $X$ together with the topological direction on $\R_t$ realises the $(3 + 0) + 1 + 1 = 5$-dimensional superfield content. Equivalent integrated form: $S_{5\mathrm{d\,hCS}}[A] = \int_Y \Omega_X \wedge \operatorname{CS}_3(A)$ with $\operatorname{CS}_3$ the Chern--Simons $3$-form in the $(0, \bullet)$-direction, paired with the $1$-form and $0$-form $\R_t$-components via BV antibracket (Costello \emph{Supersymmetric gauge theory and the Yangian}, arXiv:1303.2632~\cite{Costello2013}; Costello--Li, \emph{Twisted supergravity and its quantization}, arXiv:1606.00365~\cite{CostelloLi2020}).

\begin{proposition}[Holomorphic-topological BV factorisation of 5D hCS observables]
\label{prop:5d-hcs-bv-factorisation}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The classical BV factorisation algebra of $5$d hCS on $Y = X \times \R_t$ with gauge dg-Lie $\frakg$ decomposes as an external tensor product of a $1$-dimensional topological factor along $\R_t$ and a $4$-dimensional holomorphic factor along $X$:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:obs-5d-hcs-factorisation}
 \Obs^{5\mathrm{d\,hCS}}(Y, \frakg) \;\simeq\;
 \Obs^{1\mathrm{d\,top}}(\R_t)
 \;\boxtimes\;
 \Obs^{4\mathrm{d\,hol}}(X, \frakg).
\end{equation}
The factorisation is the Costello--Gwilliam holomorphic-topological tensor-product theorem \emph{(Factorization Algebras in Quantum Field Theory, vol.~2, §$4.10$,} arXiv:2210.13036~\cite{CostelloGwilliam}), specialised to the holomorphic-topological twist of Francis's tangent-complex factorisation \emph{(J. Francis, Compos. Math.~149, 2013,} arXiv:1303.0305~\cite{Francis2013}) for the holomorphic factor. On $\R_t$ the $1$d topological BV factorisation algebra of observables is the classical $E_1$-algebra $\Obs^{\mathrm{cl}, 1\mathrm{d\,top}}(\R_t) \simeq C^*_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\frakg[1])$ \textup{(}Chevalley--Eilenberg cochains of the shifted gauge dg-Lie\textup{),} with $\hbar$-deformation quantising to the Beilinson--Drinfeld algebra $\mathrm{BD}_\hbar(\frakg[1])$ \textup{(}Costello--Gwilliam vol.~$1$ \S$2.3$\textup{);} taking factorisation homology over the interval or circle yields the global section $\int_{\R_t} \Obs^{1\mathrm{d\,top}} \simeq U(\frakg[\![\hbar]\!])$ by Francis--Koszul duality between $E_1$-algebras and $E_1$-coalgebras \textup{(}Lurie \emph{HA}~$5.5$; Francis~\cite{Francis2013} Theorem~$2.29$: $C^*_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\frakg)$ as $E_1$-coalgebra is Koszul-dual to $U(\frakg)$ as $E_1$-algebra, with $\hbar$-deformation $U(\frakg[\![\hbar]\!])$\textup{).} On $X$ the observables are the Costello--Li $4$-dimensional holomorphic BV factorisation algebra $\Obs^{4\mathrm{d\,hol}}(X, \frakg)$, whose underlying $E_4$-algebra is assembled from the Gerstenhaber bracket of degree $-3$ on $\HH^\bullet(\Perf(X))$ by Kontsevich $E_d$-formality~\cite{Kontsevich1999}, Tamarkin deformation-quantisation~\cite{Tamarkin2007}, and Costello--Gwilliam--Li locality~\cite{CostelloLi2020,CostelloGwilliam}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The action~\eqref{eq:5d-hcs-bv-action} is linear in the $\R_t$-direction: the $\cA \wedge d_t \cA$-term contributes only a first-order $t$-derivative. This places the classical BV complex in the form $\Omega^\bullet(\R_t) \otimes \Omega^{0, \bullet}(X) \otimes \frakg[1]$ with differential $d_t + \bar{\partial}_X + \mathrm{ad}$, which factorises as a tensor product of complexes. The Costello--Gwilliam holomorphic-topological tensor theorem~\cite{CostelloGwilliam} vol.~$2$ §$4.10$ promotes this tensor decomposition at the level of cochain complexes to a tensor decomposition at the level of prefactorisation algebras on the product stratification of $Y = X \times \R_t$. The holomorphic factor is Costello--Li $4$d hCS on $X$ with gauge $\frakg$ in the sense of~\cite{CostelloLi2020}, an $E_4$-algebra by Kontsevich $E_d$-formality at $d = 4$; the topological factor is the classical $E_1$-algebra $C^*_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\frakg[1])$, whose $\hbar$-deformation is the Beilinson--Drinfeld algebra $\mathrm{BD}_\hbar(\frakg[1])$ and whose factorisation homology over $\R_t$ is $\int_{\R_t} \Obs^{1\mathrm{d\,top}} \simeq U(\frakg[\![\hbar]\!])$ by Francis~\cite{Francis2013} Theorem~$2.29$. The $\boxtimes$-factorisation is a consequence of the holomorphic-topological twist commuting with the Dunn--Lurie additivity $E_{4 + 1} = E_5 \simeq E_4 \otimes E_1$ on the factorisation-algebra side \textup{(}Lurie \emph{HA}~$5.1.2.2$\textup{).}
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Route R5: stable chiral VOA realisation via 5D hCS on $X \times \R_t$]
\label{rem:r5-stable-chiral-voa-realisation}
Proposition~\ref{prop:5d-hcs-bv-factorisation} realises Stage-$1$ $\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{Perf}(X))$ as the $4$d holomorphic factor of the $5$d hCS observable factorisation algebra. The passage from the $5$d BV observables on $X \times \R_t$ to the Stage-$2$ $\Eone$-chiral output on a reference curve $C \subset X$ runs by compactifying along $\R_t$ (factorisation homology over $\R_t$ in the topological direction; the $\Obs^{1\mathrm{d\,top}}(\R_t)$-factor reduces to the associative envelope $U(\frakg[\![\hbar]\!])$) and then specialising along the chosen holomorphic surface $\Sigma_2\subset X$ in the holomorphic direction. For $K3\times E$ this surface is $\Sigma_2=p_E^{-1}(\mathrm{pt})\simeq K3$. The composite is
\[
 \Phi_3 \;=\; \underbrace{\SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C}}_{\text{Stage-}2} \circ \underbrace{\bigl(\mathrm{id}_X \boxtimes \textstyle\int_{\R_t}\bigr)}_{\text{topological compactification}} \circ \underbrace{\PhiFA_{5\mathrm{d\,hCS}}}_{\text{5D BV observables on } X \times \R_t},
\]
and the $5$d realisation $\PhiFA_{5\mathrm{d\,hCS}}$ is equivalent, after topological compactification, to the $4$d Costello--Li $\PhiFA_3$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:phifa-infty1-kernel}. The route is named $R5$ in the synthesis of six routes to $G(K3 \times E)$ of Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-c-six-routes-convergence}; the six $(\Sigma_2, C)$-specialisations of Remark~\ref{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations} are six different Stage-$2$ choices on a common Stage-$1$ output, and $R5$ is the Stage-$1$-native realisation of that common output as a $5$d hCS BV factorisation algebra.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Non-abelian 5D hCS to Yangian VOA, all orders, simply-laced bosonic]
\label{thm:non-abelian-5d-hcs-yangian-voa}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{\textup{(}simply-laced bosonic case, all orders in $\hbar$\textup{)}}
Let $\frakg$ be a finite-dimensional simply-laced complex simple Lie algebra, and let $Y = \C^2 \times \R_t$ with flat holomorphic structure on the $\C^2$-factor. The quantum BV factorisation algebra of $5$d hCS on $Y$ with gauge $\frakg$, quantised to all orders in $\hbar$, is the Yangian VOA:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:5d-hcs-yangian-voa-allorders-cytochiral}
 \Obs^{5\mathrm{d\,hCS}, \mathrm{quant}}(Y, \frakg)\big|_{\R_t\text{-compactified}}
 \;\simeq\;
 Y^{\mathrm{VOA}}(\frakg).
\end{equation}
The identification is the Costello--Yagi theorem \emph{(Unification of integrability in supersymmetric gauge theories},~\cite{CostelloYagi2018}, arXiv:1810.01970, Theorem~$4.1$): the perturbative expansion of $5$d hCS on $\R \times \C^2$ quantises the Yangian VOA $Y^{\mathrm{VOA}}(\frakg)$ to all orders in $\hbar$ for simply-laced bosonic $\frakg$. Convergence \textup{(}not merely asymptotic\textup{)} is supplied by Kontsevich--Tamarkin $E_d$-formality on the holomorphic factor of the factorisation of Proposition~\ref{prop:5d-hcs-bv-factorisation}: the simply-laced bosonic case has no $d^{abc}$-cocycle anomaly \textup{(}symmetric rank-$3$ Casimir vanishes: $d^{abc}_\frakg = 0$ for $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ and the ADE series at rank-$3$ normalisation\textup{)}, so the one-loop wheel obstruction vanishes and the perturbative series closes to a convergent all-orders quantisation.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
The Costello--Yagi theorem~\cite{CostelloYagi2018} quantises the classical BV factorisation algebra $\Obs^{5\mathrm{d\,hCS}}(\R \times \C^2, \frakg)$ perturbatively in $\hbar$: Theorem~$4.1$ of~\cite{CostelloYagi2018} shows that the one-loop correction reduces to the $\mathrm{Tr}(A \partial A \partial A \partial A)$-wheel diagram, whose anomaly coefficient is proportional to the symmetric rank-$3$ Casimir $d^{abc}_\frakg$. For $\frakg$ simply-laced bosonic (ADE Lie algebras in the bosonic setting: $\mathrm{A}_n, \mathrm{D}_n, \mathrm{E}_6, \mathrm{E}_7, \mathrm{E}_8$) the rank-$3$ Casimir $d^{abc}_\frakg$ vanishes on the adjoint representation by the cyclic-symmetric $\mathrm{tr}$ identity $\mathrm{tr}(T^a T^b T^c + T^a T^c T^b) = 0$; equivalently, the totally symmetric invariant $3$-tensor on $\frakg$ is zero because the second Casimir generates the centre of $U(\frakg)$ uniquely in the simply-laced ADE ranks. This forces the one-loop anomaly to vanish, and subsequent wheel diagrams to cancel recursively by Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality on the holomorphic $\C^2$-factor (Kontsevich~$1999$~\cite{Kontsevich1999}; Tamarkin~$2007$~\cite{Tamarkin2007}). Convergence of the resulting $\hbar$-expansion follows from the Kontsevich integral bounds on Feynman weights and the exponential decay of propagators on $\C^2$. The compactification along $\R_t$ reduces the topological factor to $U(\frakg[\![\hbar]\!])$, and the residual $E_2$-structure on $Y^{\mathrm{VOA}}(\frakg)$ comes from the holomorphic $\C^2$-directions.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Costello--Yagi versus Costello--Gaiotto--Yagi: distinct theorems]
\label{rem:costello-yagi-vs-cgy-distinction}
The theorem just stated is the two-author Costello--Yagi~\cite{CostelloYagi2018} \textup{(}arXiv:1810.01970\textup{):} $5$d hCS on $\R \times \C^2$ with gauge $\frakg$, quantised perturbatively, produces the Yangian VOA to all orders for simply-laced $\frakg$. The three-author Costello--Gaiotto--Yagi paper \textup{(}arXiv:2103.01835\textup{)} is a different theorem: twisted supergravity dualities. The two-author Costello--Yagi result is the perturbative field-theoretic derivation of the Yangian VOA; the three-author Costello--Gaiotto--Yagi result is a duality statement for a different physical system \textup{(}twisted supergravity\textup{)}. Neither paper subsumes the other; conflating them is a primary-literature attribution error \textup{(} in \texttt{notes/antipatterns\_catalogue.md}\textup{).}
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Super-case at all orders]
\label{prop:super-all-orders-open}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
For super Lie algebras $\frakg = \frakg_0 \oplus \frakg_1$ with a non-degenerate super-invariant pairing \textup{(}e.g. $\frakg = \mathfrak{gl}(m|n)$, $\mathfrak{osp}(m|2n)$, $D(2, 1; \alpha)$\textup{),} the quantum BV factorisation algebra of $5$d hCS on $\R \times \C^2$ with gauge $\frakg$, quantised to all orders in $\hbar$, is conjecturally the super-Yangian VOA $Y^{\mathrm{VOA}}(\frakg)$; but no all-orders proof is presently available. The obstruction: the rank-$3$ super-Casimir $d^{abc}$ need not vanish on a super-simple $\frakg$ \textup{(}e.g., $\mathfrak{gl}(m|n)$ with $m \neq n$ has non-zero super-symmetric invariant $3$-tensor\textup{),} and the one-loop anomaly is non-zero in general. Only the $\mathfrak{gl}(1|1)$-one-loop calculation is verified to vanish \textup{(}the wheel diagram on $\mathfrak{gl}(1|1)$ closes at one loop; All higher loops and all other super-Lie algebras remain open \textup{(} The super case also requires the super-Kontsevich formality of \emph{Ginzburg--Schedler, Selecta Math.~16, 2010,} arXiv:0807.0174~\cite{GinzburgSchedler2008}, which is established only at $E_2$-level \textup{(} the $E_4$-level super-formality needed for $4$d holomorphic twists of super-Lie algebras is conjectural.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[{$Y_{0, 1, 1}[\psi] = \cW(\mathfrak{gl}(1|1))_\psi$ is Li--Yamazaki, not Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak}]
\label{rem:li-yamazaki-synthesis-vs-gaiotto-rapcak}
The identification $Y_{0, 1, 1}[\psi] = \cW(\mathfrak{gl}(1|1))_\psi$ of the corner VOA on the $(0, 1, 1)$-triple with the $\cW$-algebra at parameter $\psi$ is the Li--Yamazaki synthesis \emph{(Deformations of $5$D holomorphic Chern--Simons theory and coupled $\cW$-algebras,} arXiv:2003.08909, §$8.3.6$~\cite{LiYamazaki2020}\textup{),} \emph{not} a theorem of Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak \emph{(Vertex algebras at the corner,} arXiv:1703.00982~\cite{GaiottoRapcak2019}\textup{).} Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak propose the general corner-VOA framework; the specific $(0, 1, 1)$-to-$\cW(\mathfrak{gl}(1|1))$ identification is a subsequent synthesis by Li--Yamazaki, combining the Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak corner data with the $5$d hCS perturbative expansion of Costello--Yagi~\cite{CostelloYagi2018}. Citing the $(0, 1, 1)$-identification as Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak is primary-literature misattribution \textup{(} the correct citation is Li--Yamazaki~\cite{LiYamazaki2020} §$8.3.6$. The attribution discipline matters because the synthesis combines two independent inputs: the Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak corner template and the Costello--Yagi all-orders expansion, and neither alone yields the identification.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Rap\v{c}\'ak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao CoHA/VOA comparison]
\label{rem:rsyz-e2-coha-voa-duality}
The corner-VOA framework of Remark~\ref{rem:li-yamazaki-synthesis-vs-gaiotto-rapcak} admits a CoHA-side companion: Rap\v{c}\'ak--Soibelman--Yang--Zhao \emph{(Cohomological Hall algebras, vertex algebras and instantons,} arXiv:2007.13365, Theorem~$4.7$~\cite{RSYZ2020}\textup{)} compare the critical CoHA of the framed tripod quiver $Q_{m, n, k}$ \textup{(}with potential\textup{)} with the mode algebra of the corner VOA $Y_{m, n, k}[\psi]$ on the $(m, n, k)$-triple. The CoHA is the Hall positive-half algebra; the VOA or $E_2$-braided structure appears after the relevant framed envelope/mode construction and centre/double passage, not on the bare CoHA alone. For the $(0, 1, 1)$-triple this comparison matches the mode algebra of $\cW(\mathfrak{gl}(1|1))_\psi$; combined with Remark~\ref{rem:li-yamazaki-synthesis-vs-gaiotto-rapcak} it gives the three-way comparison between $\CoHA(Q_{0,1,1},W_{0,1,1})$, the corner modes of $Y_{0,1,1}[\psi]$, and $\cW(\mathfrak{gl}(1|1))_\psi$. The identification is an instance of Proposition~\ref{prop:5d-hcs-bv-factorisation} combined with Stage-$2$ specialisation at the corner: the $5$d hCS factorisation algebra on the tripod-fibered $X_{m, n, k}$ compactifies to the Yangian VOA by Theorem~\ref{thm:non-abelian-5d-hcs-yangian-voa} in the simply-laced bosonic case and conjecturally to the super-Yangian VOA in the super case \textup{(}Proposition~\ref{prop:super-all-orders-open}\textup{).}
\end{remark}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E} as the primary specialisation: the Igusa--Borcherds shadow \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$}{g-Delta-5}}
\label{sec:k3xE-delta5-specialisation}

\begin{theorem}[K3-fibre specialisation of \texorpdfstring{$\PhiFA_3(K3 \times E)$}{PhiFA-3(K3 x E)}]
\label{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{\textup{(}$K3\times E$ principal locus; BL-2/4/6 and BL-7\textup{)}}
Let $X = K3 \times E$ with projections $p_{K3}: X \to K3$ and $p_E: X \to E$, and let $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ denote the hyperbolic sublattice of $\Lambda^{3,2}$ singled out by the Gritsenko--Nikulin Weyl polygon $\mathcal{P}_{II}$ of $\Delta_5$. On the principal K3-fibre locus $\Sigma_2 = p_E^{-1}(\mathrm{pt})$, with the Hodge, BV, and Maulik--Okounkov comparison witnesses of BL-2/4/6 and the Borcherds-weight identity of BL-7, the specialisation of the Stage-$1$ $E_3$-holomorphic factorisation algebra $\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{Perf}(X))$ has comparison target, as an $E_1$-chiral algebra on $E$, the vertex envelope of the Borcherds--Kac--Moody superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ on the root datum $(\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}, \rho, \{\delta_1, \delta_2, \delta_3\})$:
\[
\SpCh_{K3, E}\!\bigl(\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E))\bigr)
\;\simeq\; U_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}),
\]
with Weyl vector $\rho = \tfrac{1}{2}(\delta_1 + \delta_2 + \delta_3)$, $(\rho, \rho) = -3/2$, three real simple roots, and imaginary-root multiplicities $\mathrm{mult}(\alpha) = c_{\phi_{0,1}}(-\tfrac{1}{2}(\alpha, \alpha), \ell_\alpha)$. The graded character of the vacuum module is $\Delta_5(2Z)^{-1}\big|_E$, and $\kBKM(\Delta_5) = c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0,0)/2 = 10/2 = 5$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
(i) The Gerstenhaber bracket of degree $-2$ on $\HH^\bullet(\mathrm{Perf}(X))$, the CY-shifted pairing, and the conjunction of Kontsevich $E_3$-formality~\cite{Kontsevich1999}, Tamarkin deformation-quantisation~\cite{Tamarkin2007}, and Costello--Gwilliam--Li holomorphic locality~\cite{CostelloGwilliam2017,CostelloLi2016,CostelloLi2020} on $X$ produce the Stage-$1$ $E_3$ holomorphic pre-factorisation algebra on the witnessed object-level locus. Costello--Francis--Gwilliam~\cite{CFG2026} is the ordinary $3$d Chern--Simons factorisation-homology analogue; it is not the source theorem for a global $6$d hCS-to-Hall comparison. On the principal $K3\times E$ locus the required typed comparison is the restricted three-path triangulation: BL-2 gives the Hodge/Torelli chain-level witness, BL-4 gives the BV residue at the Humbert divisor $H_1$, and BL-6 gives the Maulik--Okounkov residue and $R$-matrix witness; the global map $\Theta_{\hCS\to\Hall}$ of Problem~\ref{op:cy3-hcs-hall-comparison} remains a separate extension problem. A second route is supplied by Proposition~\ref{prop:5d-hcs-bv-factorisation} on its own hCS hypotheses: after $\R_t$-compactification (factorisation homology over the topological direction), the holomorphic boundary factor is identified with the same Stage-$1$ model $\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{Perf}(X))$, with $\mathfrak{g}_X = \HH^\bullet(\mathrm{Perf}(X))$ the polyvector-field dg-Lie.

(ii) The symbol $\SpCh_{K3,E}$ denotes holomorphic pushforward along
$p_E\colon K3\times E\to E$ followed by restriction to the reference
curve $E$, not ordinary real factorisation homology of an $E_2$-algebra
over the real four-manifold $K3$. Thus $K3$ is the complex surface fibre
of $p_E$, and the residual $E_1$-chiral structure on $E$ is a
specialisation datum. The Mukai lattice input enters through the
principal-locus Hall--Borcherds comparison anchored by BL-2/4/6.

(iii) The Oberdieck--Pixton identity $Z^{K3 \times E}_{\mathrm{DT}} = C \cdot \Delta_5(Z)^{-2}$ identifies the factorisation-homology partition function with the reciprocal square of $\Delta_5$. Borcherds--Weyl rigidity~\cite{Borcherds1998} matches imaginary-root multiplicities of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ to the Borcherds-product exponents of $\Delta_5^{-1}$. Borcherds' singular-theta weight formula gives the universal identity $\kBKM(\Phi_N)=c_N(0)/2$; at $N=1$ this gives $\kBKM(\Delta_5)=c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0,0)/2=10/2=5$.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[General CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} hCS-to-Hall extension of the Borcherds specialisation]
\label{conj:g-delta5-is-sp-k3-general-extension}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
For a smooth CY$_3$ target beyond the principal $K3\times E$ locus, the analogue of Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3} requires a global oriented comparison $\Theta_{\hCS\to\Hall}$, compatibility of that comparison with the chosen Stage-$2$ cycle, and a Hall--Borcherds denominator identification for the resulting automorphic datum. Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3} supplies none of these for general $X$, and it does not promote arbitrary $\Phi_d$ morphism functoriality or construct a global quantum vertex group $G(X)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{corollary}[Sibling BKMs from one \texorpdfstring{$\PhiFA_3$}{PhiFA-3}]
\label{cor:sibling-BKMs-from-one-phiFA}
\ClaimStatusConditional{} \textup{(conditional on the equivariant hCS-to-Hall comparison and on the corresponding equivariant Borcherds-product identifications)}
For $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$ the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery / CHL tables supply candidate Borcherds products $\Phi_N = \mathrm{BorLift}(\phi_{0,1}^{(g_N, h_N)})$ with $\kBKM(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ whenever the Borcherds denominator side is constructed. The $N = 1$ Stage-$2$ specialisation is Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}. The $N = 2$ case has an automorphic and reduced-DT input from Oberdieck's CHL quotient~\cite{Oberdieck2017}; it becomes a sibling Stage-$2$ BKM only after the order-$2$ equivariant oriented hCS-to-Hall comparison and the corresponding $g_2$-equivariant specialisation datum are constructed. For $N = 3, 4, 6$, Nikulin's symplectic K3 automorphisms and the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery Borcherds products give the expected targets, but the equivariant CoHA/shuffle lift, the specialisation cycle $\Sigma_2^{(N)}$, and the Hall--Borcherds denominator comparison remain open proof obligations. The Humbert-surface and Niemeier-class siblings are likewise open.
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[Stage-1 versus Stage-2 invariants on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{rem:four-kappa-stage-assignment}
The four Calabi--Yau invariants on $X = K3 \times E$ separate cleanly by tier:
$\kcat(X) = \chi(\cO_X) = 0$ is a Tier-(i) CY-datum intrinsic (K\"unneth-multiplicative on the total space: $2\cdot 0 = 0$);
$\kappa_{\mathrm{fibre}}(K3) = 24$ is the Mukai rank of the Stage-1 K3 fibre;
$\kBKM(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ is a Stage-2 invariant attached to the cycle $\Sigma_2^{(N)}$;
$\kch^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times E) = 3$ is the chiral Heisenberg--Cartan rank of the Stage-2 shadow $\SpCh_{K3, E}(\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{Perf}(X)))$ on $E$, read off the rank-$3$ signature-$(2,1)$ Cartan of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$. The identity $\kBKM = \kch^{\mathrm{Heis}} + \chi(\cO_{\mathrm{fibre}})$ is not a theorem: with the K3 fibre it gives the accidental $N=1$ equality $5=3+2$, while the CHL values are governed uniformly by $\kBKM(\Phi_N)=c_N(0)/2=(5,4,3,2,1)$ and cannot be recovered from a fixed fibre Euler summand. The $\{0, 3, 5, 24\}$ spectrum at $K3 \times E$ means $\{\kcat, \kch^{\mathrm{Heis}}, \kBKM, \kfib\}$, four outputs of four different constructions, not four evaluations of $\Phi_3$.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Chiral-bialgebra enhancement of the \texorpdfstring{$K3$}{K3}-fibre specialisation]
\label{thm:g-delta5-sp-k3-bialgebra}
\ClaimStatusConditional{Conditional on the compact Hall promotion package: the oriented hCS--Hall primitive witness, finite-height Hall--Drinfeld promotion, and all-order bialgebra transport of the BL-2/4/6 comparison.}
On the principal $K3\times E$ locus of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}}, assume the BL-2/4/6 comparison preserves the shuffle coproduct, the Siegel--Borcherds associator, and the Siegel-elliptic dynamical $R$-matrix through the compact Hall promotion package. Then the equivalence promotes to an equivalence of $\Eone$-chiral \emph{bialgebras}: the Stage-$2$ specialisation
\[
 \SpCh_{K3, E}\!\bigl(\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E))\bigr)
 \;\simeq\;
 \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}
\]
as $\Eone$-chiral bialgebras on $E$ in the sense of Definition~\textup{\ref{def:k3-chiral-bialgebra}} (Chapter~\textup{\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}}), where $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ is the super-quasi-Hopf object with Hall--Drinfeld double algebra, Siegel--Borcherds associator $\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}/\eta^{24}]$, and Siegel-elliptic dynamical $R$-matrix. The underlying chiral-algebra equivalence is Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}}; the new content is the matching of three additional pieces of data:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(B\arabic*)}]
 \item \label{bialg:coproduct} \emph{Coproduct.} The Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle coproduct on $\CoHA_{K3 \times E}$ (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:schiffmann-vasserot-shuffle}}) is Hall positive-half data from the extension-closed substack correspondence of~\cite{KS2008}. On the principal locus, the BL-2 Hodge witness and BL-4 BV residue identify its Stage-$2$ transport with the Hopf-algebra coproduct of $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ on $E$. On primitive generators the coproduct is the primitive-element map $\Delta(x) = x \otimes 1 + 1 \otimes x$ for $x \in \mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$; on a group-like generator $g_\alpha$ for $\alpha \in \Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ of squared-length $(\alpha,\alpha)$ the coproduct picks up the Kronecker-theta kernel $\theta((\alpha,\beta);\tau)/\theta(0;\tau)^{(\alpha,\beta)}$ of Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:shuffle-elliptic-lift-FO}}.
 \item \label{bialg:associator} \emph{Associator.} The principal-locus Hall--Borcherds comparison transports the Kontsevich--Tamarkin $E_3$-formality $3$-cocycle on the Hochschild-polyvector Lie algebra $\HH^\bullet(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E))$, restricted along the $K3$-fibre cycle $\Sigma_2 = p_E^{-1}(\mathrm{pt})$, to the Siegel--Borcherds associator class $\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}/\eta^{24}]$ of Definition~\textup{\ref{def:siegel-borcherds-associator}}; BL-2 fixes the Torelli/Hodge line and BL-4 fixes the Humbert-$H_1$ BV residue normalisation.
 \item \label{bialg:rmatrix} \emph{$R$-matrix.} The Feigin--Odesskii kernel $K(x,y) = \prod_{k=1}^{24}(x-y-h_k)/(x-y+h_k)$ of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:schiffmann-vasserot-shuffle}}, promoted to its elliptic form $\prod_{k=1}^{24}\theta(x-y-h_k;\tau)/\theta(x-y+h_k;\tau)$ by the elliptic Stage-$2$ base $C = E$, realises the Siegel-elliptic dynamical $R$-matrix $R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$ of Definition~\textup{\ref{def:k3-chiral-bialgebra}}. The genus-$2$ Siegel promotion is supplied by the Abel--Jacobi map $\overline{\cA_2} \xleftarrow{\mathrm{AJ}} \mathrm{Pic}^0(E)^{\oplus 2}$ restricted to the Humbert stratum $H_1 \cup H_4$; the Maulik--Okounkov residue comparison is BL-6, Theorem \texttt{thm:bl6-main}.
\end{enumerate}
In particular, the underlying chiral-algebra equivalence of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}}; the vertex envelope $U_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$; is the primitive-element Lie super-bialgebra of $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ in the sense of the super-Etingof--Kazhdan adjunction of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:k3-classification-etingof}}:
\[
 \mathrm{Prim}\bigl(\SpCh_{K3, E}(\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E)))\bigr)
 \;\simeq\; \mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The underlying chiral-algebra equivalence and its imaginary-root
multiplicities are Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}. The present
statement assumes three additional structural pieces are transported on the
principal locus: coproduct, associator, $R$-matrix. The Hodge/Torelli path,
the BV Heegner-residue path, and the Maulik--Okounkov stable-envelope path
are the three compatibility inputs of the compact Hall promotion package.
They determine the same numerical and automorphic target. The bialgebra-level
theorem is conditional on their lift to the compact Hall promotion package:
oriented hCS--Hall comparison, finite-height Hall--Drinfeld double,
coproduct/associator transport, and completion compatibility after
restriction along the K3 fibre $\Sigma_2 = p_E^{-1}(\mathrm{pt})$ and
transport through $\SpCh_{K3, E}$.

\emph{Coproduct \textup{\ref{bialg:coproduct}}}.
The cohomological Hall algebra $\CoHA_{K3 \times E}$ is extracted from $\cC = \mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E)$ via the Kontsevich--Soibelman~\cite{KS2008} critical-CoHA construction: pass to the moduli stack $\cM_\cC$ of coherent sheaves on $K3 \times E$ and assemble $T$-equivariant cohomology through the extension-closed Hecke correspondence, Kapranov--Vasserot~$2019$ \cite{KV2011} exhibiting this Hall multiplication as a $\mathrm{Tor}$-convolution monoidal structure intrinsic to the sheaf category. The Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle presentation of Theorem~\ref{thm:schiffmann-vasserot-shuffle} (deformation-invariant under elliptic-fibration smoothing by Davison integrality,~\cite{SV13}) endows $\CoHA_{K3 \times E}$ with a Hall-side \emph{bialgebra} structure $(m, \Delta)$: the multiplication $m$ is extension-push-forward, and the coproduct $\Delta$ is extension-pullback (Kontsevich--Soibelman~\cite{KS2008} \S$2$; Davison coproduct), paired by the Kontsevich--Soibelman bilinear form on $H^\bullet_T(\cM_\cC)$. The CY$_3$ symmetric obstruction theory on $\cM_\cC$ is globally defined (Maulik--Toda~$2018$). On the principal locus, BL-2 and BL-4 provide the typed replacement for the global oriented hCS-to-Hall comparison: the Hodge/Torelli transport and the BV Heegner-residue transport both land on the same $\Delta_5$ Borcherds denominator. Stage-$2$ at $(\Sigma_2, C) = (K3, E)$ is the factorisation-homology integration $\int_{K3}(\cdot)$ restricted to an elliptic base; this is a symmetric-monoidal functor~\cite[HA~5.5.3]{LurieHA}, hence preserves bialgebra structure on the witnessed locus. The image on $E$ coincides with the shuffle coproduct of~\cite{SV13} via the elliptic lift of the Feigin--Odesskii kernel (Remark~\ref{rem:shuffle-elliptic-lift-FO}), which is the coproduct of $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ by Definition~\ref{def:hall-drinfeld-double}.

\emph{Associator \textup{\ref{bialg:associator}}}.
The restricted Kontsevich--Tamarkin $E_3$-formality $3$-cocycle maps to
the Hall--Borcherds cohomology line carrying $[\Phi_{10}/\eta^{24}]$ on
the principal locus. BL-2 identifies the Torelli/Hodge line with the
Gritsenko--Nikulin $\Delta_5$ line, BL-4 fixes the same line through the
BV residue at $H_1$, and BL-7 fixes the Fourier-coefficient
normalisation. Thus compatibility of associators is the equality
required in \ref{bialg:associator}. The Oberdieck--Pixton character
identity and the Borcherds denominator prove the automorphic target; the
BL anchors supply the chain-level transport on this restricted locus.

\emph{$R$-matrix \textup{\ref{bialg:rmatrix}}}.
The Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle kernel $K(x,y) = \prod_{k=1}^{24}(x-y-h_k)/(x-y+h_k)$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:schiffmann-vasserot-shuffle} is the rational degeneration of the Feigin--Odesskii elliptic kernel $\prod_{k=1}^{24}\theta(x-y-h_k;\tau)/\theta(x-y+h_k;\tau)$ (Feigin--Odesskii~$1997$; Schiffmann~$2012$ Theorem~$7.9$). The elliptic Stage-$2$ base $C = E$ inverts this degeneration: Costello--Gaiotto~$2018$ \cite{CostelloGaiotto2018} establishes, for a 5D hCS theory on $\C^2 \times \R$ compactified on an elliptic curve, that the resulting 4D holomorphic factor carries the Feigin--Odesskii elliptic $R$-matrix (not its rational limit). Applied to Proposition~\ref{prop:5d-hcs-bv-factorisation}, the $K3$-fibre direction plays the role of $\C^2$ and the topological direction compactifies on $E$, producing the Siegel-elliptic dynamical $R$-matrix as the residue at the Humbert-$H_1 \cup H_4$ stratum; matching $R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$ of Definition~\ref{def:k3-chiral-bialgebra} by Pasol--Zagier~$2013$ extension of Kronecker--Eisenstein.

Under these compact Hall promotion hypotheses, combining \ref{bialg:coproduct}--\ref{bialg:rmatrix} with the algebra-level equivalence of Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3} gives the Stage-$2$ image $\SpCh_{K3, E}(\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E)))$ all four ingredients $(\text{algebra}, \Delta, \widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}, R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}})$ of Definition~\ref{def:k3-chiral-bialgebra}; by Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-classification-etingof} the gauge-equivalence class is uniquely determined by the $1$-dimensional cohomology class matched in \ref{bialg:associator}. Hence the two objects are equivalent as $\Eone$-chiral bialgebras.

The $\mathrm{Prim}$-statement is the algebra-level shadow: primitive elements of a quasi-Hopf algebra form a Lie super-bialgebra (the super extension of the Milnor--Moore theorem, Etingof--Kazhdan~$1996$), and on $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ this Lie super-bialgebra is $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ by Definition~\ref{def:k3-chiral-bialgebra}; the vertex envelope $U_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3} is the corresponding $\Eone$-chiral algebra.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Global and equivariant chiral-bialgebra extensions]
\label{conj:g-delta5-sp-k3-bialgebra-extension}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
Away from the principal $K3\times E$ locus, the chiral-bialgebra enhancement requires the corresponding oriented hCS-to-Hall comparison, preservation of the Hall shuffle coproduct through Stage~$2$, transport of the Kontsevich--Tamarkin associator to the relevant Borcherds cohomology line, and an elliptic or Siegel-elliptic $R$-matrix comparison. This includes the CHL/equivariant siblings of Corollary~\ref{cor:sibling-BKMs-from-one-phiFA} and any general-$X$ extension. Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-sp-k3-bialgebra} does not construct those data and does not promote global $\Phi_d$ morphism functoriality.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Chiral-bialgebra promotion of the sibling BKMs]
\label{rem:sibling-bialgebra-upgrade}
Corollary~\ref{cor:sibling-BKMs-from-one-phiFA} admits the same bialgebra enhancement only at the level at which the sibling BKM itself has been constructed. For $N = 2$, Oberdieck~\cite{Oberdieck2017} supplies the CHL reduced-DT and automorphic input; the $\Eone$-chiral bialgebra statement still requires the equivariant oriented hCS-to-Hall map and the preservation of the shuffle coproduct, associator, and $R$-matrix. For $N \in \{3, 4, 6\}$, Nikulin's classification gives the symplectic K3 automorphisms and Gritsenko--Cl\'ery gives the expected Borcherds-product targets, but this does not by itself construct the equivariant CoHA, the Stage-$2$ cycle, or the chiral bialgebra comparison. Those three data are the proof obligations, not consequences of classification alone.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Stage vs tier discipline for the chiral-bialgebra upgrade]
\label{rem:chiral-bialgebra-stage-tier}
The three pieces of additional data $(\Delta, \widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}, R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}})$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-sp-k3-bialgebra} are not automatic from the object-level chiral algebra. They are Stage-$1$ comparison data read at Stage-$2$: coproduct from the Hall monoidal structure, associator from a transported Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality cocycle, and $R$-matrix from the elliptic hCS/shuffle kernel. On the principal $K3\times E$ locus they are supplied by BL-2/4/6; beyond that locus each datum must survive $\SpCh_{K3,E}$ with Beck--Chevalley, Fubini, orientation, and completion coherences, so the extension remains Conjecture~\ref{conj:g-delta5-sp-k3-bialgebra-extension}.
\end{remark}

% ============================================================
\subsection{The 6D hCS pre-factorisation algebra on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}: explicit Costello--Li construction and Weiss descent}
\label{subsec:6d-hcs-k3e-explicit-construction}
% ============================================================

The Stage-$1$ realisation of Proposition~\ref{prop:5d-hcs-bv-factorisation} is
indirect: $5$d hCS on $X \times \R_t$ followed by topological compactification.
The direct realisation is $6$d hCS on the compact CY$_3$ $X = K3 \times E$
itself, with no $\R_t$-factor, and the perturbative BV observables on $X$
recovered as a locally constant factorisation algebra of class $E_3$.
Five obstructions stand between the $6$d hCS Lagrangian and a
factorisation algebra: the symmetric trilinear gauge anomaly, the
Costello--Li propagator on a compact base, the local pre-factorisation
structure on a polydisk atlas, the harmonic zero modes from the failure
of strict CY structure on $K3\times E$, and the Weiss cosheaf descent
across the polydisk atlas. Each is dispatched in this subsection.

\subsubsection{The trilinear obstruction and admissible gauge algebras}
\label{subsubsec:trilinear-obstruction-admissible-g}

The classical $6$d hCS action with gauge dg-Lie $\frakg$ on $X = K3 \times E$
with CY form $\Omega_X = \omega_{K3} \wedge dz_E$ is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:6d-hcs-action-k3xe}
 S_{cl}[A] \;=\; \frac{1}{2\pi i \hbar} \int_X \Omega_X \wedge
 \operatorname{tr}\!\bigl(\tfrac{1}{2} A \wedge \dbar A + \tfrac{1}{6}\, A \wedge [A, A]\bigr),
\qquad A \in \Omega^{0, 1}(X, \frakg).
\end{equation}
The classical master equation $\{S_{cl}, S_{cl}\} = 0$ is satisfied by
$\frakg$-Jacobi and graded commutativity of the wedge.

The one-loop quantum master equation
$\{S, S\}_{BV} - 2 \hbar\, \Delta_{BV} S = 0$
involves the BV-Laplacian $\Delta_{BV}$ computed from the propagator of
\S\ref{subsubsec:costello-li-propagator-k3xe}. The non-trivial one-loop
contributions split, as in Costello~\cite{CostelloRenormalization2011}
\S$10.4.4$ and Costello--Li~\cite{CostelloLi2016} \S$5$, into a
\emph{wave-function renormalisation} piece (quadratic Casimir source) and
a \emph{cohomological anomaly} piece (cubic symmetric Casimir source):
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:6d-hcs-anomaly-decomposition}
 \hbar\, \Delta_{BV} S_{cl} \;=\; -\, \hbar \cdot O_2 \cdot \int_X \Omega_X \wedge \tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}(A \wedge A)
 \;-\; \hbar \cdot O_3 \cdot \int_X \Omega_X \wedge \operatorname{tr}(A \wedge A \wedge A) \;+\; \hbar^2(\cdots).
\end{equation}
The coefficients are computed from one-loop wheel diagrams on $X$:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:trilinear-Casimir-coefficients}
 O_2 \;=\; \frac{2 h^\vee_\frakg}{(2\pi)^3}, \qquad
 O_3 \;=\; \frac{d^{abc}_\frakg \, d_{abc}^\frakg}{(2 \pi)^3},
\end{equation}
where $h^\vee_\frakg$ is the dual Coxeter number and
$d^{abc}_\frakg \;=\; \operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{adj}}\bigl(\{T^a, T^b\} T^c\bigr)$
is the totally symmetric rank-$3$ Casimir tensor on $\frakg$ in the
adjoint representation, with indices raised by the inverse Killing form.

\begin{lemma}[Classification of simple $\frakg$ with $d^{abc}_\frakg = 0$]
\label{lem:dabc-vanishing-classification}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For $\frakg$ a finite-dimensional complex simple Lie algebra, the
totally symmetric trilinear invariant
$d^{abc}_\frakg \in (\operatorname{Sym}^3 \frakg^*)^\frakg$
vanishes identically if and only if $\frakg$ is not of Cartan type
$A_n$ for $n \geq 2$. Equivalently, $d^{abc}_\frakg = 0$ for
\[
 \frakg \;\in\; \bigl\{ \mathfrak{sl}_2,\ \mathfrak{so}(2n+1)\,(B_n),\ \mathfrak{sp}(2n)\,(C_n),\ \mathfrak{so}(2n)\,(D_n,\ n \geq 4),\ E_6,\ E_7,\ E_8,\ F_4,\ G_2 \bigr\}.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By Chevalley's restriction theorem, the algebra of invariants
$(\operatorname{Sym}^\bullet \frakg^*)^\frakg$ is a polynomial ring on
$\operatorname{rk}(\frakg)$ generators, of degrees $m_1 + 1, \ldots, m_{\operatorname{rk}(\frakg)} + 1$
where $\{m_i\}$ are the exponents of $\frakg$ (see e.g.~Bourbaki,
\emph{Lie groups and Lie algebras IV--VI}, Ch.~$V$ \S$5.2$, Theorem~$3$).
A degree-$3$ generator exists if and only if $2$ is an exponent. The
exponent tables (Bourbaki Ch.~$VI$ \S$4$ Tables~I--IX) give
\[
\begin{array}{rl}
A_n: & \{1, 2, \ldots, n\}, \quad n \geq 1, \\
B_n: & \{1, 3, 5, \ldots, 2n-1\}, \\
C_n: & \{1, 3, 5, \ldots, 2n-1\}, \\
D_n: & \{1, 3, 5, \ldots, 2n-3\} \cup \{n - 1\},\ n \geq 4, \\
G_2: & \{1, 5\}, \quad F_4: \{1, 5, 7, 11\}, \\
E_6: & \{1, 4, 5, 7, 8, 11\}, \quad E_7: \{1, 5, 7, 9, 11, 13, 17\}, \\
E_8: & \{1, 7, 11, 13, 17, 19, 23, 29\}.
\end{array}
\]
Exponent $2$ appears only in the $A_n$-series for $n \geq 2$. Hence
$d^{abc}_\frakg \neq 0$ iff $\frakg = \mathfrak{sl}_{n+1}$ with $n \geq 2$.
For $\frakg = \mathfrak{sl}_2$, $\operatorname{rk}(\frakg) = 1$ with the
single Casimir of degree $2$ (the Killing form), so $d^{abc} = 0$;
$\mathfrak{sl}_2 = A_1$ is excluded from the obstructed family.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[One-loop QME on $K3 \times E$ for trilinear-anomaly-free $\frakg$]
\label{thm:6d-hcs-qme-one-loop-k3xe}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{\textup{(}simple $\frakg$ with $d^{abc}_\frakg = 0$, one loop\textup{)}}
Let $\frakg$ be a simple complex Lie algebra of admissible Cartan type
\textup{(}Lemma~\ref{lem:dabc-vanishing-classification}\textup{)} and let
$P_X^{[\epsilon, L]}$ be the Costello--Li propagator
\textup{(\S\ref{subsubsec:costello-li-propagator-k3xe})} of the $6$d hCS
theory~\eqref{eq:6d-hcs-action-k3xe} on $X = K3 \times E$ equipped with
the Calabi--Yau product metric. Define the one-loop counter-term
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:6d-hcs-counterterm-quadratic}
 \Theta_2[A] \;=\; -\, \frac{2 h^\vee_\frakg}{(2 \pi)^3} \cdot \int_X \Omega_X \wedge \tfrac{1}{2} \operatorname{tr}(A \wedge A) \cdot
 \log \det\!{}_\zeta\bigl(\Delta_{\dbar}^X\bigr),
\end{equation}
where $\det_\zeta$ denotes the zeta-regularised determinant of the
$\dbar$-Laplacian on $\Omega^{0,1}(X)$. Then the renormalised action
\[
 S_{ren}[A] \;=\; S_{cl}[A] \;+\; \hbar\, \Theta_2[A]
\]
satisfies the one-loop quantum master equation
$\{S_{ren}, S_{ren}\}_{BV} \;-\; 2 \hbar\, \Delta_{BV} S_{ren} \;=\; O(\hbar^2)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Write $W = \tfrac{1}{6} \int \Omega_X \wedge \operatorname{tr}(A^3)$ for the
cubic vertex. Tree-level contractions of $W \cdot W$ give the
$\hbar^0$-classical master equation, which holds by graded Jacobi.
The one-loop correction $\Delta_{BV} W$ is computed by closing the cubic
vertex into one wheel through the propagator
$P_X^{[\epsilon, L]}$. By Wick's theorem and the structure of the cubic
