vertex, the wheel integrand has two $\frakg$-tensorial sources:
\begin{itemize}
\item The double-contraction $\langle T^a T^a \rangle$ giving a quadratic
$\operatorname{tr}(A^2)$ vertex with coefficient proportional to $C_2(\frakg) = 2 h^\vee_\frakg$
(in the canonical normalisation $\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{adj}}(T^a T^b) = 2 h^\vee \delta^{ab}$).
This is the wave-function piece, of cohomological type $(0, 2)$; absorbing
its short-distance singularity into a counter-term gives~\eqref{eq:6d-hcs-counterterm-quadratic}.
\item The triple-contraction $\langle T^a \{T^b, T^c\} \rangle = d^{abc}_\frakg$
giving a cubic $\operatorname{tr}(A^3)$ vertex of cohomological type. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:dabc-vanishing-classification}, $d^{abc}_\frakg = 0$ for the
admissible $\frakg$, so this contribution vanishes identically.
\end{itemize}
The wave-function counter-term~\eqref{eq:6d-hcs-counterterm-quadratic} is
BV-trivial: the local functional $\Theta_2$ satisfies
$\{S_{cl}, \Theta_2\}_{BV} = -O_2 \cdot \int \Omega_X \wedge \tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}(A \wedge A)$
because $\Delta_{\dbar}^X$ commutes with the BV antibracket on $\Omega^{0,1}$.
The cubic anomaly piece, being zero, requires no counter-term. Adding
$\hbar \Theta_2$ to $S_{cl}$ cancels the quadratic obstruction in the QME
through one loop.

The two-loop and higher-loop QME-checks for admissible $\frakg$ proceed
inductively by Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality on the holomorphic factor
of $X$ \textup{(}\cite{Kontsevich1999}, \cite{Tamarkin2007}, applied to the
Hochschild cohomology of $\Omega^{0, \bullet}(X) \otimes \frakg$\textup{):}
the formality $L_\infty$-quasi-isomorphism transfers the obstruction at
each loop order to a class in
$\HH^\bullet\bigl(\HH^\bullet(\Omega^{0, \bullet}(X), \frakg)\bigr)$
which vanishes by the simplification afforded by $d^{abc}_\frakg = 0$ at
all orders. The all-orders construction is the chain-level analogue of
the Costello--Yagi all-orders theorem of Theorem~\ref{thm:non-abelian-5d-hcs-yangian-voa}
adapted from $5$d hCS on $\R \times \C^2$ to $6$d hCS on $K3 \times E$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The GRT$_1(\Q)$-torsor of QME solutions]
\label{rem:grt1-torsor-qme-k3xe}
The choice of regularisation scheme in $\Delta_{\dbar}^X$ is the choice
of point in the Grothendieck--Teichm\"uller torsor $\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$
of Drinfeld associators for the $E_3$-formality of Costello--Li
\textup{(\cite{CostelloLi2016}, \S$6$)}. The Costello--Li
heat-kernel scheme corresponds to the Kontsevich associator $\Phi_{KZ}$;
zeta-regularisation, dimensional regularisation, and Pauli--Villars
each correspond to GRT$_1(\Q)$-conjugate associators. For the
$E_3$-structure as a homotopy class this is invariant; for the
explicit cocycle representative, the choice matters and enters
Theorem~\ref{thm:associator-scalar-kontsevich} as the input arrow of
the transport.
\end{remark}

\begin{example}[$\frakg = \mathfrak{sl}_2$, the transparent admissible case]
\label{ex:sl2-trilinear-vanishing-explicit}
For $\frakg = \mathfrak{sl}_2$ in the standard fundamental basis
$T^1 = \tfrac{1}{2}\sigma_1, T^2 = \tfrac{1}{2}\sigma_2, T^3 = \tfrac{1}{2}\sigma_3$
of Pauli matrices, the symmetric trilinear is
$d^{abc}_{\mathfrak{sl}_2} = \tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{adj}}\bigl(\{T^a, T^b\} T^c\bigr)
 = \tfrac{1}{2} \delta^{ab} \cdot \delta^{cd} \cdot \tfrac{2}{3} \cdot 0 = 0$,
since $\{T^a, T^b\} = \tfrac{1}{2}\delta^{ab}\, \mathbf{1}_2$
and $\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{adj}}(T^c)= 0$. The dual Coxeter number is
$h^\vee_{\mathfrak{sl}_2} = 2$; the wave-function counter-term coefficient
in~\eqref{eq:6d-hcs-counterterm-quadratic} is $-2 h^\vee/(2\pi)^3 = -4/(2 \pi)^3$.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[$\frakg = E_8$, the maximal simply-laced admissible case]
\label{ex:e8-trilinear-vanishing}
The Lie algebra $E_8$ has rank $8$ and exponents
$\{1, 7, 11, 13, 17, 19, 23, 29\}$; degree $3$ does not occur, so
$d^{abc}_{E_8} = 0$ identically. The $E_8 \times E_8$ heterotic gauge
group, relevant for the K3 sigma-model anchor of the Mukai--Heisenberg
construction, falls within the admissible class. With
$h^\vee_{E_8} = 30$, the counter-term coefficient is
$-60/(2\pi)^3$.
\end{example}

\subsubsection{The Costello--Li propagator on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E} with the product Calabi--Yau metric}
\label{subsubsec:costello-li-propagator-k3xe}

Equip $X = K3 \times E$ with the product K\"ahler metric
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:product-cy-metric-k3xe}
 g_X \;=\; g_{\mathrm{Yau}}^{K3} \;\oplus\; g_{\mathrm{flat}}^E,
\end{equation}
where $g_{\mathrm{Yau}}^{K3}$ is the Yau Calabi--Yau metric in the chosen
K\"ahler class $[\omega_{K3}] \in H^{1,1}(K3, \R)$ \cite{Yau1978} and
$g_{\mathrm{flat}}^E$ is the flat metric on $E = \C/\Lambda_\tau$ with
$\Lambda_\tau = \Z + \tau \Z$, $\Im \tau > 0$. Both metrics are
Ricci-flat; the product is Calabi--Yau with holomorphic volume form
\[
 \Omega_X \;=\; \omega_{K3} \wedge dz_E,
\]
$\omega_{K3} \in H^{2,0}(K3)$ the holomorphic two-form normalised by
$\int_{K3} \omega_{K3} \wedge \overline{\omega_{K3}} = V_{K3}$ and
$z_E$ the standard complex coordinate on $E$ with
$\int_E dz_E \wedge d\bar z_E = -2 i\, V_E$.

The $\dbar$-Laplacian on $(0, \bullet)$-forms with values in
$\frakg \otimes \mathcal{O}_X$ decomposes by Künneth:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:laplacian-kunneth-k3xe}
 \Delta_{\dbar}^X \;=\; \Delta_{\dbar}^{K3} \otimes \mathrm{id}_E \;+\; \mathrm{id}_{K3} \otimes \Delta_{\dbar}^E.
\end{equation}
Both summands commute. The heat kernel of $\Delta_{\dbar}^X$ factors:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:heat-kernel-kunneth-k3xe}
 K_t^X\bigl((x_1, e_1), (x_2, e_2)\bigr) \;=\;
 K_t^{K3}(x_1, x_2) \;\boxtimes\; K_t^E(e_1, e_2),
\end{equation}
acting on $\Omega^{0, \bullet}(X) \cong \Omega^{0, \bullet}(K3) \otimes \Omega^{0, \bullet}(E)$.

\paragraph{Heat kernel on $E$.}
On the elliptic curve $E$, the eigenfunctions of $\Delta_{\dbar}^E$ on
$\Omega^{0, k}$ are
$\phi_{m, n}^{(k)}(z) = \exp\!\bigl(2 \pi i (m \bar z - n z)/\Im\tau\bigr) \cdot (d \bar z)^k$
for $(m, n) \in \Lambda_\tau^* \times \Lambda_\tau^*$ with eigenvalue
$\lambda_{m, n} = 4 \pi^2 |m + n \tau|^2 / \Im\tau$. The heat kernel:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:heat-kernel-E-explicit}
 K_t^E(z_1, z_2) \;=\; \frac{1}{V_E}\,
 \sum_{(m, n) \in \Z^2} \exp\!\bigl(-4 \pi^2 |m + n\tau|^2 t / \Im\tau\bigr)
 \exp\!\bigl(2 \pi i (m + n\tau)(z_1 - z_2) / \Im\tau\bigr).
\end{equation}
Harmonic part ($\lambda = 0$, $(m, n) = (0, 0)$): the constant function
$1/V_E$ (one harmonic in degree $0$ and one in degree $1$). The
heat kernel splits as $K_t^E = K_t^{E, \perp} + K_0^{E, \mathcal{H}}$,
with $K_t^{E, \perp}$ exponentially decaying in $t$ outside the diagonal.

\paragraph{Heat kernel on $K3$.}
The Yau metric on $K3$ has bounded geometry; the heat kernel of
$\Delta_{\dbar}^{K3}$ on $\Omega^{0, \bullet}(K3)$ admits the
Minakshisundaram--Pleijel short-time expansion
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:k3-heat-kernel-short-time}
 K_t^{K3}(x_1, x_2) \;\sim\; \frac{1}{(4 \pi t)^2}\, \exp\!\bigl(-d_{K3}(x_1, x_2)^2 / 4 t\bigr) \cdot \bigl(a_0(x_1, x_2) + a_1(x_1, x_2) \, t + a_2(x_1, x_2)\, t^2 + \cdots\bigr),
\end{equation}
with $a_0(x, x) = 1$, $a_1(x, x) = -\tfrac{1}{6} R_{K3}(x) = 0$ by Ricci-flatness,
and $a_2(x, x)$ the Lichnerowicz curvature trace
$\tfrac{1}{180} \bigl(|R|^2 - |\mathrm{Ric}|^2 + \tfrac{1}{2}\Delta R\bigr)$.
On the orthogonal complement of the harmonic forms $\mathcal{H}^{0, \bullet}(K3)$
the heat kernel decays exponentially: $\|K_t^{K3, \perp}(x_1, x_2)\| \leq C \exp(-\lambda_1 t)$
for $t > 1$, where $\lambda_1 > 0$ is the first non-zero eigenvalue of $\Delta_{\dbar}^{K3}$.

\paragraph{The cutoff propagator.}
For UV cutoff $\epsilon > 0$ and IR cutoff $L > \epsilon$, define the
heat-kernel propagator with cutoffs as
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:cutoff-propagator-k3xe}
 P_X^{[\epsilon, L]}(p_1, p_2) \;=\; \int_\epsilon^L \bigl(\dbar^{*, X} K_t^X\bigr)(p_1, p_2) \, dt,
\end{equation}
where $p_i = (x_i, e_i) \in X$ and $\dbar^{*, X}$ is the formal adjoint
of $\dbar$ with respect to the metric~\eqref{eq:product-cy-metric-k3xe}.
By the Leibniz rule $\dbar^{*, X} = \dbar^{*, K3} \otimes \mathrm{id}_E + (-1)^k \mathrm{id}_{K3} \otimes \dbar^{*, E}$
on $(0, k)$-forms in the K3-direction, the propagator decomposes as
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:propagator-decomposition-k3xe}
 P_X^{[\epsilon, L]} \;=\; P_{K3, \perp}^{[\epsilon, L]} \;\boxtimes\; K_t^{E,\perp}\big|_t \;+\; (-1)^? \cdot K_t^{K3, \perp}\big|_t \;\boxtimes\; P_{E, \perp}^{[\epsilon, L]},
\end{equation}
modulo the harmonic projection (terms with $K_t^{\mathcal{H}}$ pieces),
where $P^{[\epsilon, L]}_{K3} = \int_\epsilon^L \dbar^{*, K3} K_t^{K3}\, dt$
and similarly for $E$, and the sign $(-1)^?$ depends on the form-degree of
the input. The harmonic projection $P_{\mathcal{H}}^X$ vanishes in the
above formula because $\dbar^{*}$ kills harmonics; the harmonic forms are
treated separately as zero modes \textup{(\S\ref{subsubsec:zero-modes-k3xe})}.

\paragraph{Diagonal singularity.}
For $p_1 = p_2$ in a single chart, $K_t^X(p, p) \sim t^{-3} \cdot a_0$
as $t \to 0$ (six real dimensions, leading short-time singularity). The
adjoint $\dbar^{*, X}$ adds one factor of $\bar{\partial} d_X$, so
$\dbar^{*, X} K_t^X \sim t^{-3} \cdot \bar{\partial}(d_X^2 / t) = O(t^{-4} d_X(p_1, p_2))$
in the $\partial \bar z$-direction, giving
$P_X^{[\epsilon, L]}(p_1, p_2) \sim 1 / d_X(p_1, p_2)^4$ as
$d_X(p_1, p_2) \to 0$ for fixed $\epsilon, L$. This matches the Costello--Li
$\C^3$-propagator singularity.

\paragraph{Renormalised propagator.}
The limit $\epsilon \to 0$, $L \to \infty$ extracts:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:renormalised-propagator-k3xe}
 P_X(p_1, p_2) \;:=\; \lim_{\substack{\epsilon \to 0^+ \\ L \to \infty}}\, \Bigl( P_X^{[\epsilon, L]}(p_1, p_2) \;-\; (\text{harmonic projection drift}) \Bigr),
\end{equation}
which exists as a distribution on $(X \times X) \setminus \mathrm{Diag}$
and an integrable kernel against compactly supported test sections. The
``harmonic projection drift'' subtracts the $L$-linear divergence from
the $\lambda = 0$-eigenspace; explicitly, this drift equals
$L \cdot P_{\mathcal{H}}^X / V_X$ acting after pairing with $\dbar^{*}$,
which is zero on harmonics.

\begin{proposition}[Costello--Li propagator on $K3 \times E$]
\label{prop:costello-li-propagator-k3xe}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
With the product Calabi--Yau metric~\eqref{eq:product-cy-metric-k3xe},
the cutoff propagator $P_X^{[\epsilon, L]}$ defined by~\eqref{eq:cutoff-propagator-k3xe}
exists as a smooth section of $\bigl((\Omega^{0, \bullet}_X \otimes \frakg) \boxtimes (\Omega^{0, \bullet}_X \otimes \frakg)\bigr)$
on $X \times X \setminus \mathrm{Diag}$ for all $0 < \epsilon < L < \infty$.
The renormalised limit $P_X = \lim_{\epsilon \to 0, L \to \infty} P_X^{[\epsilon, L]}$
exists as a distributional kernel; it is symmetric in degree
$\binom{0, 1}{0, 2}$ on $\Omega^{0, \bullet}_X \otimes \Omega^{0, \bullet}_X$,
satisfies $\dbar P_X + (-1)^? P_X \dbar = \mathbf{1} - P_{\mathcal{H}}^X$ as
operators on $\Omega^{0, \bullet}(X)$, and has Schwartz-kernel
short-distance asymptotics $P_X(p_1, p_2) \sim 1/d_X(p_1, p_2)^4$
near the diagonal. The propagator factors via Künneth as in~\eqref{eq:propagator-decomposition-k3xe}
in any open subset of $X$ that is a product of a $K3$-open and an
$E$-open.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Existence of the cutoff propagator follows from the existence of the
$\dbar$-Laplacian heat kernel on the closed K\"ahler manifold $X$
\textup{(}standard parabolic-PDE theory; see e.g.~Berline--Getzler--Vergne,
\emph{Heat Kernels and Dirac Operators}, \S$2.7$\textup{).} Smoothness
off the diagonal follows from the smoothness of $K_t^X$ off-diagonal
for $t > 0$. K\"unneth factorisation~\eqref{eq:heat-kernel-kunneth-k3xe}
follows from
$\Delta_{\dbar}^X = \Delta_{\dbar}^{K3} + \Delta_{\dbar}^E$
\eqref{eq:laplacian-kunneth-k3xe} and commutativity of the summands.

The decomposition~\eqref{eq:propagator-decomposition-k3xe} into K3- and
E-pieces follows from the Leibniz rule for $\dbar^{*, X}$ on a product:
$\dbar^{*, X}\bigl(\alpha_{K3} \boxtimes \beta_E\bigr)
 = \dbar^{*, K3}(\alpha_{K3}) \boxtimes \beta_E
 + (-1)^{\deg \alpha_{K3}} \alpha_{K3} \boxtimes \dbar^{*, E}(\beta_E)$,
combined with the heat-kernel factorisation.

The compatibility identity $\dbar P_X + P_X \dbar = \mathbf{1} - P_{\mathcal{H}}^X$
is the standard Hodge-theoretic content: $P_X = \dbar^{*} G_X$ where $G_X$
is the Green's operator of $\Delta_{\dbar}^X$ on the orthogonal of
harmonics, satisfying $G_X \Delta_{\dbar}^X = \mathbf{1} - P_{\mathcal{H}}^X$.

Short-distance asymptotics: $K_t^X(p, p) \sim 1/(4\pi t)^3$ as $t \to 0$
on a $3$-complex-dimensional K\"ahler manifold; integrating
$\dbar^{*} K_t^X(p_1, p_2) \sim t^{-4} d_X(p_1, p_2) \exp(-d_X^2/4t)$
against $dt$ from $\epsilon$ to $L$ produces a kernel of order
$1/d_X^4$ near the diagonal, recovering the Costello--Li
$\C^3$ asymptotic $1/r^4$ in $6$ real dimensions.

The renormalised limit exists because (i) for $L \to \infty$, the
non-harmonic part of the heat kernel decays exponentially with rate
$\lambda_1 > 0$ (spectral gap of $\Delta_{\dbar}^X$ on a closed
manifold), so $\int^\infty K_t^{X, \perp} dt < \infty$ off-diagonal;
(ii) for $\epsilon \to 0$, the diagonal singularity is integrable in any
six-dimensional ball (since $1/r^4$ in $\R^6$ is integrable). The
harmonic-projection drift $L \cdot P_{\mathcal{H}}^X$ is removed by
construction since $\dbar^{*}$ annihilates harmonics.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Locally constant pre-factorisation algebra on the polydisk atlas}
\label{subsubsec:pre-fa-polydisk-k3xe}

Let $\mathcal{A} = \{D_\alpha\}_{\alpha \in I}$ be a finite open cover of
$X = K3 \times E$ by holomorphic polydisks: each $D_\alpha$ admits a
biholomorphism $\phi_\alpha: D_\alpha \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathcal{P}_\alpha$
to a polydisk $\mathcal{P}_\alpha = \prod_{i=1}^3 \{|z_i| < r_i\} \subset \C^3$
respecting the holomorphic volume form: $\phi_\alpha^* (dz_1 \wedge dz_2 \wedge dz_3) = c_\alpha \cdot \Omega_X|_{D_\alpha}$
for a non-vanishing transition function $c_\alpha \in \mathcal{O}(D_\alpha)^\times$.
Such an atlas exists: $K3$ admits a finite cover by Stein open subsets
biholomorphic to bidisks (Yau metric provides bounded geometry; cf.~Demailly,
\emph{Complex Analytic and Differential Geometry}, \S$X.1$); $E$ admits a
finite cover by disks; the product gives a finite polydisk cover of $X$.

\begin{definition}[Local hCS BV pre-factorisation algebra on a polydisk]
\label{def:hcs-pre-fa-local}
\ClaimStatusDefinition{}
For $D \subset X$ a polydisk and $\frakg$ admissible
\textup{(Lemma~\ref{lem:dabc-vanishing-classification})}, the local hCS
BV cochain complex on $D$ is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:hcs-bv-cochains-local}
 \Obs^{q,\hCS}_D(U) \;:=\; \operatorname{Sym}\!\Bigl(\bigl(\Omega^{0, \bullet}_c(U, \frakg)[1] \;\oplus\; \Omega^{0, \bullet}_c(U, \frakg)^\vee[2 - 3]\bigr)\Bigr)[\![\hbar]\!],
\end{equation}
for $U \subset D$ open, where the $[1]$-shift accounts for the BV
ghost-number-$1$ field $A$, the $[2 - 3] = [-1]$-shift gives ghost
number $2$ for the antifield $A^*$ pairing with $\Omega_X$ via Serre
duality $\Omega^{0, k} \leftrightarrow \Omega^{0, 3-k}$, and
$\Omega^{0, \bullet}_c(U, \frakg)$ denotes compactly supported sections.
The differential is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:hcs-bv-differential-local}
 d_{BV}^q \;=\; \dbar \;+\; \hbar\, \Delta_{P_X^{<L}} \;+\; Q_{\mathrm{int}},
\end{equation}
with $\Delta_{P_X^{<L}}$ the BV Laplacian of the cutoff propagator
restricted to $D \times D$, and $Q_{\mathrm{int}} = \{ I_{\mathrm{cubic}}, - \}_{BV}$
the interaction differential from the cubic vertex
$I_{\mathrm{cubic}}[A] = \tfrac{1}{6} \int \Omega_X \wedge \operatorname{tr}(A^3)$
of~\eqref{eq:6d-hcs-action-k3xe}.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[Local pre-FA structure on a polydisk]
\label{prop:hcs-pre-fa-local-structure}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{\textup{(}local statement on a polydisk\textup{)}}
For $D \subset X$ a polydisk and $\frakg$ admissible, the assignment
$U \mapsto \Obs^{q,\hCS}_D(U)$ of Definition~\ref{def:hcs-pre-fa-local}
defines a pre-factorisation algebra on $D$. The structure maps
\[
 m_{V_1, V_2; V}: \Obs^{q,\hCS}_D(V_1) \otimes \Obs^{q,\hCS}_D(V_2) \to \Obs^{q,\hCS}_D(V)
\]
for disjoint $V_1 \sqcup V_2 \subset V$ are the BV-product (Wick contraction
of compactly disjoint inputs) followed by Costello renormalisation flow.
The pre-FA is locally constant in the holomorphically locally constant
sense of Costello--Gwilliam~\cite{CostelloGwilliam} \emph{vol.~1}, \S$3.1$:
on any contractible open subset $V \subset D$ the homotopy type of
$\Obs^{q,\hCS}_D(V)$ is independent of the conformal-equivalence class of $V$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The construction is the Costello--Li $\C^3$-construction of
\cite{CostelloLi2016} \S$5$ applied to a polydisk
$\mathcal{P} \subset \C^3$. The choice of admissible $\frakg$ ensures
the QME holds on $D$ at one loop (Theorem~\ref{thm:6d-hcs-qme-one-loop-k3xe}).
The pre-FA structure maps $m_{V_1, V_2; V}$ are
defined by Wick contraction with the cutoff propagator $P_D^{<L}$
restricted to $D \times D$. The propagator is
$P_D^{<L} = P_X^{<L} \big|_{D \times D}$, the restriction of
Proposition~\ref{prop:costello-li-propagator-k3xe}.

Holomorphic local constancy on contractible $V$: the BV cochain complex
$\Obs^{q,\hCS}_D(V)$ has cohomology
$H^\bullet(\Omega^{0, \bullet}(V) \otimes \frakg, \dbar)
 \otimes_{\mathrm{(BV)}} \cdots$, which by the holomorphic Poincar\'e
lemma on a contractible polydisk reduces to $\frakg$-cochains in
ghost number $0, 1, 2, 3$, independent of $V$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Lurie + Kontsevich--Tamarkin: locally constant FA gives $E_3$]
\label{prop:lurie-kt-formality-e3-local}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Let $D \subset X$ be a polydisk biholomorphic to $\mathcal{P} \subset \C^3$
\textup{(}via $\phi: D \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathcal{P}$\textup{).} The
holomorphically locally constant factorisation algebra
$\Obs^{q,\hCS}_D$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:hcs-pre-fa-local-structure}
restricts on $\mathcal{P}$ to a locally constant factorisation algebra
in the topological sense \textup{(}Costello--Gwilliam~\cite{CostelloGwilliam}
vol.~$1$ \S$4.6$\textup{)}, equivalent by Lurie's theorem
\textup{(}\emph{Higher Algebra}~\cite{LurieHA}, Theorem~$5.5.4.10$\textup{)}
to an $E_3$-algebra $A_D = \Obs^{q,\hCS}_D(D)$. The $E_3$-structure on
the cohomology $H^\bullet(A_D)$ is determined up to a contractible space
of choices by Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality
\textup{(\cite{Kontsevich1999, Tamarkin2007})}, with the
Costello--Li scheme of Proposition~\ref{prop:costello-li-propagator-k3xe}
selecting the Kontsevich associator $\Phi_{KZ}$ within the
$\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-torsor.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Lurie's theorem~\cite{LurieHA} Theorem~$5.5.4.10$ identifies the
$\infty$-category of locally constant factorisation algebras on $\R^n$
with the $\infty$-category of $E_n$-algebras. Applied to
$\mathcal{P} \cong \R^6$: locally constant factorisation algebras on
$\mathcal{P}$ correspond to $E_6$-algebras. The hCS observable algebra
$\Obs^{q,\hCS}_D$ is not just topologically locally constant: it is
\emph{holomorphically} locally constant. By Costello--Gwilliam
\cite{CostelloGwilliam} vol.~$2$ \S$10.4$, holomorphically locally
constant factorisation algebras on $\C^d$ correspond to $E_d$-algebras,
because the $E_{2d}$-structure descends to an $E_d$-structure under
the holomorphic locality (the antiholomorphic directions $\bar\partial$
are gauge-fixed away). For $\C^3$, this gives $E_3$.

The $E_3$-structure on cohomology is governed by Kontsevich's
$E_d$-formality theorem \cite{Kontsevich1999} for the Hochschild
cochains of $\C^d$: the $E_d$-algebra structure on the polyvector
fields $\mathrm{PV}^\bullet(\C^d)$ is formal, with the formality
$L_\infty$-quasi-isomorphism Tamarkin-deformed by a Drinfeld associator
\cite{Tamarkin2007}. The space of associators is a torsor under
$\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$ \cite{Drinfeld1990} (Drinfeld--Grothendieck--Teichm\"uller
group); the Kontsevich integral selects the Kontsevich associator
$\Phi_{KZ}$ corresponding to the Costello--Li heat-kernel scheme.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Local cohomology of the BV complex on a polydisk]
\label{rem:local-bv-cohomology-polydisk}
On a contractible polydisk $\mathcal{P} \cong \C^3$, the cohomology of
$\Obs^{q,\hCS}_\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{P})$ as an $E_3$-algebra is
isomorphic, after BV reduction, to the universal enveloping algebra
$U(\frakg)[\![\hbar]\!]$ of the gauge dg-Lie equipped with the
canonical $E_3$-structure deformed by the perturbative corrections
encoded in the BCOV one-loop effective action \textup{(}see
\S\ref{subsubsec:zero-modes-k3xe} for the global content on
$K3 \times E$ where the BCOV correction vanishes\textup{).} The
$E_3$-algebra $U(\frakg)$ here is the Beilinson--Drinfeld $E_3$-enveloping,
not the $E_1$-associative enveloping; the two coincide on cohomology of
a contractible space.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection{Harmonic zero modes and the BCOV vanishing on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}}
\label{subsubsec:zero-modes-k3xe}

The compact CY$_3$ $X = K3 \times E$ is not strict CY: while
$\omega_X = \mathcal{O}_X$, the Hodge numbers
$h^{0, k}(X) = (1, 1, 1, 1)$ for $k = 0, 1, 2, 3$
\textup{(K\"unneth from $h^{0, \bullet}(K3) = (1, 0, 1)$ and
$h^{0, \bullet}(E) = (1, 1)$)} produce harmonic forms in degrees $1$
and $2$ that obstruct the strict-CY-template assumption $h^{0, k} = 0$
for $0 < k < d$ \textup{(}cf.~\cite{CostelloLi2016}\textup{).}

\paragraph{Harmonic basis.}
The harmonic spaces are one-dimensional in each degree:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:harmonic-basis-k3xe}
\begin{array}{rl}
\mathcal{H}^{0, 0}(X) &= \C \cdot 1, \\
\mathcal{H}^{0, 1}(X) &= \C \cdot d\bar z_E, \\
\mathcal{H}^{0, 2}(X) &= \C \cdot \bar\omega_{K3}, \\
\mathcal{H}^{0, 3}(X) &= \C \cdot \bar\omega_{K3} \wedge d\bar z_E.
\end{array}
\end{equation}
Tensoring with $\frakg$ gives the zero-mode space
$\mathcal{Z}_X^{\frakg} := \frakg \otimes \mathcal{H}^{0, \bullet}(X)
 = \frakg^{(0)} \oplus \frakg^{(1)} \oplus \frakg^{(2)} \oplus \frakg^{(3)}$,
each summand of dimension $\dim \frakg$, total dimension
$4 \dim \frakg$. For $\frakg = \mathfrak{sl}_2$: $4 \cdot 3 = 12$.

\paragraph{BV pairing on zero modes.}
Define the BV pairing on $\mathcal{Z}_X^\frakg$ via Serre duality:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:bv-pairing-zero-modes-k3xe}
 \langle \alpha \otimes \zeta_1, \beta \otimes \zeta_2 \rangle_{BV}
 \;:=\; \operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{adj}}(\alpha \beta) \cdot \int_X \Omega_X \wedge \zeta_1 \wedge \zeta_2,
\end{equation}
non-zero only when $\zeta_1 \wedge \zeta_2 \in \mathcal{H}^{0, 3}(X)$ for
the integral to extract the volume.

\begin{lemma}[Non-degeneracy of the BV pairing on zero modes]
\label{lem:bv-pairing-zero-modes-nondegenerate}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The BV pairing~\eqref{eq:bv-pairing-zero-modes-k3xe} is non-degenerate.
The non-zero pairings are:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\langle \alpha \otimes 1, \beta \otimes \bar\omega_{K3} \wedge d\bar z_E \rangle_{BV}
 = \operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{adj}}(\alpha \beta) \cdot V_X \cdot \kappa_0$,
\item $\langle \alpha \otimes d\bar z_E, \beta \otimes \bar\omega_{K3} \rangle_{BV}
 = \operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{adj}}(\alpha \beta) \cdot V_{K3} \cdot V_E \cdot \kappa_1$,
\end{itemize}
with normalisation constants $\kappa_0 = -2 i$ and $\kappa_1 = -2 i$
from $dz \wedge d\bar z = -2 i\, dx \wedge dy$ in each holomorphic
direction. The pairing is symplectic of total rank $4 \dim \frakg$ on
$\mathcal{Z}_X^\frakg$, with $\frakg^{(0)} \cong (\frakg^{(3)})^\vee$ and
$\frakg^{(1)} \cong (\frakg^{(2)})^\vee$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The pairing factors as
$\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{adj}}(\alpha \beta)$ on the $\frakg$-side
(non-degenerate Killing form) and the integral $\int_X \Omega_X \wedge (-)$
on the form-side. The form integral gives non-zero results only on
elements pairing to $\mathcal{H}^{0, 3}(X)$. Direct computation of the
two non-trivial pairings:
\begin{align*}
 \int_X \Omega_X \wedge \overline{\Omega_X}
 &= \int_X \omega_{K3} \wedge dz_E \wedge \bar\omega_{K3} \wedge d\bar z_E \\
 &= \biggl(\int_{K3} \omega_{K3} \wedge \bar\omega_{K3}\biggr) \cdot \biggl(\int_E dz_E \wedge d\bar z_E\biggr)
 \;=\; V_{K3} \cdot (-2i) V_E \;=\; -2 i \cdot V_X,
\end{align*}
where $V_X = V_{K3} \cdot V_E$. So $\kappa_0 = -2 i$, identical to $\kappa_1$
since the second pairing is the same integral by Künneth.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Cubic vertex restricted to zero modes.}
Write the harmonic part of $A$ as
$A_0 = \alpha \otimes d\bar z_E + \beta \otimes \bar\omega_{K3} + \gamma \otimes \bar\omega_{K3} \wedge d\bar z_E$
\textup{(}the $\frakg^{(0)}$-piece sits in ghost number $0$ and does not
contribute to $A$ which has ghost number $1$\textup{).} The cubic vertex
restricted to harmonic zero modes is
\begin{align}
\label{eq:cubic-vertex-zero-modes}
 I_{\mathrm{cubic}}[A_0]
 &= \frac{1}{6} \int_X \Omega_X \wedge \operatorname{tr}(A_0 \wedge A_0 \wedge A_0) \notag \\
 &= \frac{1}{6} \int_X \Omega_X \wedge \operatorname{tr}\!\bigl((\alpha \otimes d\bar z_E)^3 + 3 (\alpha \otimes d\bar z_E)^2 (\beta \otimes \bar\omega_{K3}) + \cdots\bigr).
\end{align}
Each term is zero for purely form-theoretic reasons:
\begin{itemize}
\item $(d\bar z_E)^3 = 0$ \textup{(}elliptic curve $E$ is one-complex-dimensional, no
$(0, 3)$-form on $E$\textup{)};
\item $(d\bar z_E)^2 \wedge \bar\omega_{K3} = 0$ for the same reason
\textup{(}two factors of $d\bar z_E$\textup{);}
\item $d\bar z_E \wedge \bar\omega_{K3}^2 = 0$ \textup{(}$K3$ is two-complex-dimensional,
no $(0, 4)$-form\textup{);}
\item $\bar\omega_{K3}^3 = 0$ for the same reason.
\end{itemize}
Hence the cubic vertex on harmonic zero modes vanishes identically:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:cubic-vertex-zero-modes-vanishes}
 I_{\mathrm{cubic}}[A_0] \;=\; 0 \quad \text{for all } A_0 \in \mathcal{Z}_X^\frakg.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{One-loop effective action: BCOV vanishing.}

\begin{theorem}[BCOV anomaly vanishes on $K3 \times E$]
\label{thm:bcov-anomaly-vanishes-k3xe}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The Costello--Li one-loop holomorphic anomaly class
$\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}} \in H^{0, 1}(X)$, defined by
\textup{(\cite{CostelloLi2016} Proposition~$5.2$;
cf.~\cite{BCOV1993})}
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:bcov-anomaly-class}
 \alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}} \;:=\; \frac{\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)}{24}\, [\Omega_X]^{0, 1},
\end{equation}
vanishes for $X = K3 \times E$. Consequently, the one-loop BCOV
correction to the effective action on harmonic zero modes is zero,
and the perturbative quantum factorisation algebra
$\Obs^{q,\hCS}(X)$ has trivial one-loop holomorphic anomaly.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By multiplicativity of the topological Euler characteristic on
products,
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3 \times E) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3) \cdot \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(E)
 = 24 \cdot 0 = 0$,
since $E$ is a torus with $\chi_{\mathrm{top}} = 0$. Substituting into~\eqref{eq:bcov-anomaly-class}:
$\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}(K3 \times E) = 0$. The Costello--Li one-loop
correction is the BCOV-anomaly cocycle paired with the gauge dg-Lie
trace; its vanishing on $X$ implies the one-loop effective action on
harmonic zero modes is the classical action restricted to zero modes.
By~\eqref{eq:cubic-vertex-zero-modes-vanishes}, the classical cubic
vertex vanishes on zero modes, so the full one-loop effective action
on $\mathcal{Z}_X^\frakg$ is the free quadratic pairing
$\tfrac{1}{2} \langle A_0, A_0 \rangle_{BV}$ alone.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Zero-mode partition function]
\label{cor:zero-mode-partition-function-k3xe}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{\textup{(}up to BV measure normalisation\textup{)}}
The zero-mode contribution to the BV partition function of $6$d hCS on
$X = K3 \times E$ is the Gaussian
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:zero-mode-partition}
 Z_0 \;=\; \int_{\mathcal{Z}_X^\frakg} \mathrm{vol}_{BV} \cdot \exp\!\bigl(\tfrac{1}{2} \langle A_0, A_0 \rangle_{BV}\bigr)
 \;=\; \mathrm{Pf}\!\bigl(\Omega_{BV}^{(0)}\bigr)^{-1/2} \cdot V_X^{-\dim \frakg/2},
\end{equation}
with $\Omega_{BV}^{(0)}$ the symplectic BV form on
$\mathcal{Z}_X^\frakg$ from Lemma~\ref{lem:bv-pairing-zero-modes-nondegenerate}.
The Pfaffian factor is a topological invariant of $\frakg$ and the
metric class $[\omega_{K3}]$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:bcov-anomaly-vanishes-k3xe} reduces the zero-mode
effective action to the free quadratic. Gaussian integration over
$\mathcal{Z}_X^\frakg \cong \frakg^4$ with the BV symplectic form gives
the stated answer; the volume factor $V_X^{-\dim\frakg/2}$ comes from
the metric-dependent normalisation of the harmonic forms.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Higher-loop corrections.}
At two loops and higher, the BCOV holomorphic anomaly equation
\textup{(}Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa~\cite{BCOV1993}\textup{)}
governs the perturbative corrections. For $X = K3 \times E$ with
$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X) = 0$, all anomaly-source terms in the BCOV
recursion vanish, so the loop-corrected effective action on zero modes
is the tree-level classical action plus controlled quantum corrections
that are themselves chain-level identifiable.

\subsubsection{Weiss cosheaf descent on the polydisk atlas}
\label{subsubsec:weiss-cosheaf-descent-k3xe}

\begin{definition}[Weiss cover and Weiss cosheaf condition]
\label{def:weiss-cover-cosheaf}
\ClaimStatusDefinition{}
Let $X$ be a topological space and $\mathfrak{F}$ a pre-factorisation
algebra on $X$. A \emph{Weiss cover} of an open $U \subset X$ is a
collection $\mathcal{W} = \{V_i\}_{i \in J}$ of opens
$V_i \subset U$ such that for every finite subset $S \subset U$ there
exists $i \in J$ with $S \subset V_i$. The pre-factorisation algebra
$\mathfrak{F}$ is a \emph{factorisation algebra} (satisfies the
\emph{Weiss cosheaf condition}) if for every open $U$ and every Weiss
cover $\mathcal{W}$ of $U$ the natural map
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:weiss-cosheaf-condition}
 \operatorname*{colim}_{S \in \mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{W})}
 \biggl( \bigotimes_{V \in S} \mathfrak{F}(V) \biggr) \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\; \mathfrak{F}(U)
\end{equation}
is an equivalence in the homotopy category of cochain complexes, where
$\mathfrak{N}(\mathcal{W})$ is the simplicial nerve of the Weiss cover.
\textup{(}See Costello--Gwilliam~\cite{CostelloGwilliam} vol.~$1$
Definition~$6.1.4$.\textup{)}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Weiss cosheaf descent for hCS observables on $K3 \times E$]
\label{thm:weiss-cosheaf-k3xe}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Let $\mathcal{A} = \{D_\alpha\}_{\alpha \in I}$ be a finite polydisk
atlas of $X = K3 \times E$ and $\frakg$ admissible
\textup{(Lemma~\ref{lem:dabc-vanishing-classification})}. Define the
$X$-pre-factorisation algebra $\Obs^{q,\hCS}_X$ by gluing the local
pre-factorisation algebras of Definition~\ref{def:hcs-pre-fa-local} via
the Costello--Li propagator $P_X$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:costello-li-propagator-k3xe}. Then $\Obs^{q,\hCS}_X$
is a factorisation algebra: the Weiss cosheaf condition
\eqref{eq:weiss-cosheaf-condition} holds for every open $U \subset X$
and every Weiss cover $\mathcal{W}$ of $U$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The proof proceeds in three steps:

\emph{Step 1 (local Weiss).} On each polydisk $D_\alpha$, the
pre-factorisation algebra $\Obs^{q,\hCS}_{D_\alpha}$ satisfies the
Weiss cosheaf condition by Costello--Gwilliam~\cite{CostelloGwilliam}
vol.~$2$, Proposition~$10.4.5$ \textup{(}local case for holomorphic
factorisation algebras on a polydisk in $\C^d$\textup{).} This uses the
holomorphic locality of the BV interaction on the polydisk and the
non-degenerate BV pairing.

\emph{Step 2 (Mayer--Vietoris on overlaps).}
For an open $U \subset X$ contained in the union of two polydisks
$D_\alpha \cup D_\beta$, the overlap $D_\alpha \cap D_\beta$ is itself
an open complex submanifold of $X$ with a holomorphic embedding into
each chart. The pre-factorisation algebra restricts compatibly:
$\Obs^{q,\hCS}_{D_\alpha}|_{D_\alpha \cap D_\beta}
 = \Obs^{q,\hCS}_{D_\beta}|_{D_\alpha \cap D_\beta}
 = \Obs^{q,\hCS}_{D_\alpha \cap D_\beta}$,
because both restrictions use the same $X$-global propagator
$P_X|_{(D_\alpha \cap D_\beta)^2}$. The Mayer--Vietoris sequence
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mayer-vietoris-pre-fa}
 \Obs^{q,\hCS}_X(D_\alpha \cup D_\beta)
 \;\simeq\;
 \mathrm{hofib}\!\Bigl( \Obs^{q,\hCS}_{D_\alpha}(D_\alpha) \oplus \Obs^{q,\hCS}_{D_\beta}(D_\beta) \xrightarrow{\Delta - \mathrm{id}} \Obs^{q,\hCS}_{D_\alpha \cap D_\beta}(D_\alpha \cap D_\beta) \Bigr)
\end{equation}
is the homotopy fibre of the difference map, computing the descent of
local cohomology to global. The hocolim of the Mayer--Vietoris diagram
over a Weiss cover of $D_\alpha \cup D_\beta$ converges to the
$D_\alpha \cup D_\beta$-global observable algebra by the standard
Mayer--Vietoris argument for cosheaves on a manifold.

\emph{Step 3 (global descent over the finite atlas).}
For $U = X$ and $\mathcal{W}$ a Weiss cover refining the polydisk atlas
$\mathcal{A}$, the Mayer--Vietoris descent of Step~2 iterates over the
nerve of $\mathcal{A}$. Since $|\mathcal{A}|$ is finite, the iteration
terminates. The crucial input is the spectral gap of $\Delta_{\dbar}^X$
on the closed K\"ahler manifold $X$: the convergence of the
\v{C}ech--Mayer--Vietoris cocomplex
\[
 \prod_\alpha \Obs^{q,\hCS}_X(D_\alpha) \;\to\; \prod_{\alpha < \beta} \Obs^{q,\hCS}_X(D_\alpha \cap D_\beta) \;\to\; \prod_{\alpha < \beta < \gamma} \Obs^{q,\hCS}_X(D_\alpha \cap D_\beta \cap D_\gamma) \;\to\; \cdots
\]
to the global observable algebra $\Obs^{q,\hCS}_X(X)$ uses (i) the
exponential decay of the heat kernel $K_t^{X, \perp}$ off the diagonal
\textup{(}from the spectral gap $\lambda_1 > 0$\textup{)} and (ii) the
finiteness of $\mathcal{A}$. Without spectral gap, the Cech-Mayer-Vietoris
cocomplex might fail to converge globally; on the closed manifold $X$,
both inputs are present.

The harmonic zero modes of \S\ref{subsubsec:zero-modes-k3xe} contribute
to the $\check{C}^0$-term of the cocomplex via the constant projection
$P_{\mathcal{H}}^X$; their finite-dimensionality
\textup{(}$4 \dim \frakg$\textup{)} and the Gaussian zero-mode
integration of Corollary~\ref{cor:zero-mode-partition-function-k3xe}
make the global cocomplex converge to a well-defined factorisation
algebra. The Mayer--Vietoris convergence is a special case of
\cite{CostelloGwilliam} vol.~$2$, Theorem~$10.5.1$ for the closed
K\"ahler case, the new content here being the explicit $K3 \times E$
verification with finite atlas and explicit propagator.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Compactness, spectral gap, and the role of finite atlas]
\label{rem:compactness-spectral-gap-finite-atlas}
The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:weiss-cosheaf-k3xe} uses three properties
of $K3 \times E$ specifically: (i) the closed-manifold heat-kernel
spectral gap $\lambda_1 > 0$ for $\Delta_{\dbar}^X$; (ii) the finite
polydisk atlas $|\mathcal{A}| < \infty$; (iii) the $4$-dimensional
harmonic zero-mode space, finite-dimensional. On non-compact CY$_3$
(local surfaces, $\C^3$, the conifold) the proof structure persists,
but the harmonic spaces are infinite-dimensional and the global
cocomplex requires additional spectral hypotheses
\textup{(}$\Omega$-equivariant cohomology or compactly supported
formulations\textup{).} The compact $K3 \times E$ case is the cleanest
verification of Weiss cosheaf descent for $6$d hCS in this volume.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Output: the perturbative $E_3$ factorisation algebra on $K3 \times E$.}

\begin{theorem}[The $6$d hCS factorisation algebra on $K3 \times E$]
\label{thm:6d-hcs-factorisation-algebra-k3xe}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{\textup{(}admissible $\frakg$, perturbative,
formal in $\hbar$\textup{)}}
For $X = K3 \times E$ with the product Calabi--Yau metric and
admissible gauge dg-Lie $\frakg$ \textup{(}simple complex Lie algebra
with $d^{abc}_\frakg = 0$, e.g.\ $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ or $E_8$\textup{)},
the perturbative BV observables of $6$d hCS form a holomorphically
locally constant factorisation algebra
\[
 \Obs^{q,\hCS}(X, \frakg) \;\in\; \operatorname{FA}^{\hol}(X; \operatorname{Ch}^{\hbar})
\]
on $X$, valued in $\hbar$-adic cochain complexes, with the following
properties:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\textup{(i)}] \textup{(QME).} Satisfies the one-loop quantum master
equation with the wave-function counter-term~\eqref{eq:6d-hcs-counterterm-quadratic}
selecting the Kontsevich associator within the
$\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-torsor of associators
\textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:6d-hcs-qme-one-loop-k3xe}\textup{).}
\item[\textup{(ii)}] \textup{(Locally $E_3$.)} On any polydisk
$D \subset X$, the holomorphically locally constant restriction
$\Obs^{q,\hCS}(X, \frakg)|_D$ is equivalent to an $E_3$-algebra by
Lurie + Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality
\textup{(}Proposition~\ref{prop:lurie-kt-formality-e3-local}\textup{),}
with the $E_3$-structure pinned by the Costello--Li
propagator~\eqref{eq:renormalised-propagator-k3xe}.
\item[\textup{(iii)}] \textup{(Zero modes.)} The harmonic zero-mode
contribution is the Gaussian $Z_0$
of~\eqref{eq:zero-mode-partition} over $\mathcal{Z}_X^\frakg \cong \frakg^4$;
the BCOV anomaly vanishes
\textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:bcov-anomaly-vanishes-k3xe}\textup{).}
\item[\textup{(iv)}] \textup{(Weiss descent.)} The pre-factorisation
algebra promotes to a genuine factorisation algebra by Weiss cosheaf
descent on the finite polydisk atlas
\textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:weiss-cosheaf-k3xe}\textup{).}
\end{enumerate}
The $\frakg = \mathfrak{sl}_2$ case is the most transparent; the
$\frakg = E_8$ case is relevant for the $E_8 \times E_8$ heterotic
context.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Combine Theorems~\ref{thm:6d-hcs-qme-one-loop-k3xe},
\ref{thm:bcov-anomaly-vanishes-k3xe}, \ref{thm:weiss-cosheaf-k3xe}
with Propositions~\ref{prop:costello-li-propagator-k3xe},
\ref{prop:hcs-pre-fa-local-structure}, \ref{prop:lurie-kt-formality-e3-local}.
Each step has been established conditionally on the admissible-$\frakg$
hypothesis, the product-CY metric, and the closed-manifold spectral gap.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Comparison with the $5$d-realisation route of Proposition~\ref{prop:5d-hcs-bv-factorisation}]
\label{rem:6d-vs-5d-realisations-k3xe}
Theorem~\ref{thm:6d-hcs-factorisation-algebra-k3xe} is the direct $6$d
construction of $\PhiFA_3(\Perf(K3 \times E))$; Proposition~\ref{prop:5d-hcs-bv-factorisation}
is the $5$d realisation that recovers the same Stage-$1$ output after
$\R_t$-compactification. Both routes are valid and produce the same
$E_3$-factorisation-algebra on $K3 \times E$ \textup{(}up to the
$\mathrm{GRT}_1(\Q)$-torsor of associators\textup{).} The $6$d route is
the geometric Stage-$1$ realisation \emph{native} to the K\"ahler-CY$_3$
$K3 \times E$; the $5$d route is the integrability-flavoured derivation
suitable for the Yangian VOA all-orders comparison
\textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:non-abelian-5d-hcs-yangian-voa}\textup{).}
The two routes converge to the same chain-level Stage-$1$ output by the
holomorphic-topological tensor theorem.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[What this construction does \emph{not} establish]
\label{rem:6d-hcs-k3e-open-ends}
The construction of Theorem~\ref{thm:6d-hcs-factorisation-algebra-k3xe}
is perturbative and formal in $\hbar$. It does \emph{not}:
(i) Establish all-orders convergence of the perturbative $\hbar$-series
on the compact base $K3 \times E$ \textup{(}analogue of Costello--Yagi
\cite{CostelloYagi2018} for $5$d on $\R \times \C^2$, open in $6$d on
compact CY$_3$\textup{)}.
(ii) Identify
$\Obs^{q,\hCS}(K3 \times E, \frakg) = \PhiFA_3(D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E)))$
as $E_3$-factorisation algebras
\textup{(}separate theorem; the abstract-categorical Stage-$1$ assembled
from the Hochschild calculus of $D^b(K3 \times E)$ via Kontsevich--Tamarkin
formality on $\HH^\bullet(\Coh(K3 \times E))$\textup{)}.
(iii) Recover non-perturbative effects: instanton contributions, DT
generating function $Z^{K3 \times E}_{\mathrm{DT}} = C \cdot \Delta_5(Z)^{-2}$,
GW corrections, or D-brane states. Those live outside the perturbative
BV expansion.
(iv) Construct the inverse functor recovering $D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E))$
from $\Obs^{q,\hCS}(K3 \times E, \frakg)$ \textup{(}the open S$5$
obstruction in the CY-$\mathsf{A}_3$ existence-and-rigidity stratification
of \S\ref{subsec:cy-a3-existence-rigidity}\textup{)}.
The output of Theorem~\ref{thm:6d-hcs-factorisation-algebra-k3xe} is the
explicit chain-level perturbative Stage-$1$ functor on the principal
$K3 \times E$ locus, not a global non-perturbative quantum vertex group
$G(K3 \times E)$.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Direct Kontsevich--Tamarkin computation of the associator scalar}
\label{subsec:associator-scalar-direct}

On the principal locus, Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-sp-k3-bialgebra}
uses the BL-2/4/6 transport of the restricted Kontsevich--Tamarkin
formality $3$-cocycle to the Siegel--Borcherds cohomology line. A
direct computation from the Kontsevich integrand is stronger: it would
construct the transport without importing the BL witnesses and identify
the scalar multiplying $[\Phi_{10}/\eta^{24}]$ in the $1$-dimensional
space $H^3(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})^{\Z/2, K(1)}$ of
Bruinier~\cite{Bruinier2002} Proposition~$5.1$.

\begin{theorem}[Associator scalar from the formality integrand]
\label{thm:associator-scalar-kontsevich}
\ClaimStatusConditional{} \textup{(conditional on a direct Kontsevich-integrand transport of the formality cocycle to $H^3(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})$)}
Assume a non-zero direct transport of the restricted Kontsevich--Tamarkin $E_3$-formality $3$-cocycle to the one-dimensional space $H^3(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})^{\Z/2, K(1)}$ of Bruinier~\cite{Bruinier2002} Proposition~$5.1$, independent of the BL-2/4/6 principal-locus transport used in Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-sp-k3-bialgebra}. Under the common Borcherds normalisation, the scalar $\lambda$ relating the transported class to the generator $[\Phi_{10}/\eta^{24}]$ is
\[
 \lambda \;=\; \frac{c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0, 0)}{2}\;=\; 5.
\]
The value equals $\kBKM(\Delta_5)$. The open part is the typed
transport from the CY$_3$ formality cocycle to the Hall--Borcherds
cohomology class, not the Borcherds weight normalisation.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The theorem assumes the non-arithmetic input: a typed transport of the
restricted CY$_3$ formality cocycle to the one-dimensional
Hall--Borcherds cohomology line. Once that transport is fixed and is
non-zero, the remaining scalar is a normalisation question in the
primitive Borcherds product. The Gritsenko--Nikulin lift used for
$\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is the lift of the half-genus
$\phi_{0,1}^{EZ}$ with $q^0$ term $y^{-1}+10+y$; hence its constant
coefficient is $c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0,0)=10$. Borcherds' weight formula
therefore gives
\[
 \mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5)=\frac{c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0,0)}{2}=5 .
\]
The common Borcherds normalisation in the hypotheses says that the
transported associator generator is measured in this primitive
$\Delta_5$ line, not in the squared K3 elliptic-genus line
$\Phi_{10}=\Delta_5^2$. Therefore the transported scalar is
\[
 \lambda=\frac{c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0,0)}{2}=5
 =\kBKM(\Delta_5).
\]
What remains open is exactly the premise: constructing the transport and
checking non-vanishing from the CY$_3$ formality cocycle. The
Oberdieck--Pixton character identity and the Borcherds weight formula
fix the arithmetic normalisation, but they do not replace that transport
proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The scalar is intrinsic, not a normalisation convention]
\label{rem:associator-scalar-intrinsic}
The scalar is intrinsic only after the transport datum has been built:
changing the Hall--Borcherds orientation, completion, or cocycle
normalisation changes the representative, so those choices must be
fixed before the phrase ``the scalar'' has mathematical content. With
the primitive $\Delta_5$ normalisation fixed, the arithmetic value is
forced to be $5$ by Borcherds' weight formula. The missing proof is the
field-theoretic equality between the transported Kontsevich--Tamarkin
class and the Hall--Borcherds generator; no character identity proves
that equality by itself.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Explicit \texorpdfstring{$g_N$}{g-N}-twined shuffle refinement at \texorpdfstring{$N \in \{3, 4, 6\}$}{N in 3, 4, 6}}
\label{subsec:shuffle-refinement-CHL}

Corollary~\ref{cor:sibling-BKMs-from-one-phiFA} isolates the CHL sibling
targets $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{(N)}$ for $N \in \{2, 3, 4, 6\}$ from the
Nikulin classification of order-$N$ symplectic K3 automorphisms and the
Gritsenko--Cl\'ery Borcherds products. The $N = 2$
Oberdieck~\cite{Oberdieck2017} case supplies reduced-DT and automorphic
input. For $N \in \{3, 4, 6\}$ the concrete
Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle lift, the equivariant CoHA, and the
Stage-$2$ cycle remain proof obligations.

\begin{conjecture}[$g_N$-equivariant Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle at $N \in \{3, 4, 6\}$]
\label{thm:gN-shuffle-refinement}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
Let $g_N$ be a symplectic K3 automorphism of order $N \in \{3, 4, 6\}$,
classified by Nikulin $1980$ (\emph{Finite groups of automorphisms of
K\"ahlerian surfaces of type K3}, Trudy Mosk.\ Mat.\ Obs.~$38$). Assume
the $g_N$-orbifold CoHA exists, the Mukai-lattice orbit decomposition is
computed on the chosen fixed chamber, and the oriented equivariant
hCS-to-Hall comparison is constructed. Then the expected
Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle kernel has the form
\[
 K^{(g_N)}(x, y) \;=\; \prod_{k = 1}^{r_N}\!\frac{\theta(x - y - h_k^{(g_N)}; \tau)}{\theta(x - y + h_k^{(g_N)}; \tau)},
 \qquad
 r_N \;=\; \#\bigl(\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}/\langle g_N\rangle
 \textup{ effective weight-orbits in the chosen chamber}\bigr),
\]
where $\{h_k^{(g_N)}\}$ are the $g_N$-averaged Nakajima weight generators
on the equivariant cohomology of the $g_N$-fixed Hilbert scheme. Under
the same hypotheses, the $g_N$-equivariant shuffle product would endow
$\CoHA^{g_N}_{K3 \times E}$ with a bialgebra
$(m^{(g_N)}, \Delta^{(g_N)})$ whose Stage-$2$ image on $E$ matches the
coproduct of the CHL-$N$ sibling $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{(N)}$. The
unproved data are precisely the orbit count $r_N$, the equivariant CoHA,
the Stage-$2$ cycle, and the Hall--Borcherds denominator comparison.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Proof obligations for the equivariant shuffle refinement]
The Nikulin classification supplies the finite-order symplectic
automorphisms, but classification does not construct the equivariant
shuffle algebra. A proof of Conjecture~\ref{thm:gN-shuffle-refinement}
requires four independent inputs:
\begin{itemize}
\item the exact $g_N$-fixed and $g_N$-orbit Mukai-lattice arithmetic in the
normalisation used by the CHL Borcherds product;
\item a $g_N$-orbifold critical CoHA with shuffle presentation and
coproduct;
\item an oriented equivariant hCS-to-Hall map compatible with the
chosen specialisation cycle;
\item a Hall--Borcherds denominator comparison identifying the resulting
vacuum character with the relevant $\Phi_N$.
\end{itemize}
Primary targets: Nikulin $1980$; Bryan--Oberdieck--Pandharipande--Yin
\cite{BOPY2018}; Oberdieck~\cite{Oberdieck2017};
Schiffmann--Vasserot~\cite{SV13}; Nakajima~\cite{Nakajima1999};
Pasol--Zagier~$2013$~\cite{PasolZagier2013}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Cycle-type verification table]
\label{rem:gN-cycle-types}
The Nikulin $1980$ (\emph{Finite groups of automorphisms of K\"ahlerian surfaces of type K3}, Trudy Mosk.\ Mat.\ Obs.~$38$) cycle types at $N \in \{2, 3, 4, 6\}$ are:
\[
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{array}{@{}lcccc@{}}
\toprule
N & \text{Cycle type on } H^\bullet(K3) & \text{rk}\,\Lambda^{g_N}_{\mathrm{Muk}} & r_N & \kBKM(\Phi_N) \\
\midrule
2 & 1^{16} 2^4 & 19 & 12 & 5 \;\text{(paramodular)}\; / \; 4 \;\text{(CHL-avg)} \\
3 & 1^{10} 3^4 & 11 & 7 & 3 \\
4 & 1^8 2^2 4^2 & 10 & 5 & 2 \\
6 & 1^6 2^2 3^1 6^1 & 8 & 4 & 1 \\
\bottomrule
\end{array}
\]
The $r_N$ column is a conjectural orbit-count target for the
$g_N$-twined elliptic shuffle kernel, not a theorem of Nikulin's
classification. The $\kBKM$ values are read, where the corresponding
Borcherds product is constructed, from the twined Borcherds weight
$c_N(0)/2$ in the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery normalisation. The two scopes for
$\kBKM$ at $N = 2$; paramodular $5$ on the diagonal--averaged input,
CHL-averaged $4$ on the orbifold input; are the Family~(i)/(ii)
dichotomy of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-universal-kBKM-two-scopes}; the
shuffle-refinement scope remains conjectural until the four proof
obligations of Conjecture~\ref{thm:gN-shuffle-refinement} are discharged.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The \texorpdfstring{$d = 5$}{d = 5} Fake Monster chiral bialgebra via Dunn--Lurie \texorpdfstring{$E_5 \simeq E_2 \otimes E_2 \otimes E_1$}{E5 = E2 tensor E2 tensor E1}}
\label{subsec:fake-monster-d5-bialgebra}

The Fake Monster BKM $\mathfrak{g}_{\Phi_{12}}$ is the rank-$26$ Borcherds~\cite{Borcherds1990Invent} superalgebra attached to the Lorentzian lattice $II_{25, 1} = \Lambda_{24} \oplus II_{1, 1}$ on the Leech lattice $\Lambda_{24}$. The compact-CY$_3$ rank obstruction rules out a $d = 3$ placement (Remark~\ref{rem:k3ebkm-fake-monster-dunn-lurie}): no transverse surface $\Sigma_2 \subset X^{\mathrm{CY}_3}$ supplies a rank-$24$ positive-definite sublattice. The correct placement is at $d = 5$ on $X_{\mathrm{FM}} = K3 \times K3 \times E$, routed by the Dunn--Lurie decomposition $E_5 \simeq E_2 \otimes E_2 \otimes E_1$.

\begin{theorem}[Fake Monster chiral bialgebra at \texorpdfstring{$d = 5$}{d = 5} via Dunn--Lurie]
\label{thm:fake-monster-d5-bialgebra}
\ClaimStatusConditional{} \textup{(conditional on the $d = 5$ hCS-to-Hall comparison analogous to Problem~\ref{op:cy3-hcs-hall-comparison}, the existence of a $d = 5$ Stage-$1$ functor $\PhiFA_5$, and a Niemeier-lattice embedding $II_{25,1} \hookrightarrow H^{\bullet}(X_{\mathrm{FM}}, \Z)$ compatible with the Mukai truncation on each K3-factor and the elliptic embedding on $E$)}
Let $X_{\mathrm{FM}} = K3 \times K3 \times E$ and let $\PhiFA_5(\mathrm{Perf}(X_{\mathrm{FM}}))$ be the Stage-$1$ $E_5$-holomorphic factorisation algebra on $X_{\mathrm{FM}}$. Under the Dunn--Lurie equivalence
\[
 E_5 \;\simeq\; E_2 \otimes_{\mathrm{Dunn}} E_2 \otimes_{\mathrm{Dunn}} E_1
 \qquad \text{(Lurie~\textit{HA}~\textup{Thm.\ 5.1.2.2})}
\]
in the symmetric-monoidal $(\infty, 1)$-category of $(\infty, 1)$-operads, the Stage-$2$ specialisation along $(\Sigma_4, E) = (K3 \times K3, E)$ produces an $\Eone$-chiral bialgebra on $E$
\[
 \SpCh_{\Sigma_4, E}\!\bigl(\PhiFA_5(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times K3 \times E))\bigr)
 \;\simeq\;
 \mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{FakeMonster}}
\]
whose primitive Lie super-bialgebra is $\mathfrak{g}_{\Phi_{12}}$, whose algebra layer is the vertex envelope $U_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Phi_{12}})$, whose coproduct is the Dunn--Lurie-routed tensor shuffle compatible with the $II_{25,1}$-Cartan decomposition $26 = 10 + 10 + 6$, whose associator is the Borcherds--Weyl $3$-cocycle on $\mathrm{O}^+(II_{26, 2})$ twisted by the $\eta^{-24}$ denominator, and whose $R$-matrix is the rank-$26$ orthogonal RTT $R^{\Phi_{12}}(u)$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3ebkm-fake-monster-rtt}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The proof has three components: Dunn--Lurie routing, bialgebra piece identification, and Cartan decomposition.

\emph{Component~(i): Dunn--Lurie routing.}
Lurie~\textit{HA}~Theorem~$5.1.2.2$ establishes $E_{m+n} \simeq E_m \otimes_{\mathrm{BV}} E_n$ in the symmetric-monoidal $(\infty, 1)$-category of $(\infty, 1)$-operads; iterating yields $E_5 \simeq E_2 \otimes E_2 \otimes E_1$. The associativity coherence of the Boardman--Vogt tensor product is automatic in the symmetric-monoidal structure (Lurie~\textit{HA}~$2.1.3.3$), so the two parenthesisations $(E_2 \otimes E_2) \otimes E_1$ and $E_2 \otimes (E_2 \otimes E_1)$ produce canonically equivalent $(\infty, 1)$-operads. At the chain level, the Fresse $2017$ strict model (\emph{Homotopy of operads and Grothendieck--Teichm\"uller groups}, AMS Math.\ Surveys Monogr.~$217$) via the Barratt--Eccles $W$-construction provides explicit chain-level associator $2$-cells. For the CY$_5$ holomorphic factorisation algebra on $X_{\mathrm{FM}}$, the geometric routing is:
\begin{itemize}
\item The first $E_2$-factor acts on the first K3 (via its $h^{1,1}$-polarisation and the symplectic $2$-form).
\item The second $E_2$-factor acts on the second K3 (same).
\item The $E_1$-factor is longitudinal along the elliptic curve $E$.
\end{itemize}
Stage-$2$ specialisation $\SpCh_{K3 \times K3, E}$ integrates out the two $E_2$-factors (factorisation homology over the two K3 factors, which is symmetric-monoidal by Lurie~\textit{HA}~$5.5.3$) and preserves the residual $E_1$-chirality on the elliptic base.

\emph{Component~(ii): bialgebra pieces.}
The algebra layer is the Hall monoidal structure on $\mathrm{Perf}(X_{\mathrm{FM}})$ transported through $\PhiFA_5$, supplying the vertex envelope $U_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Phi_{12}})$ on the Niemeier-datum specialisation after the two-K3-factor $E_2$-integration; the coproduct is the $g_N$-trivial (i.e.\ untwined, $N = 1$ analogue on Leech) Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle on the Leech-lattice CoHA $\CoHA_{\Lambda_{24}}$ of Route~B in Theorem~\ref{thm:k3ebkm-fake-monster-rtt}; the associator is the restricted Kontsevich--Tamarkin $E_5$-formality $3$-cocycle, matching the Borcherds--Weyl cocycle on $\mathrm{O}^+(II_{26, 2})$ twisted by $\eta^{-24}$ via the same mechanism as Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-sp-k3-bialgebra}~\ref{bialg:associator} applied in rank $26$; the $R$-matrix is Theorem~\ref{thm:k3ebkm-fake-monster-rtt}.

\emph{Component~(iii): Cartan decomposition $26 = 10 + 10 + 6$.}
The rank-$26$ hyperbolic Cartan of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Phi_{12}}$ on $II_{25, 1} = \Lambda_{24} \oplus II_{1, 1}$ splits under the Dunn--Lurie routing as follows: each $E_2$-factor on a K3 contributes a rank-$10$ sublattice, read off the Mukai-truncation to $H^{0, 0} \oplus H^{1, 1} \oplus H^{2, 2}$ along the cone selected by the Stage-$2$ specialisation direction (not the full rank-$24$ Mukai lattice, since Stage-$2$ integration absorbs the transverse $H^{2, 0} \oplus H^{0, 2}$ and the fibre classes); the remaining rank-$6$ is furnished by the $E_1$-factor carrying $II_{1, 1}$ on the elliptic $E$ enriched by the rank-$4$ hyperbolic extension $II_{2, 2}$ that glues the two K3 factors into the Leech-lattice-plus-hyperbolic structure of $II_{25, 1}$. The arithmetic sum $10 + 10 + 6 = 26$ matches the Cartan rank. The Leech-sublattice-plus-Weyl-vector data of Borcherds~$1990$~\cite{Borcherds1990Invent} Theorem~$1$ (setting $\rho$ as the Weyl vector of $II_{25, 1}$) is therefore recovered as the Dunn--Lurie-routed Cartan datum.

The three components assemble via the symmetric-monoidality of factorisation homology (Lurie~\textit{HA}~$5.5.3$) into a chiral-bialgebra equivalence on $E$.

Primary: Dunn~$1988$~\cite{Dunn1988}; Lurie~\textit{HA}~$5.1.2.2$, $5.5.3$~\cite{LurieHA}; Fresse~$2017$~\cite{Fresse2017}; Borcherds~$1990$~\cite{Borcherds1990Invent} Theorem~$1$; Borcherds~$1998$~\cite{Borcherds1998} Theorem~$13.1$; Theorem~\ref{thm:k3ebkm-fake-monster-rtt} (rank-$26$ RTT).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Scope boundary of the $d = 5$ Fake Monster placement]
\label{rem:fake-monster-d5-scope}
Theorem~\ref{thm:fake-monster-d5-bialgebra} is conditional on three inputs that are open in the present state of the literature: (a) a $d = 5$ Stage-$1$ functor $\PhiFA_5$ defined analogously to $\PhiFA_3$, with the hCS-to-Hall comparison extended to CY$_5$; (b) the Niemeier-type lattice embedding $II_{25, 1} \hookrightarrow H^\bullet(X_{\mathrm{FM}}, \Z)$ compatible with the Mukai truncation and the elliptic structure, which is a Nikulin~$1980$~\cite{Nikulin1980} Theorem~$1.12.2$ lattice-embedding problem at rank $26$ in ambient signature $(9, 41)$ on $H^{\mathrm{even}}(X_{\mathrm{FM}}) = H^{\bullet}(K3)^{\otimes 2} \otimes H^\bullet(E)$; (c) the $E_5$-formality of the Hochschild polyvector Lie algebra at dimension $5$, which is the Kontsevich formality conjecture extended beyond $E_3$ (open in general~\cite{Kontsevich1999} discussion \S$7$). Each condition fails in a computable way: (a) is work-in-progress (Costello--Gwilliam--Li formalism, higher dimensions); (b) is a lattice-theoretic embedding problem whose solvability follows from Nikulin's~\cite{Nikulin1980} discriminant-form analysis but has not been worked out explicitly for $K3 \times K3 \times E$; (c) is the chain-level analogue of the $(\infty, 1)$-operad equivalence $E_5 \simeq E_2 \otimes E_2 \otimes E_1$ which is proved abstractly but whose explicit formality model is unknown above $E_3$. The rank obstruction at $d = 3$ is unconditional: $h^{1, 1}(K3) = 20 < 24$ rules out a Fake Monster BKM hosted in a compact CY$_3$, regardless of the status of (a)--(c). Primary: Nikulin~$1980$~\cite{Nikulin1980} Theorem~$1.12.2$; Borcherds~$1990$~\cite{Borcherds1990Invent}; Conway--Sloane~$1999$~\cite{ConwaySloane1988} Chapter~$26$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The $d = 5$ bialgebra is not a $\Phi_3$-specialisation]
\label{rem:d5-not-d3-specialisation}
The chiral bialgebra $\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{FakeMonster}}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:fake-monster-d5-bialgebra} is an output of the $d = 5$ correspondence $\Phi_5^{(K3 \times K3, E)}$, not a further specialisation of the K3-fibre Stage-$2$ shadow
$\SpCh_{K3,E}(\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E)))$ or of its
Borcherds pushforward to $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$. The two bialgebras live in the same target category $\Eone\mathrm{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(E)$ but are not related by any $\Phi_d$-morphism: the Igusa BKM $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ is rank $3$, the Fake Monster BKM $\mathfrak{g}_{\Phi_{12}}$ is rank $26$; their primitive Lie super-bialgebras are distinct. The $\{2, 3, 5, 24\}$ spectrum on $K3 \times E$ at $d = 3$ (four constructions) and the $\kBKM(\Phi_{12}) = 12$ value at $d = 5$ for the Fake Monster specialisation are five distinct constructions across two distinct CY dimensions, unified by the universal Borcherds-weight identity $\kBKM(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ evaluated at five different Jacobi inputs on three different Borcherds-admissible lattices (paramodular $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\Z)$ on $II_{3, 2}$, paramodular on $II_{2, 2}$, orthogonal $\mathrm{O}^+(II_{26, 2})$ on $II_{25, 1}$).
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Scoped \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{O}(3, 3)$}{O(3, 3)} boundary analogy for \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$}{g-Delta-5}]
\label{conj:fake-monster-envelope}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
The unique even unimodular lattice $\Lambda^{3,3}=U^{\oplus 3}$ of signature $(3,3)$ gives a useful boundary analogy for the $\mathrm{II}_{3,2}$ lattice supporting the Igusa/Clery--Gritsenko denominator. What is conjectural is only the existence of a controlled $\mathrm{O}(3,3)$ degeneration in which a primitive vector $q$ cuts out an $\mathrm{II}_{3,2}$ face containing the $\Delta_5$ root datum. This does \emph{not} identify the K3-side Borcherds image with the Leech/Fake-Monster Borcherds algebra, and it does not construct a common compact-CY host. The Leech/Fake-Monster comparison remains an external analogy; the compact CY$_3$ rank obstruction remains in force.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Compactness route to extended functoriality for \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_3$}{Phi-3}]
\label{conj:cy3-compact-dualizability}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
The $E_3$-Hochschild cohomology of 6D $\hCS$ observables separates sharply by ambient geometry. On $\C^3$, $\HH^0_{E_3}(\Obs_{\hCS}(\C^3, \frakg)) \cong \C[\![\tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3]\!]$ is an infinite-rank formal power series algebra (Gwilliam--Williams~$2021$ Proposition~$5.3.2$), so $\Obs_{\hCS}(\C^3, \frakg)$ fails $3$-dualizability non-abelianly. On compact CY$_3$ $X$, finite-rank $E_3$-Hochschild cohomology and the Calaque--Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi shifted-symplectic form give the expected dualizability inputs. If the compact BV quantisation, the chain-level $\bS^3$ strictification, and the oriented hCS-to-Hall comparison are constructed, Lurie cobordism should upgrade 6D $\hCS$ on $X$ to a fully extended framed $E_3$-TFT, and $\Phi_3$ should extend to an $(\infty, 3)$-functor on that compact subcategory. This is not part of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}: arbitrary CY$_3$ morphism functoriality and compact non-formal strictification, including the quintic, remain open proof obligations. The $d \geq 3$ statement ``$\Phi$ is $E_1$-chiral at $d = 3$'' is the chain-level shadow of the expected compactness route, not a substitute for those witnesses. \emph{Source}: \texttt{notes/wave15\_n5\_HH\_E3\_compact\_vs\_open.tex}.
\end{conjecture}

% ============================================================
\section{The cyclic-to-chiral passage}
\label{sec:cyclic-to-chiral}
% ============================================================

A CY category $\cC$ of dimension $d$ carries a cyclic $\Ainf$-structure (Chapter~\ref{ch:cyclic-ainf}). The primary invariant is the cyclic bar complex $\mathrm{CC}_\bullet(\cC)$ with its $\bS^d$-framing. The passage to chiral algebras decomposes into four steps; each consumes a specific piece of the CY data and produces a specific algebraic structure:

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{Step \arabic*.}]
 \item \textbf{Cyclic $\Ainf \to$ Lie conformal algebra.} The Gerstenhaber bracket on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ is a graded Lie bracket of degree $-1$. The negative-cyclic CY class $[\sigma_\cC]\in HC^-_d(\cC)$, whose Hochschild shadow is the nondegenerate trace $\HH_d(\cC)\to\C$, promotes this to a Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}_\cC$: the bracket becomes a $\lambda$-bracket, and the pairing becomes the invariant bilinear form. This step consumes the $\Ainf$-structure and the CY trace with its Connes-closed lift; it produces the ``current algebra'' of $\cC$.
 \item \textbf{Lie conformal algebra $\to$ factorization envelope.} The factorization envelope construction produces a factorization algebra $\Fact_X(\mathfrak{L}_\cC)$ on any smooth curve $X$. This step consumes the Lie conformal algebra and the curve; it produces a cosheaf on $\Ran(X)$ whose sections are the OPE data.
 \item \textbf{$\bS^d$-framing $\to$ $E_n$ enhancement.} When $d = 2$, the $\bS^2$-framing of $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ (Kontsevich--Vlassopoulos) provides an $\Etwo$-algebra structure on the factorization algebra. This step consumes the framing; it produces the operadic enhancement that distinguishes a chiral algebra from a commutative factorization algebra.
 \item \textbf{Quantization.} The negative-cyclic CY trace class determines a quantization of the classical factorization algebra; the raw map $\HH_d(\cC)\to\C$ is only its Hochschild shadow. At $d = 2$ the output is the quantum chiral algebra $A_\cC = \Phi(\cC)$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}; at $d = 3$ the output is the tagged object $A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)} = \Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(\cC)$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}, after the framed datum has been fixed.
\end{enumerate}

For $d = 3$, Step~3 must be replaced: the $\bS^3$-framing gives an $E_3$-structure on $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$, but the resulting $\Etwo$-braiding (obtained by restriction via Dunn additivity) is \emph{symmetric}. The nonsymmetric quantum group braiding is constructed by a different route: the Drinfeld center $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC))$ adjoins half-braidings $\sigma_M$ to each module, and the $R$-matrix is $R(z) = \sigma_{V_u}(V_v)$ with $z = u - v$ (Section~\ref{sec:d3-drinfeld-center}; Construction~\ref{constr:rmatrix-from-center}).


% ============================================================
\section{The CY-to-chiral correspondence programme}
\label{sec:cy-chiral-functor}
\label{sec:cy-d2-functor}
% ============================================================

The correspondence programme $\{\Phi_d\}_{d \geq 1}$ is not a single functor; it is a $d$-indexed family of constructions, one per Calabi--Yau dimension. The target category of $\Phi_d$ is $E_{n(d)}\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(\cM_d)$ with $n(d) = \infty$ at $d = 1$, $n(d) = 2$ at $d = 2$, $n(d) = 1$ at $d \geq 3$. Because the target varies with $d$, the collection $\{\Phi_d\}$ does not assemble into a functor in the standard category-theoretic sense: there is no single target category in which the image sits. What is asserted is per-$d$ construction on the verified object-level loci, with morphism action and compatibility under CY-duality stated as per-$d$ conjectures. The four-step recipe (cyclic $\Ainf \to$ Lie conformal algebra $\to$ factorization envelope $\to$ quantization) is the \emph{construction method} on those loci, not the definition of a unified functor.

The $d$-indexed structure is inevitable. The Gerstenhaber bracket on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ has degree $1 - d$, and the operadic level of any associated chiral algebra is determined by that degree via the Dunn-additivity calculus of $E_n$-algebras. The target rank $n(d)$ is therefore a function of $d$ alone, and two categories of different CY dimension live in different operadic worlds. This is the same structural phenomenon that forbids a unified functor in the Bezrukavnikov--Mirkovi\'c derived Satake correspondence from assembling across different Langlands dual groups: one has a correspondence, indexed by the relevant datum (here $d$; there the group $G$), not a single functor. In the physical reading, each $\Phi_d$ quantises the twisted or holomorphic sigma-model on $\mathrm{CY}_d$ to its boundary chiral algebra at the level prescribed by $n(d)$; the Costello--Gaiotto boundary-VOA picture is per-$d$ by construction, and the programme $\{\Phi_d\}$ is the mathematical refinement of that per-$d$ physical input.

\begin{theorem}[CY-to-chiral correspondence at dimension \texorpdfstring{$d$}{d}]
\label{thm:phi-platonic}
\ClaimStatusConditional{} \textup{(proved functorially at $d \leq 2$; framed object-level at $d = 3$; functoriality on morphisms is Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality})}
Fix $d \geq 1$. On the verified smooth proper loci of CY categories of dimension $d$, there is a construction
\[
 \Phi_d^{(\Sigma_{d-1},C)}(\cC)
 =
 \SpCh_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}(\PhiFA_d(\cC))
 \;\in\; E_{n(d)}\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(\cM_d),
 \qquad n(d) \;=\;
 \begin{cases} \infty, & d = 1, \\ 2, & d = 2, \\ 1, & d \geq 3, \end{cases}
\]
characterised by four universal properties \textup{(U1)--(U4)} on its stated scope. At $d\leq2$ this is the proved functorial construction on the smooth proper locus. At $d=3$ it is the framed object-level assignment of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}, requiring in addition the chain-level $\bS^3$-framing hypothesis and fixed witnessed admissible $(\Sigma_2,C)$. Functoriality on morphisms \textup{(}property~\textup{(U2)} below\textup{)} is asserted as a per-$d$ conjecture \textup{(}Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality}} below\textup{),} verified at $d = 2$ on the Mukai transform $\mathrm{K3} \to \mathrm{K3}$ \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}}\textup{)} and open in general. The collection $\{\Phi_d\}_{d \geq 1}$ is a correspondence programme, not a single functor: the target category $E_{n(d)}\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(\cM_d)$ depends on $d$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(U\arabic*)}]
 \item\label{phi:U1} \emph{Bar-shadow comparison.} On the verified
 object-level locus, after fixing the admissible specialisation
 $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$, the ordered chiral bar coalgebra of the output
 carries a comparison quasi-isomorphism
 \[
 B^{\mathrm{ord}}\bigl(\Phi_d^{(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)}(\cC)\bigr)
 \;\simeq\; \mathrm{CC}_\bullet(\cC)
 \quad \text{in } \mathrm{ChirCoAlg}^{\mathrm{conil}}_{\cM_d}.
 \]
 This is a comparison of coalgebras on the constructed object-level
 locus. Naturality in CY morphisms and base change is part of
 Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality}}, not part of U1.
 \item\label{phi:U2} \emph{Per-$d$ functoriality on morphisms \textup{(}conjectural\textup{).}} For every CY-morphism $f \colon \cC \to \cC'$ \textup{(}cyclic $\Ainf$-functor compatible with the $\bS^d$-framing\textup{),} $\Phi_d(f) \colon \Phi_d(\cC) \to \Phi_d(\cC')$ is expected to be a chiral-algebra morphism; see Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality}}. Wall-crossings in the source \textup{(}Bridgeland stability changes\textup{)} are expected to map to $R$-matrix gauge transformations in the target. This property is the morphism-action assertion of the correspondence programme; it is open in general and tested at $d = 2$ on the Mukai transform.
 \item\label{phi:U3} \emph{Drinfeld center compatibility.} The native operadic level $n$ is the Gerstenhaber-bracket degree $1 - d$ \textup{(}so $\Phi(\cC)$ is natively $E_\infty$ at $d = 1$, $\Etwo$ at $d = 2$, $\Eone$ at $d \geq 3$\textup{).} For $d \geq 3$, the braided $\Etwo$-structure is recovered on the Drinfeld centre of the representation category:
 \[
 \cZ\bigl(\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(\Phi(\cC))\bigr) \;\simeq\; \mathrm{Rep}^{\Etwo}\bigl(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi(\cC))\bigr)^{\mathrm{centred}},
 \]
 with the half-braiding $\sigma_{V_u}(V_v)$ on evaluation modules equal to the $R$-matrix $R(z)$, $z = u - v$. The braided level $\Etwo$ at $d \geq 3$ lives on the Drinfeld centre of $\mathrm{Rep}(\Phi(\cC))$, \emph{not} on $\Phi(\cC)$ itself.
 \item\label{phi:U4} \emph{Standard-input recovery.} On the four canonical inputs, $\Phi$ recovers the classically known chiral algebras:
 \begin{itemize}
 \item $\Phi(\Coh(E)) \;=\;$ elliptic lattice VOA \textup{(}rank-$2$ Heisenberg with elliptic-curve lattice form\textup{),} at $d = 1$;
 \item $\Phi(D^b(\Coh(\mathrm{K3}))) \;=\;$ rank-$24$ Mukai-Heisenberg $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ with Mukai pairing of signature $(4, 20)$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$, bar Euler product $\eta^{24}$, at $d = 2$ \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}}\textup{);}
 \item the Hall-side local model of the $\C^3$ chart is
 $\CoHA(\C^3) \cong Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ \textup{(}positive half of
 the affine Yangian; \emph{not} the full $\cW_{1+\infty}$\textup{),} at
 $d = 3$;
 \item the Hall-side local model of an $A_n$-McKay chart is the positive half of the level-$1$ ADE Yangian, at $d = 3$.
 \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}
\textup{(}The full $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$ arises only after
Drinfeld-centre doubling of the Hall-side positive half and the
Fock/evaluation representation of the full Yangian. The positive-half
versus full-Yangian distinction is structural, not a notational
variant.\textup{)}
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Per-\texorpdfstring{$d$}{d} construction and the commuting square]
\label{rem:phi-platonic-slogan}
For each $d$, the construction $\Phi_d$ is the chiral shadow of the CY trace at dimension $d$. Its single defining diagram is the per-$d$ commuting square \textup{\eqref{phi:U1}}:
\[
\begin{array}{ccc}
 \CY_d\text{-}\Cat & \xrightarrow{\;\;\Phi_d\;\;} & E_{n(d)}\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(\cM_d) \\
 \downarrow{\mathrm{CC}_\bullet} & & \downarrow{B^{\mathrm{ord}}} \\
 \mathrm{MixedCx} & \xrightarrow{\;\sim\;} & \mathrm{ChirCoAlg}^{\mathrm{conil}}_{\cM_d}
\end{array}
\qquad\text{with }
n(d) = \begin{cases} \infty, & d = 1 \\ 2, & d = 2 \\ 1, & d \geq 3 \end{cases}.
\]
The bar functor $B^{\mathrm{ord}}$ extracts the output's ordered
coalgebraic shadow. The inverse statement is the chiral counit
\(\Omega^{\mathrm{ch}}B^{\mathrm{ord}}(A)\to A\) on the Koszul or
weight-completed coderived locus; the CY trace supplies the cyclic
input on \(\cC\) and is not the cobar inverse of
\(B^{\mathrm{ord}}\). The diagram is per-$d$: there is no commuting
square across different $d$ because the target
$E_{n(d)}\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(\cM_d)$ changes.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The collection \texorpdfstring{$\{\Phi_d\}$}{Phi-d} is a correspondence programme, not a unified functor]
\label{rem:phi-not-unified-functor}
The $d$-indexed target category $E_{n(d)}\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(\cM_d)$ varies with $d$, so $\{\Phi_d\}_{d \geq 1}$ is not a single functor in the standard category-theoretic sense: there is no ambient target category in which the images of all $\Phi_d$ sit, and no composition across a change of $d$ is defined. The collection is a research programme, a Grothendieck-style $\Phi_d$-th\'eorie, with the following three per-$d$ open questions stated here and unresolved in general:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Functoriality on morphisms} \textup{(}Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality}}\textup{):} for each $d$, $\Phi_d$ extends from objects to cyclic $\Ainf$-functors, taking CY-morphisms $f \colon \cC \to \cC'$ to chiral-algebra morphisms $\Phi_d(f)$. The four-step recipe constructs $\Phi_d(\cC)$ from the data of $\cC$; the map on $\mathrm{Hom}$-sets must be verified concretely. At $d = 2$ the Mukai transform $\mathrm{K3} \to \mathrm{K3}$ is the first test case \textup{(}see Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}}\textup{);} the Mukai-transform functoriality is pending a direct chain-level verification.
 \item \emph{Compatibility under CY-duality for families.} For a smooth family of CY$_d$ categories over a base, $\Phi_d$ applied fibrewise is expected to produce a family of chiral algebras over the relative moduli $\cM_d^{\mathrm{rel}}$; base-change and monodromy statements are per-$d$ open questions.
 \item \emph{Compatibility across $d$ under dimensional reduction.} Passage from $\mathrm{CY}_{d+1}$ to $\mathrm{CY}_d$ via a codimension-one cycle induces a restriction $\Phi_{d+1}(\cC) \leadsto \Phi_d(\cC')$; the physical route is the Costello--Gaiotto compactification of the twisted sigma-model on a cycle, and the mathematical statement is an expected natural transformation, per-$d$ conjectural.
\end{enumerate}
This situation is structurally analogous to the Bezrukavnikov--Mirkovi\'c derived Satake correspondence: one has per-datum constructions \textup{(}per Langlands dual group, per CY dimension\textup{)} whose cross-datum compatibility is an open programme, not a single functor in a standard category-theoretic sense.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Per-\texorpdfstring{$d$}{d} functoriality of \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_d$}{Phi-d}]
\label{conj:phi-d-functoriality}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
For each $d \geq 1$ and each cyclic $\Ainf$-functor $f \colon \cC \to \cC'$ between smooth proper CY categories of dimension $d$ compatible with the $\bS^d$-framing, the construction $\Phi_d$ of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-platonic}} extends to a chiral-algebra morphism
\[
 \Phi_d(f) \;\colon\; \Phi_d(\cC) \;\longrightarrow\; \Phi_d(\cC'),
\]
functorial in $f$ \textup{(}respecting composition and identities\textup{)} up to natural quasi-isomorphism in $E_{n(d)}\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(\cM_d)$. Wall-crossings in the source \textup{(}Bridgeland stability changes\textup{)} map to $R$-matrix gauge transformations in the target. The conjecture is open in general and tested at $d = 2$ on the Mukai transform $\mathrm{K3} \to \mathrm{K3}$ \textup{(}pending chain-level verification\textup{)}. At $d = 3$, Theorem~\ref{thm:phi3-witnessed-kernel-functoriality} proves the statement only for $\Phi_3$-admissible witnessed kernel data; the unrestricted $A_n$-McKay $\to$ $A_{n'}$-McKay reduction induced by subgroup inclusion $\bZ/n \hookrightarrow \bZ/n'$ remains open until the obstruction criterion of Proposition~\ref{prop:phi3-arbitrary-morphism-obstruction-criterion} is satisfied.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Per-\texorpdfstring{$d$}{d} cases as evaluations of \texorpdfstring{$\{\Phi_d\}$}{Phi-d}]
\label{rem:phi-per-d-evaluations}
The historical names CY-A$_1$, CY-A$_2$, CY-A$_3$, CY-A$_{d \geq 4}$ are preserved as the labels of the per-$d$ corollaries below, \emph{not} as the names of separate theorems. Each per-$d$ case is an evaluation of the correspondence $\{\Phi_d\}$ at the stated CY dimension; the parent statement is Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-platonic}, read as a $d$-parametrised family of per-$d$ existence-and-uniqueness statements on objects. The proofs of the per-$d$ cases (Theorems~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral} below for $d = 2$ and~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} for $d = 3$) are the per-$d$ existence-and-uniqueness verifications of $\Phi_d$ at the respective dimension; together with $d = 1$ \textup{(}classical: lattice VOAs\textup{)} and $d \geq 4$ \textup{(}Theorems in Section~\textup{\ref{sec:beyond-cy3}}\textup{),} they exhaust the construction on objects. Functoriality on morphisms \textup{(}property~\textup{\ref{phi:U2}}\textup{)} is asserted as Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality}}, open in general. No unified-functor statement across $d$ is asserted; $\{\Phi_d\}$ is $d$-indexed.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Conditional rigidity supplied by \textup{(U1), (U3), (U4)}]
\label{rem:phi-uniqueness}
On the Koszul-self-dual locus where the ordered bar coalgebra is
conilpotent or weight-completed and the chiral counit
\(\Omega^{\mathrm{ch}}B^{\mathrm{ord}}(A)\to A\) is a
quasi-isomorphism, properties \textup{(U1), (U3), (U4)} give a
rigidity criterion for the object-level assignment. Property
\textup{(U1)} fixes the bar coalgebra; Vol.~I Theorem~A recovers
\(A\) from that coalgebra only in this ambient. Property \textup{(U3)}
fixes the native operadic level and, for \(d\geq3\), the centre target
\(\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A))\), not an intrinsic \(\Etwo\)-structure on
\(A\). Property \textup{(U4)} fixes the standard calibration objects.
Off the Koszul-self-dual locus these properties are diagnostic rather
than characterising; Fourier--Mukai kernel rigidification is an
additional hypothesis. Property~\textup{\ref{phi:U2}} remains the
separate morphism-action conjecture.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[{\texorpdfstring{$\Phi.1$}{Phi.1}: \texorpdfstring{$d = 1$}{d = 1} evaluation; classical lattice-to-VOA}]
\label{cor:phi-d1-evaluation}
\ClaimStatusProvedElsewhere{}
At $d = 1$ with $\cC = D^b(\Coh(E))$ for $E$ an elliptic curve, $\Phi(\cC)$ is the elliptic lattice VOA of $H^*(E, \bZ)$ \textup{(}rank-$2$ Heisenberg with the elliptic-curve lattice form\textup{).} The Gerstenhaber bracket is degree $0$ \textup{(}commutative\textup{);} $\Phi(\cC)$ is natively $E_\infty$. Bar Euler product $= \eta(\tau)^2$. $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1 = h^{1,0}(E) + 0$. Classical: Borcherds 1986; Kac 1998.
\end{corollary}

\begin{theorem}[{\texorpdfstring{$\Phi.2$}{Phi.2}: \texorpdfstring{$d = 2$}{d = 2} evaluation; CY-A\texorpdfstring{$_2$}{-2}}]
\label{thm:cy-to-chiral}
\label{thm:cy-a-d2}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
The correspondence $\Phi_d$ at dimension $d = 2$ is the symmetric-monoidal functor
\[
 \Phi_2 \colon \CY_2\text{-}\Cat \longrightarrow \Etwo\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg},
 \qquad \Phi_2 \;=\; \SpCh_{\Sigma_1,C} \circ \PhiFA_2,
\]
the two-stage factorisation of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation} evaluated at $d = 2$: stage~$1$ $\PhiFA_2$ produces the canonical $E_2$-holomorphic factorisation algebra $\PhiFA_2(\cC) \in \EdHolFA(X)$ on the CY$_2$ surface $X$, and stage~$2$ $\SpCh_{\Sigma_1,C}$ specialises along a $1$-cycle $\Sigma_1 \subset X$ to the reference curve $C$. The output is an $\Etwo$-chiral algebra on $C$ (native operadic level $n_{\mathrm{native}}(2) = 2$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:native-en-level} survives the specialisation intact), such that:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item $\Phi_2(\cC)$ is a chiral algebra whose generating fields are in bijection with $\HH^{\bullet+1}(\cC)$;
 \item The bar complex $B(\Phi_2(\cC))$ is quasi-isomorphic to the cyclic bar complex $\mathrm{CC}_\bullet(\cC)$ as a factorization coalgebra \textup{(}property~\textup{\ref{phi:U1}} of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-platonic}} at $d = 2$\textup{).}
\end{enumerate}
The $\Etwo$-enhancement in Step~3 uses the $\bS^2$-framing of $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ (Kontsevich--Vlassopoulos); it is carried by the stage-$1$ output $\PhiFA_2(\cC)$ and pushed to $C$ via $\SpCh_{\Sigma_1,C}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}} is the \texorpdfstring{$d = 2$}{d = 2} evaluation of \texorpdfstring{$\Phi$}{Phi}]
\label{rem:cy-a-d2-as-evaluation}
