This statement is Corollary $\Phi.2$ in the language of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-platonic}}: the four universal properties \textup{(U1)--(U4)}, evaluated at $d = 2$, force $\Phi$ to land in $\Etwo$-chiral algebras \textup{(}property~\textup{\ref{phi:U3}}: $n_{\mathrm{native}}(2) = 2$\textup{),} and the four-step recipe of Section~\textup{\ref{sec:cyclic-to-chiral}} is the explicit construction. The K3 evaluation \textup{(}property~\textup{\ref{phi:U4}}: $\Phi(D^b(\Coh(\mathrm{K3}))) = \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$\textup{)} is verified explicitly in Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}}. The $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_X)$ identification at $d = 2$ \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}} below\textup{)} is the scalar shadow of \textup{\ref{phi:U1}} composed with the Hodge-filtered supertrace mechanism.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[CY modular characteristic at \texorpdfstring{$d = 2$}{d = 2}: Theorem CY-A\texorpdfstring{$_2$}{-2}(iii)]
\label{prop:cy-kappa-d2}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
% The free-field argument gives kappa_ch = supertrace on generators = chi^CY,
% and at d=2 Serre duality (S_C = [2]) kills the one-loop correction from
% quantization Step 4. See also Proposition~prop:kappa-cat-chi-cy in
% modular_koszul_bridge.tex for the same result stated categorically.
For $\cC$ a smooth proper CY category of dimension $d = 2$ \emph{with $h^{1,0}(X) = 0$} (equivalently, $\HH_{-1}(\cC) = 0$), and $A_\cC = \Phi(\cC)$ the chiral algebra of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}},
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC) \;=\; \chi^{\CY}(\cC) \;\stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=}\; \chi(\cO_X) \;=\; \sum_{q=0}^{d} (-1)^q\, h^{0,q}(X).
\]
The condition $h^{1,0} = 0$ is essential for this class-A theorem: it forces $\HH_{-1}(\cC) = 0$, which is needed for the Serre-duality parity argument to kill the Heisenberg correction in the specialisation step (see proof). For abelian surfaces, $h^{1,0} = 2$ and $\HH_{-1} \neq 0$; the compact total-space Hodge supertrace remains $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_A)=\chi(\cO_A)=0$, while the separate chiral-Heisenberg rank is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(A)=2$ (additive rank $1+1$ from the elliptic factors). These are different readings, not a failure of the compact Hodge-supertrace convention. In the abstract categorical setting, $\chi^{\CY}(\cC)$ is the Hodge-filtered trace: it extracts the $F^0$-graded piece of the Hochschild homology via the HKR filtration, \emph{not} the raw alternating sum $\sum_i (-1)^i \dim \HH_i(\cC)$. The latter gives $\sum_i (-1)^i \sum_q h^{d-i,q}$, which for K3 yields $-16$ (since $\dim H^*(X, \Omega^0_X) = 2$, $\dim H^*(X, \Omega^1_X) = 20$, $\dim H^*(X, \Omega^2_X) = 2$, and $2 - 20 + 2 = -16$), not $\chi(\cO_{\mathrm{K3}}) = 2$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The generating space of $A_\cC$ is $\HH^{\bullet+1}(\cC)$, and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ equals the supertrace of the identity on the
Hodge-filtered generating space at $d = 2$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$. By HKR,
$\HH_i(\cC) \cong \bigoplus_q H^q(X, \Omega^{d-i}_X)$, and the
supertrace through the Hodge-to-de Rham spectral sequence extracts the
$F^0/F^1$ piece, yielding the \emph{classical} value
$\chi(\cO_X) = \sum_q (-1)^q h^{0,q}(X)$. The quantization step (CY-A,
Step~4) may shift the Heisenberg specialisation by a correction living
in $\HH^{d+1}(\cC) \cong \HH_{-1}(\cC)^\vee$. At $d = 2$ with
$h^{1,0} = 0$, $\HH_{-1}(\cC) = 0$ (since $\HH_{-1} = h^{1,0}$ for
CY$_2$), so no Heisenberg correction appears. The chiral characteristic
then agrees with $\chi(\cO_X)$, and that value is $\kcat$. For K3
surfaces both invariants have value $2$. For abelian surfaces
($h^{1,0} = 2$, $\HH_{-1} \neq 0$), the class-A hypothesis fails: the
Heisenberg rank is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(A) = 2$, while
the compact Hodge supertrace, the categorical Euler, and $\chi(\cO_A)$
all have value $0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Why \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_X)$}{kappa-ch = chi(O-X)} and not \texorpdfstring{$\sum (-1)^i \dim \HH_i$}{sum (-1)-i dim HH-i}: the Hodge filtration mechanism]
\label{rem:kappa-hodge-mechanism}
The identification $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC) = \chi(\cO_X)$ is the conjunction of two facts, one algebraic (the bar complex sees the Hodge filtration, not the total Hochschild dimensions) and one analytic (CY Serre duality kills all quantum corrections at $d = 2$). We explain both in detail, since the formula is easily confused with a raw alternating sum of Hochschild dimensions. For K3, the raw alternating sum $\sum_{p=0}^{d}(-1)^p \dim H^*(X, \Omega^p_X) = 2 - 20 + 2 = -16$; neither this nor the unsigned total $24 = \dim H^*(K3, \bC)$ equals $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Fact 1: the genus-$1$ bar coefficient is a Hodge-filtered supertrace.}

\smallskip\noindent
The Vol~I modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA)$ (Theorem~D, Theorem~\ref{thm:modular-characteristic} in Vol~I) is defined as the genus-$1$ curvature coefficient of the bar complex: the scalar $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ such that $\mathrm{obs}_1(\cA) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA) \cdot \lambda_1 \in H^2(\overline{\cM}_{1,1})$. For $d$ free bosons, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = d$; for the Virasoro algebra at central charge $c$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = c/2$. In all cases, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is the \emph{supertrace} of the identity on the generating space:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA) \;=\; \sum_{n} (-1)^n\, \dim V_n,
\]
where $V = \bigoplus_n V_n$ is the graded generating space of $\cA$ (conformal primaries). For the Heisenberg algebra $\cH_d$ at level $1$, the generating space is $d$ copies of a single bosonic field, so $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = d$.

Now suppose $\cA = \Phi(\cC)$ arises from a CY$_d$ category $\cC = D^b(\Coh(X))$. The generating space is $V = \HH^{\bullet+1}(\cC)$. By Hochschild--Kostant--Rosenberg, the Hochschild homology carries the \emph{Hodge filtration}:
\[
 \HH_i(\cC) \;\cong\; \bigoplus_{q} H^q\bigl(X,\, \Omega_X^{d-i}\bigr) \;=\; \bigoplus_q h^{d-i,\,q},
\]
where $h^{p,q} = \dim H^q(X, \Omega^p_X)$. The supertrace on $V$ is \emph{not} the alternating sum of the total dimensions $\dim \HH_i$; it is the alternating sum of the $F^0$-graded piece of the Hodge filtration. Concretely, the Hodge-to-de~Rham spectral sequence $E_1^{p,q} = H^q(X, \Omega^p_X) \Rightarrow H^{p+q}_{\mathrm{dR}}(X)$ induces a filtration on the generating space, and the genus-$1$ bar differential respects this filtration. The bar obstruction at genus $1$ computes the graded Euler characteristic of the $F^0 = \Omega^0 = \cO_X$ column:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:kappa-hodge-filtered}
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC) \;=\; \sum_{q=0}^{d} (-1)^q\, h^{0,q}(X) \;=\; \chi(\cO_X).
\end{equation}
This is the holomorphic Euler characteristic, extracting only the $(0,q)$-Hodge types.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Fact 2: Serre duality at $d = 2$ kills all quantum corrections.}

\smallskip\noindent
The correspondence $\Phi_d$ has four construction steps: (1)~HKR decomposition of $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$, (2)~Lie conformal algebra from the Gerstenhaber bracket, (3)~$\bS^d$-framing from the CY trace, (4)~quantization (passage from classical to quantum chiral algebra). Steps~1--3 are manifestly compatible with the Hodge filtration, so the classical value of $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is $\chi(\cO_X)$. The question is whether Step~4 introduces a quantum correction $\delta\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$.

At $d = 2$, the CY Serre functor is $\mathbb{S}_\cC \simeq [2]$: it acts as the cohomological shift by $2$. This has two consequences:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
 \item \emph{Parity cancellation.} The one-loop anomaly of the quantization map is an element of $\HH^{d+1}(\cC)$, the Hochschild obstruction group for deformations. At $d = 2$, this is $\HH^3(\cC)$. For a smooth proper CY$_2$ category, Serre duality forces $\HH^3(\cC) \cong \HH^{-1}(\cC)^\vee = 0$ (since $\HH_{-1}$ vanishes for K3 by the vanishing of odd Hodge numbers $h^{1,0} = h^{0,1} = 0$). Therefore the one-loop correction to $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is zero: $\delta\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$.
 \item \emph{Serre involution on the bar complex.} The Serre duality $\mathbb{S}_\cC \simeq [2]$ induces an involution on the genus-$1$ bar cohomology that pairs the contribution from $\HH_i$ with that from $\HH_{d-i}$. For $d = 2$, this pairs $\HH_0$ with $\HH_2$ and fixes $\HH_1$. The bar differential at genus $1$ acts by zero on the $\mathbb{S}$-fixed part and pairs the remaining terms, so the bar Euler characteristic reduces to the $\mathbb{S}$-trace. The $\mathbb{S}$-trace on $\HH_0 = H^{0,0} \oplus H^{1,1} \oplus H^{2,0}$ is:
 \[
 \mathrm{str}_{\mathbb{S}}(\HH_0) \;=\; h^{0,0} - 0 + h^{2,0} \;=\; 1 + 1 \;=\; 2
 \]
 for K3. The $h^{1,1} = 20$ generators of $\HH_0$ are \emph{killed} by the Serre pairing. More precisely: the Serre functor pairs each $(1,1)$-class with its dual via $\int_X \alpha \wedge \beta$, and this pairing is an isomorphism $H^{1,1} \xrightarrow{\sim} (H^{1,1})^\vee$. In the genus-$1$ bar complex, these paired contributions cancel in the supertrace, leaving only the unpaired $F^0$-column classes $h^{0,0}$ and $h^{0,2}$ (which is $h^{2,0}$ by conjugation).
\end{enumerate}

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{The K3 case in detail.}

\smallskip\noindent
The HKR decomposition gives $\dim \HH_0(K3) = 22$, sourced from three Hodge types:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.1}
\begin{tabular}{lccl}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Hodge type in $\HH_0$} & \textbf{Dimension} & \textbf{Contributes to $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$?} & \textbf{Mechanism} \\
 \midrule
 $H^0(\cO_S) = H^{0,0}$ & $1$ & Yes & $F^0$-column; Serre-unpaired \\
 $H^1(\Omega^1_S) = H^{1,1}$ & $20$ & No & Serre-paired: $\omega(\alpha,\beta) = \int \alpha \wedge \beta$ \\
 $H^2(\cO_S) = H^{0,2}$ (via $H^0(\Omega^2_S) = H^{2,0}$) & $1$ & Yes & $F^0$-column; Serre-unpaired \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The raw Hochschild alternating sum is $\sum_{p=0}^{2}(-1)^p \dim H^*(S, \Omega^p_S) = 2 - 20 + 2 = -16$ (Hodge-graded), or equivalently $\sum_{n=-2}^{2} (-1)^n \dim \HH_n = 1 - 0 + 22 - 0 + 1 = 24$ (homological-graded, the topological Euler characteristic). Neither equals $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$. The correct quantity is the Hodge-filtered supertrace~\eqref{eq:kappa-hodge-filtered}:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3) \;=\; h^{0,0} - h^{0,1} + h^{0,2} \;=\; 1 - 0 + 1 \;=\; 2 \;=\; \chi(\cO_{K3}).
\]

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Why $d \geq 3$ is harder.}

\smallskip\noindent
At $d = 3$, the Serre functor is $\mathbb{S}_\cC \simeq [3]$. The obstruction group $\HH^{d+1}(\cC) = \HH^4(\cC)$ is no longer forced to vanish by parity: $\HH^4 \cong \HH_{-1}^\vee$, and whether $\HH_{-1}$ vanishes depends on $h^{1,0}(X)$. The naive Hodge-filtered supertrace still gives $\chi(\cO_X) = \sum_q (-1)^q h^{0,q}$, but for every strict CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$ this is $1 - 0 + 0 - 1 = 0$ by Serre duality. The actual $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ at $d = 3$ is generically \emph{nonzero} (e.g.\ $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\text{quintic}) = -25/3$), so $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \neq \chi(\cO_X)$ at $d = 3$. The discrepancy arises because the $\bS^3$-framing introduces a correction to the supertrace that is absent at $d = 2$: the Serre involution $\mathbb{S}_\cC \simeq [3]$, being odd-dimensional, does \emph{not} pair the Hodge contributions as it does at $d = 2$, and the BCOV holomorphic anomaly produces a nontrivial shift. The correct dimension-stratified formula for $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ at $d = 3$ is given in Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy-kappa-identification} below.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Comparison with other $\kappa_\bullet$-values (the $\kappa_\bullet$-spectrum).}

\smallskip\noindent
The Hodge filtration mechanism explains why the algebraization invariant $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ differs from the other members of the $\kappa_\bullet$-spectrum. Two of these are manifold invariants, independent of algebraization:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}} = 24$ for K3 counts the \emph{total} rank of $H^*(K3, \bZ)$, i.e.\ all Hodge types. It equals $\sum \dim \HH_i$, not the Hodge-filtered supertrace. This is a manifold invariant.
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = \chi(\cO_X) = 2$ is the holomorphic Euler characteristic, a manifold invariant identical to $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ at $d = 2$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:kappa-cat-chi-cy} in the modular Koszul bridge chapter).
\end{itemize}
The remaining member is an algebraization invariant:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 5$ for $K3 \times E$ is the weight of the Borcherds automorphic product $\Delta_5$, a quantity determined by the BKM imaginary root lattice. It encodes the full automorphic/BPS package, not just the single-copy genus-$1$ bar coefficient.
\end{itemize}
\emph{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ sees only the $\cO_X$-column of the Hodge diamond: the genus-$1$ bar complex, filtered by the Hodge-to-de~Rham spectral sequence, has its non-$F^0$ contributions killed by the CY Serre pairing.}
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[{CY-2 \texorpdfstring{$[2]$}{[2]}-shift with \texorpdfstring{$\widetilde{M}_{24}$}{widetildeM-24} Serre cocycle}]
\label{thm:cy-to-chiral-cy2-shift-M24}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For the K3 input $\cC = D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3)$, the CY-to-chiral correspondence
$\Phi_2$ carries the following canonical shift data:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item the Hochschild cohomology is bi-graded by HKR as
 \[
 \HH^\bullet(\cC)
 \;=\;
 \bigoplus_{p+q=\bullet} H^p\bigl(K3,\Lambda^q T_{K3}\bigr);
 \]
\item the bar-cobar Koszul shift is $[2]$, not $[3]$;
\item any apparent $[3]$-shift comes from replacing the CY-$2$ input category by an
 auxiliary ambient CY-$3$ space such as $K3\times\mathbb{C}$ or
 $K3\times\mathbb{C}^3$, which does not affect the input dimension of $\Phi_2$;
\item under $\widetilde{M}_{24}$-equivariance, the underlying Serre functor remains
 $[2]$, but the equivariant descent data is twisted by a projective class whose source
 lies in
 \[
 H^2(M_{24},\U(1))
 \;\cong\;
 \mathbb{Z}/12,
 \]
 and whose image in the Serre-functor automorphism group has order $6$.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently,
\[
 (\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5})^{!}
 \;\simeq\;
 V(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})^{\mathrm{coalg}}[2]\otimes \eta_{\widetilde{M}_{24}}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Item~\textup{(i)} is the HKR identification for a smooth projective surface. Since K3
is CY-$2$, its Serre functor on $D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3)$ is the cohomological shift
$[2]$, so the Koszul shift attached to $\Phi_2$ is likewise $[2]$; this proves
item~\textup{(ii)}. Item~\textup{(iii)} records that $\Phi_2$ is evaluated on the
category $D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3)$ itself, so the shift is controlled by the CY dimension
of the input category and not by the ambient factorization envelope on the curve
$X$ used in the construction.

For item~\textup{(iv)}, the projective $M_{24}$-equivariant structure on the K3 chiral
bialgebra is already computed in Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}:
Theorem~\ref{thm:m24-schur-cocycle} gives the Schur-cover action, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-cy2-serre-cocycle} together with
Remark~\ref{rem:schur-multiplier-M24-Z12} identifies the induced Serre-side cocycle as
the order-$6$ image of the order-$12$ Schur multiplier. Transporting that K3-lane
calculation through the present $\Phi_2$ formalism gives the displayed Koszul-dual
formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Mukai doubling and the Koszul conductor \texorpdfstring{$K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}=8$}{K-kappa-ch=8}]
\label{rem:cy-to-chiral-mukai-doubling-K-ch}
The categorical central charge of the Mukai-enhanced K3 category
$D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3)$ equipped with its Mukai pairing of signature $(4,20)$ is
$c_+(\mathrm{Mukai}(K3))=4$ (positive signature), so the Beilinson--Drinfeld
Koszul-conductor identity gives $K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}=2\,c_+(\mathrm{Mukai}(K3))=8$.
This is the new Theorem-C entry for the Lorentzian-lattice-parametric
$\mathcal{B}$-family stratification:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}+\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^!
 \;\in\;
 \{0,\,8,\,13,\,250/3,\,98/3\}
 \qquad\text{(Theorem~C enlarged on the $\mathcal{B}$-family)}.
\]
The order-$8$ monodromy of the Humbert divisor $H_1\subset\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ of
the parameter base (Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic},
Theorem~\ref{thm:humbert-order-K-kappa}) equals this $K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}$ on the
$\mathcal{B}$-family; on this family, $\hbar^2\cdot K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}=-1$
(Bruinier 2002, Proposition~5.1 Heegner-Chern-class reciprocity).
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[K\"unneth factorisation of \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_{d_1+d_2}$}{Phi-d-1+d-2} on split CY products]
\label{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
\index{CY-to-chiral functor!Kunneth factorisation}
\index{Phi_d!Kunneth on split products}
Let $\cC_1 = D^b(\Coh(X_1))$ and $\cC_2 = D^b(\Coh(X_2))$ be smooth proper CY categories of dimensions $d_1$ and $d_2$, with $X_1, X_2$ compact K\"ahler CY manifolds equipped with independent Ricci-flat metrics. Set $d = d_1 + d_2$ and $X = X_1 \times X_2$. Then $\cC = D^b(\Coh(X)) \simeq \cC_1 \boxtimes \cC_2$ \textup{(}Bondal--Orlov \cite{BondalOrlov2003}\textup{),} and the CY-to-chiral correspondence of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-platonic}} factorises:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:phi-kunneth-formula}
 \Phi_{d}(\cC_1 \boxtimes \cC_2) \;\simeq\; \Phi_{d_1}(\cC_1) \;\boxtimes\; \Phi_{d_2}(\cC_2)
\end{equation}
as $E_{n(d)}$-chiral algebras on the moduli base $\cM_d$, where the tensor product on the right is the external factorisation tensor product \textup{(}Francis--Gaitsgory \cite{FrancisGaitsgory2012} \S 3\textup{),} and the equivalence is a quasi-isomorphism at the level of factorisation coalgebras. The target operadic level is $n(d) = 1$ for $d \ge 3$, reading the product-of-CY$_2$ case as CY$_4$ rather than CY$_2$.

\textit{Hypotheses.} The factorisation~\eqref{eq:phi-kunneth-formula} holds under the following split-product conditions:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(K\arabic*)}]
\item\label{kunneth:H1} \emph{Independent Ricci-flat metrics.} The Calabi--Yau metric on $X$ is the product of Ricci-flat metrics on $X_1$ and $X_2$ \textup{(}no mixing term; cf.\ Yau \cite{Yau1978}\textup{).} Equivalently, the $\bS^d$-framing on $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ factors as $\bS^{d_1} \boxtimes \bS^{d_2}$ under the K\"unneth decomposition of Hochschild homology.
\item\label{kunneth:H2} \emph{BCOV holomorphic-anomaly compatibility.} The BCOV anomaly cocycle $[\alpha_{\mathrm{BCOV}}] \in \HH^2(\cC)$ factors as $\alpha^{(1)}_{\mathrm{BCOV}} \otimes 1 + 1 \otimes \alpha^{(2)}_{\mathrm{BCOV}}$ under the K\"unneth decomposition. At $d_1 = 2, d_2 = 1$ \textup{(}strict K3 $\times$ elliptic curve\textup{)} this holds unconditionally by Maulik--Pandharipande \cite{MaulikPandharipande2013}; at $d_1 = d_2 = 1$ \textup{(}abelian surface\textup{)} it holds by the absence of BCOV corrections at $d \le 2$.
\item\label{kunneth:H3} \emph{Stability-product sublocus.} The identification is witnessed on the product-stable sublocus $\StabOp(X_1) \times \StabOp(X_2) \hookrightarrow \StabOp(X_1 \times X_2)$ \textup{(}Bayer--Macr\`i--Stellari \cite{BayerMacriStellari2016}\textup{);} away from this sublocus, wall-crossings in $\StabOp(X_1 \times X_2)$ may deform the chiral algebra by $R$-matrix gauge transformations that do not factor.
\end{enumerate}

\textit{Verified instances.}
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item $X = K3 \times E$, $d_1 = 2, d_2 = 1$, $d = 3$: on the product-stable framed locus,
\[
\Phi_3^{(K3,E)}(K3 \times E) \simeq \Phi_2(K3) \boxtimes \Phi_1(E)
= \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}(K3)} \boxtimes H_1(E).
\]
The verification is against Vol~I Theorem~A \textup{(}bar Euler product $\eta^{24} \cdot \eta^2 = \eta^{26}$, matching the Mukai-plus-elliptic-lattice denominator\textup{);} against Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:beauville-kappa-formula}} \textup{(}$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times E) = 2 + 1 = 3$\textup{);} and against the Oberdieck--Pixton DT/PT correspondence \cite{OberdieckPixton2019InventMath} which identifies the generating series of $K3 \times E$ DT invariants as the K3 Noether--Lefschetz series \textup{(}Jacobi form\textup{)} multiplied by the elliptic theta series on the product-stable sublocus.
\item $X = T^2 \times T^4$, $d_1 = 1, d_2 = 2$, $d = 3$: on the product-stable framed locus, $\Phi_3^{(T^4,T^2)}(T^2 \times T^4) \simeq \Phi_1(T^2) \boxtimes \Phi_2(T^4)$. Here $T^4$ is an abelian surface with $h^{1,0} = 2$, so $\Phi_2(T^4) = \cH_6$ \textup{(}Heisenberg on the Mukai lattice $H^*(T^4, \bZ)$ of rank $8$, reduced to rank $6$ after killing the $h^{1,0}$-direction; cf.\ Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}}\textup{).} The product gives $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(T^2 \times T^4) = 1 + 2 = 3$ \textup{(}matching the Beauville table, Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:beauville-kappa-formula}}\textup{).}
\item $X = E \times E \times E = T^6$, $d_1 = d_2 = d_3 = 1$, $d = 3$: iterating \eqref{eq:phi-kunneth-formula} on the product-stable framed locus gives $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(T^6) \simeq \Phi_1(E) \boxtimes \Phi_1(E) \boxtimes \Phi_1(E) = H_1^{\boxtimes 3}$. It yields $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(T^6) = 1 + 1 + 1 = 3$, matching Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:beauville-kappa-formula}} entry $T^6$.
\end{enumerate}

\textit{Non-example} \textup{(}failure of K\"unneth\textup{).} For a non-split Kodaira--Thurston manifold $X = \mathrm{KT}^3$ \textup{(}compact three-dimensional non-K\"ahler symplectic manifold with non-product complex structure\textup{),} hypothesis~\textup{\ref{kunneth:H1}} fails: the complex structure does not split as a product of lower-dimensional CY complex structures. Accordingly, no tagged $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(\mathrm{KT}^3)$ obtained from this product theorem admits a K\"unneth factorisation; any chiral image would contain BCOV mixing terms corresponding to the nontrivial Nijenhuis tensor.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Bondal--Orlov equivalence $D^b(\Coh(X_1 \times X_2)) \simeq D^b(\Coh(X_1)) \boxtimes D^b(\Coh(X_2))$ induces the K\"unneth decomposition on Hochschild homology:
\[
 \HH_\bullet(\cC) \;\cong\; \HH_\bullet(\cC_1) \otimes \HH_\bullet(\cC_2),
\]
and the cyclic $\Ainf$-structure splits as the external product of cyclic $\Ainf$-structures on the factors \textup{(}Kontsevich--Vlassopoulos \cite{KontsevichVlassopoulos2022} \S 4\textup{).} By hypothesis~\ref{kunneth:H1}, the $\bS^d$-framing is the external product $\bS^{d_1} \boxtimes \bS^{d_2}$.

The four-step construction of $\Phi_d$ \textup{(}Section~\textup{\ref{sec:cyclic-to-chiral}}\textup{)} applied factorwise yields:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
\item \emph{Gerstenhaber bracket:} splits as $\{\cdot, \cdot\}_{\HH^\bullet(\cC)} = \{\cdot, \cdot\}_{\HH^\bullet(\cC_1)} \otimes 1 + 1 \otimes \{\cdot, \cdot\}_{\HH^\bullet(\cC_2)}$ by K\"unneth for Hochschild cohomology.
\item \emph{Factorisation envelope:} $\Fact_X(\mathfrak{L}_{\cC_1} \oplus \mathfrak{L}_{\cC_2}) \simeq \Fact_X(\mathfrak{L}_{\cC_1}) \otimes_{\Ran} \Fact_X(\mathfrak{L}_{\cC_2})$ by Francis--Gaitsgory \cite{FrancisGaitsgory2012} Proposition~3.2.5.
\item \emph{$E_n$-enhancement:} the $\bS^d$-framing splits by hypothesis~\ref{kunneth:H1}; Dunn additivity \textup{(}Lurie \cite[\textsection 5.1]{LurieHA}\textup{)} identifies $E_{n(d_1+d_2)}$ via $E_{n_1} \otimes_{\mathrm{Dunn}} E_{n_2} \simeq E_{n_1+n_2}$, which at the level of the CY-to-chiral target gives the correct $n(d)$ by the Gerstenhaber-bracket-degree calculation $1 - d = (1 - d_1) + (1 - d_2) - 1$.
\item \emph{Quantisation:} the CY trace on $\HH_d(\cC) \cong \HH_{d_1}(\cC_1) \otimes \HH_{d_2}(\cC_2)$ is the external product of traces. By hypothesis~\ref{kunneth:H2}, the one-loop anomaly cocycle factors additively, so quantisation commutes with the external product.
\end{enumerate}

The resulting $\Phi_d(\cC)$ equals $\Phi_{d_1}(\cC_1) \boxtimes \Phi_{d_2}(\cC_2)$ as $E_{n(d)}$-chiral algebras, up to the quasi-isomorphism induced by the canonical $A_\infty$-gauge on the external product. Uniqueness follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-platonic}: both sides satisfy (U1)--(U4) for the input $\cC_1 \boxtimes \cC_2$, so they are quasi-isomorphic.

The product-stability hypothesis~\ref{kunneth:H3} is invoked only when reading the chiral algebra against the Bridgeland stability manifold; on the product-stable sublocus $\StabOp(X_1) \times \StabOp(X_2)$ the slicings of the factors induce a slicing on the product, and the Harder--Narasimhan filtration of $\Phi_d(\cC)$ factors accordingly. Off this sublocus, the K\"unneth factorisation holds as an abstract quasi-isomorphism but the Bridgeland-slicing witness is lost.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Subscript discipline on split products]
\label{rem:phi-subscript-discipline}
Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product} resolves the subscript ambiguity: on a product $X = X_1 \times X_2$ with $\dim_\C X_i = d_i$, the CY-to-chiral functor applied to the \emph{full} product category is $\Phi_{d_1+d_2}$, not $\Phi_{d_i}$. The naive reading $\Phi_{d_i}(X_1 \times X_2)$ is a type error. When the product splits \textup{(}hypotheses~\ref{kunneth:H1}--\ref{kunneth:H2}\textup{),} $\Phi_{d_1+d_2}$ decomposes as $\Phi_{d_1} \boxtimes \Phi_{d_2}$; this is the only legitimate way to see lower-$d$ factors of $\Phi$ on a higher-dimensional product.

In particular: $\Phi_3^{(K3,E)}(K3 \times E) = \Phi_2(K3) \boxtimes \Phi_1(E)$ on the split framed locus, and at $d = 3$ with $E_1$-chiral target. The expression $\Phi_2(K3 \times E)$ is \emph{ill-typed} and should be read as shorthand for the tagged $\Phi_3$ product-stable branch when context forces it. Similarly, on $K3 \times K3$ \textup{(}a CY$_4$\textup{),} the correct functor is $\Phi_4$; the decomposition $\Phi_4(K3 \times K3) = \Phi_2(K3) \boxtimes \Phi_2(K3)$ holds on the product-stable sublocus of $\StabOp(K3 \times K3)$.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Explicit \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_4(K3 \times K3)$}{Phi-4(K3 x K3)} via K\"unneth]
\label{thm:phi-4-k3-k3-kunneth-explicit}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}\textup{ (K\"unneth factorisation; $K3$-factor identification)}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}\textup{ (pointwise $\bS^4$-framing: $[p_1(T_X)] \neq 0$, Corollary~\ref{cor:phi-4-p1-vanishing-stratum})}
\index{CY-to-chiral functor!$\Phi_4$ on $K3 \times K3$}%
\index{K3 $\times$ K3!explicit chiral image}%
Let $X = K3 \times K3$ be the product of two projective K$3$ surfaces with independent Ricci-flat Calabi--Yau metrics, and $\cC = D^b(\Coh(X))$. On the product-stable sublocus $\StabOp(K3) \times \StabOp(K3) \hookrightarrow \StabOp(K3 \times K3)$, the CY-to-chiral functor at $d = 4$ factorises as an $E_1$-chiral algebra on $\Ran(\Sigma)$:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:phi-4-k3-k3-explicit}
 \boxed{\;\;
 \Phi_4(K3 \times K3) \;\simeq\; \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}(K3) \;\boxtimes\; \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}(K3),
 \;\;}
\end{equation}
where each factor $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}(K3) = \Phi_2(K3)$ is the rank-$24$ Mukai--Heisenberg chiral algebra on the Mukai lattice $H^\bullet(K3, \Z)$ with pairing $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ of signature $(4, 20)$ \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}}\textup{).} Equivalently, reading against the BKM-lattice data of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:k3-cy2-serre-cocycle}},
\[
 \Phi_4(K3 \times K3) \;\simeq\; \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5} \boxtimes \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}
\]
where $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5} = \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ denotes the $K3$ chiral bialgebra equipped with its $\widetilde{M}_{24}$-Schur-cover and order-$6$ Serre cocycle. The external factorisation tensor product $\boxtimes$ is Francis--Gaitsgory \cite{FrancisGaitsgory2012} \S$3$, and the equivalence is a quasi-isomorphism of $E_1$-chiral algebras on the factorisation stack over $\Ran(\Sigma)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Both factors of $X = K3 \times K3$ are strict compact K\"ahler CY$_2$ manifolds admitting independent Ricci-flat metrics (Yau \cite{Yau1978}); the BCOV anomaly cocycle is absent at $d \le 2$, so hypotheses~\ref{kunneth:H1}--\ref{kunneth:H2} of Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product}} hold unconditionally. Hypothesis~\ref{kunneth:H3} is witnessed on the product-stable sublocus $\StabOp(K3) \times \StabOp(K3)$ by Bayer--Macr\`i--Stellari \cite{BayerMacriStellari2016}, where the slicings of the two K$3$ factors induce a compatible slicing on the product. Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product}} with $d_1 = d_2 = 2$ gives the factorisation~\eqref{eq:phi-4-k3-k3-explicit} at the level of factorisation coalgebras. The target operadic level is $n(d) = 1$ by $d = 4 \ge 3$; the Dunn-additivity identification $E_{n_1} \otimes_{\mathrm{Dunn}} E_{n_2} \simeq E_{n_1 + n_2}$ with $n_1 = n_2 = 2$ and the Gerstenhaber-bracket-degree calculation $1 - d = (1 - 2) + (1 - 2) - 1 = -3$ at $d = 4$ are consistent. Identification of each factor with $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}(K3)$ is Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Five verification paths for \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_4(K3 \times K3)$}{Phi-4(K3 x K3)}]
\label{rem:phi-4-k3-k3-verification}
\index{multi-path verification!$\Phi_4(K3 \times K3)$}%
The identification~\eqref{eq:phi-4-k3-k3-explicit} is verified against five independent paths; each clarifies a different invariant and distinguishes the product CY$_4$ from the non-product sextic $X_6 \subset \bP^5$.

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(V\arabic*)}]
\item \emph{Hodge supertrace \textup{(}K\"unneth-multiplicative\textup{).}} By Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}}, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(X) = \Xi(X) = \sum_q (-1)^q h^{0,q}(X)$. For $X = K3$, $(h^{0,0}, h^{0,1}, h^{0,2}) = (1, 0, 1)$ gives $\Xi(K3) = 2$. K\"unneth multiplicativity of $\chi(\cO_{-})$ by Hirzebruch--Riemann--Roch yields $\Xi(K3 \times K3) = 2 \cdot 2 = 4$, equal to $\chi(\cO_{K3 \times K3})$. The categorical invariant $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times K3) = \chi(\cO_{K3 \times K3}) = 4$. Warning: the naive quotient $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3 \times K3)/24 = 576/24 = 24$ is the $d = 3$ MNOP-style MacMahon exponent, \emph{not} the native $d = 4$ Hodge supertrace; it labels a distinct topological invariant that coincides numerically with $\dim_\C H^\bullet(K3) = 24$ by an unrelated K3-specific identity.

\item \emph{Heisenberg-level rank-additive invariant.} The Heisenberg-level invariant $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$ of Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:beauville-kappa-formula}} is rank-additive across product-stable factorisations: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3) = \mathrm{rk}(\mathrm{Muk}(K3)) = 24$ is the rank of the Mukai lattice, and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times K3) = 24 + 24 = 48$ is the rank of the Heisenberg on the K\"unneth-summed Mukai lattice $\mathrm{Muk}(K3) \oplus \mathrm{Muk}(K3)$. The bar Euler product $\chi(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}} \boxtimes \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}; q) = \eta(q)^{-48}$ follows from the two independent $\eta^{-24}$ factors, supplying a generating-function cross-check.

\item \emph{Bondal--Orlov factorisation of $D^b(\Coh(K3 \times K3))$.} The Bondal--Orlov equivalence $D^b(\Coh(K3 \times K3)) \simeq D^b(\Coh(K3)) \boxtimes D^b(\Coh(K3))$ induces the Hochschild factorisation $\HH_\bullet(\cC) \cong \HH_\bullet(\cC_1) \otimes \HH_\bullet(\cC_2)$, and the cyclic $A_\infty$-structure splits as the external product of cyclic $A_\infty$-structures on each K$3$ factor (Kontsevich--Vlassopoulos \cite{KontsevichVlassopoulos2022} \S$4$). The four-step cyclic-to-chiral passage of Section~\textup{\ref{sec:cyclic-to-chiral}} applied factorwise reproduces~\eqref{eq:phi-4-k3-k3-explicit}.

\item \emph{Sextic comparison \textup{(}non-K\"unneth stratum\textup{).}} The smooth sextic $X_6 \subset \bP^5$ is \emph{not} a product: hypothesis~\ref{kunneth:H1} fails, and Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product}} does not apply. The $\mathbb{P}^1$-family $\Phi_4(X_6)$ of Construction~\textup{\ref{constr:phi-4-p1-family}} is irreducible and non-decomposable, with Hodge data $(h^{1,1}, h^{3,1}, h^{2,2}, h^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}) = (1, 426, 1752, 1750)$ and central charges $c = 2606$ at the $\sigma_4 \to 0$ limit and $c = 3466$ at the $\sigma_3 \to 0$ limit (Example~\textup{\ref{ex:sextic-cy4-associator}}). The Hodge supertrace $\Xi(X_6) = 1 - 0 + 0 - 0 + 1 = 2 \neq 4$ distinguishes sextic from $K3 \times K3$ at the $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ level. Topological contrast: $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3 \times K3) = 24^2 = 576$ (K\"unneth-multiplicative); $N(X_6) = 5400$ vs.\ $N(K3 \times K3) = 4608$ (Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:cy4-pontryagin-number-stratum}}), both off the $N = 0$ stratum. The sextic carries an irreducible chiral image; $K3 \times K3$ splits.

\item \emph{Hilbert-scheme comparison $K3^{[2]} \times K3^{[2]}$.} The product of two Hilbert squares is a different projective CY$_4$: each factor $K3^{[2]}$ is a $4$-dimensional hyperk\"ahler (real dimension $8$), and $K3^{[2]} \times K3^{[2]}$ is an $8$-complex-dimensional product. Applying Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product}} at $d_1 = d_2 = 4$ gives $\Phi_8(K3^{[2]} \times K3^{[2]}) \simeq \Phi_4(K3^{[2]}) \boxtimes \Phi_4(K3^{[2]})$ on the product-stable sublocus. The Markman framework (\cite{Markman2010IMRN}, IMRN 2010, Thm $1.2$) identifies the tautological class on $K3^{[2]}$ via the Nakajima vertex-operator lift of the K3 Heisenberg (Nakajima \cite{Nakajima1999}, Ann.\ of Math.\ 145 (1997), \S$9.3$); the G\"ottsche generating series (G\"ottsche \cite{Gottsche1990}) at partition $\pi = (1,1)$ of $n = 2$ produces the leading stratum $\Sym^2 \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ within $\Phi_4(K3^{[2]})$. The external product factorisation~\eqref{eq:phi-4-k3-k3-explicit} sits inside the $(\pi, \pi') = ((1,1), (1,1))$ top-codimension stratum of $\Phi_8(K3^{[2]} \times K3^{[2]})$, after modding out the $\Sigma_2 \times \Sigma_2$ Nakajima symmetrisation, consistent with Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-8-hilb4-k3}} specialised at $K3^{[4]}$ partition $\pi = (1,1,1,1)$.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent\textit{Framing status.} Corollary~\textup{\ref{cor:phi-4-p1-vanishing-stratum}} records $[p_1(T_{K3 \times K3})] = \sum_{i=1}^{2} \pi_i^*[p_1(K3)] \neq 0$ with $\int p_1 = -96$ and $N(K3 \times K3) = 4608$, placing $K3 \times K3$ off the $\bS^4$-framed stratum. The pointwise identification~\eqref{eq:phi-4-k3-k3-explicit} therefore holds as a $\mathbb{P}^1$-family identification of $E_1$-chiral algebras carrying a canonical $\Z$-shifted symplectic structure of integer level $\ell(X, \alpha) = \int_X p_1(T_X) \cup \alpha$ for $\alpha \in H^4(X; \Z)$ (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-4-eff-p1-vanishing}}), rather than as a single pointwise $\bS^4$-framed section. The K\"unneth equivalence is compatible with the $\mathbb{P}^1$-family: $\Phi_4(K3 \times K3) \to \bP^1_{(\sigma_3:\sigma_4)}$ factors as the fibrewise external product of the two $\Phi_2(K3) \to \mathrm{pt}$ fibrations (each $d = 2$ base is a point), and the $\bS^4$-obstruction class pulls back additively across the two $K3$ factors.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Associator vanishing at \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times K3$}{K3 x K3}: iteration-shadow prediction]
\label{rem:phi-4-k3-k3-assoc-vanishing}
\index{associator!$K3 \times K3$ vanishing}%
Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:cy4-p1-family-associator-sextic}} predicts $\Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}(K3 \times K3; \tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3) = 0$ because $\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}}^{(4)}(K3 \times K3) = \int_{K3 \times K3} H^4 = 0$ on the Mukai-polarised product (neither K$3$ factor inherits an ample hyperplane class from a single $\bP^n$; falsification test \textup{(T2)} of Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:cy4-associator-falsification}}). The K\"unneth decomposition~\eqref{eq:phi-4-k3-k3-explicit} makes this structurally transparent: the iterated-product associator, measuring the failure of associativity in the triple chiral tensor product, vanishes whenever both K$3$-factor BCOV Yukawa quartics vanish independently, which they do by the $d = 2$ absence of BCOV corrections. A non-zero measurement of $\Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}(K3 \times K3)$ in an independent computation would falsify both Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:cy4-p1-family-associator-sextic}} and the K\"unneth factorisation~\eqref{eq:phi-4-k3-k3-explicit}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Four invariant readings on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times K3$}{K3 x K3}: subscript discipline]
\label{rem:phi-4-k3-k3-kappa-quartet}
\index{kappa-subscript discipline!$K3 \times K3$}%
\index{$K3 \times K3$!four kappa-subscript readings}%
The K\"unneth image $\Phi_4(K3 \times K3) \simeq \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}} \boxtimes \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ carries four numerically distinct kappa-subscript readings, each attached to a different construction. An unsubscripted kappa on the product CY$_4$ is therefore forbidden; every instance must carry its subscript:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(K\arabic*)}]
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3 \times K3) = \Xi(K3 \times K3) = 2 \cdot 2 = 4$ \textup{(}Hodge supertrace, K\"unneth-multiplicative via HRR; Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}}\textup{).}
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times K3) = \chi(\cO_{K3 \times K3}) = 4$ \textup{(}categorical Euler characteristic; coincides with $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ at $d = 2$ per Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}} and propagates K\"unneth-multiplicatively under products of $d = 2$ factors\textup{).}
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times K3) = 24 + 24 = 48$ \textup{(}Heisenberg-level rank-additive invariant on $\mathrm{Muk}(K3) \oplus \mathrm{Muk}(K3)$; generator-rank counting on $\HH^{\bullet+1}$; Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:beauville-kappa-formula}}\textup{).}
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}(K3 \times K3) = \dim_\bC H^\bullet(K3 \times K3, \bC) = 24 \cdot 24 = 576$ \textup{(}total Mukai--Hodge rank, all types; manifold invariant, coincides with $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}$\textup{).} The $K3$-factor value $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}(K3) = 24$ is the per-factor lattice rank; its square realises K\"unneth on unsigned totals.
\end{enumerate}
The four readings decouple along the additive-vs-multiplicative axis of Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:cy-kappa-evidence}}: $(\mathrm{K}1)$ and $(\mathrm{K}2)$ are K\"unneth-multiplicative; $(\mathrm{K}3)$ is rank-additive; $(\mathrm{K}4)$ is K\"unneth-multiplicative on totals. None of these four equals the MacMahon quotient $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24 = 576/24 = 24$; that quotient is the $d = 3$ BCOV reading (Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:cy-kappa-identification}}), not a $d = 4$ reading, and its numerical coincidence with the per-factor $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}(K3) = 24$ is a K$3$-specific accident ($\dim H^\bullet(K3) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3)$).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Sextic \texorpdfstring{$X_6 \subset \bP^5$}{X-6 subset P-5} vs.\ quintic \texorpdfstring{$X_5 \subset \bP^4$}{X-5 subset P-4}: do not conflate]
\label{rem:phi-4-sextic-vs-quintic}
\index{sextic CY$_4$!vs.\ quintic CY$_3$}%
The sextic $X_6 \subset \bP^5$ is a smooth CY$_4$ of complex dimension $4$, not $3$. Its Hodge supertrace is $\Xi(X_6) = 1 - 0 + 0 - 0 + 1 = 2$ (middle Hodge numbers $h^{0,q}$ for $1 \le q \le 3$ vanish on a smooth Fermat-type sextic CY$_4$). The quintic $X_5 \subset \bP^4$ is a CY$_3$ with $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(X_5) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X_5)/24 = -200/24 = -25/3$ (BCOV branch of Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:beauville-kappa-formula}}). The sextic and quintic are different manifolds in different dimensions; the numerical value $-25/3$ belongs to the CY$_3$ quintic, \emph{not} the CY$_4$ sextic. The sextic's modular characteristic is computed via the $\bP^1$-family $\Phi_4(X_6)$ of Construction~\textup{\ref{constr:phi-4-p1-family}}, with central charges $c = 2606, 3466$ at the two $\bP^1$-family limits (Example~\textup{\ref{ex:sextic-cy4-associator}}). The distinction between $K3 \times K3$ (K\"unneth-decomposable CY$_4$) and $X_6$ (irreducible CY$_4$, non-decomposable) is governed by hypothesis~\ref{kunneth:H1} of Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product}}, which fails for $X_6$ (Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:phi-4-k3-k3-verification}}~(V4)).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Markman cross-check: \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_4(K3 \times K3)$}{Phi-4(K3 x K3)} vs.\ \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_8(K3^{[4]})$}{Phi-8(K3-[4])}]
\label{rem:phi-4-k3-k3-markman-phi8}
\index{Markman!$K3 \times K3$ vs.\ $K3^{[4]}$}%
The Hilbert scheme $K3^{[4]}$ and the product $K3 \times K3$ are distinct CY inputs at different dimensions: $K3^{[4]}$ is an $8$-complex-dimensional irreducible hyperk\"ahler of $K3^{[4]}$-deformation type, landing in $\Phi_8$ (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-8-hilb4-k3}}), while $K3 \times K3$ is a $4$-complex-dimensional product CY$_4$, landing in $\Phi_4$ (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-4-k3-k3-kunneth-explicit}}). The comparison proceeds through the intermediate $K3^{[2]} \times K3^{[2]}$: Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product}} at $d_1 = d_2 = 4$ yields $\Phi_8(K3^{[2]} \times K3^{[2]}) \simeq \Phi_4(K3^{[2]}) \boxtimes \Phi_4(K3^{[2]})$ on the product-stable sublocus. The Nakajima--G\"ottsche partition stratification identifies the top-codimension stratum $\pi = (1,1)$ of $\Phi_4(K3^{[2]})$ as $\Sym^2 \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ after $\Sigma_2$-symmetrisation (Nakajima \cite{Nakajima1999} \S$9.3$); the Markman tautological class \cite{Markman2010IMRN} Thm $1.2$ witnesses the Beauville--Bogomolov form on each $K3^{[2]}$ factor. The K\"unneth factorisation~\eqref{eq:phi-4-k3-k3-explicit} sits inside the $(\pi, \pi') = ((1,1), (1,1))$ stratum of $\Phi_8(K3^{[2]} \times K3^{[2]})$ after the $\Sigma_2 \times \Sigma_2$ quotient, matching Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-8-hilb4-k3}} specialised to $K3^{[4]}$ partition $\pi = (1,1,1,1)$. Numerical cross-check via $\chi(\cO_{-})$ as K\"unneth-multiplicative/partition-stratified invariant:
\begin{align*}
\chi(\cO_{K3 \times K3}) &= 2 \cdot 2 = 4 & &\text{(product CY$_4$, K\"unneth)}, \\
\chi(\cO_{K3^{[2]}}) &= 3 & &\text{(G\"ottsche \cite{Gottsche1990})}, \\
\chi(\cO_{K3^{[2]} \times K3^{[2]}}) &= 3 \cdot 3 = 9 & &\text{(product of hyperk\"ahler fourfolds)}, \\
\chi(\cO_{K3^{[4]}}) &= 5 & &\text{(G\"ottsche; Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-8-hilb4-k3}})}.
\end{align*}
The four values $\{4, 5, 9, 3\}$ are pairwise distinct, certifying that $\Phi_4(K3 \times K3)$, $\Phi_8(K3^{[4]})$, $\Phi_8(K3^{[2]} \times K3^{[2]})$, and $\Phi_4(K3^{[2]})$ are four inequivalent chiral algebras, none conflatable with any other via K\"unneth restricted to product-stable loci.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[CY modular characteristic at \texorpdfstring{$d = 3$}{d = 3}: dimension-stratified formula]
\label{conj:cy-kappa-identification}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
For $\cC$ a smooth proper CY$_3$ category satisfying the framed object-level hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}, and after choosing a witnessed admissible specialisation datum $(\Sigma_2,C)$, write $A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)}=\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(\cC)$ when the construction is available. The construction-native modular characteristic is conjectured to satisfy the following dimension-stratified formula:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item For compact CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0}(X) = 0$: the curved BCOV branch has $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{curved}}(A_\cC) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X) / 24$.
 \item For product CY$_3$ of the form $S \times E$: the Heisenberg reduction has $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(A_\cC) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_S) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(A_E)$ \textup{(}rank additivity\textup{)}.
 \item For local surfaces $\mathrm{Tot}(K_S \to S)$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S) / 2$ \textup{(}MNOP degree-zero\textup{)}.
\end{enumerate}
In particular, construction-native $d=3$ branches need not equal $\chi(\cO_X) = \kcat(X)$. For every strict compact CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$, the holomorphic Euler characteristic $\chi(\cO_X) = \sum_{q=0}^{3} (-1)^q h^{0,q} = 1 - 0 + 0 - 1 = 0$, while the BCOV branch can carry a nonzero curved chiral invariant, for example $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{curved}}(\text{quintic}) = -25/3$. For $K3 \times E$ the compact total-space Hodge supertrace is instead $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_{K3 \times E}) = \sum_q(-1)^q h^{0,q}(K3 \times E) = 0$, while the Stage-2 Heisenberg--Cartan reduction has the separate value $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times E) = 3$ (Remark~\ref{rem:beauville-kappa-formula-subscript-split}). The identification $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \kcat = \chi(\cO_X)$ is proved for the class-A $d = 2$ locus (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}}); outside that locus every equality must specify the native construction.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Why the Heisenberg rank need not equal \texorpdfstring{$\chi(\cO_X)$}{chi(O-X)}]
\label{rem:cy-kappa-evidence}
The root cause is a clash between two structural properties:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$ is \emph{rank-additive} under the Heisenberg specialisation: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(A_{X \times Y}) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(A_X) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(A_Y)$ whenever the specialisation is the chiral-de~Rham/free-boson rank reading.
 \item $\chi(\cO_X)$ is \emph{multiplicative} under products: $\chi(\cO_{X \times Y}) = \chi(\cO_X) \cdot \chi(\cO_Y)$ (K\"unneth).
\end{itemize}
These are compatible for simply connected CY$_2$ manifolds with
$h^{1,0} = 0$ (where both give $\chi(\cO_X)$ by
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}}), but separate for abelian
surfaces ($h^{1,0} \neq 0$) and under products with $d = 1$ factors. For
$K3 \times E$, the Heisenberg--Cartan reading gives
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times E) = 2 + 1 = 3$
(Beauville-additive). The compact Hodge supertrace gives
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_{K3 \times E})=0$; the categorical Euler
$\kcat(K3 \times E)$ and $\chi(\cO_{K3 \times E})$ have the same value
by K\"unneth multiplicativity, $2 \cdot 0 = 0$. Already at $d = 1$:
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_E)=0$ as a compact Hodge supertrace, while
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(E) = 1$ is the free-boson rank.

The construction-dependent correction $\delta\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{branch}} := \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{branch}} - \chi(\cO_X)$ is controlled by the branch:
\begin{itemize}
 \item Heisenberg branch: $\delta\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$ is the free-boson rank contribution from surviving $h^{1,0}$ directions.
 \item Class-A compact Hodge-supertrace branch: $\delta\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$ by definition of the compact trace $\sum_q(-1)^q h^{0,q}$.
 \item Curved BCOV branch at $d = 3$, compact $h^{1,0} = 0$: $\delta\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{curved}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ when the BCOV anomaly construction applies. The $\bS^3$-framing introduces a correction that Serre duality $\mathbb{S}_\cC \simeq [3]$ does not cancel.
\end{itemize}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 69 tests in \texttt{kappa\_ch\_d3\_formula.py} covering the CY-D refutation, the dimension-stratified formula, additivity vs.\ multiplicativity, and the quantum correction analysis for 13 standard CY manifolds.
\end{remark}


\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}: total-space \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$}{kappa-ch = 0}, Heisenberg \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}} = 3$}{kappa-ch-Heis = 3}]
\label{rem:beauville-kappa-formula-subscript-split}
The compact total-space chiral Hodge supertrace on $X = K3 \times E$ is
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_{K3 \times E})
 = \sum_q(-1)^q h^{0,q}(K3 \times E)
 = (1-1)(1+1)
 = 0.
\]
The K\"unneth-multiplicative Hirzebruch--Riemann--Roch reading $\Xi(K3) \cdot \Xi(E) = 2 \cdot 0 = 0$ is the categorical invariant
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3 \times E) \;=\; \chi(\cO_{K3 \times E}) \;=\; 0,
\]
a Tier-(i) CY datum intrinsic (Remark~\ref{rem:four-kappa-stage-assignment}). The Stage-2 reduction used in the $\Delta_5$ presentation is a different construction: the Heisenberg--Cartan rank of the $E_1$-chiral shadow $\SpCh_{K3, E}(\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{Perf}(X)))$ on the reference curve $C = E$. Three surviving Mukai modes assemble into the rank-$3$ chiral-Heisenberg Cartan of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}), hence
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times E) = 2 + 1 = 3.
\]
The Borcherds weight is again separate: $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=5$.

\emph{Qualifier discipline.} When the rank-additive Heisenberg-level sum across the Beauville--Bogomolov decomposition is named in its own right; $\mathrm{rk}$-sum of Heisenberg levels of chiral de Rham / free-boson factors; we write $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$. It is not the compact total-space Hodge supertrace $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_{K3 \times E})$ and it is not the automorphic weight $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$.
\end{remark}


\begin{proposition}[Beauville reduction formula for \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$}{kappa-ch-Heis} at \texorpdfstring{$d = 3$}{d = 3}]
\label{prop:beauville-kappa-formula}
\ClaimStatusConditional{Product branch proved; strict compact BCOV branch conjectural.}
For a \emph{compact} CY$_3$ manifold $X$, the Heisenberg-level modular characteristic (cf.\ Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:beauville-kappa-formula-subscript-split}}) is recorded by the branch formula
\begin{equation}\label{eq:kappa-beauville}
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(X)
 \;=\;
 \frac{\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)}{24}
 \;+\;
 \bigl[\, h^{1,0}(X) > 0 \,\bigr]
 \cdot
 \bigl(\, h^{3,0}(X) + 2 \,\bigr),
\end{equation}
where $[\,\cdot\,]$ denotes the Iverson bracket \textup{(}$1$ if the condition holds, $0$ otherwise\textup{)}.

The formula unifies two branches:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Strict CY$_3$} ($h^{1,0} = 0$, $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ holonomy): $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ \textup{(}BCOV holomorphic anomaly, \textsc{conjectural}\textup{)}.
 \item \emph{Product CY$_3$} ($h^{1,0} > 0$): $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}} = h^{3,0}(X) + 2$ \textup{(}\textsc{proved} by additivity and the $d \leq 2$ result\textup{)}.
\end{enumerate}

The mechanism: by the Beauville--Bogomolov decomposition theorem, every compact K\"ahler manifold with $c_1 = 0$ decomposes \textup{(}up to finite \'etale cover\textup{)} into irreducible factors. For $\dim_\C = 3$, the allowed decomposition types are:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{tabular}{lccccl}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Type} & $h^{1,0}$ & $h^{3,0}$ & \textbf{Factors} & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$ & \textbf{Status} \\
 \midrule
 Strict CY$_3$ & $0$ & $1$ & irreducible & $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ & Conjectural \\
 $K3 \times E$ & $1$ & $1$ & $K3 + E$ & $2 + 1 = 3$ & Proved \\
 $\mathrm{Enr} \times E$ & $1$ & $0$ & $\mathrm{Enr} + E$ & $1 + 1 = 2$ & Proved \\
 $T^6 = E^3$ & $3$ & $1$ & $E + E + E$ & $1 + 1 + 1 = 3$ & Proved \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
No other types exist: $h^{1,0} = 2$ is impossible for a compact CY$_3$ \textup{(}any CY$_2$ factor with $h^{1,0} = 1$ does not exist; K3 has $h^{1,0} = 0$, abelian surfaces have $h^{1,0} = 2$ and decompose further\textup{)}.

In the product branch ($h^{1,0} > 0$), $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X) = 0$ since $X$ contains an elliptic curve factor with $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(E) = 0$. Thus the two summands in~\eqref{eq:kappa-beauville} have disjoint support.

\smallskip
\noindent\textit{Proof of the product branch.}
On the product-stable Stage-$2$ Heisenberg locus, the specialisation of $D^b(X \times Y)$ is the tensor product of the specialisations of the factors. By Theorem~D \textup{(}Vol~I\textup{)}, the Heisenberg-level invariant $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$ is additive under tensor products (this is the rank-additivity of Heisenberg levels summed over free-boson factors; it is NOT Route-A Hodge-supertrace multiplicativity, cf.\ Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:beauville-kappa-formula-subscript-split}}). For $K3 \times E$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}} = \chi(\cO_{K3}) + h^{1,0}(E) = 2 + 1 = 3$ \textup{(}using Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}} for K3 at $d = 2$ where Route A and Route B coincide\textup{)}. For Enriques $\times\, E$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}} = \chi(\cO_{\mathrm{Enr}}) + 1 = 1 + 1 = 2$. For $T^6 = E^3$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}} = 3 \cdot 1 = 3$.

The Beauville formula $h^{3,0}(X) + 2$ encodes additivity: for
$S \times E$ with $h^{1,0}(S) = 0$, we have
$h^{3,0}(S \times E) = h^{2,0}(S) \cdot h^{1,0}(E) = h^{2,0}(S)$.
The Heisenberg reading on $S$ has value
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(S) = 1 + h^{2,0}(S)$, hence the
product reading gives
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(S \times E) = h^{3,0}(X) + 2$.

\smallskip
\noindent\textit{Consistency check.} For the abelian surface,
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(T^4) = 2$: the two elliptic
factors contribute one free-boson rank each. By contrast,
$\chi(\cO_{T^4}) = 0$. Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}} is
restricted to $h^{1,0} = 0$; abelian surfaces violate this hypothesis.
Once the Heisenberg value on $T^4$ is fixed, the decomposition
$T^6 = T^4 \times E$ gives
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(T^6) = 2 + 1 = 3$, consistent
with $T^6 = E^3$ giving $3 \cdot 1 = 3$.

\smallskip
\noindent\textit{Verification}: 65 tests in \texttt{kappa\_ch\_universal\_formula.py} covering all 18 CY$_3$ families in the grand atlas, the Beauville decomposition, the abelian surface fix, and the $h^{1,0} = 2$ impossibility. Tests operate on the Heisenberg-level sum and therefore witness $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$, not Route-A $\Xi$.
\end{proposition}


\begin{remark}[Enriques surface: orbifold and \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch} halving]
\label{rem:enriques-orbifold-kappa}
\ClaimStatusHeuristic{}
An Enriques surface $S$ is not a strict $\CY_2$ input in the sense of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}}, because $\omega_S$ is nontrivial $2$-torsion and the Serre functor on $D^b(\Coh(S))$ is $(-)\otimes \omega_S[2]$, not $[2]$. It is still the first orbifold test case next to the K3 example of Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:cy-kappa-evidence}}. Let $\pi \colon X \to S$ be the universal cover, where $X$ is a K3 surface and the deck involution acts freely. Then $\chi(\cO_X) = 2$ and $\chi(\cO_S) = 1$, so the K3 value $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$ suggests the orbifold value $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$ if $\Phi$ extends to this torsion-canonical quotient surface.

Descent identifies $D^b(\Coh(S))$ with the $\mathbb{Z}/2$-equivariant derived category of the cover, so one expects an orbifold relation
\[
 \Phi\bigl(D^b(\Coh(S))\bigr) \,\simeq\, \Phi\bigl(D^b(\Coh(X))\bigr)^{\mathbb{Z}/2}
\]
for a suitable equivariant factorization-envelope construction. At the scalar level this predicts the halving principle
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A^G) \,=\, \frac{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)}{|G|},
\]
the chiral shadow of the classical formula $\chi(\cO_S) = \chi(\cO_X)/|G|$ for a free finite quotient. In the Vol~I Igusa normalization, this same orbifold picture suggests replacing the K3 lift $\Phi_{10}$ by a $\mathbb{Z}/2$-twisted lift of weight $5$ on the quotient side. That weight drop gives heuristic evidence for $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ halving on the Borcherds side, even though the precise Enriques automorphic normalization is a separate question.

Turning this into a theorem requires an orbifold extension of the correspondence programme $\{\Phi_d\}$, equivariant descent for factorization envelopes, and the twisted Borcherds-lift technology needed to compare the quotient with its K3 cover.
\end{remark}


\begin{conjecture}[\texorpdfstring{$\Phi$}{Phi} respects \texorpdfstring{$\bZ/2$}{Z/2}-orbifold structure for Enriques]
\label{conj:phi-enriques-z2-orbifold}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
Let $X$ be a K3 surface with a fixed-point-free involution $\iota$, and let $S = X/\iota$ be the associated Enriques surface. Suppose the correspondence $\Phi_2$ of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}} extends to an equivariant setting (i.e.\ to smooth proper categories with $\bZ/2$-action whose Serre functor is $2$-torsion rather than trivial). Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item There is an equivalence of chiral algebras
 \[
 \Phi\bigl(D^b(\Coh(S))\bigr) \;\simeq\; \Phi\bigl(D^b(\Coh(X))\bigr)^{\bZ/2},
 \]
 where the right-hand side denotes the $\iota^*$-invariant subalgebra of the K3 chiral algebra.
 \item The $\bZ/2$-invariant subalgebra inherits the structure of an $\Etwo$-chiral algebra from the ambient K3 algebra. The $\Eone$-bar complex satisfies
 \[
 B\bigl(\Phi(D^b(\Coh(S)))\bigr) \;\simeq\; B\bigl(\Phi(D^b(\Coh(X)))\bigr)^{\bZ/2}
 \]
 as factorization coalgebras.
 \item The full $\bZ/2$-orbifold chiral algebra $\Phi(D^b(\Coh(X))) \rtimes \bZ/2$ (including the twisted sector) carries a larger shadow tower whose invariant subalgebra recovers $\Phi(D^b(\Coh(S)))$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Evidence and scope for Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:phi-enriques-z2-orbifold}}]
\label{rem:enriques-z2-evidence}
\ClaimStatusHeuristic{}
Three lines of evidence support the conjecture.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
 \item \emph{Derived category descent.} The functor $\pi^* \colon D^b(\Coh(S)) \hookrightarrow D^b(\Coh(X))$ identifies $D^b(\Coh(S))$ with the $\iota^*$-invariant subcategory of $D^b(\Coh(X))$. (For a free action, this is the content of equivariant descent; see Bridgeland--King--Reid for the general framework.) If $\Phi$ commutes with taking $G$-invariants, item~(i) follows.
 \item \emph{Hochschild homology halving.} The Hochschild homology of the Enriques surface satisfies $\dim \HH_0(S) = 1$, $\dim \HH_1(S) = 0$, $\dim \HH_2(S) = 0$, giving $\chi^{\CY}(S) = 1 - 0 + 0 = 1$. (Here $\HH_2(S) = H^0(\omega_S) = 0$ because $\omega_S$ is nontrivial $2$-torsion; the torsion in $H^1(S, \bZ) = \bZ/2$ does not contribute to Hochschild homology over $\bC$.) This is half the K3 value $\chi^{\CY}(X) = 1 - 0 + 1 = 2$, consistent with the halving principle $\chi^{\CY}(S) = \chi^{\CY}(X)/|G|$ for the free $\bZ/2$-quotient. The precise relationship is: $\HH_\bullet(S) \simeq \HH_\bullet(X)^{\bZ/2}$ as graded vector spaces, where $\iota^*$ acts as $+1$ on $\HH_0(X) = H^0(\cO_X)$ and as $-1$ on $\HH_2(X) = H^0(\omega_X)$ (since $\iota^* \omega_X = -\omega_X$), so the invariant piece $\HH_2(X)^{\bZ/2} = 0$ recovers $\HH_2(S) = 0$.
 \item \emph{Chiral de Rham.} The chiral de Rham complex $\Omega^{\mathrm{ch}}$ is defined on any smooth variety. For the Enriques surface, $H^*(\Omega^{\mathrm{ch}}(S)) \simeq H^*(\Omega^{\mathrm{ch}}(X))^{\bZ/2}$ by functoriality of the chiral de Rham sheaf under \'etale morphisms. This gives the chiral-algebra-level analogue of item~(i) at the level of vertex algebra cohomology.
\end{enumerate}
The principal obstruction is Step~4 of the cyclic-to-chiral passage (Section~\ref{sec:cyclic-to-chiral}): the CY trace on $D^b(\Coh(S))$ is $2$-torsion, so the quantization requires a $\bZ/2$-equivariant refinement.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Enriques \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$}{kappa-BKM}-spectrum]
\label{conj:enriques-kappa-spectrum}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
Let $S$ be an Enriques surface, $X$ its K3 universal cover, $E$ an elliptic curve, and assume Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:phi-enriques-z2-orbifold}} and the extension of $\Phi$ to generalized CY$_3$ inputs. Then the three modular characteristics satisfy:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(S) = 1 = \chi(\cO_S)$, half the K3 value $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(X) = 2$.
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(S \times E) = 4$, the weight of the Allcock Borcherds product on $O(2, 10)$. This is verified computationally by \texttt{enriques\_shadow.py} (72 tests; see Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:enriques-kappa-anomaly}} in the bar-cobar bridge chapter).
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(S \times E) = \chi^{\CY}(D^b(\Coh(S \times E))) = \chi(\cO_{S \times E})$, the categorical Euler characteristic. By K\"unneth multiplicativity of $\chi(\cO_{-})$, this is $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(S \times E) = \chi(\cO_S) \cdot \chi(\cO_E) = 1 \cdot 0 = 0$.
\end{enumerate}
The ratio $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(X \times E) / \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(S \times E) = 5/4$ (not $2$) reflects the fact that $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ is the automorphic weight, which is sensitive to the full BPS spectrum across the fiber and not simply the $|G|$-fold quotient of the scalar invariant. The discrepancy $5/4 \neq 2$ is the \emph{Enriques $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$-anomaly}: the BKM weight does not halve under the $\bZ/2$ quotient because the Borcherds product on $O(2, 10)$ is not the restriction of the Igusa cusp form on $O(2, 18)$ but rather an independent automorphic form (the Allcock product) whose weight is determined by the Enriques lattice.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[BKM lattice structure: Enriques vs.\ K3]
\label{rem:enriques-bkm-lattice}
\ClaimStatusHeuristic{}
The lattice-theoretic origin of the $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$-anomaly is as follows.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
 \item The K3 lattice is $\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}} = U^3 \oplus E_8(-1)^2$, of rank $22$ and signature $(3, 19)$. The BKM algebra for $\mathrm{K3} \times E$ is constructed via the Borcherds lift on $O(2, 18)$ (the orthogonal complement of a hyperbolic plane in $\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}} \oplus U$), and $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = \mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5) = 5$.
 \item The Enriques lattice is $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Enr}} = U \oplus E_8(-1)$, of rank $10$ and signature $(1, 9)$. The involution $\iota$ acts on $H^2(X, \bZ)$ with invariant sublattice $U(2) \oplus E_8(-2)$ (the $\iota$-invariant part of $\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}}$, rescaled by the index-$2$ inclusion). The BKM algebra for $\mathrm{Enr} \times E$ is constructed via the Borcherds lift on $O(2, 10)$ (from $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Enr}} \oplus U$), and $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 4$ (the Allcock product weight).
 \item The embedding $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Enr}} \hookrightarrow \Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}}$ (the inclusion of the $\iota$-invariant sublattice, up to rescaling) induces a restriction map on automorphic forms. This restriction does \emph{not} send $\Delta_5$ on $O(2, 18)$ to the Allcock product on $O(2, 10)$: the two forms live on orthogonal groups of different rank, and the restriction involves a Fourier--Jacobi expansion along the complementary lattice $E_8(-1)$ that produces a form of weight $4$, not $5$. The weight drop by $1$ (from $5$ to $4$) reflects the $E_8(-1)$ theta-function contribution of weight $4$ in the Fourier--Jacobi coefficient.
 \item The BKM root systems differ accordingly. The K3 BKM superalgebra has root lattice containing $\Lambda_{\mathrm{K3}}$; the Enriques BKM superalgebra has root lattice containing $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Enr}}$. The real simple roots of the Enriques BKM algebra are the $(-2)$-vectors of $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Enr}}$, forming the Vinberg diagram of the Enriques automorphism group.
\end{enumerate}
The $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$-anomaly ($5/4$ rather than $2$) is therefore a lattice-theoretic phenomenon: the BKM weight is controlled by the rank and root structure of the relevant even lattice, not by the index of the covering map.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Enriques automorphic forms and moonshine]
\label{rem:enriques-automorphic}
\ClaimStatusHeuristic{}
The automorphic side of the Enriques construction involves the following objects.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
 \item The \emph{Allcock Borcherds product} is an automorphic form of weight $4$ on $O(2, 10)$ with known divisor (the Heegner divisors of norm $-2$ vectors in $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Enr}} \oplus U$). Its square root (a form of weight $2$ on a double cover) is related to the Enriques period map.
 \item Gritsenko and Nikulin constructed a ``Enriques modular form'' of weight $4$ on $O(2, 10)$ using a quasi-pullback of the Borcherds form $\Phi_{12}$ on $O(2, 26)$. This is the same object as the Allcock product, identified through different techniques.
 \item In the moonshine direction: the Mathieu group $M_{12}$ acts on the Enriques lattice (Mukai, Kondo), paralleling the $M_{24}$ action on the K3 lattice. The ``Enriques moonshine'' question asks whether the $\bZ/2$-twisted elliptic genus of the K3 sigma model (which computes the Enriques partition function) decomposes into $M_{12}$ representations in a manner analogous to Mathieu moonshine for K3. Gaberdiel--Hohenegger--Volpato have shown that the symmetry group of Enriques sigma models embeds in $O(10, \mathbb{F}_2)^+$, and partial decompositions into $M_{12}$ characters exist.
 \item From the $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$-spectrum perspective: the weight-$4$ Allcock product determines $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\mathrm{Enr} \times E) = 4$. If Enriques moonshine produces a weight-$4$ mock modular form (the twisted analogue of the K3 mock modular form $H(\tau)$ of weight $1/2$ that underlies Mathieu moonshine), the shadow of that form would encode the Enriques BKM denominator, connecting the automorphic weight to the chiral shadow tower via the general mechanism of Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:enriques-kappa-spectrum}}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}


\begin{proposition}[Kummer orbifold chiral algebra: Steps 1--4]
\label{prop:kummer-orbifold}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
Let $\cH_1$ denote the rank-$1$ Heisenberg factorization algebra on $\bC$, viewed as an
$\Einf$-algebra (the free Heisenberg is commutative at the $E_n$ level for all $n$;
the $\Einf$-structure is what permits iterated factorization homology on $T^4$).
Let $\iota \colon T^4 \to T^4$ denote the involution $x \mapsto -x$ with $16$ fixed
points $\{p_1, \ldots, p_{16}\} = (T^4)^{\bZ_2}$. The first four steps of the
Kummer route construct the singular orbifold chiral algebra $\cA^{\mathrm{orb}}$
using only proved technology (Ayala--Francis factorization homology and classical
orbifold VOA theory), independently of the CY-to-chiral correspondence $\Phi_2$ of
Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}}.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Step 1.} \emph{The torus integral: $4$-fold iterated Hochschild homology.}
Factorization homology on $T^4 = (S^1)^4$ computes as iterated circle integrals
(Ayala--Francis):
\[
 \int_{T^4} \cH_1 \;\simeq\; \HH(\HH(\HH(\HH(\cH_1)))).
\]
For the rank-$1$ Heisenberg $\cH_1$, the circle integral is
$\HH(\cH_1) \simeq \cH_1 \otimes \Omega^*(S^1)$, which as a graded vector space is
$\cH_1 \oplus \cH_1[-1]$. Iterating four times:
\[
 \int_{T^4} \cH_1 \;\simeq\; \cH_1 \otimes H^*(T^4, \bC) \;\simeq\;
 \cH_1 \otimes \bC^{16},
\]
a rank-$16$ Heisenberg with $\dim H^*(T^4) = \sum_{k=0}^4 \binom{4}{k} = 16$
generators graded by cohomological degree:
$1 + 4 + 6 + 4 + 1$ in degrees $0, 1, 2, 3, 4$.
The central charge is $c = 4$ (one per circle factor).
The character is $\prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^{-16}$.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Step 2.} \emph{The $\bZ_2$-action on Hochschild homology.}
The involution $\iota \colon x \mapsto -x$ on $T^4$ acts on $H^k(T^4, \bC)$ by
$(-1)^k$ (sign on odd cohomology, trivial on even).
The $\bZ_2$-invariant subspace of $H^*(T^4)$ is
$H^0 \oplus H^2 \oplus H^4 = \bC^{1+6+1} = \bC^8$.
The untwisted sector of the orbifold chiral algebra is therefore
\[
 \Bigl(\int_{T^4} \cH_1\Bigr)^{\bZ_2} \;\simeq\;
 \cH_1 \otimes H^{\mathrm{even}}(T^4, \bC) \;\simeq\; \cH_8,
\]
a rank-$8$ Heisenberg. Its character is $\prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^{-8}$
and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_8) = 0$ (abelian torus, $\chi(\cO_{T^4}) = 0$;
the orbifold has not yet resolved).

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Step 3.} \emph{The $\bZ_2$-twisted sectors at the $16$ fixed points.}
At each fixed point $p_i \in (T^4)^{\bZ_2}$, the tangent space $T_{p_i} T^4 \cong \bC^2$
carries the $\bZ_2$-action $v \mapsto -v$ (the $A_1$ singularity $\bC^2/\bZ_2$).
The twisted sector module $T_i$ at each fixed point is the $\bZ_2$-twisted
ground state of the Heisenberg: a highest-weight module with conformal weight
$h = 1/4$ per complex dimension, so $h = 2 \times (1/4) = 1/2$ from the
$\bC^2$ normal directions. At the level of characters, each twisted sector
contributes
\[
 \chi(T_i; q) \;=\; \frac{q^{1/2}}{\prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^2}
\]
(one $\bZ_2$-twisted Heisenberg per complex tangent direction).
The $16$ twisted sectors together contribute
\[
 16 \cdot \frac{q^{1/2}}{\prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^2}.
\]

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Step 4.} \emph{The orbifold chiral algebra before resolution.}
The full orbifold chiral algebra of $T^4/\bZ_2$ (before blowing up) is
\[
 \cA^{\mathrm{orb}} \;=\;
 \Bigl(\int_{T^4} \cH_1\Bigr)^{\bZ_2}
 \;\oplus\; \bigoplus_{i=1}^{16} T_i.
\]
Its full character is
\[
 \chi(\cA^{\mathrm{orb}}; q) \;=\;
 \frac{1}{\prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^8}
 \;+\; \frac{16\,q^{1/2}}{\prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^2}.
\]
This is the singular Kummer orbifold chiral algebra. Its modular
characteristic is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA^{\mathrm{orb}}) = 2$, already matching
the smooth K3 value $\chi(\cO_{K3}) = 2$. The justification:
$\chi(\cO_{T^4/\bZ_2})$ for the singular Kummer orbifold equals
$\chi(\cO_{K3})$ because the crepant resolution $\mathrm{Km}(T^4) \to T^4/\bZ_2$
does not change $\chi(\cO)$ (McKay correspondence for $A_1$: each
exceptional $\bC\bP^1$ contributes $\chi(\cO_{\bC\bP^1}) - \chi(\cO_{\mathrm{pt}}) = 1 - 1 = 0$
to the holomorphic Euler characteristic). At the chiral algebra level,
the $16$ twisted sectors carry $A_1$-type OPE singularities that shift
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ from the torus value $0$ to the K3 value $2$: specifically,
the enhanced symmetry at the orbifold point is $V_1(\mathfrak{so}_4)^{\oplus 4}$
(level-$1$ affine $\mathfrak{so}_4$, four copies; Route~3 of
Section~\ref{sec:k3e-six-routes}), and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ of this
nonabelian sector equals $4 \times \tfrac{1}{2} = 2$ (each $\mathfrak{so}_4
\cong \mathfrak{su}_2 \oplus \mathfrak{su}_2$ at level~$1$ contributes
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1/2$).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Step~1 is the Ayala--Francis K\"unneth theorem for factorization homology:
$\int_{(S^1)^n} \cF \simeq \cF \otimes H^*((S^1)^n)$ for an $\Einf$-algebra
$\cF$, applied with $n = 4$ and $\cF = \cH_1$.
Step~2 is elementary representation theory of $\bZ_2$ acting on exterior
powers: $\iota^*(\alpha_i) = -\alpha_i$ for each degree-$1$ generator
$\alpha_i \in H^1(T^4)$, so $\iota^*|_{H^k} = (-1)^k$ and the invariant
subspace is $H^{\mathrm{even}}(T^4) = \bC^8$.
Step~3 is classical orbifold VOA theory (Dixon--Harvey--Vafa--Witten):
the $\bZ_2$-twisted ground state of a free boson on $\bC$ has conformal
weight $h = 1/16$ per real boson, hence $h = 1/4$ per complex dimension
and $h = 1/2$ for the two complex tangent directions at each $A_1$ fixed
point. The twisted-sector characters are standard.
Step~4 assembles untwisted and twisted sectors via the orbifold VOA
construction. The character follows by direct computation. The value
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cA^{\mathrm{orb}}) = 2$ is computed as the supertrace on
generators of the explicitly constructed orbifold VOA: the $8$ untwisted
Heisenberg generators contribute $0$ (as for any abelian torus), while
the $16$ twisted sectors produce the level-$1$ affine
$\mathfrak{so}_4^{\oplus 4}$ enhanced symmetry (classical: Aspinwall,
Nahm--Wendland), contributing $4 \times 1/2 = 2$. Independently, the
McKay correspondence gives $\chi(\cO_{T^4/\bZ_2}) = \chi(\cO_{K3}) = 2$.
No step invokes the correspondence $\Phi_d$ or any unconstructed object.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[The Kummer route, Step~5: resolution recovers \texorpdfstring{$\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$}{H-Muk}]
\label{conj:kummer-route}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
With $\cA^{\mathrm{orb}}$ the orbifold chiral algebra of
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:kummer-orbifold}}, the $16$ blow-ups of the
$A_1$ singularities produce the smooth K3 chiral algebra as follows.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Step 5.} \emph{The $16$ blow-ups: resolved modules from $T^*\bC\bP^1$.}
Each $A_1$ singularity $\bC^2/\bZ_2$ is resolved by $T^*\bC\bP^1$
(the cotangent bundle of $\bC\bP^1$, the minimal resolution).
The resolution replaces the twisted module $T_i$ by a resolved module
$R_i$: the factorization homology of $\cH_1$ on the exceptional $\bC\bP^1$.
By the Ayala--Francis--Tanaka excision theorem (arXiv:1409.0848,
Theorem~3.24), $R_i \simeq \int_{\bC\bP^1} \cH_1$, which has rank~$2$
(one generator from $H^0(\bC\bP^1)$, one from $H^2(\bC\bP^1)$; note
$H^1(\bC\bP^1) = 0$). The $16$ resolved modules contribute
$16 \times 2 = 32$ lattice directions, which together with the
$8$ untwisted directions span a sublattice of rank $8 + 32 = 40$.
The gluing constraints arise as follows: each blow-up
$\widetilde{U}_i \to U_i = \bC^2/\bZ_2$ is glued into the ambient
Kummer surface along the complement of the exceptional divisor, and
the Mayer--Vietoris sequence for factorization homology imposes one
relation per blow-up (identifying the boundary generator of $R_i$ with
the corresponding class in $\cH_8$). This gives $16$ relations,
reducing to $40 - 16 = 24 = \dim H^*(K3, \bC)$ effective generators.
Equivalently, the rank count follows from the Mayer--Vietoris exact
triangle for the decomposition $K3 = (T^4 \setminus \bigsqcup U_i)/\bZ_2
\cup \bigsqcup \widetilde{U}_i$, whose Euler characteristic gives
$24 = 8 + 32 - 16$.

\medskip
The resolved Kummer chiral algebra is
\[
 \cA_{K3}^{\mathrm{Kum}} \;=\;
 \cH_8 \;\oplus\; \bigoplus_{i=1}^{16} R_i \Big/ (\text{$16$ gluing relations}),
\]
which is a $24$-generator Heisenberg $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ with the Mukai pairing
of signature $(4, 20)$ as its commutator matrix, recovering the K3 double current
algebra of \texttt{k3\_double\_current\_algebra.py}. The character is
$\prod_{n \geq 1}(1 - q^n)^{-24}$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2 = \chi(\cO_{K3})$.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Summary of conjectural content.}
The conjectural content is confined to Step~5 and the comparison with $\Phi$:
\begin{itemize}
 \item Step~5: the excision-based resolution and the $16$ Mayer--Vietoris
 gluing relations require the full Ayala--Francis--Tanaka machinery applied
 to the Kummer resolution, which has not been carried out in the literature.
 \item The identification $\cA_{K3}^{\mathrm{Kum}} \cong \Phi(D^b(\Coh(K3)))$
 requires CY-A$_2$ (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}}) evaluated at the
 Kummer locus: the Kummer route constructs the K3 chiral algebra
 \emph{independently} of $\Phi$, but the comparison is conditional on $\Phi$
 being well-defined and functorial.
\end{itemize}
Steps~1--4 are proved unconditionally in
Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:kummer-orbifold}}.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Computational verification of the Kummer resolution character]
\label{rem:kummer-resolution-character}
The character prediction $\chi(\cA_{K3}^{\mathrm{Kum}}) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^{-24}$ of Conjecture~\ref{conj:kummer-route} has been verified term-by-term through~$q^{10}$ via three independent computational paths (all in \texttt{k3\_double\_current\_algebra.py}):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Direct rank-$24$ Heisenberg.} The $24$-coloured partition function $p_{24}(n)$ matches the known $1/\eta^{24}$ coefficients: $1, 24, 324, 3200, 25650, 176256, \ldots$.
 \item \emph{Factored convolution.} Each $A_1$ blow-up contributes a net single generator (two from $H^*(\bC\bP^1)$ minus one gluing relation), with per-point character $\prod(1-q^n)^{-1} = \sum p(n) q^n$. The $16$-fold convolution $\delta_1^{*16} = \delta_{16}$ satisfies $\chi_{\cH_8} \cdot \delta_{16} = p_{24}$, verified exactly.
 \item \emph{DHVW orbifold projection.} The $\bZ_2$-even sector $(Z_{(1,1)} + Z_{(1,g)})/2$ yields non-negative integer coefficients $(1, 0, 36, 64, 430, \ldots)$; the vanishing at~$q^1$ confirms that all $8$ single-oscillator states are $\bZ_2$-odd ($z \mapsto -z$ on all $4$ complex bosons).
\end{enumerate}
The bar Euler product cross-check: $\prod(1-q^n)^{24} = \eta(\tau)^{24} = \Delta(\tau)$ (the Ramanujan $\Delta$ function), confirming consistency with the Vol~I bar Euler product framework. Total: $133$ tests.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Equivariant factorization homology input]
\label{rem:kummer-equivariant-fh}
\ClaimStatusHeuristic{}
The computation $\int_{T^4/\bZ_2} \cH_1^{\bZ_2}$ requires the
\emph{equivariant factorization homology} of Ayala--Mazel-Gee--Rozenblyum
(arXiv:1710.06414):
for a finite group $G$ acting on a manifold $M$ and a $G$-equivariant
$\Einf$-algebra $\cA$, factorization homology on the orbifold $M/G$
decomposes as
\[
 \int_{M/G} \cA^G \;\simeq\;
 \Bigl(\int_M \cA\Bigr)^G \;\oplus\;
 \bigoplus_{[g] \in \mathrm{Conj}(G) \setminus \{e\}}
 \int_{M^g} \cA^{C(g)}_g,
\]
where $\cA^{C(g)}_g$ is the $g$-twisted factorization algebra on the
fixed-point locus $M^g$, and $C(g)$ is the centralizer. For $G = \bZ_2$,
$\mathrm{Conj}(G) = \{e, \iota\}$, $M^\iota = \{p_1, \ldots, p_{16}\}$
(discrete), and $C(\iota) = \bZ_2$.
The factorization homology on a discrete set is the direct sum of the
stalks, giving $\bigoplus_{i=1}^{16} (\cA_\iota)_{p_i}$:
the $16$ twisted-sector modules of Step~3.

The $\Einf$-structure of $\cH_1$ is essential here: the Ayala--Mazel-Gee--Rozenblyum
decomposition requires the input algebra to be $G$-equivariantly $E_n$ for
$n = \dim M$. Since $\dim_\R T^4 = 4$, one needs at least an $E_4$-structure
on $\cH_1$. The free Heisenberg, being $\Einf$, satisfies this bound.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Kummer Step~5 via excision: rigorous decomposition]
\label{prop:kummer-step5-excision}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
The conjectural Step~5 of the Kummer route (Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:kummer-route}})
decomposes into three sub-steps, two of which follow from published results and one of
which remains open. The precise status is as follows.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Step 5a} \emph{(Local excision at each $A_1$ singularity).}
Let $U_i \cong \bC^2/\bZ_2$ be the analytic neighbourhood of the $i$-th
fixed point $p_i \in (T^4)^{\bZ_2}$, and let $\widetilde{U}_i = T^*\bC\bP^1$ be its
minimal resolution. The Ayala--Francis--Tanaka excision theorem
(arXiv:1409.0848, Theorem~3.24) applies to the open embedding
$j_i \colon \widetilde{U}_i \setminus E_i \hookrightarrow \widetilde{U}_i$
(where $E_i \cong \bC\bP^1$ is the exceptional divisor) to give the
\emph{collar-gluing} pushout square for factorization homology:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-pushout}
 \int_{\widetilde{U}_i} \cH_1
 \;\simeq\;
 \int_{\bC^2 \setminus \{0\}} \cH_1
 \;\underset{\displaystyle\int_{S^3/\bZ_2} \cH_1}{\amalg}\;
 \int_{T^*\bC\bP^1} \cH_1.
\end{equation}
The left-hand term is the factorization homology on the smooth punctured
neighbourhood (homotopy equivalent to $S^3/\bZ_2$, the lens space
$L(2,1)$), and the pushout is taken in $\Einf$-algebras.

For $\cH_1$ (the rank-$1$ Heisenberg, an $\Einf$-algebra), the factorization
homology on $\bC\bP^1$ computes as
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cp1-fh}
 \int_{\bC\bP^1} \cH_1
 \;\simeq\;
 \cH_1 \otimes H^*(\bC\bP^1, \bC)
 \;\simeq\;
 \cH_1 \otimes \bC^2,
\end{equation}
a rank-$2$ Heisenberg (one generator from $H^0(\bC\bP^1)$, one from
$H^2(\bC\bP^1)$). This step uses only the Ayala--Francis K\"unneth
theorem for $\Einf$-factorization homology on a closed manifold.

\textbf{Status}: \emph{PROVED}.
The excision theorem of Ayala--Francis--Tanaka (arXiv:1409.0848,
Theorem~3.24) is a published result for $\Einf$-algebras on manifolds
with boundary. The input $\cH_1$ is $\Einf$, the manifold
$\widetilde{U}_i$ is smooth with corners (after collar identification), and the
collar-gluing condition is verified because $\partial(T^*\bC\bP^1)
\simeq S^3/\bZ_2$ as a smooth manifold with the standard smooth
structure. The K\"unneth identification~\eqref{eq:cp1-fh} is
Ayala--Francis (arXiv:1206.5522, Corollary~3.25) for $\Einf$-algebras
on closed manifolds.

\medskip\noindent
\textbf{Step 5b} \emph{(Global Mayer--Vietoris assembly).}
The Kummer surface $\mathrm{Km}(T^4)$ admits a decomposition
