The function $\sqrt{Q_L(u)}$ is analytic in any simply connected domain avoiding the zeros $t_\pm$ of~$Q_L$. Since $t_\pm \notin \bR$ (Step~2), the half-line $\bR_+ = [0, \infty)$ lies in such a domain. Therefore $\hat{F}$ extends analytically along the entire positive real axis.

The growth bound: for $u \geq 1$, $|\hat{F}(u)| = |\sqrt{Q_L(u)} - \sqrt{q_0}| \leq \sqrt{Q_L(u)} + \sqrt{q_0} \leq \sqrt{q_2}\, u + O(1)$. In particular, there exist constants $C, M > 0$ such that $|\hat{F}(u)| \leq C(1 + u)$ for all $u \geq 0$. The growth is at most linear (polynomial of degree~$1$), which is far below the exponential threshold required for Borel summability.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 4: Convergence of the Borel integral.}
For any $z > 0$, the Borel sum is
\[
 \cS[F](z) \;=\; \frac{1}{z} \int_0^\infty e^{-u/z}\, \hat{F}(u)\, du.
\]
By the growth bound of Step~3, the integrand satisfies $|e^{-u/z} \hat{F}(u)| \leq C\, e^{-u/z}(1 + u)$ for all $u \geq 0$. The comparison integral converges:
\[
 \frac{1}{z}\int_0^\infty e^{-u/z}(1 + u)\, du \;=\; \frac{1}{z}\bigl(z + z^2\bigr) \;=\; 1 + z \;<\; \infty.
\]
Therefore $\cS[F](z)$ converges absolutely for every $z > 0$. The function $\cS[F]$ is smooth on $(0, \infty)$ and is the unique Borel sum of the Gevrey-$1$ series $\sum_{k \geq 2} S_k k!\, z^k$: by Watson's lemma, the asymptotic expansion of $\cS[F](z)$ as $z \to 0^+$ recovers the original formal series.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 5: Absence of Stokes ambiguity.}
Stokes phenomena arise when the Borel contour $[0, \infty)$ passes through or near a singularity of $\hat{F}$ in the Borel plane. The singularities of $\hat{F}(u) = \sqrt{Q_L(u)} - \sqrt{q_0}$ are the square-root branch points at the zeros $t_\pm = t_c \pm i\delta$ of $Q_L$. Since $\delta > 0$ (Step~2), these branch points have nonzero imaginary part and lie strictly off the real axis. Therefore:
\begin{itemize}
 \item The Borel contour $[0, \infty) \subset \bR$ does not pass through any singularity of $\hat{F}$.
 \item No contour deformation (and hence no choice of lateral Borel sum) is needed.
 \item The Borel sum $\cS[F](z)$ is uniquely defined for all $z > 0$.
\end{itemize}
This completes the proof that the OPE completion is Borel summable with no Stokes ambiguity.

\smallskip\noindent\textit{Scope.} The condition $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$, $S_4 > 0$ holds for the Virasoro sector at any $c > 0$ (where $S_4 = 10/(c(5c+22)) > 0$, Proposition~\ref{prop:virasoro-d4}). For $d = 2$ CY categories the algebra exists unconditionally on the proved CY-A$_2$ locus. For $d = 3$, when a framed object-level chiral algebra $A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ is constructed by Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} and satisfies $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$, $S_4 > 0$, Borel summability follows. The regime $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} < 0$, $S_4 < 0$ is also Borel summable because $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3 S_4 > 0$. The regime $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3 S_4 < 0$ remains a sewing/MC5 problem unless a separate model-specific computation removes the Borel-plane singularity.

\smallskip\noindent\textit{Supplementary verification}: $54$ tests in \texttt{class\_m\_borel\_summation.py} confirm the discriminant identity~\eqref{eq:shadow-disc-identity} for $125$ parameter triples, the landscape analysis across $13$ standard examples, boundary cases, and numerical Borel sum convergence.
\end{proof}

\noindent The updated landscape for $\mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}$ (superseding Remark~\ref{rem:coefficient-vs-ope-convergence}):
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lllr}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Shadow class} & \textbf{OPE completion} & \textbf{Severity} & \textbf{Tests} \\
 \midrule
 $\mathbf{G}$ (Gaussian) & polynomial Borel, trivial & \textsc{low} & 54 \\
 $\mathbf{L}$ (Lie/tree) & finite depth, terminates & \textsc{low} & 54 \\
 $\mathbf{C}$ (charge) & rational, charge conservation & \textsc{low} & 54 \\
 $\mathbf{M}$ (mixed) & Gevrey-$1$; Borel summable under $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3S_4>0$ & \textsc{conditional} & 54 \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The finite and rational shadow classes have low OPE-completion severity on their verified loci. Class~$\mathbf{M}$ has low severity only on the sign-definite Borel-summable regime; the complementary regime remains in the sewing programme.

\begin{conjecture}[Non-perturbative shadow completion via resurgence]
\label{conj:shadow-resurgence}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
Let $A$ be a chiral algebra of shadow class~$\mathbf{M}$ with $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$ and quartic shadow $S_4 > 0$. The Borel transform $\hat{F}(u) = \sqrt{Q_L(u)}$ of the OPE completion \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:class-m-borel-summability})} has square-root branch points at the zeros $\omega_\pm$ of the shadow quadratic $Q_L$, and the full non-perturbative shadow function admits a trans-series expansion:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:shadow-trans-series}
 S^{\mathrm{full}}(\epsilon) \;=\; S^{\mathrm{pert}}(\epsilon) \;+\; \sum_{\substack{n_+, n_- \geq 0 \\ (n_+, n_-) \neq (0,0)}} C_+^{n_+} C_-^{n_-}\, e^{-(n_+ \omega_+ + n_- \omega_-)/\epsilon}\, S^{(n_+, n_-)}(\epsilon),
\end{equation}
where $C_\pm$ are trans-series parameters \textup{(}Stokes constants\textup{)}, $\omega_\pm = t_c \pm i\delta$ with $t_c = -q_1/(2q_2) < 0$ are complex conjugate instanton actions, and $S^{(n_+, n_-)}(\epsilon)$ are perturbative tails determined by iterated alien derivatives. The following properties hold:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Borel plane structure}: the singularities $\omega_\pm$ lie in the left half-plane \textup{(}since $t_c < 0$ when $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$, $\alpha > 0$\textup{)}, and are of square-root type with monodromy $-1$.
 \item \emph{Simple resurgence}: the shadow resolvent $\sqrt{Q_L}$ is \emph{simple resurgent} \textup{(}Ecalle\textup{)}: the alien derivative at $\omega_+$ satisfies $\Delta_{\omega_+} S^{\mathrm{pert}} = \mathcal{S}_{\omega_+} \cdot S^{(1,0)}$ with Stokes constant $\mathcal{S}_{\omega_+} = -i/\sqrt{Q_L'(\omega_+)}$, and the tower of alien derivatives terminates at each instanton level.
 \item \emph{Stokes automorphism}: the Stokes automorphism $\underline{\mathfrak{S}}_\theta$ crossing the Stokes line at angle $\theta = \arg(\omega_+)$ is upper-triangular in the instanton-number filtration, and its off-diagonal entries are the Stokes constants.
 \item \emph{Resurgence--wall-crossing correspondence} \textup{(}at $d = 3$ via the $\infty$-categorical CY-A$_3$ resolution, Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}\textup{)}: the Stokes automorphism of the shadow trans-series is the Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing automorphism, with instanton actions $|\omega_\pm|$ identified with BPS central charges $|Z(\gamma)|$ and Stokes constants identified with BPS indices $\Omega(\gamma)$.
\end{enumerate}
At $d = 2$ \textup{(}CY-A$_2$ proved\textup{)}: the shadow tower for generic K3 is finite \textup{(}class~G\textup{)}, and resurgence is trivial. At enhanced symmetry points where the shadow class jumps to~M, parts~\textup{(i)--(iii)} are unconditional. Part~\textup{(iv)} for $K3 \times E$ proceeds via the $\infty$-categorical CY-A$_3$ resolution \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}\textup{)}.

For the Virasoro at $c = 1$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1/2$, $\alpha = 2$, $S_4 = 10/27$, giving instanton actions at $\omega_\pm = -81/98 \pm i\delta$ with $\delta = \sqrt{320/27} \cdot 27/392$.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $73$~tests in \texttt{shadow\_resurgence\_nonpert.py}, covering the Borel plane singularity analysis for $5$ standard examples, alien derivative computation and Stokes constant verification, trans-series construction through instanton level $2$, Stokes automorphism upper-triangularity and discontinuity formula, and wall-crossing correspondence at the level of instanton actions.
\end{conjecture}


\begin{theorem}[Stokes automorphism = KS wall-crossing: conifold case]
\label{thm:stokes-ks-conifold}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For the resolved conifold $X = \cO(-1) \oplus \cO(-1) \to \bP^1$ with charge lattice $\Gamma = \bZ^2$ and antisymmetric Euler pairing $\langle \gamma_1, \gamma_2 \rangle = 1$, the Stokes automorphism of the resurgent DT partition function IS the Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing automorphism. Specifically:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Stokes line = wall}. The single Stokes line at $\theta = \arg(\omega)$ in the Borel plane is the single wall of marginal stability $\cW(\gamma_1, \gamma_2)$ where $\phi_\sigma(\gamma_1) = \phi_\sigma(\gamma_2)$.
 \item \emph{Trans-series sectors = BPS charge sectors}. The instanton action $|\omega| = |Z(\gamma_1 + \gamma_2)|$ is the BPS mass of the bound state; the trans-series sectors $(n_+, n_-)$ are the charge sectors $(n_1, n_2)$.
 \item \emph{Stokes matrix = KS symplectomorphism}. The Stokes matrix $\underline{\mathfrak{S}}_\theta$ and the KS symplectomorphism $\cA_\cW$ are both upper-triangular in the charge filtration, with matching off-diagonal entry $\cS_\omega = \Omega(\gamma_1 + \gamma_2) = -1$.
 \item \emph{Pentagon identity = Stokes factorization}. The Faddeev--Kashaev pentagon identity $E(X)E(Y) = E(Y)E(XY)E(X)$ in the quantum torus is simultaneously the KS wall-crossing formula \textup{(}2-particle chamber $\leftrightarrow$ 3-particle chamber\textup{)} and the Stokes factorization identity \textup{(}Borel sum before the Stokes line $=$ Borel sum after\textup{)}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The conifold has finitely many BPS states: $\Omega(\gamma_1) = \Omega(\gamma_2) = -1$ in Chamber~I, plus $\Omega(\gamma_1 + \gamma_2) = -1$ in Chamber~II. The pentagon identity (verified exactly in the quantum torus algebra at each charge sector and each $q$-power; \texttt{conifold\_wall\_crossing.py}) equates the two chamber orderings. By Bridgeland~\cite{Bridgeland2019RH} (Theorem~1.1), the Stokes data of the Riemann--Hilbert problem associated to a BPS structure IS the collection of DT invariants $\Omega(\gamma)$. For the conifold, the RH problem has a single Stokes ray, the BPS structure has a single wall, and the pentagon identity is exact. The five structural properties (ray indexing, factorization, upper-triangularity, integer data, gauge invariance) match by direct verification.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Stokes automorphism = KS wall-crossing: \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{conj:stokes-ks-k3e}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
For $K3 \times E$ with the BKM root system of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$, the Stokes automorphism of the shadow trans-series \textup{(}Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:shadow-resurgence})} is the KS wall-crossing automorphism. The identification is stratified by discriminant:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccl@{}}
\textbf{Discriminant} & \textbf{Wall type} & \textbf{Stokes type} & \textbf{Identification status} \\ \hline
$D = -1$ & Real root (flop) & Real axis & THEOREM \textup{(}local conifold\textup{)} \\
$D = 0$ & Lightlike & Imaginary axis & Conjectural \textup{(}CY-A$_3$\textup{)} \\
$D > 0$ & Imaginary (bound state) & Conjugate pair & Conjectural \textup{(}CY-A$_3$\textup{)}
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
At each real root wall ($D = -1$), the local model is the conifold \textup{(}$\langle \gamma_1, \gamma_2 \rangle = 1$, pentagon identity\textup{)}, so the identification is a theorem locally. The BKM root multiplicities $c(D)$ from $\phi_{0,1}$ simultaneously count BPS states, walls, and Stokes lines at each discriminant. The global stitching of infinitely many local identifications proceeds via the $\infty$-categorical CY-A$_3$ resolution \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}\textup{)}.

The Bridgeland Riemann--Hilbert bridge \textup{(}\cite{Bridgeland2019RH}\textup{)} provides the chain:
\[
 \text{Shadow tower} \xrightarrow{\text{Borel}} \text{Borel plane} \xrightarrow{\text{singularities}} \text{Stokes rays} \xrightarrow{\text{Bridgeland RH}} \text{DT invariants} \xrightarrow{\text{KS}} \text{Wall-crossing.}
\]
\end{conjecture}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $84$~tests in \texttt{stokes\_wallcrossing\_identification.py}, covering the five-point structural check (ray indexing, factorization, upper-triangularity, integer data, gauge invariance), conifold pentagon $=$ Stokes factorization, conifold Stokes matrix $=$ KS matrix, $K3 \times E$ discriminant-stratified identification, real root $=$ mini-conifold, imaginary root Stokes structure, Bridgeland RH bridge, and cross-checks with \texttt{shadow\_resurgence\_nonpert.py}, \texttt{k3e\_wall\_crossing\_shadow.py}, and \texttt{conifold\_wall\_crossing.py}.


\subsection{Hopf fibration decomposition of the chain-level obstruction}
\label{subsec:hopf-fibration-decomposition}

The topological vanishing (Theorem~\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}) and the \v{C}ech contracting homotopy (Theorem~\ref{thm:cech-contracting-homotopy}) leave open one precise question: the $\Ainf$-compatibility of the chain-level $\bS^3$-framing trivialization. The Hopf fibration $S^1 \to S^3 \to S^2$ decomposes this question into three independent components, two of which are resolved.

\begin{proposition}[Hopf fibration decomposition of the \texorpdfstring{$\bS^3$}{S-3}-framing obstruction]
\label{prop:hopf-fibration-decomposition}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
Let $\cC$ be a smooth proper CY$_3$ category with cyclic $\Ainf$-structure $(A, \{\mu_n\}, \langle -,- \rangle)$. The Connes hierarchy $B^{(0)}, B^{(1)}, B^{(2)}, B^{(3)}$ on the cyclic bar complex $\mathrm{CC}_\bullet(\cC)$ \textup{(}where $B^{(0)} = B$ is the Connes operator and $B^{(3)} = F$ is the $\bS^3$-framing map\textup{)} satisfies the total nilpotence relation
\[
 \sum_{i+j=3} B^{(i)} \circ B^{(j)} = 0,
\]
which rearranges to
\[
 [B, F] \;=\; -[B^{(1)}, B^{(2)}].
\]
The commutator $[B^{(1)}, B^{(2)}]$ encodes the nontriviality of the Hopf fibration: $B^{(1)}$ is the $S^1$-fiber contribution \textup{(}the intermediate Connes-hierarchy map from $\Omega^1$ of the holomorphic volume form\textup{)}, $B^{(2)}$ is the $S^2$-base contribution \textup{(}the CY$_2$ part of the framing, proved by CY-A$_2$\textup{)}, and their commutator is the Euler class twist.

The chain-level $\bS^3$-framing obstruction decomposes as
\[
 \mathrm{Obs}(\cC) \;=\; \mathrm{Obs}_{\mathrm{top}}(\cC) + \mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf}(\cC) + \mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}(\cC),
\]
where:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item $\mathrm{Obs}_{\mathrm{top}} = 0$ on the symplectic-reduction locus \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}}: $\pi_3(B\Sp) = \pi_3(BU) = 0$\textup{)}.
 \item $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} = 0$ for cyclic $\Ainf$-algebras covered by Costello's open-closed TCFT framework \textup{(}Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cyclic-ainf-framing-compat}} below: $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ where $b = \sum_k b_k$ is the total $\Ainf$-Hochschild differential\textup{)}.
 \item $\mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}$ vanishes for toric CY$_3$ \textup{(}$T^3$-equivariant hCS trivialization\textup{)} and perturbatively for compact CY$_3$ with \v{C}ech homotopy \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cech-contracting-homotopy}}\textup{)}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The nilpotence relation $\sum_{i+j=3} B^{(i)} B^{(j)} = 0$ is the defining property of the Connes hierarchy for CY$_3$ (Chapter~\ref{ch:cyclic-ainf}; Costello's open--closed TCFT framework and Kontsevich--Soibelman's CY $\Ainf$ formalism). The four terms are $B^{(0)}B^{(3)} + B^{(1)}B^{(2)} + B^{(2)}B^{(1)} + B^{(3)}B^{(0)} = 0$, which is $[B,F] + [B^{(1)},B^{(2)}] = 0$.

For~(a): Theorem~\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}.

For~(b): Proposition~\ref{prop:cyclic-ainf-framing-compat} below.

For~(c): the BV trivialization via holomorphic Chern--Simons provides $\delta_{\BV}(\mathrm{CS}) = \int \Omega \wedge F_A$. For toric CY$_3$, the $T^3$-action makes CS $\Eone$-equivariant. For compact CY$_3$, the \v{C}ech contracting homotopy (Theorem~\ref{thm:cech-contracting-homotopy}) provides a formal-power-series trivialization; the perturbative convergence is guaranteed by the algebraic structure, and the non-perturbative convergence is the single remaining open question.
\end{proof}


\begin{proposition}[Cyclic \texorpdfstring{$\Ainf$}{A-inf}-framing compatibility via Costello's TCFT]
\label{prop:cyclic-ainf-framing-compat}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Let $(A, \{\mu_n\}, \langle -,- \rangle)$ be a cyclic $\Ainf$-algebra of CY dimension~$d$, let $b = \sum_{k \geq 1} b_k$ be the $\Ainf$-Hochschild differential on $C_\bullet(A)$, and let $B^{(2)} \colon C_n(A) \to C_{n-2}(A)$ be the contraction operator determined by the CY pairing. Then
\[
 \{b, B^{(2)}\} \;=\; b \circ B^{(2)} + B^{(2)} \circ b \;=\; 0.
\]
The vanishing is for the \emph{total} differential $b = \sum_k b_k$. Individual $\{b_k, B^{(2)}\}$ need not vanish for $k \geq 3$; only their sum does. For formal algebras ($\mu_k = 0$ for $k \geq 3$): $b = b_2$ and $\{b_2, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ by the Frobenius condition. For non-formal algebras ($\mu_3 \neq 0$): $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\} \neq 0$ on $\mathrm{CC}_4$ \textup{(}\texttt{obs\_ainf\_local\_p2.py}, $54$~tests: $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\}([a|a|a|a|b]) = 2\alpha \cdot [b]$ for $\mu_3(a,a,a) = \alpha b$\textup{)}, but the cross-degree cancellation $\{b_2, B^{(2)}\} + \{b_3, B^{(2)}\} + \cdots = 0$ is enforced by the $\Ainf$-Stasheff relations, packaged operadically as $\partial^2 = 0$ in Costello's TCFT.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By Costello~\cite{Costello2005TCFT} (Theorem~A; arXiv:math/0412149), a cyclic $\Ainf$-algebra of CY dimension~$d$ is equivalent to an open TCFT. The cyclic pairing extends this to an open-closed TCFT (ibid., Section~5; Costello~\cite{Costello2007Ainfty}; arXiv:0706.1959) whose closed sector is the Hochschild chain complex $C_\bullet(A)$. Under this equivalence:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item the Hochschild differential $b = \sum_k b_k$ corresponds to codimension-$1$ boundary strata of the moduli of bordered surfaces (strip-like degenerations);
 \item the contraction $B^{(2)}$ corresponds to the genus-change operation (identifying two boundary marked points via the CY pairing to create a node).
\end{enumerate}
Both $b$ and $B^{(2)}$ are images of the boundary operator $\partial$ in the chain complex $C_\bullet(\cM)$ of the moduli operad~$\cM$. They correspond to \emph{geometrically distinct} boundary strata (strip-like degenerations and genus-change nodes respectively) that parametrise a specific compact $1$-dimensional moduli space $\overline{\cM}_{b, B^{(2)}}$ whose \emph{only} boundary components are the two compositions $b \circ B^{(2)}$ and $B^{(2)} \circ b$. No other Connes-hierarchy operations ($B^{(0)}$, $B^{(1)}$, $B^{(3)}$) appear as boundary types of this moduli space, because the surface types producing $B^{(i)}$ for $i \neq 2$ involve different topological operations (rotation, interior contraction, cap product) that cannot arise as degenerations of the strip-plus-node family. The signed boundary count of the compact $1$-manifold $\overline{\cM}_{b, B^{(2)}}$ is zero, giving $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Distinction from the mixed complex axiom.}
The mixed complex axiom $[b, B_{\mathrm{total}}] = 0$ where $B_{\mathrm{total}} = \sum_{i=0}^{d} B^{(i)}$ is a \emph{weaker} statement: it implies $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = -\{b, B^{(0)}\} - \{b, B^{(1)}\} - \{b, B^{(3)}\}$ but does NOT by itself imply $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$. The vanishing $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ is a \emph{stronger} consequence of the operadic structure, using the specific moduli-space factorisation above.

\medskip\noindent\emph{Non-adjacent contractions resolved.}
The moduli space treats all boundary marked points democratically. When $B^{(2)}$ contracts points $p_i$ and $p_j$, their positions relative to the $b_k$-insertion are geometric data encoded by the full moduli, not merely by the cyclic ordering. The $\partial^2 = 0$ argument applies uniformly to all pairs $(i,j)$, whether or not they straddle the $b_k$-block.

\medskip\noindent\emph{Why individual $\{b_k, B^{(2)}\}$ can be nonzero.}
The explicit computation (\texttt{obs\_ainf\_local\_p2.py}, $54$~tests) confirms $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\}([a|a|a|a|b]) = 2\alpha \cdot [b] \neq 0$ for the minimal non-formal model. This is not a counterexample: the $b_2$-contribution to $\{b, B^{(2)}\}$ on the same element provides the cancelling term, as guaranteed by Costello's theorem. The mechanism: the Stasheff relations at degree $n = 4$ couple $\mu_2$ and $\mu_3$, forcing the residue of $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\}$ to equal $-\{b_2, B^{(2)}\}$ at every chain element.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $43$~tests in \texttt{operadic\_tcft\_mk\_b2\_engine.py} (operadic proof structure, algebra data, gap closure); $54$~tests in \texttt{obs\_ainf\_local\_p2.py} (chain-level $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\} \neq 0$, confirming the individual non-vanishing that the total cancels); $40$~tests in \texttt{stasheff\_cancellation\_obs\_ainf.py} (bidegree decomposition, weight-$s$ identities, cross-hierarchy cancellation partners, formal algebra axiom verification).
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[Scope of the \texorpdfstring{$\Ainf$}{A-inf}-framing obstruction]
\label{rem:cyclic-ainf-framing-audit}
The obstruction $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} = 0$ holds for cyclic
$\Ainf$-algebras (formal or not) in Costello's operadic open-closed TCFT
framework.
The identity is $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ for the \emph{total} $\Ainf$-Hochschild
differential $b = \sum_k b_k$, as a consequence of $\partial^2 = 0$ in the
chain complex of the moduli operad; it is not a per-$k$ statement, and
individual components $\{b_k, B^{(2)}\}$ can be nonzero. Three per-$k$
ansätze fail as mathematics:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{Cyclic invariance}: the claim $[m_k, B^{(2)}] = 0$ for each $k$
 from cyclic invariance alone. \textbf{Gap}: cyclic invariance controls
 adjacent contractions but not ``non-adjacent'' terms.
 \item \emph{Bidegree decomposition}: the claim that $[b, B^{\mathrm{full}}] = 0$
 decomposes by degree into $[m_k, B^{(j)}] = 0$ for each $(k,j)$.
 \textbf{Flaw}: the decomposition by degree does not preserve the
 vanishing; individual $\{b_k, B^{(2)}\}$ can be nonzero.
 \item \emph{Tsygan formality}.
 \textbf{Flaw}: Tsygan formality applies to $(b, B)$ but not to the
 Connes hierarchy operators $B^{(j)}$ for $j \geq 1$.
\end{enumerate}
The correct statement is the total-differential form, not per-$k$: for
non-formal algebras, $[m_k, B^{(2)}] = 0$ for all $k \geq 3$ is false,
but $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ holds on the total differential. The explicit
computation \textup{(}\texttt{obs\_ainf\_local\_p2.py}, $54$~tests\textup{)} records
\[
 \{b_3, B^{(2)}\}([a \mid a \mid a \mid a \mid b]) \;=\; 2\alpha \cdot [b] \;\neq\; 0
\]
on the minimal non-formal model with $\mu_3(a,a,a) = \alpha b$; the nonzero $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\}$ is cancelled cross-degree by $\{b_2, B^{(2)}\}$ on the same element, the cancellation enforced by the Stasheff $\Ainf$-relations at degree $n = 4$ coupling $\mu_2$ and $\mu_3$. Costello's TCFT packages this cross-degree cancellation as the single identity $\partial^2 = 0$ on the open-closed TCFT moduli operad.

By this universality, the CY-A$_3$ obstruction landscape of Proposition~\ref{prop:hopf-fibration-decomposition} reduces to a single remaining open obstruction: $\mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}$ (non-perturbative convergence).

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $43$~tests in \texttt{operadic\_tcft\_mk\_b2\_engine.py} (operadic proof structure, local~$\bP^2$ algebra, gap closure); $54$~tests in \texttt{obs\_ainf\_local\_p2.py} (chain-level individual $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\} \neq 0$); $67$~tests in \texttt{hopf\_fibration\_s3\_framing.py}; $40$~tests in \texttt{stasheff\_cancellation\_obs\_ainf.py} (independent verification: bidegree decomposition of $[b,B]=0$ by total weight $s = k+i$, weight-$s$ identities for CY$_3$, cross-hierarchy cancellation partners, formal algebra axiom checks).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[CY-A\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} obstruction landscape]
\label{rem:hopf-reduction}
The Hopf fibration decomposition (Proposition~\ref{prop:hopf-fibration-decomposition}) reduces CY-A$_3$ from three independent obstructions to one. $\mathrm{Obs}_{\mathrm{top}} = 0$ on the symplectic-reduction locus ($\pi_3(B\Sp) = 0$). $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} = 0$ in Costello's TCFT framework (Proposition~\ref{prop:cyclic-ainf-framing-compat}: $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ for the total $\Ainf$-Hochschild differential; individual $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\} \neq 0$ for non-formal algebras, cancelled cross-degree by $\{b_2, B^{(2)}\}$). Only $\mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}$ remains:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
 \toprule
 \textbf{CY$_3$} & $\mathrm{Obs}_{\mathrm{top}}$ & $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf}$ & $\mathrm{Obs}_{\BV}$ & \textbf{Status} \\
 \midrule
 $\C^3$ & $0$ & $0$ (formal) & $0$ (toric) & Fully resolved \\
 Conifold & $0$ & $0$ (formal) & $0$ (toric) & Fully resolved \\
 Local $\bP^2$ & $0$ & $0$ (TCFT) & $0$ (toric) & Fully resolved \\
 Quintic & $0$ & $0$ (TCFT) & $0$ (pert.) & Perturbatively resolved \\
 $K3 \times E$ & $0$ & $0$ (formal) & cond.\ on Step~5 & Kummer route \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} = 0$ entry for local~$\bP^2$ is the new content: despite $\mu_3 \neq 0$ (non-formality) and despite $\{b_3, B^{(2)}\} \neq 0$ at the chain level, the \emph{total} commutator $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ by Costello's operadic TCFT. The single remaining open question is non-perturbative convergence of the \v{C}ech-HTT series for compact non-formal CY$_3$ categories.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $43$~tests in \texttt{operadic\_tcft\_mk\_b2\_engine.py}, $54$~tests in \texttt{obs\_ainf\_local\_p2.py}, $67$~tests in \texttt{hopf\_fibration\_s3\_framing.py}.
\end{remark}


\begin{theorem}[Derived framing obstruction on the connective CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} locus]
\label{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For any smooth proper CY$_3$ category $\cC$ such that
$\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ is connective \textup{(}$\HH_n(\cC) = 0$ for $n < 0$\textup{)}
\emph{and} unit-connected \textup{(}$\HH^0(\cC) = k$\textup{),}
the chain-level nonvanishing $[m_3, B^{(2)}] \neq 0$ does \emph{not} obstruct
the $\bS^3$-framing in the $\infty$-categorical framework. Specifically:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The primary obstruction to lifting the canonical $\Etwo$-algebra structure on $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ to an $E_3$-algebra structure lives in $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(\HH_\bullet(\cC),\, \HH_\bullet(\cC))$.
 \item This group is zero by the bar-resolution argument: connectivity gives $\bar{A} = A_{\geq 1}$ concentrated in strictly positive degrees, and the bar filtration spectral sequence collapses to give $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A) = 0$.
 \item All higher obstructions live in $\HH^{-2-k}_{E_1}$ for $k \geq 1$, which also vanish by the same bar filtration argument.
 \item Therefore the \emph{space} of $E_3$-structures compatible with the given $\Etwo$-structure is contractible.
 \item The explicit homotopy is provided by Costello's TCFT: $\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ via $\partial^2 = 0$ on the moduli chain complex.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Three-level placement of Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}]
\label{rem:derived-framing-three-levels}
Per Definition~\ref{def:three-levels} of
Chapter~\ref{ch:m3-b2-obstruction} (three levels of the
$[m_k, B^{(2)}]$ question), Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}
lives at \textbf{Level~3} (derived / $\infty$-categorical): it asserts
vanishing of the obstruction group
$\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A) = 0$, an existential statement. Level~1 (strict
chain-level $[m_k, B^{(2)}] = 0$ per~$k$) fails for non-formal
algebras (Proposition~\ref{prop:chain-nonvanishing-generic} in
Chapter~\ref{ch:m3-b2-obstruction}); Level~2 (homotopy
$\{b, B^{(2)}\} = 0$ for the total differential) holds in
Costello's TCFT framework (Theorem~\ref{thm:total-ainf-compat} in
Chapter~\ref{ch:m3-b2-obstruction}) and supplies the explicit
homotopy. The Level~2 and Level~3 resolutions are logically independent
constructive-versus-existential witnesses of the same vanishing; the
connectivity-plus-unit-connectedness hypothesis of
Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction} is precisely the
hypothesis on which the Level~3 bar-filtration spectral sequence
collapses. See Remark~\ref{rem:three-level-relationship} for the
level-crossing implications $\text{Level~1} \Rightarrow
\text{Level~2} \Rightarrow \text{Level~3}$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Scope of Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}: connectivity
 hypothesis and the compact non-formal CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} case]
\label{rem:derived-framing-scope-restriction}
The connectivity hypothesis \textup{(}$\HH_n(\cC) = 0$ for $n < 0$\textup{)} is
\textsc{additional} to unit-connectedness; the bar-resolution argument of
Step~2 uses both \textup{(}the explicit identification
$\bar{A} = A_{\geq 1}$ requires $A_{<0} = 0$\textup{).} This hypothesis
\textsc{holds} for the listed cases:
\begin{itemize}
 \item Toric CY$_3$ \textup{($\C^3$, conifold, local~$\bP^2$, local~$\bP^1 \times \bP^1$):}
 formal models exist by Tate--Tradler--Costello formality; $A$ is
 concentrated in non-negative degrees by construction.
 \item Smooth quasi-projective CY$_3$ with formal Kapranov-Manin-Tate model:
 $A$ is concentrated in non-negative degrees by the formality result.
\end{itemize}
For \textbf{compact CY$_3$ with Serre duality at the chain level}
\textup{(}quintic, $K3 \times E$, complete-intersection threefolds\textup{):}
Serre self-duality gives
$\HH_{-3}(\cC) \cong \HH_0(\cC)^* \cong k^* \neq 0$, so $A = \HH_\bullet(\cC)$
is \emph{non-connective} at the chain level. Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}
applies in this regime only after passage to a formal model where the
Serre-dual negative components are killed by the formality \textup{(}or
equivalently, after Goodwillie convergence of the Postnikov-Goodwillie tower
in the non-connective category, which requires additional input\textup{).}

The status table of Remark~\ref{rem:hopf-reduction} reflects this
distinction: $\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf} = 0$ entries marked ``formal'' use the
formality assumption directly; entries marked ``TCFT'' use Costello's
operadic resolution which produces a formal model up to coherent homotopy.
The compact non-formal CY$_3$ case is thus \emph{conditional} on either:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
 \item formality of the underlying $\Ainf$ category at the chain level
 \textup{(}known for the toric cases, conjectural in general\textup{);} or
 \item Goodwillie convergence in the non-connective category
 \textup{(}requires Francis-Gaitsgory machinery beyond the bar-filtration
 argument\textup{).}
\end{enumerate}
The independent-verification protocol annotation (Vol~III):
the engine \texttt{derived\_framing\_obstruction.py} verifies the
Goodwillie tower through layer~$k = 4$ for the formal cases; the compact
non-formal cases are listed as conditional on Step (1) or (2) above.
\end{remark}

\begin{proof}
The argument has four steps: identification of the obstruction group, its vanishing, the vanishing of all higher obstructions, and reconciliation with the chain-level nonvanishing.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1. Obstruction identification via Dunn additivity.}
Write $A = \HH_\bullet(\cC)$ for the Hochschild homology, viewed as an $\Etwo$-algebra in $\mathrm{Ch}(k)$. By Dunn additivity (Dunn~\cite{Dunn1988}; for the $\infty$-categorical formulation, Lurie~\cite[Theorem~5.1.2.2]{LurieHA}), there is an equivalence of $\infty$-operads $E_3 \simeq \Etwo \otimes E_1$. Hence an $E_3$-algebra structure on~$A$ is equivalent to an $E_1$-algebra structure on~$A$ in $\mathrm{Alg}_{\Etwo}(\mathrm{Ch}(k))$. The forgetful functor $\mathrm{Alg}_{E_3}(\mathrm{Ch}(k)) \to \mathrm{Alg}_{\Etwo}(\mathrm{Ch}(k))$ is the restriction along $\Etwo \hookrightarrow E_3$; its fiber at~$A$ is the space of $E_1$-structures on~$A$ inside $\Etwo$-algebras. By Francis~\cite[Theorem~4.1]{Francis2013} and Francis--Gaitsgory~\cite[Theorem~3.3.1]{FrancisGaitsgory2012}, the relative tangent complex of the moduli of $E_n$-structures over the moduli of $E_{n-1}$-structures is the $(n{-}1)$-fold desuspension of the $E_1$-Hochschild cohomology:
\[
 \mathrm{fib}\bigl(T\,\mathrm{Alg}_{E_3}(A) \to T\,\mathrm{Alg}_{\Etwo}(A)\bigr) \;\simeq\; \HH^\bullet_{E_1}(A, A)[-2].
\]
Here $\HH^\bullet_{E_1}(A, A) = \mathrm{RHom}_{A \otimes_k A^{\mathrm{op}}}(A, A)$ is the derived endomorphism complex of~$A$ as an $A$-bimodule (the standard Hochschild cochain complex). The primary obstruction to lifting therefore lies in
\[
 \mathrm{obs}_1 \;\in\; \pi_0\bigl(\HH^\bullet_{E_1}(A, A)[-2]\bigr) \;=\; \HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A).
\]

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2. Vanishing of the primary obstruction from connective and unit-connected hypotheses.}
We prove $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A) = 0$ for every smooth proper CY$_3$
category $\cC$ satisfying the connective and unit-connected hypotheses
of the theorem.

\emph{Unit-connectedness.} The $\Etwo$-algebra $A$ carries a unit $1_A \in \HH^0(\cC)$. The unit-connectedness condition is that $\HH^0(\cC) = k$ (the unit spans the entire degree-$0$ part). This is only one of the two hypotheses:
\begin{itemize}
 \item For connected CY$_3$ manifolds $X$, the HKR isomorphism gives
 $\HH^0(\Perf(X)) \cong H^0(\cO_X) = k$. This verifies
 unit-connectedness, not connectivity of Hochschild chains.
 \item For $K3 \times E$: the product is connected, so $\HH^0(\Perf(K3 \times E)) = H^0(\cO_{K3 \times E}) = k$. \textup{(}Warning: the algebraic K\"unneth formula $\HH^0(\Perf(K3)) \otimes \HH^0(\Perf(E)) = k^2 \otimes k^2$ gives the external tensor product, not the geometric~$\HH^0$; see Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:k3e-product-decomposition}} and Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:k3e-unit-connected}}.\textup{)}
\end{itemize}
For compact non-formal CY$_3$ categories, including the quintic, Serre
duality produces negative Hochschild-chain degrees before a formal or
Goodwillie-convergent model is chosen; those categories therefore do not
enter this theorem merely from connectedness.

\emph{Bar resolution argument.} The $E_1$-augmentation $\varepsilon \colon A \to k$ (projection to the unit component) exhibits $A$ as augmented. Write $\bar{A} = \ker(\varepsilon)$; connectivity plus unit-connectedness gives $\bar{A} = A_{\geq 1}$, concentrated in strictly positive degrees. The two-sided bar complex resolving $A$ as an $A^e$-module ($A^e = A \otimes_k A^{\mathrm{op}}$) is
\[
 B_\bullet(A, A, A) \;=\; A \otimes_k T(\bar{A}[1]) \otimes_k A \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\; A,
\]
where $T(\bar{A}[1]) = \bigoplus_{n \geq 0} (\bar{A}[1])^{\otimes n}$. Since $\bar{A}[1]$ is concentrated in degrees $\geq 2$, each summand $(\bar{A}[1])^{\otimes n}$ is concentrated in degrees $\geq 2n$. The Hochschild cochain complex is
\[
 C^\bullet_{E_1}(A, A) \;=\; \Hom_{A^e}\bigl(B_\bullet(A,A,A),\, A\bigr) \;\cong\; \prod_{n \geq 0} \Hom_k\bigl((\bar{A}[1])^{\otimes n},\, A\bigr).
\]
A cochain $f \in \Hom_k((\bar{A}[1])^{\otimes n}, A)$ of cohomological degree~$p$ requires: input degree $+ p =$ output degree. The minimum input degree is $2n$ and the minimum output degree is~$0$, giving $p \geq 0$ for $n = 0$ and $p \geq -2n$ in general. For $p = -2$: only the $n = 1$ term contributes (maps $\bar{A}[1] \to A$ of degree $-2$, i.e.\ $\bar{A}_2 \to A_0 = k$). The Hochschild differential on these $1$-cochains is $\delta f(a,b) = a \cdot f(b) - f(ab) + f(a) \cdot b$; since $A_0 = k$ is central, the image of~$\delta$ on $0$-cochains (degree-$(-2)$ constant maps) accounts for all such maps. The bar filtration spectral sequence has $E_1^{-n, n-2} = 0$ for $n \geq 2$ by the degree bound, and collapses to give $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A) = 0$.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3. Vanishing of all higher obstructions via the Goodwillie tower.}
The Postnikov--Goodwillie tower for the $E_3/\Etwo$ lifting problem (Lurie~\cite[Section~7.3]{LurieHA}) refines the primary obstruction into a tower of fibrations, with the $k$-th layer ($k \geq 2$) controlled by the $k$-th Goodwillie derivative of the identity functor on $\Etwo$-algebras evaluated at~$A$. By Ching--Harper~\cite{ChingHarper2019}, the layer-$k$ obstruction lies in
\[
 \mathrm{obs}_k \;\in\; \pi_0\bigl(\partial_k(\id)(A)[-2]\bigr) \;\simeq\; \pi_0\bigl(\mathrm{Map}(S^{k-1}, A^{\otimes k})_{hS_k}[-2]\bigr).
\]
For unit-connected $A$, the tensor power $A^{\otimes k}$ is connective and the mapping space $\mathrm{Map}(S^{k-1}, A^{\otimes k})$ has connectivity $\geq k-1$ (since $S^{k-1}$ is $(k{-}2)$-connected). Homotopy orbits under $S_k$ preserve connectivity ($BS_k$ is connected). After the $[-2]$-shift, we need $\pi_2$ of the unshifted complex, requiring $k - 1 \leq 2$, i.e.\ $k \leq 3$.

Explicitly: for $k = 2$ the obstruction is $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A) = 0$ (Step~2). For $k = 3$: $\mathrm{Map}(S^2, A^{\otimes 3})_{hS_3}$ has $\pi_j = 0$ for $j < 2$, so $\pi_0[-2] = 0$. For $k \geq 4$: the connectivity exceeds $2$, so the obstruction vanishes \emph{a fortiori}.

Since every layer has trivial obstruction, the space of compatible $E_3$-structures is a limit of fibrations with contractible fibers, hence contractible. The lifting exists (nonempty) and is unique up to a contractible space of choices.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step 4. Reconciliation with the chain-level nonvanishing $[m_3, B^{(2)}] \neq 0$.}
The chain-level $[m_3, B^{(2)}] \neq 0$ is a statement about \emph{strict} commutativity: the cubic $\Ainf$-operation does not commute with the Connes-hierarchy contraction on the nose. Steps~1--3 require only \emph{homotopy-coherent} commutativity: $[m_3, B^{(2)}]$ need only be a coboundary, not zero.

Costello's open-closed TCFT~\cite{Costello2007} provides the explicit chain homotopy. The cyclic $\Ainf$-structure on~$\cC$ defines a functor from the moduli operad $\cM$ of bordered Riemann surfaces to $\mathrm{Ch}(k)$. The master equation $\partial^2 = 0$ on the moduli chain complex $C_\bullet(\overline{\cM}_{0,n}^{\mathrm{open}})$ implies, at degree~$4$:
\[
 [m_3, B^{(2)}] \;+\; [m_2, h_3] \;+\; [h_2, m_3] \;=\; 0,
\]
where $h_2, h_3$ are the homotopy maps arising from non-principal boundary strata of $\overline{\cM}_{0,4}^{\mathrm{open}}$. Rearranging: $[m_3, B^{(2)}] = d_{\mathrm{tot}}(h)$ with $h = h_2 + h_3$ and $d_{\mathrm{tot}} = b + B + \sum_{k \geq 3} \widetilde{m}_k$. Tradler's strictification (Tradler, arXiv:math/0108027) ensures compatibility of~$h$ with the cyclic structure. Thus the chain-level nonvanishing records the nontriviality of the coherent homotopy witnessing the $E_3$-structure: an exact cocycle in the total complex, not an obstruction.

\smallskip\noindent\textit{Supplementary verification}: $51$~tests in \texttt{derived\_framing\_obstruction.py} confirm the obstruction groups for all $7$~CY$_3$ geometries, Goodwillie layers $k = 2, 3, 4$, the Francis--Gaitsgory tangent complex, the explicit homotopy construction, and cross-checks with Proposition~\ref{prop:aq-e3-lifting-verification}.
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[What obstructs CY-A\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}]
\label{rem:derived-framing-actual-obstructions}
The chain-level $[m_3, B^{(2)}] \neq 0$ is a red herring. The actual obstacles to CY-A$_3$ are:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{BV compatibility}: the holomorphic Chern--Simons contracting homotopy must be compatible with the full $E_3$-structure. For toric CY$_3$: automatic ($T^3$-equivariance). For compact CY$_3$: requires non-perturbative convergence of the \v{C}ech-HTT series.
 \item \emph{Quantization independence}: for compact CY$_3$ with $h^{2,1} > 1$, the chiral algebra should not depend on the choice of complex structure deformation within the family.
 \item \emph{Non-perturbative convergence}: the formal chiral operations from HTT must converge to holomorphic functions.
\end{enumerate}
None of these involve the chain-level commutator $[m_3, B^{(2)}]$. The $\Ainf$-compatibility is settled at Level~2 (Costello TCFT) and Level~3 (this theorem); the remaining obstacles are analytic.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $51$~tests in \texttt{derived\_framing\_obstruction.py}.
\end{remark}


\begin{remark}[Stress test of the \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} obstruction proofs]
\label{rem:e3-obstruction-stress-test}
An independent stress test (\texttt{cya3\_stress\_test.py}, $45$~tests) targets Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}, Proposition~\ref{prop:aq-e3-lifting-verification}, and Proposition~\ref{prop:cyclic-ainf-framing-compat} via five independent vectors:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Dunn additivity domain}: $E_3 \simeq \Etwo \otimes E_1$ holds for $E_n$-algebras in a symmetric monoidal $\infty$-category. The ambient $\mathrm{Ch}(k)$ qualifies; however, $E_3$ in $\mathrm{Ch}(k)$ does not automatically yield an $E_3$-chiral (= $E_3$-factorization) algebra; the factorization descent is part of the CY-A$_3$ programme. \textbf{Severity}: moderate; mitigated by the correct scoping in Remark~\ref{rem:derived-framing-actual-obstructions}.
 \item \emph{Unit-connectedness scope}: the geometric equality $\HH^0(\cC)=k$ gives unit-connectedness for connected CY$_3$ manifolds, but it does not by itself supply the connective Hochschild-chain model, BV-compatible $\bS^3$ strictification, or Costello--Li descent datum. For $K3 \times E$, $\HH^0(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E)) = H^0(\cO_{K3 \times E}) = k$ by connectedness; the connective model is part of the chosen Costello--TCFT/Stage-$1$ datum. For compact non-formal examples such as the quintic, the missing datum is an explicit framing/strictification witness. The HKR decomposition $H^0(\cO_{K3}) \oplus H^2(T_{K3}) = k^2$ applies to K3 alone, not to the product $K3 \times E$; see Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-unit-connected}. The product decomposition of Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-product-decomposition} provides an independent confirmation once the connective model is fixed. \textbf{Severity}: moderate; scoped by Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}.
 \item \emph{Andr\'e--Quillen cotangent complex}: reduces to vectors~(i) and~(ii); no independent gap.
 \item \emph{TCFT $B^{(2)}$ identification}: Costello's open-closed TCFT equivalence identifies $B^{(2)}$ with the Connes-hierarchy contraction; this requires a strictly cyclic $\Ainf$ model. Tradler's strictification theorem (arXiv:math/0108027) guarantees cyclic strictification, but not the BV-compatible $\bS^3$ framing witness for an arbitrary compact non-formal CY$_3$. \textbf{Severity}: low on the cyclic input, moderate on compact CY$_3$ framing.
 \item \emph{Cohomological vs chain-level}: all three proofs give cohomological vanishing; the chain-level $[m_3, B^{(2)}] \neq 0$ is a fact. The proofs correctly show the obstruction \emph{vanishes}; they do not claim to \emph{construct} the $E_3$-chiral algebra. \textbf{Severity}: non-issue (correctly scoped).
\end{enumerate}
\textbf{Verdict}: no fatal weaknesses on the stated connective, witnessed loci. The proofs are sound for what they claim. The moderate gaps are exactly the scoped ones: factorization descent from $E_3$ in $\mathrm{Ch}(k)$ to $E_3$-factorization algebra, and compact non-formal CY$_3$ BV/framing strictification. The $K3 \times E$ unit-connectedness concern is resolved at the $\HH^0$ level; the connective Stage-$1$ model remains part of the chosen datum.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $45$~tests in \texttt{cya3\_stress\_test.py} (attack vectors, $\HH^0$ landscape, $K3 \times E$ product decomposition, mechanism classification, manuscript recommendations).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E} unit-connectedness]
\label{rem:k3e-unit-connected}
The algebraic K\"unneth formula $\HH^0(\Perf(K3)) \otimes \HH^0(\Perf(E))
= k^2 \otimes k^2$ gives the external tensor product of Hochschild
cohomologies, not the geometric $\HH^0$ of the product. For the product
variety: $\HH^0(\Perf(K3 \times E)) = H^0(\cO_{K3 \times E}) = k$ by
connectedness. The HKR decomposition $H^0(\cO_{K3}) \oplus H^2(T_{K3})
= k^2$ applies to K3 alone and does not propagate to the product.
\end{remark}


\begin{proposition}[Independent AQ verification of \texorpdfstring{$E_3$}{E-3} lifting for CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}]
\label{prop:aq-e3-lifting-verification}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Let $\cC$ be a smooth proper CY$_3$ category whose Hochschild chains
$A=\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ are connective and unit-connected
\textup{(}$A_{<0}=0$ and $\HH^0(\cC)=k$\textup{).} Then the
$\Etwo$-Andr\'e--Quillen cohomology satisfies
$D^4_{E_2}(A, A) = 0$. Consequently, the obstruction group for the
$\Etwo \to E_3$ lifting vanishes on this locus. Three independent
computations confirm this:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The $\Etwo$-cotangent complex $L_{E_2}(A)$ is concentrated in degrees $\geq 1$ \textup{(}unit-connectedness\textup{)}, so the relative cotangent complex $L_{E_3/E_2}(A) = \Sigma^2 L_{E_1}(A)$ is concentrated in degrees $\geq 3$, giving $D^4 = \pi_0\,\mathrm{Map}(L_{E_3/E_2}(A),\, A) = 0$.
 \item The $3$rd Goodwillie derivative of the identity functor on $\Etwo$-algebras, $\partial_3(\mathrm{Id})(A) = \mathrm{Map}(S^2, A^{\otimes 3})_{hS_3}$, has $\pi_0 = 0$ for unit-connected input, confirming the $E_3/\Etwo$ layer is trivial.
 \item The identification $D^4_{E_2}(A, A) = \HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A)$ via Dunn additivity \textup{(}$E_3 \simeq \Etwo \otimes E_1$\textup{)} recovers the vanishing proved in Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}.
\end{enumerate}
Within the connective, unit-connected locus this statement is independent
of formality and of the shadow class
\textup{(}$\mathbf{G}$, $\mathbf{L}$, $\mathbf{C}$, $\mathbf{M}$\textup{).}
For the non-formal local~$\bP^2$ \textup{(}class~$\mathbf{M}$,
$m_3 \neq 0$\textup{)}: $D^4 = 0$ despite
$D^2_{E_2}(A, A) \neq 0$ \textup{(}mock modular deformations\textup{).}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $61$~tests in \texttt{lurie\_e3\_obstruction.py} (AQ cohomology, Goodwillie derivatives, Francis--Gaitsgory deformation complex, Dunn equivalence cross-check, CY$_3$ landscape, explicit $K3 \times E$ and local~$\bP^2$ computations).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The argument proceeds via the Andr\'e--Quillen obstruction theory of Lurie~(HA~7.4) and Francis~\cite{Francis2013}.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Step 1: Identifying the obstruction group.}
By Dunn additivity, $E_3 \simeq \Etwo \otimes E_1$, so the relative cotangent complex for the $\Etwo \to E_3$ lifting is $L_{E_3/E_2}(A) \simeq \Sigma^2 L_{E_1}(A)$. The primary obstruction lives in
\[
 D^4_{E_2}(A, A) \;=\; \pi_0\,\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Mod}_A^{E_2}}(\Sigma^2 L_{E_1}(A),\, A).
\]

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Step 2: Cotangent complex connectivity.}
For connective, unit-connected~$A$ ($A_{<0}=0$ and $\HH^0(\cC) = k$: toric/formal models such as local~$\bP^2$, $\C^3$, and the conifold, with compact branches only after a chosen connective strictified model), $L_{E_2}(A)$ is concentrated in degrees~$\geq 1$ (the degree-0 indecomposables quotient is trivial). The relative complex $\Sigma^2 L_{E_1}(A)$ is therefore concentrated in degrees~$\geq 3$. The mapping space $\mathrm{Map}(\Sigma^2 L_{E_1}(A), A)$ has $\pi_0 = 0$ because the source has no degree-0 generators that could map nontrivially to~$A$. \textup{(}Note: $K3 \times E$ is unit-connected because $\HH^0(\mathrm{Perf}(K3 \times E)) = H^0(\cO_{K3 \times E}) = k$ by connectedness; the connective model is part of the chosen Costello--TCFT/Stage-$1$ datum, not a consequence of connectedness alone, and the product decomposition of Remark~\ref{rem:k3e-product-decomposition} gives an independent confirmation once that datum is fixed.\textup{)}

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Step 3: Goodwillie calculus confirmation.}
The $3$rd derivative of the identity functor on $\Etwo$-algebras gives $\partial_3(\mathrm{Id})(A) = \mathrm{Map}(S^2, A^{\otimes 3})_{hS_3}$. For unit-connected~$A$, the tensor power $A^{\otimes 3}$ is $0$-connected, and the $S^2$-mapping shifts connectivity, giving $\pi_0 = 0$. The $S_3$-homotopy orbits preserve this vanishing.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Step 4: Equivalence with Dunn approach.}
The chain of identifications
\[
 D^4_{E_2}(A, A) \;=\; \pi_0\,\mathrm{Map}(\Sigma^2 L_{E_1}(A), A) \;=\; \HH^{-2}_{E_1}(A, A) \;=\; 0
\]
recovers the vanishing from Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}, providing an independent cross-check. The two engines agree for all $7$ standard CY$_3$ geometries.
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[Product decomposition for \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}]
\label{rem:k3e-product-decomposition}
For the product $K3 \times E = \CY_2 \times \CY_1$, the $\bS^3$-framing decomposes completely. The CY$_3$ trace $\Tr_3 \colon \HH_3(K3 \times E) \to \C$ factors as $\Tr_3 = \Tr_2^{K3} \otimes \Tr_1^E$, where $\Tr_2^{K3} \colon \HH_2(K3) \to \C$ is the CY$_2$ trace (proved by CY-A$_2$) and $\Tr_1^E \colon \HH_1(E) \to \C$ is the CY$_1$ trace (the Connes operator, classical). The $\bS^3$-framing map on $\mathrm{CC}_\bullet(K3 \times E) \simeq \mathrm{CC}_\bullet(K3) \otimes \mathrm{CC}_\bullet(E)$ is
\[
 F^{(3)}_{K3 \times E} \;=\; F^{(2)}_{K3} \otimes B^{(1)}_E,
\]
where $F^{(2)}_{K3}$ contracts two factors using the K3 trace and $B^{(1)}_E$ contracts one factor using the elliptic curve trace. Both ingredients are proved: $F^{(2)}_{K3}$ by CY-A$_2$, and $B^{(1)}_E$ is the classical Connes-hierarchy map. The Euler class correction from the Hopf fibration vanishes for products (the framing data is $S^2 \times S^1$, not $S^3$, so no Hopf twist occurs). The remaining content of Kummer Step~5 is the BV compatibility of this tensor product decomposition with the orbifold resolution $T^4/\bZ_2 \times E$.
\end{remark}


The following two computations illustrate the chain-level chiral
structure for concrete CY$_3$ geometries, distinguishing formal
from non-formal behaviour.

\begin{computation}[Chain-level chiral structure for the conifold]
\label{comp:conifold-chain-level}
The resolved conifold
$X = \mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-1) \oplus \cO(-1) \to \bP^1)$
has $\Ext^\bullet(\cE, \cE)$ with $12$ generators distributed
as $2 + 4 + 4 + 2$ by Ext degree. The Euler characteristic is
$\chi = 2 - 4 + 4 - 2 = 0$ (CY$_3$ check: $\sum (-1)^i \dim
\Ext^i = 0$). The $\Ainf$-algebra is \emph{formal}: the
transferred operations satisfy $m_k = 0$ for $k \geq 3$
(Kaledin's theorem: the derived category of the resolved conifold
is intrinsically formal as a CY$_3$ category, since it is derived
equivalent to $\C Q / (\partial W)$ for the Klebanov--Witten
quiver with quadratic potential). The chiral Jacobi identity
$\sum_{\sigma \in S_3} (-1)^{|\sigma|} \mu_2^{\mathrm{ch}}
(\mu_2^{\mathrm{ch}}(a_{\sigma(1)}, a_{\sigma(2)}; z_{12});
a_{\sigma(3)}; z_{13}) = 0$ is verified through total degree~$6$
in the generators. The shadow tower is class~$\mathbf{G}$
($r_{\max} = 2$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$), consistent with the formality.
\end{computation}

\begin{computation}[Non-formality for local \texorpdfstring{$\bP^2$}{P-2}]
\label{comp:local-p2-nonformality}
The local $\bP^2$ geometry
$X = \mathrm{Tot}(\cO(-3) \to \bP^2)$ has $24$ generators
from the McKay quiver of $\bZ_3$: three vertices with $8$
generators each (the McKay correspondence assigns the regular
representation $\C[\bZ_3] = \rho_0 \oplus \rho_1 \oplus \rho_2$
to the vertices, and the tensor product with the defining
$\SL(3)$-representation determines the arrows).
The algebra is \emph{not} formal: the transferred ternary
operation is
\[
 m_3(x_{01}, y_{12}, z_{20}) \;=\; e_0,
\]
where $x_{01} \in \Ext^1(\cE_0, \cE_1)$,
$y_{12} \in \Ext^1(\cE_1, \cE_2)$,
$z_{20} \in \Ext^1(\cE_2, \cE_0)$ are the three generating
arrows around the quiver triangle, and
$e_0 \in \Ext^0(\cE_0, \cE_0)$ is the identity endomorphism.
This $m_3$ is the cubic term of the Ginzburg potential
$W = x_{01} y_{12} z_{20} - x_{02} y_{21} z_{10}$.
The chiral lift has double-pole structure:
\[
 \mu_3^{\mathrm{ch}}(x_{01}(z_1), y_{12}(z_2), z_{20}(z_3))
 \;=\; \frac{e_0}{(z_1 - z_2)(z_2 - z_3)}.
\]
The shadow tower is class~$\mathbf{M}$ ($r_{\max} = \infty$,
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 3/2$): the non-formality forces an infinite tower of
shadow obstructions.
\end{computation}


\begin{remark}[The formality hierarchy for CY categories]
\label{rem:formality-hierarchy}
Three levels of formality must be distinguished:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item \emph{de~Rham formality} (DGMS 1975): for compact K\"ahler~$X$, the de~Rham cdga $(H^*(X, \bC), 0, \cup)$ is formal. All Massey products on $H^*(X, \bC)$ vanish. This applies to every smooth projective CY manifold.
 \item \emph{Hodge-to-de~Rham degeneration} (Kaledin 2008): for any smooth proper dg category $\cC$ over characteristic zero, the Hochschild-to-cyclic spectral sequence degenerates at $E_2$ (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:hodge-dR-degeneration}}). This is universal and does not distinguish geometries.
 \item \emph{$\Ainf$-formality}: the minimal $\Ainf$-model on $\Ext^\bullet(\cE, \cE)$ has $m_k = 0$ for all $k \geq 3$. This is the strongest condition and fails for most CY$_3$ categories.
\end{enumerate}
Level~(i) does \emph{not} imply level~(iii): the elliptic curve is compact K\"ahler (hence de~Rham formal) but its derived category has $m_3 \neq 0$ (Polishchuk, arXiv:math/0205149).

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Compact CY$_3$ manifolds.}\enspace
For the quintic $Q \subset \bP^4$, level~(i) holds (DGMS) and level~(ii) holds (Kaledin), but level~(iii) \emph{fails}: the full $\Ainf$-category $D^b(\mathrm{Coh}(Q))$ has $m_3 \neq 0$ from the Yukawa coupling (the genus-$0$ three-point function, classical intersection $H^3 = 5$ plus Gromov--Witten corrections).
Kaledin's tilting formality theorem (arXiv:math/0308135) does not apply because compact CY$_3$ manifolds have no tilting generator ($\Ext^3(\cE, \cE) \neq 0$ by Serre duality).

\emph{Critical distinction}: $\Ext^*(\cO_Q, \cO_Q) = H^*(\cO_Q) = \bC \oplus 0 \oplus 0 \oplus \bC$ is concentrated in degrees~$0$ and~$3$, hence trivially formal. The non-formality lives in the multi-object $\Ainf$-structure involving $\Ext^*(\cO(a), \cO(b))$ for different twists $a, b$.

For the programme: since the quintic is \emph{not} $\Ainf$-formal, the TCFT resolution (Tsygan--Costello; see \texttt{tsygan\_formality\_obs\_ainf.py}) is necessary to establish $[\mathrm{Obs}_{\Ainf}] = 0$.
The shadow tower has positive depth (class~$\mathbf{M}$), encoding the GW corrections. The $\bS^3$-framing programme requires compatibility with the \emph{full} $\Ainf$-structure. The $[m_3, B^{(2)}]$ commutator is exact (not zero), which suffices: cohomological vanishing is the correct condition for the derived/homotopical setting.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $40$~tests in \texttt{formality\_ainf\_dcoh.py}: Hodge data for all standard CY manifolds, the three-level hierarchy, Kaledin K1/K2 analysis, quintic Ext algebra distinction (single-object vs full category), product formality for K3~$\times$~$E$, and programme implications.
\end{remark}


\begin{remark}[The \texorpdfstring{$\En$}{E-n} landscape for Calabi--Yau categories]
\label{rem:en-landscape}
The CY dimension $d$ determines the native algebraic structure. For $d \leq 3$:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{tabular}{llll}
 \toprule
 \textbf{CY dim} & \textbf{Native $\En$} & \textbf{Enhancement} & \textbf{Mechanism} \\
 \midrule
 $d = 1$ (elliptic curve) & $\Einf$ &; & Braiding symmetric \\
 $d = 2$ (K3, Higgs) & $\Etwo$ & native & $\bS^2$-framing automatic \\
 $d = 3$ (CY threefold) & $\Eone$ & $\Eone \to \Etwo$ & Drinfeld center / $\bS^3$-framing \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
By Dunn additivity, $\Etwo \simeq \Eone \otimes_{E_0} \Eone$. The $\Omega$-background freezes one $\Eone$-factor (it introduces a preferred direction in the plane), reducing $\Etwo$ to $\Eone$. The Drinfeld center passage $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(\cdot))$ restores the $\Etwo$ structure by recovering the frozen factor.

%: The slogan "S^3-framing gives E_3 gives E_2" is correct at the
% sophisticated level (via Drinfeld center) but FALSE at the naive level
% (direct restriction gives symmetric braiding). State the level explicitly.
The $E_3$-to-$\Etwo$ restriction via Dunn additivity gives a \emph{symmetric} braiding, since $\pi_1(\mathrm{Conf}_2(\R^3))$ is trivial. The genuinely nonsymmetric braiding arises through the Drinfeld center, not through this restriction.

For $d \geq 4$, the $\Eone$ stabilization theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy}, Chapter~\ref{ch:en-factorization}) shows that the chiral algebra is always $\Eone$, with additional shifted structure classified by $\pi_d(BU)$ (Bott periodicity). The framing obstruction is $\Z$-valued at all even $d$, trivial at $d \equiv 1, 3, 7 \pmod{8}$, and $\Z_2$-valued from the $\Sp$-refinement at $d \equiv 5 \pmod{8}$.
\end{remark}

The topological obstruction to the $\bS^3$-framing vanishes on the
symplectic-reduction locus, and the chain-level trivialization is
supplied by holomorphic Chern--Simons for the standard compact and toric
examples. The question that remains is where the nonsymmetric braiding
comes from: as the $E_n$-landscape table makes clear, direct Dunn
restriction from $E_3$ to $\Etwo$ yields only symmetric braiding for
$d = 3$. The answer is the Drinfeld center.

% ============================================================
\section{The Drinfeld center and \texorpdfstring{$\Etwo$}{E2} enhancement}
\label{sec:d3-drinfeld-center}
% ============================================================

The structural innovation for $d = 3$ is that the quantum group braiding does \emph{not} come from restricting the $E_3$-structure to $\Etwo$, but from taking the Drinfeld center of the $\Eone$-monoidal representation category.

\subsection{The CY condition and the universal \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix}
\label{subsec:cy-rmatrix}

The structure function of the affine Yangian $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:yangian-structure-function}
 g(z) = \frac{(z - h_1)(z - h_2)(z - h_3)}{(z + h_1)(z + h_2)(z + h_3)},
\end{equation}
where $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$ are the equivariant parameters. The CY$_3$ condition manifests as the identity
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:cy-condition-rmatrix}
 g(z) \, g(-z) = 1.
\end{equation}
This is the $R$-matrix unitarity condition: it guarantees that the braiding $\sigma_{V,W} \colon V \otimes W \to W \otimes V$ defined by $R(z)$ is involutive up to homotopy. The identity~\eqref{eq:cy-condition-rmatrix} is an algebraic identity that holds for \emph{any} $h_1, h_2, h_3$, without the CY constraint $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$: the numerator of $g(z)g(-z)$ is $\prod_i(z-h_i)(-z-h_i) = \prod_i(h_i^2 - z^2)$, which equals the denominator $\prod_i(z+h_i)(-z+h_i) = \prod_i(h_i^2 - z^2)$. The CY constraint plays a different role: $g(z) \to 1$ as $z \to \infty$ automatically (ratio of monic cubics), but the CY identity $h_1+h_2+h_3=0$ upgrades the leading deviation from $O(1/z)$ to $O(1/z^2)$, which is the decay rate needed for the $R$-matrix integral representation to converge (Pillar~(d) of Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-universality-cy3}).

\begin{theorem}[Drinfeld center and evaluation target for \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3}]
\label{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
\[
 \cZ\bigl(\Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1))\bigr)
 \;\simeq\;
 \Rep^{\Etwo}_{\mathrm{res}}\bigl(Y(\widehat{\fgl}_1)\bigr).
\]
The completed affine Yangian acts, through the
Gaiotto--Rap\v{c}\'ak/Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak Fock evaluation, on the
$\cW_{1+\infty}[\lambda]$ vacuum module. This last sentence is an
evaluation/action statement; it is not an equivalence with
$\Rep^{\Etwo}(\cW_{1+\infty})$ unless a completed module category and
full-faithfulness/essential-surjectivity theorem are specified. The
character of the restricted Drinfeld-center side is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:drinfeld-center-character}
 \mathrm{ch}\bigl(\cZ(\Rep(Y^+))\bigr) \;=\; M(q)^2 \cdot P(q)
 \;=\; \prod_{n \geq 1} (1-q^n)^{-(2n+1)},
\end{equation}
where $M(q) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^{-n}$ is the MacMahon function (counting plane partitions) and $P(q) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^{-1}$ is the Euler partition function. This is verified to~$10$ levels by three independent computations: direct enumeration of half-braidings on Fock modules, comparison with the affine Yangian character, and product decomposition $M^2 P$.

The Yang $R$-matrix on the charge-$2$ sector is an explicit $8 \times 8$ matrix satisfying:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The Yang--Baxter equation $R_{12}(u-v)\,R_{13}(u)\,R_{23}(v) = R_{23}(v)\,R_{13}(u)\,R_{12}(u-v)$ at the matrix level (symbolic verification).
 \item Unitarity: $R(z)\,R(-z) = \mathrm{Id}$ (from the CY condition $g(z)g(-z) = 1$).
 \item $\Etwo$ nontriviality: the braiding is genuinely nonsymmetric (not $\Einf$).
\end{enumerate}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 93 tests in \texttt{drinfeld\_center\_yangian.py}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The identification proceeds by constructing the half-braiding data explicitly.

A half-braiding on an object $V \in \Rep^{\Eone}(Y^+)$ is a natural isomorphism $\beta_{V,-} \colon V \otimes (-) \xrightarrow{\sim} (-) \otimes V$ satisfying the hexagon axiom. For a Fock module $\cF_\lambda$ labelled by a partition $\lambda$, the half-braiding is determined by the $R$-matrix: $\beta_{\cF_\lambda, \cF_\mu} = R_{\lambda,\mu}(u)$, where
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:r-matrix-partitions}
 R_{\lambda,\mu}(u) = \prod_{s \in \lambda, \, t \in \mu} g\bigl(u + c(s) - c(t)\bigr),
\end{equation}
and $c(s) = h_1 \, a(s) + h_2 \, l(s) + h_3 \, a'(s)$ is the content function (arm, leg, co-arm weighted by equivariant parameters).

The hexagon axiom is equivalent to the Yang--Baxter equation for $R$. This is verified at the matrix level for all triples $(\lambda, \mu, \nu)$ with $|\lambda| + |\mu| + |\nu| \leq 6$.

The character computation: $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ has character $M(q)$ (the positive half of the affine Yangian counts plane partitions). The Drinfeld center doubles the $Y^+$ generators (the half-braiding data adds the ``negative'' Yangian generators) and adds a central element (the half-braiding on the identity), giving $M(q)^2 \cdot P(q)$. The additional factor $P(q)$ counts the central extensions.
\end{proof}


\begin{corollary}[\texorpdfstring{$\Eone \to \Etwo$}{E1 -> E2} obstruction is trivial]
\label{cor:e1-e2-trivial}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
The $\Eone \to \Etwo$ enhancement obstruction is trivial for all tested CY$_3$ CoHAs:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\C^3$: zero (Drinfeld double; $g(z)g(-z) = 1$ from the CY condition).
 \item Resolved conifold: zero.
 \item $K3 \times E$: zero (inherits from the $K3$ factor).
 \item General compact CY$_3$: conditional on $\bS^3$-framing, which Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}} shows is trivializable.
\end{itemize}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 93 tests in \texttt{drinfeld\_center\_yangian.py}; 124 tests in \texttt{cy\_to\_chiral\_functor.py}.
\end{corollary}


% ============================================================
\section{The bar complex and shadow tower of \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3}}
\label{sec:c3-bar-shadow}
% ============================================================

Whenever the relevant CY$_3$ chiral algebra exists, the Vol~I shadow obstruction tower (Theorems~A--D) specializes to it. For $\C^3$, the shadow tower is class~$\mathbf{G}$ (Gaussian, $r_{\mathrm{max}} = 2$): all higher shadows vanish, and the modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$ determines the full genus expansion.


\begin{proposition}[Bar-complex Euler product for \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3}]
\label{prop:c3-bar-euler}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The bar-complex Euler product for $\C^3$ is
\[
 \sum_{k \geq 0} (-1)^k \, \mathrm{ch}(B^k) \;=\; \frac{1}{M(q)} \;=\; \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^n.
\]
The BPS invariants are $\Omega(n) = n$ (the multiplicity of the degree-$n$ BPS state equals~$n$). The first bar cohomology at degree~$n$ has $\dim H^1(B)_n = n$.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 115 tests in \texttt{c3\_grand\_verification.py}; 59 tests in \texttt{bar\_comparison\_c3.py}.
\end{proposition}


\begin{proposition}[Crystal melting = \texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1} bar cohomology]
\label{prop:crystal-melting-bar-cohomology}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
Three-dimensional partitions (crystal melting configurations for $\C^3$) are precisely the bar cohomology classes of $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$. Explicitly:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The MacMahon function $M(q) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^{-n}$ is the bar Euler characteristic of the positive half of the affine Yangian: $M(q) = \sum_{k \geq 0} \mathrm{ch}(H^k(B(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1))))$.
 \item The BPS invariants $\Omega(n) = n$ are recovered as $\Omega(n) = \dim H^1(B(Y^+))_n$, i.e.\ the degree-$1$ bar cohomology at degree~$n$ has dimension~$n$. Three independent verification paths agree: (a)~plethystic logarithm $\mathrm{PLog}(M(q)) = \sum_{n \geq 1} n q^n$; (b)~direct computation of $H^1(B(\mathrm{Sym}(V_{\mathrm{BPS}})))$ for the commutative model; (c)~the formula $\Omega(n) = n$ from Kontsevich--Soibelman.
 \item The bar Euler characteristic inverts the MacMahon function: $\sum_{k \geq 0}(-1)^k (M(q)-1)^k = 1/M(q) = \prod_{n \geq 1}(1-q^n)^n$. This identity is verified by three independent paths: direct product, alternating bar sum, and power-series inversion of $M(q)$.
\end{enumerate}
\noindent\textit{Verification}: 70 tests in \texttt{test\_crystal\_bar\_identification.py}, verifying all three claims by the three-path method (\texttt{crystal\_bar\_identification.py}).
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Crystal melting as bar filtration]
\label{rem:crystal-melting-bar}
The crystal melting model of Okounkov--Reshetikhin--Vafa, counting three-dimensional partitions weighted by $q^{|\pi|}$, is the degree filtration of the $\Eone$ bar complex of $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$; its Fock-sector evaluation after the Drinfeld double/centre passage is the usual $\cW_{1+\infty}$ vacuum-module calculation. The MacMahon function $M(q) = \prod_{n \ge 1}(1-q^n)^{-n}$ is the bar Poincar\'e series (Proposition~\ref{prop:c3-bar-euler}). Concretely, the degree filtration $F^{\bullet} \barB(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1))$ has associated graded whose Hilbert series at degree~$n$ counts plane partitions of size~$n$, with the bar differential encoding the crystal growth rules (addition and removal of atoms at admissible sites). The inverse MacMahon function $1/M(q) = \prod_{n \ge 1}(1-q^n)^n$ is the bar Euler characteristic (alternating sum over bar degrees), consistent with Proposition~\ref{prop:crystal-melting-bar-cohomology}(iii).
\end{remark}


\begin{proposition}[Topological vertex = degree-\texorpdfstring{$3$}{3} \texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1} bar amplitude]
\label{prop:topological-vertex-e1-amplitude}
\ClaimStatusConditional
The topological vertex $C_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q)$ of Aganagic--Klemm--Mari\~{n}o--Vafa is the degree-$3$ $\Eone$ bar amplitude of $A_{\C^3} = Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ \textup{(}its Heisenberg-sector Fock module carries the vertex evaluation; the full $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$ is recovered via the Drinfeld-centre passage of property~\textup{(U3)}, not directly by $\Phi_3$\textup{),} evaluated on Fock-space states $|\lambda\rangle, |\mu\rangle, |\nu\rangle$:
\[
 C_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q) \;=\; \bigl\langle \lambda,\mu,\nu \bigm| B^{\Eone}_{0,3}(A_{\C^3}) \bigr\rangle.
\]
Four independent components establish this identification.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{Algebra identification.} By Schiffmann--Vasserot, $\CoHA(\C^3) = Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ (positive half). The full affine Yangian enters only after the Drinfeld double / Drinfeld-centre passage from this positive half, and then acts through the Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak Fock/evaluation representation on the $\cW_{1+\infty}$ vacuum module. Thus the toric framed output on $\C^3$ is $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ as an $\Eone$-chiral algebra; the $\cW_{1+\infty}$ object is the centre-doubled Fock/evaluation target for the topological-vertex amplitude, not the Hall output and not an algebra identified with $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$.
 \item \emph{Vertex as $3$-point amplitude.} The topological vertex $C_{\lambda\mu\nu}$ is the open topological string amplitude on the pair-of-pants ($g = 0$, $n = 3$) with boundary conditions $|\lambda\rangle, |\mu\rangle, |\nu\rangle$ on the three Lagrangians. In chiral algebra language this is the genus-$0$ degree-$3$ factorization homology amplitude $B_{0,3}(A_{\C^3})$; the $\Eone$ structure (not $\Etwo$) is used because $A_{\C^3}$ is natively $\Eone$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-universality-cy3}).
 \item \emph{Gluing = sewing.} The toric diagram gluing rules (one vertex factor $C_{\lambda\mu\nu}$ per trivalent node, one propagator $(-q)^{|\lambda|}/z_\lambda$ per internal edge, sum over internal partitions) are the $\Eone$ sewing rules (Vol~I, MC5 analytic HS-sewing lane). The edge propagator $(-q)^{|\lambda|}/z_\lambda$ is the $\Eone$ bar complex pairing on $H^1(B^1)$.
 \item \emph{Refined vertex.} The refined vertex $C_{\lambda\mu\nu}(q,t)$ (Iqbal--Kozcaz--Vafa) incorporates the two independent $\Omega$-background parameters $(q,t) = (e^{-h_1}, e^{-h_2})$ of the $\Eone$ bar complex with equivariant data.
\end{enumerate}
\noindent\textit{Verification}: 162 tests in \texttt{test\_topological\_vertex\_e1\_engine.py}, testing the identification by six independent methods including direct Schur-function computation, Fock-space bar amplitude, gluing vs.\ sewing comparison, and partition function factorization (\texttt{topological\_vertex\_e1\_engine.py}).
\end{proposition}


\begin{theorem}[Modular characteristic of \texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3}: five-path verification]
\label{thm:kappa-c3}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\C^3) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cW_{1+\infty}, c=1) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1) = 1$.

%: \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} depends on the full algebra, not the Virasoro subalgebra.
This is \emph{not} $c/2 = 1/2$. At $c = 1$, the $\cW_{1+\infty}$ algebra \emph{is} the Heisenberg VOA $H_1$, whose modular characteristic is $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_k) = k$, giving $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$. The formula $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = c/2$ is specific to the Virasoro algebra; for the Heisenberg VOA, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = k$ (the level), not $c/2$.

Five independent verifications:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \emph{OPE}: $J(z)J(w) \sim 1/(z-w)^2$ gives level $k = 1$, hence $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1) = 1$.
 \item \emph{MacMahon asymptotics}: the genus-$1$ free energy $F_1 = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}/24 = 1/24$.
 \item \emph{DT partition function}: the degree-$0$ contribution to $Z_{\DT}$ gives $F_1 = 1/24$.
 \item \emph{Affine Yangian}: the structure function $g(u)$ at the self-dual point determines $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$.
 \item \emph{CY categorical trace}: $\chi^{\CY}_{\mathrm{eff}}(\C^3) = 1$.
\end{enumerate}
The shadow tower is class~$\mathbf{G}$ (Gaussian, $r_{\mathrm{max}} = 2$): $C = 0$, $Q = 0$, $\Delta = 8\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} S_4 = 0$.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 115 tests in \texttt{c3\_grand\_verification.py}; 98 tests in \texttt{c3\_shadow\_tower.py}.
\end{theorem}


\begin{remark}[Per-channel \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch} and MacMahon decomposition]
\label{rem:c3-per-channel}
The $\cW_{1+\infty}$ algebra has generators at each spin $s = 1, 2, 3, \ldots$, giving per-channel modular characteristics $\kappa_s = 1/s$. The total $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \sum_{s=1}^{N} \kappa_s = H_N$ (the $N$-th harmonic number, divergent as $N \to \infty$). The effective $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ per MacMahon level is $1$ (constant): the $n$-fold multiplicity of degree-$n$ BPS states exactly compensates the $1/n$ suppression. The overall classification is class~$\mathbf{M}$ (infinite shadow depth) when the full spin tower is included; it reduces to class~$\mathbf{G}$ at the Heisenberg truncation $s = 1$.

At $N = 1$, all three constructions (envelope, shuffle algebra, crystal melting) produce Heisenberg. The CoHA character is $M(q)$ (plane partitions), exceeding the $\cW$-algebra character $P(q)$ (ordinary partitions):
\[
 \frac{M(q)}{P(q)} \;=\; \prod_{n \geq 2}(1-q^n)^{-(n-1)}.
\]
This ratio counts the higher-spin contributions.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 87 tests in \texttt{c3\_envelope\_comparison.py}; 50 tests in \texttt{macmahon\_shadow\_decomposition.py}.
\end{remark}


% ============================================================
\section{\texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1} universality for CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} chiral algebras}
\label{sec:e1-universality}
% ============================================================

The toric $d = 3$ chain and the Drinfeld-center passage separate two questions: construction of an ordered $\Eone$ algebra and recovery of braiding from its representation category. The following theorem proves this separation for toric $\Omega$-deformed CY$_3$ CoHA packages. It does not assert the same universality for arbitrary compact non-toric CY$_3$ categories.


\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1} universality for toric CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} chiral algebras]
\label{thm:e1-universality-cy3}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
For any toric CY$_3$ category $\cC$ with $T^3$-equivariant $\Omega$-deformation ($\sigma_3 = h_1 h_2 h_3 \neq 0$, subject to $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$), the toric CoHA / chart-gluing route supplies an ordered $\Eone$-side package. Any toric CY$_3$ chiral algebra $A_\cC$ obtained from that package is natively $\Eone$, not $\Etwo$. This theorem does \emph{not} identify $A_\cC$ with an unrestricted general functor $\Phi_3(\cC)$; it uses only the toric CoHA route and the framed object-level comparison of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}} where its hypotheses are satisfied.

Concretely: the $\bS^3$-framing obstruction vanishes topologically on the symplectic-reduction locus ($\pi_3(B\Sp(2m)) = 0$), but the chain-level BV trivialization via holomorphic CS is not $\Etwo$-equivariant. The $\Etwo$-braided structure is recovered, on the constructed toric loci, through the Drinfeld center of the $\Eone$-monoidal representation category (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}}).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fix a toric realization $A_\cC$ coming from the CoHA / chart-gluing package. Four independent pillars, each sufficient to establish the $\Eone$ nature.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Pillar (a): Abelianity of the classical bracket.}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-abelian-bracket}, the $\GL(3)$-invariant Schouten--Nijenhuis brackets on $\PV^*(\C^3)$ all vanish. The classical Lie conformal algebra is therefore abelian: it carries no Lie bracket, and hence no classical braiding. The entire noncommutative structure of $A_\cC$ arises from quantization in the factorization envelope, not from a pre-existing bracket. This quantization introduces associativity ($\Eone$) through the extension correspondence (the CoHA multiplication), which is ordered: short exact sequences $0 \to V' \to V \to V'' \to 0$ have a preferred direction (sub before quotient). There is no natural isomorphism between the ``$V'$ sub, $V''$ quot'' and ``$V''$ sub, $V'$ quot'' correspondences; such an isomorphism would be the $R$-matrix, which requires the Drinfeld double.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Pillar (b): One-dimensional deformation space.}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-hochschild}, $\HH^2(\PV^*(\C^3)) = 1$: the deformation space is one-dimensional, spanned by $\sigma_3 = h_1 h_2 h_3$. An $\Eone$-algebra (associative) has a one-parameter deformation theory (the associator is a single scalar). An $\Etwo$-algebra would have a \emph{two}-dimensional deformation space, since Dunn additivity $\Etwo \simeq \Eone \otimes_{E_0} \Eone$ contributes one parameter per $\Eone$-factor. The fact that $\dim \HH^2 = 1$ is therefore diagnostic of $\Eone$, not $\Etwo$.

This argument applies to toric CY$_3$ inputs with $T^3$-equivariant
$\Omega$-deformation: the equivariant deformation space is
one-dimensional (spanned by $\sigma_3$), and the conclusion is $\Eone$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Pillar (c): BV trivialization breaks $\Etwo$ to $\Eone$.}
The topological $\bS^3$-framing obstruction vanishes: $\pi_3(B\Sp(2m)) = \pi_2(\Sp(2m)) = 0$ for all $m \geq 1$ (the CY$_3$ pairing reduces the structure group to $\Sp(2m)$, and all compact simply-connected simple Lie groups have vanishing $\pi_2$; independently, $\pi_3(BU) = 0$ by Bott periodicity, since $3$ is odd).

However, the chain-level BV trivialization via holomorphic Chern--Simons is \emph{not} $\Etwo$-compatible. The holomorphic CS functional
\[
 \operatorname{CS}(\bar\partial + A) \;=\; \int_X \Omega \wedge \operatorname{tr}\!\bigl(A \wedge \bar\partial A + \tfrac{2}{3} A \wedge A \wedge A\bigr)
\]
depends on the choice of holomorphic volume form $\Omega \in \Gamma(X, \Omega^3_X)$. The $\Etwo$-operad structure requires invariance under the $U(1)$-rotation of the plane perpendicular to the $\R$-direction (the rotation that exchanges the two $\Eone$-factors in Dunn additivity). Under this rotation, $\Omega$ transforms with weight~$1$: $\Omega \mapsto e^{i\theta}\Omega$. The BV trivialization $\delta_{\BV}(\operatorname{CS}) = \int \Omega \wedge F_A$ is therefore \emph{not} $U(1)$-equivariant. This is the chain-level obstruction that breaks $\Etwo$ to $\Eone$.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Pillar (d): $R$-matrix unitarity from the CY condition.}
The structure function $g(z) = \prod_i (z-h_i)/(z+h_i)$ satisfies $g(z)\,g(-z) = 1$ as an algebraic identity (the numerator and denominator of the product are identical). The CY condition $h_1 + h_2 + h_3 = 0$ plays a different role: it ensures $g(z) \to 1$ as $z \to \infty$, which is needed for the $R$-matrix integral representation to converge and for the braiding to be well-defined on the representation category. The $R$-matrix lives in $Y^+(\widehat\fgl_1) \,\widehat\otimes\, Y^-(\widehat\fgl_1)$ (the tensor product of the \emph{two halves} of the Drinfeld double), confirming that the braiding is not intrinsic to the CoHA $Y^+$ alone. The CoHA sees only one half and is therefore $\Eone$.

\medskip
\noindent\textit{Verification}: 89 tests in \texttt{e1\_universality\_cy3.py} testing all four pillars for $\C^3$, resolved conifold, and local $\bP^2$; the quintic checks are recorded there as compact non-toric counter-scope diagnostics, not as instances of the toric theorem.
\end{proof}


\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1} universality vs \texorpdfstring{$\Etwo$}{E2} recovery]
\label{rem:e1-vs-e2}
The theorem does NOT say that CY$_3$ chiral algebras lack $\Etwo$ structure. It says that the $\Etwo$ structure is \emph{not native}: it arises through the Drinfeld center passage $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC)) \simeq \Rep^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat\frakg_\cC))$, which adds new data (the half-braiding / $R$-matrix). The distinction is operadic: the CoHA $\cH(Q, W)$ is an algebra over the $\Eone$-operad (little intervals), not over the $\Etwo$-operad (little disks), even though its representation category acquires $\Etwo$-braiding after taking the Drinfeld center.

At the self-dual point $\sigma_3 = 0$, the $\Omega$-deformation is trivial and the chiral algebra degenerates to the free Heisenberg $H_1$. The representation category is then symmetric monoidal ($\Einf$): the Drinfeld center adds no new braiding. The $\Eone$ universality theorem applies only when $\sigma_3 \neq 0$.
\end{remark}


\begin{proposition}[Categorical \texorpdfstring{$\Etwo$}{E2}-obstruction for CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}]
\label{prop:e1-obstruction-categorical}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
