%: correct at the motivic Hall algebra level, but naive BCH does NOT
% reproduce phi_{0,1} multiplicities. State the level explicitly.
The three levels of Conjecture~\ref{conj:shadow-bps-dt} form a hierarchy: \ref{level:motivic} $\Rightarrow$ \ref{level:virtual} $\Rightarrow$ \ref{level:genus1}. The motivic identification \ref{level:motivic} is the fundamental statement; the genus-$1$ identity \ref{level:genus1} is a numerical shadow of the full coalgebra comparison. The intermediate level \ref{level:virtual} captures the tautological intersection theory of $\overline{\mathcal{M}}_g$ (the Faber--Pandharipande coefficients $\lambda_g^{\mathrm{FP}}$) but loses the off-diagonal data in the motivic Hall algebra.

Two distinctions control the passage between levels:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{Scalar vs.\ refined shadow.} The scalar shadow partition function \eqref{eq:v3-shadow-partition-function} uses only $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ and gives $\prod (1-q^n)^{-\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}$. For $\C^3$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$ gives $\prod (1-q^n)^{-1} = P(q)$ (the Euler partition function), not $M(q) = \prod (1-q^n)^{-n}$ (the MacMahon function). The MacMahon function requires the refined shadow with $\Omega(n) = n$, which comes from the full bar cohomology $H^1(B^{\Eone})_n$. The scalar shadow captures $F_1$ but not the full $Z_{\DT}$.
\item \emph{Uniform-weight vs.\ multi-weight.} At genus $g \geq 2$, the scalar formula $F_g = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot \lambda_g^{\mathrm{FP}}$ holds on the uniform-weight lane (Vol~I, Theorem~D). For CY$_3$ chiral algebras with generators of multiple conformal weights, the cross-channel correction $\delta F_g^{\mathrm{cross}} \neq 0$ modifies the higher-genus free energies. The full DT free energies require these corrections.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Detailed evidence for Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:shadow-bps-dt}}]
\label{rem:shadow-bps-evidence-detail}
The four cases in Table~\ref{tab:shadow-bps-evidence} have different logical dependencies.

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]

\item \textbf{$\C^3$: proved at the motivic level.}
The toric CY-to-chiral chain produces $A_{\C^3}=Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$; the full $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c=1$ is recovered only after Drinfeld doubling/centre passage. The bar Euler product is $1/M(q) = \prod (1-q^n)^n$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:c3-bar-euler}), inverting the MacMahon function. The bar cohomology gives $\Omega(n) = n = \Omega_{\DT}(n)$ at all degrees ($115$ tests). The motivic comparison holds: $B^{\Eone}(Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1))$ as a graded $\Eone$-coalgebra matches the motivic DT coalgebra of $\C^3$ via the Schiffmann--Vasserot identification $\CoHA(\C^3) = Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$.

\item \textbf{Toric CY$_3$: proved via the CoHA.}
For any smooth toric CY$_3$ $X_\Sigma$ with McKay quiver atlas $(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)_{\alpha \in I}$, the toric chart gluing theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-chart-gluing}) assembles the global $\Eone$-chiral algebra $A_{X_\Sigma}$. The $\CoHA$ of $(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ is the positive half of the quantum vertex chiral group $G(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ (Kontsevich--Soibelman, Schiffmann--Vasserot, Davison). The $A_\infty$-maps of $A_{X_\Sigma}$ (with $m_2$ given by the Hall product and $m_k$ for $k \geq 3$ by higher-order contact terms) are encoded in the bar differential of $B^{\mathrm{ord}}(A_{X_\Sigma})$, and the bar Euler characteristic recovers the DT partition function. The CoHA is the \emph{algebra} whose structure maps appear in the bar differential; the bar complex is the \emph{coalgebra} constructed from it.

\item \textbf{$K3 \times E$: observational at genus~$1$, motivic level uses the formal $\Phi_3$ locus.}
On the formal Hochschild locus containing $K3\times E$, the CY-to-chiral
correspondence at $d=3$ supplies the candidate
$A_{K3 \times E}^{(K3,E)}
=\Phi_3^{(K3,E)}(D^b(\Coh(K3\times E)))$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}, with the formal-locus scope understood).
The genus-$1$ data is read from distinct invariants, not from one scalar.
The total-space Hodge supertrace is
\[
\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3\times E)
=\sum_q(-1)^q h^{0,q}(K3\times E)=0
\]
by K\"unneth and odd-dimensional Serre duality. The rank-$3$
Heisenberg--Cartan number attached to the Stage-$2$ Igusa/BKM
specialisation is a separate route invariant, denoted here
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}=3$, not the total-space
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$. The Borcherds automorphic weight is
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}=5=\mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5)$. The automorphic/BKM
genus-$1$ coefficient is therefore $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}/24=5/24$; it
does not follow from $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$, which vanishes on compact
CY$_3$ total spaces. The full DT partition function
$Z_{\DT}(K3 \times E)=C/\Delta_5^2$ involves the Igusa cusp form, whose
Borcherds product formula
$\Delta_5=p\prod(1-p^nq^lr^m)^{f(4nm-l^2)}$ is a three-variable
generalisation of the bar Euler product (Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-times-e}).
The passage from the scalar shadow to the full $\Delta_5$ requires the
BKM root system, which encodes all BPS states across all charge lattice
directions.

\item \textbf{Quintic: conditional on $\Phi_3$.}
The quintic has $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24 = -25/3$ (this is one of the cases where $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ holds; Theorem~\ref{thm:chi-neq-kappa}). The genus-$1$ DT free energy $F_1 = -25/72$ matches. The BCOV holomorphic anomaly equation (Theorem~\ref{thm:hae-mc-structural}) provides the higher-genus comparison, but the existence of $A_X$ as an $\Eone$-chiral algebra for the quintic depends on the CY-to-chiral correspondence at $d = 3$.

\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}


\subsection{The motivic shadow zeta function}
\label{subsec:motivic-shadow-zeta}

The shadow--BPS/DT correspondence (Conjecture~\ref{conj:shadow-bps-dt}) operates at three levels, the strongest being the motivic identification~\eqref{eq:shadow-dt-motivic}. The \emph{motivic shadow zeta function} packages the motivic bar complex data into a single Dirichlet series whose specialisations recover both the numerical shadow zeta (Vol~I) and arithmetic invariants of the underlying CY$_3$.

\begin{definition}[Motivic shadow zeta function; \ClaimStatusConjectured]
\label{def:motivic-shadow-zeta}
Let $\cC$ be a CY$_3$ category and $A_\cC$ its $\Eone$-chiral algebra (conditional on Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} for non-toric geometries). The \emph{motivic shadow zeta function} is the Dirichlet series with coefficients in $K_0(\mathrm{Var}/\C)[\bL^{\pm 1/2}]$:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:motivic-shadow-zeta}
 \zeta_{A_\cC}^{\mathrm{mot}}(s) \;=\; \sum_{n \geq 1} [B_n^{\Eone}(A_\cC)]^{\mathrm{mot}} \cdot n^{-s},
\end{equation}
where $[B_n^{\Eone}(A_\cC)]^{\mathrm{mot}} \in K_0(\mathrm{Var}/\C)[\bL^{\pm 1/2}]$ is the motivic class of the degree-$n$ bar space, and $\bL = [\mathbb{A}^1]$ is the Lefschetz motive.
\end{definition}

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$\C^3$}{C-3} motivic shadow zeta; \ClaimStatusProvedHere]
\label{prop:c3-motivic-shadow-zeta}
For $\C^3$, the motivic bar dimensions are given by the Behrend--Bryan--Szendro\"{\i} single-letter index:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:c3-motivic-bar}
 [B_n^{\Eone}(\C^3)]^{\mathrm{mot}} = f_n \;=\; \sum_{k=0}^{n-1} \bL^{k + 3/2} \;=\; \bL^{3/2} \cdot [\bP^{n-1}],
\end{equation}
and the motivic shadow zeta is:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:c3-motivic-shadow-zeta-explicit}
 \zeta_{\C^3}^{\mathrm{mot}}(s) = \sum_{n \geq 1} \bL^{3/2} \cdot [\bP^{n-1}] \cdot n^{-s}.
\end{equation}
Under the Euler characteristic specialisation $\chi \colon \bL \to 1$:
\[
 \chi\bigl(\zeta_{\C^3}^{\mathrm{mot}}(s)\bigr) = \sum_{n \geq 1} n \cdot n^{-s} = \zeta_{\mathrm{Riemann}}(s - 1),
\]
recovering the numerical shadow zeta (Vol~I) via the Riemann zeta function.

The motivic bar Euler product inverts the motivic MacMahon function:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:motivic-bar-euler-c3}
 \prod_{n \geq 1} \bigl(1 - f_n \cdot q^n\bigr) \;=\; \frac{1}{Z^{\mathrm{mot}}_{\DT}(\C^3)}.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The identification $[B_n^{\Eone}] = f_n$ follows from the BBS formula (arXiv:0909.5088, Theorem~1.2): the motivic DT partition function $Z^{\mathrm{mot}}(\C^3) = \mathrm{Exp}_*(f)$ where $f_n = \sum_{k=0}^{n-1} \bL^{k+3/2}$, and the plethystic logarithm recovers $f = \mathrm{PLog}(Z^{\mathrm{mot}})$. The Euler characteristic $\chi(f_n) = n$ (each of the $n$ terms $\bL^{k+3/2}$ contributes $\chi(\bL^{k+3/2}) = 1$), giving $\chi(\zeta^{\mathrm{mot}}(s)) = \sum n^{1-s} = \zeta(s-1)$. The bar Euler product~\eqref{eq:motivic-bar-euler-c3} is formal power series inversion: $Z^{\mathrm{mot}} \cdot (1/Z^{\mathrm{mot}}) = 1$.

Computational verification: 57 tests (\texttt{test\_motivic\_shadow\_zeta.py}), verifying Euler specialisation at $s = 0, 1, 2, 3, 4$, the product identity $Z \cdot (1/Z) = 1$ at the motivic level, weight filtration consistency, and $p$-adic formula agreement for $p = 2, 3, 5$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The \texorpdfstring{$p$}{p}-adic shadow]
\label{rem:padic-shadow}
For a CY$_3$ $X$ defined over $\Q$ with good reduction at a prime $p$, the \emph{$p$-adic shadow zeta} is the specialisation at $\bL = p^2$ (the geometric Frobenius convention, avoiding half-integer $p$-powers):
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:padic-shadow-zeta}
 \zeta_{A_X}^{(p)}(s) \;=\; \sum_{n \geq 1} [B_n^{\mathrm{mot}}]\big|_{\bL = p^2} \cdot n^{-s}.
\end{equation}
For $\C^3$ at the geometric Frobenius:
\[
 f_n\big|_{\bL = p^2} = \sum_{k=0}^{n-1} p^{2k+3} = \frac{p^3(p^{2n} - 1)}{p^2 - 1},
\]
giving $\zeta_{\C^3}^{(p)}(s) = \frac{p^3}{p^2 - 1} \bigl[\sum_{n \geq 1} p^{2n} n^{-s} - \zeta_{\mathrm{Riemann}}(s)\bigr]$.

For $K3 \times E$ (conditional on Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}), the $p$-adic shadow should encode the Frobenius eigenvalues $\alpha_{i,p}$ on $H^2(K3, \Q_\ell)$, connecting the shadow obstruction tower to the $L$-function of $K3$. This connection is conjectural: the $22$-dimensional Galois representation on $H^2(K3)$ enters through $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}} = 24$ (lattice rank), and the precise relation between the $p$-adic bar dimensions and the Frobenius trace $\sum_i \alpha_{i,p}^n$ requires computing the bar complex of the framed specialisation $A_{K3 \times E}^{(K3,E)}=\Phi_3^{(K3,E)}(D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E)))$ at the motivic level (CY-D programme).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The \texorpdfstring{$p$}{p}-adic shadow for \texorpdfstring{$K3$}{K3} at \texorpdfstring{$d=2$}{d=2}]
\label{rem:padic-shadow-k3}
At $d=2$ (where CY-A$_2$ \emph{is} proved), the Mukai Heisenberg algebra $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ provides a concrete testing ground for the $p$-adic shadow. The Mukai lattice $\Lambda = H^*(K3, \Z) \cong U^3 \oplus E_8(-1)^2$ has rank~$24$, signature $(4, 20)$, and is unimodular. The bar complex of $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ has motivic dimensions $[B_n^{\mathrm{mot}}] = 24 \cdot \bL^n$ (class~G, with $b_n = 24 = \kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}$ for all $n$), so the $p$-adic shadow zeta (at the geometric Frobenius $\bL = p^2$) is:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:k3-padic-shadow-split}
 \zeta_{H_{\mathrm{Muk}}}^{(p)}(s) \;=\; 24 \sum_{n \geq 1} p^{2n} \cdot n^{-s} \;=\; 24 \cdot \mathrm{Li}_s(p^2).
\end{equation}
This is the \emph{split model}: all Frobenius eigenvalues on $H^2(K3)$ equal~$p$.

For a specific $K3$ surface $X$ over $\mathbb{F}_p$ (the Fermat quartic $x_0^4 + x_1^4 + x_2^4 + x_3^4 = 0$ in $\bP^3$), the Frobenius trace $a_1(p) = \mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}_p \mid H^2(X, \Q_\ell))$ is computable from the point count via
\[
 a_1(p) = \#X(\mathbb{F}_p) - 1 - p^2.
\]
The Weil conjectures bound $|a_1(p)| \leq 22p$. Explicit computation (\texttt{padic\_shadow\_k3.py}, 68~tests) gives:
\[
 \begin{array}{c|cccc}
 p & 2 & 3 & 5 & 7 \\ \hline
 \#X(\mathbb{F}_p) & 7 & 16 & 0 & 64 \\
 a_1(p) & 2 & 6 & -26 & 14 \\
 |a_1(p)| / (22p) & 0.05 & 0.09 & 0.24 & 0.09
 \end{array}
\]
The vanishing $\#X(\mathbb{F}_5) = 0$ reflects the Gepner-point phenomenon: $5 \mid \deg(X)$ and $4 \mid (p-1)$, so the character sum cancellation is maximal.

The \emph{Frobenius-refined} $p$-adic bar dimension at charge $n=1$ is:
\[
 [B_1]|_{\mathrm{Frob}} = 1 + a_1(p) + p^2 = \#X(\mathbb{F}_p),
\]
the point count itself, linking the lowest shadow coefficient directly to the arithmetic of $X$. At higher charges, Newton's identity relates $\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Frob}^n \mid H^2)$ to the coefficients of the degree-$22$ polynomial $P_2(t) = \det(1 - \mathrm{Frob} \cdot t \mid H^2)$:
\[
 Z(X/\mathbb{F}_p, t) = \frac{1}{(1-t) \, P_2(t) \, (1-p^2 t)}.
\]
The Hasse--Weil $L$-function $L(H^2(X), s) = \prod_p P_2(p^{-s})^{-1}$ is therefore encoded, prime by prime, in the Frobenius-refined shadow zeta: the Dirichlet coefficients of $\zeta_{H_{\mathrm{Muk}}}^{(p)}(s)$ determine and are determined by the Frobenius eigenvalues on $H^*(X)$.

The Mukai lattice $\Lambda / p\Lambda$ over $\mathbb{F}_p$ is a non-degenerate $24$-dimensional quadratic space (unimodular, hence non-degenerate at all primes), with Witt index~$12$. At $p=2$, this is a type~II space (even, Arf invariant~$0$), with $2^{23} + 2^{11} - 1 = 8{,}390{,}655$ isotropic vectors.

Finally, the Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa observation of $M_{24}$ symmetry in the $K3$ elliptic genus constrains the Frobenius data: at primes dividing $|M_{24}| = 2^{10} \cdot 3^3 \cdot 5 \cdot 7 \cdot 11 \cdot 23$, the character of the $23$-dimensional $M_{24}$ representation provides a heuristic bound on the Frobenius trace. Whether the $p$-adic shadow zeta inherits the full Mathieu moonshine structure remains an open problem.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Weight filtration and the shadow tower]
\label{rem:weight-filtration-shadow}
The motivic class $[B_n^{\mathrm{mot}}]$ carries a weight filtration from mixed Hodge theory: $[B_n] = \sum_w a_{n,w} \bL^{w/2}$. The motivic shadow zeta decomposes by weight:
\[
 \zeta_A^{\mathrm{mot},w}(s) = \sum_n a_{n,w} \cdot n^{-s} \qquad\text{(weight-$w$ component)}.
\]
For $\C^3$: the weight-$w$ component (where $w = 2k+3$ for $k = 0, \ldots, n-1$) first appears at $n = k + 1$, giving $\zeta^{\mathrm{mot},w}(s) = \sum_{n \geq (w-1)/2} n^{-s}$, a shifted Riemann zeta.

The weight filtration is respected by the shadow tower: the MC equation $D\Theta + \tfrac{1}{2}[\Theta, \Theta] = 0$ of Vol~I holds at each weight grade separately, because the motivic CoHA multiplication preserves weights. For $\C^3$ (class~G), the tower terminates at degree~$2$ with $S_2^{\mathrm{mot}} = \bL^{3/2}$ and $\chi(S_2^{\mathrm{mot}}) = 1 = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$, so the weight filtration is trivially respected. For class~M geometries (local~$\bP^2$, quintic), the weight filtration provides a finer invariant of the infinite shadow tower.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Motivic integration on the CY bar complex]
\label{rem:motivic-integration-bar}
The motivic shadow zeta function of Definition~\ref{def:motivic-shadow-zeta} admits a geometric interpretation through Kontsevich's motivic integration on arc spaces. For a smooth CY$_d$ manifold $X$ with trivial canonical bundle $\omega_X \simeq \cO_X$, the jet space $J_n(X)$ parametrises $n$-th order formal arcs $\gamma \colon \Spec(k[\![t]\!]/t^{n+1}) \to X$, and the motivic class satisfies $[J_n(X)] = [X] \cdot \bL^{nd}$ (the CY condition ensures the truncation maps are $\bA^d$-fibrations without twist; Denef--Loeser). The motivic DT integral
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:motivic-dt-integral}
 \int_{J_\infty(\mathrm{RPerf}(X))} \bL^{-\mathrm{ord}_\omega}\, d\mu \;=\; Z^{\mathrm{mot}}_{\DT}(X) \;=\; \prod_{n \geq 1} (1 - [B_n^{\mathrm{mot}}]\, q^n)^{-1},
\end{equation}
over the arc space of the derived stack of perfect complexes $\mathrm{RPerf}(X)$, identifies the motivic bar dimensions $[B_n^{\mathrm{mot}}]$ as the plethystic logarithm of the motivic DT partition function ($\mathrm{PLog}(Z^{\mathrm{mot}})(n) = [B_n^{\mathrm{mot}}]$). Three specialisations are verified:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item \emph{Euler characteristic} ($\bL^k \mapsto 1$): for $\C^3$, $Z^{\mathrm{mot}} \to M(q) = \prod (1-q^n)^{-n}$ (MacMahon); for K3 at $d = 2$ (CY-A$_2$ proved), $Z^{\mathrm{mot}} \to 1/\eta^{24}$ with $[B_n^{\mathrm{mot}}] \mapsto 24 = \kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}$ for all $n$.
 \item \emph{$p$-adic} ($\bL \mapsto p^2$, geometric Frobenius): for $\C^3$, the $p$-adic bar dimension is $[B_n]|_{p} = p^3(p^{2n} - 1)/(p^2 - 1)$; for K3, $[B_n]|_p = 24 \cdot p^{2n}$, involving the Frobenius eigenvalues on $H^2(K3)$.
 \item \emph{Hodge realisation} ($\bL^k \mapsto (-y)^k$): the Hodge polynomial $\chi_y([B_n^{\mathrm{mot}}])$ carries the Hodge--Deligne numbers of the bar space, and the refined partition function $Z^{\mathrm{ref}}(q, y) = \prod_{n,k} (1 - (-y)^{k+3/2} q^n)^{-1}$ specialises to $M(q)$ at $y = 1$ and to the K-theoretic DT invariant at $y = -1$.
\end{enumerate}
The motivic plethystic exponential uses Adams operations ($\psi_m(\bL^{k/2} q^n) = \bL^{mk/2} q^{mn}$) and is computed via $Z = \exp(\sum_{m \geq 1} \psi_m(f)/m)$. For K3: the class~G structure forces $[B_n^{\mathrm{mot}}] = 24 \cdot \bL^n$ for all $n$, so the motivic bar complex is \emph{weight-pure} (each bar space has a single Hodge weight $2n$). For $\C^3$: $[B_n^{\mathrm{mot}}] = \sum_{k=0}^{n-1} \bL^{k+3/2}$ (the BBS single-letter index of Behrend--Bryan--Szendroi), so the bar spaces are weight-mixed.

The arc space interpretation is unconditional at $d = 2$ (CY-A$_2$ proved). At $d = 3$, the identification~\eqref{eq:motivic-dt-integral} for non-toric geometries proceeds via the $\infty$-categorical CY-A$_3$ resolution (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}); for toric CY$_3$, the CoHA provides the $A_\infty$-structure whose bar differential encodes the motivic DT data (Section~\ref{subsec:atlas-data-table}).

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $62$~tests in \texttt{motivic\_integration\_bar.py}, covering jet space dimensions ($\C^3$, K3), motivic integral computation and plethystic exponential--logarithm consistency, $p$-adic specialisation for $p = 2, 3, 5, 7$ at charges $n = 1, \ldots, 8$, Hodge realisation and refined partition function, and cross-verification against \texttt{motivic\_shadow\_zeta.py} and \texttt{padic\_shadow\_k3.py}.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Categorical DT moduli from the bar complex}
\label{subsec:categorical-dt-bar}

The numerical DT invariants $\Omega(\gamma)$ are recovered as bar-complex Euler characteristics at the decategorified level. For K3 this recovery is clean because the $d=2$ output is the free Mukai Heisenberg, so the Fock-space shadow is explicit. The genuinely categorical statement is subtler: the literature gives the Fock/cohomology identification on $\bigoplus_{n \ge 0} H^\bullet(\Hilb^n(S),\Q)$ and the separate derived McKay equivalence
\[
 D^b_{S_n}(S^n) \xrightarrow{\sim} D^b(S^{[n]}),
\]
but it does not construct a bar-side category whose derived category is already known to be equivalent to $D^b(S^{[n]})$. We therefore separate the proved character/cohomology bridge from the conjectural categorical lift.

\begin{proposition}[K3 Hilbert-scheme Fock bridge; \ClaimStatusConditional]
\label{prop:mukai-hilbert-fock-bridge}
Let $S$ be a smooth projective K3 surface over $\C$, and let
\[
 H_{\mathrm{Muk}} \;:=\; \Phi\bigl(D^b(\Coh(S))\bigr)
\]
be the Mukai Heisenberg of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}}. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The graded vector space $\bigoplus_{n \ge 0} H^\bullet(S^{[n]}, \Q)$ carries the Fock representation of the rank-$24$ Heisenberg algebra attached to $H^\bullet(S,\Q)$ with the Mukai pairing.
 \item The Euler-characteristic generating function is
 \[
 \sum_{n \ge 0} \chi(S^{[n]})\, q^n \;=\; \prod_{m \ge 1} (1-q^m)^{-24}.
 \]
 Equivalently,
 \[
 \eta(q)^{-24} \;=\; q^{-1}\prod_{m \ge 1}(1-q^m)^{-24}.
 \]
 \item For every $n \ge 1$, the Hilbert scheme $S^{[n]} = \Hilb^n(S)$ is a smooth irreducible holomorphic symplectic variety of complex dimension $2n$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Part~\textup{(i)} is the Nakajima--Grojnowski Heisenberg action on Hilbert schemes of points on a smooth surface: the correspondences on nested Hilbert schemes identify $\bigoplus_{n \ge 0} H^\bullet(S^{[n]},\Q)$ with the vacuum Fock representation of the Heisenberg algebra on $H^\bullet(S,\Q)$. Part~\textup{(ii)} is G\"ottsche's product formula for Euler characteristics of Hilbert schemes of points on a smooth projective surface; for a K3 surface one has $\chi(S)=24$, giving the displayed product. Part~\textup{(iii)} combines Fogarty's smoothness of $S^{[n]}$ for a smooth surface with Beauville's holomorphic symplectic form on the K3 Hilbert scheme. In particular, the fourfold statement applies only when $n=2$; in general the complex dimension is $2n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Categorical DT lift of the Mukai Heisenberg Fock sector; \ClaimStatusConjectured]
\label{conj:categorical-dt-k3-fock-lift}
Let $S$ be a smooth projective K3 surface over $\C$, let $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}=\Phi(D^b(\Coh(S)))$, and fix $n \ge 0$. Write $\mathscr{F}_n(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})$ for the charge-$n$ Fock sector of the Mukai Heisenberg. By the symbol
\[
 \mathscr{B}_n(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})
\]
in this subsection we mean a \emph{categorical lift} of $\mathscr{F}_n(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})$: a triangulated or dg category whose Grothendieck group recovers $\mathscr{F}_n(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})$ and whose creation/annihilation functors categorify the Heisenberg operators.

If such a lift is constructed together with a functor
\[
 G_n \colon D^b(\mathscr{B}_n(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})) \longrightarrow D^b_{S_n}(S^n)
\]
intertwining the Heisenberg operators with the standard nested-Hilbert-scheme correspondences, then composition with the derived McKay equivalence
\[
 \Psi_n \colon D^b_{S_n}(S^n) \xrightarrow{\sim} D^b(S^{[n]})
\]
yields an equivalence
\[
 D^b(\mathscr{B}_n(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})) \xrightarrow{\sim} D^b(S^{[n]}).
\]

Equivalently, the compact identification
\[
 D^b(B_n(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})) \simeq D^b(\Hilb^n(K3))
\]
is mathematically defensible only after replacing the undefined symbol $B_n(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})$ by a categorical lift $\mathscr{B}_n(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})$ and naming the functor $G_n$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proof}
There is presently no proof because the category $\mathscr{B}_n(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})$ and the functor $G_n$ have not been constructed in this volume. The proved inputs are separate: Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}} constructs $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$; Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:mukai-hilbert-fock-bridge}} gives the Fock/cohomology identification and the Euler-characteristic product; derived McKay identifies $D^b_{S_n}(S^n)$ with $D^b(S^{[n]})$. None of these steps, alone or in combination, produces a bar-side categorical lift or a functor from it to the derived McKay model. Hence the categorical equivalence is conjectural.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[Categorical DT moduli for CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}; \ClaimStatusConditional]
\label{conj:categorical-dt-cy3}
For a CY$_3$ category $\cC$ satisfying the hypotheses of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}} (in particular, the chain-level $S^3$-framing~\ref{hyp:framing}):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The charge-graded bar complex $B_\gamma^{\Eone}(A_\cC, \sigma)$ is a family of chain complexes over $\mathrm{Stab}(D^b(\cC))$, varying with the stability condition~$\sigma$.
 \item At each wall in $\mathrm{Stab}(D^b(\cC))$: the fibres $B_\gamma(A_\cC, \sigma_+)$ and $B_\gamma(A_\cC, \sigma_-)$ are related by a derived equivalence, whose Euler characteristic shadow is the Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing formula.
 \item The Joyce--Song integration map $I^{\mathrm{JS}} \colon B_\gamma^{\mathrm{mot}} \to \Omega(\gamma)$ is a ring homomorphism from the motivic bar complex to the numerical DT invariants.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Dependencies}: CY-A$_3$ (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Flop vs.\ Koszul at the categorical level]
\label{rem:categorical-dt-flop-koszul}
At a wall in $\mathrm{Stab}(D^b(X))$ corresponding to a birational flop $X \dashrightarrow X^+$, the derived equivalence of bar fibres is the Bondal--Orlov equivalence $D^b(X) \simeq D^b(X^+)$. This is a \emph{flop}, not a Koszul dual: the flop preserves $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$, while Koszul duality satisfies $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A^!) = \rho_K$. The two operations are categorically distinct: flops exchange chambers in the Mukai motion; Koszul duality exchanges algebra and coalgebra.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Connection to the factorization envelope}
\label{subsec:quiver-chart-envelope}

\begin{remark}[Quiver-chart gluing and factorization envelopes]
\label{rem:quiver-chart-envelope}
The quiver-chart atlas construction interfaces with the factorization envelope technology of Vol~I (Direction~3, Nishinaka--Vicedo) as follows. Each quiver chart $(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ determines a Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}_\alpha$ via the Ginzburg dg algebra $\mathrm{Gin}(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$: the degree-$1$ part carries a Schouten--Nijenhuis bracket, and the CY$_3$ pairing promotes it to a Lie conformal algebra. The factorization envelope $\Fact_X(\mathfrak{L}_\alpha)$ on a curve $X$ is the framed $\Eone$-chiral chart; $\CoHA(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ is its Hall positive-half comparison model, not the bare chiral object.

The global gluing~\eqref{eq:global-e1-chiral} then has a dual description:
\[
 A_\cC \;\simeq\; \operatorname{hocolim}_I \Fact_X(\mathfrak{L}_\alpha).
\]
The factorization-envelope functor $\Fact_X(-)$ preserves hocolims (it is a left adjoint, being the free construction on a Lie conformal algebra). The global $A_\cC$ is therefore the factorization envelope of the \emph{glued} Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}_\cC := \operatorname{hocolim}_I \mathfrak{L}_\alpha$. This provides a ``generators-and-relations'' description of $A_\cC$: the generators come from the charts, and the relations encode the mutation equivalences. For the five-step chain of $\C^3$ (Section~\ref{sec:d3-functor-chain}), the atlas has a single chart, and $\mathfrak{L}_\cC = \mathfrak{L}_{\C^3}$ is the abelian Lie conformal algebra of Step~1.
\end{remark}


\subsection{Standard CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} atlas data}
\label{subsec:atlas-data-table}

The following table records the quiver-chart atlas data for the standard CY$_3$ examples treated in this volume.

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
 \toprule
 \textbf{CY$_3$} & $|I|$ & \textbf{Quiver type} & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{native}}$ & \textbf{Shadow class} & $r_{\mathrm{max}}$ \\
 \midrule
 $\C^3$ & $1$ & Jordan & $1$ & G & $2$ \\
 Resolved conifold & $2$ & Klebanov--Witten & $1$ & G & $2$ \\
 Local $\bP^2$ & $3$ & McKay $\Z_3$ & $3/2$ & M & $\infty$ \\
 Local $\bP^1 \times \bP^1$ & $4$ & McKay $\Z_2 \times \Z_2$ & $2$ & M/G & $\infty$/$2$ \\
 $K3 \times E$ &; & Fourier--Mukai fibration & $3^{\mathrm{Heis}}$ & M & $\infty$ \\
 Quintic & $2$ & LV + Gepner & $-25/3^{\mathrm{BCOV}}$ & M & $\infty$ \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\medskip

\noindent \emph{Convention per row}: the $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ column records the native modular-characteristic reading at each geometry. For non-compact toric CY$_3$ ($\C^3$, resolved conifold, local $\bP^2$, local $\bP^1\times\bP^1$) the entry is the local reading $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$ on the base $S$ (so local $\bP^2$: $3/2$; local $\bP^1\times\bP^1$: $2$; conifold: $1$ by direct McKay). For the compact CICY quintic, the entry is the BCOV reading $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24 = -25/3$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:chi-neq-kappa} documents when BCOV holds). For $K3 \times E$, the entry is the Heisenberg-level reading $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}} = 3$. The three readings are not competing measurements of one invariant; each arises from the natural chiral-algebra construction at that geometry.

\noindent Three remarks on the table entries. First, $K3 \times E$ does not have a quiver atlas in the strict sense of Definition~\ref{def:quiver-chart-atlas}: the derived category $D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E))$ does not admit a single tilting generator, and the fibration structure requires a different gluing mechanism (the relative Fourier--Mukai, see Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-times-e}). The table records the Heisenberg-level value $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times E) = 3$ by Beauville additivity of the chiral Heisenberg--Cartan rank ($2 + 1$, Proposition~\ref{prop:categorical-euler}). The compact total-space supertrace has value $0$; the K\"unneth-multiplicative categorical Euler $\kcat(K3 \times E)$ and $\chi(\cO_{K3 \times E})$ have the same value by Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}. The Borcherds automorphic weight has value $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 5 = \mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5)$. These are separate invariants from different constructions. Second, the quintic has $|I| = 2$ charts: one at large volume (a quiver chart from the Beilinson collection restricted to $X$) and one at the Gepner point (a matrix factorization category $\MF(W_{\mathrm{Fermat}})$, which is NOT a quiver chart; see Remark~\ref{rem:gepner-lv-dichotomy}). Third, the shadow class and depth $r_{\mathrm{max}}$ refer to the Heisenberg truncation ($s = 1$ channel). At the full spin tower, the classification may differ (Remark~\ref{rem:c3-per-channel}).

The atlas data table records the $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ values from the quiver-chart gluing construction. For toric CY$_3$ varieties ($\C^3$, resolved conifold, local $\bP^2$, local $\bP^1 \times \bP^1$, and all toric crepant resolutions), Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-chart-gluing} proves the Hall-side hocolim from the McKay quiver atlas; comparison with the Costello--Li/hCS boundary algebra is conditional on the oriented comparison datum. For non-toric geometries ($K3 \times E$, the quintic), the chiral algebra $A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ is available only on the framed object-level locus of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}; the hocolim description via Conjecture~\ref{conj:e1-chart-gluing} remains a separate computational route. The next theorem states exactly what the $d = 3$ construction proves.

% ============================================================
\section{The CY-to-chiral correspondence at \texorpdfstring{$d = 3$}{d = 3}}
\label{sec:cy-to-chiral-d3-programme}
% ============================================================

The $d = 3$ statement below is the framed object-level evaluation of the two-stage construction at dimension three. The PTVV shift forces the native output to be $\Eone$ \textup{(}$n_{\mathrm{native}}(3) = 1$\textup{)}; any braided $\Etwo$-structure is recovered, where it is constructed, from the Drinfeld centre $\cZ(\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(A_\cC))$. The derived-framing theorem removes the obstruction group under the connective and unit-connected hypotheses. It does not supply arbitrary morphism functoriality, a global quantum group $G(\cC)$, or unrestricted compact non-formal CY$_3$ strictification.

On the verified K3-fibred branch of the two-stage assignment in Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation}, with the Stage-$1$ datum and the K3-fibre specialisation fixed, the output is written
\[
 \Phi_3^{(K3,E)}(D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E)))
 \;:=\;
 \SpCh_{K3, E}\bigl(\PhiFA_3(D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E)))\bigr).
\]
Here $\PhiFA_3(D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E)))$ denotes the verified Stage-$1$ $E_3$-holomorphic factorisation algebra on $K3 \times E$ coming from 6D holomorphic Chern--Simons on the CY$_3$ target~\cite{CostelloLi2016,CostelloLi2020}: Kontsevich $E_3$-formality~\cite{Kontsevich1999}, Tamarkin deformation-quantisation~\cite{Tamarkin2007}, and Costello--Gwilliam--Li holomorphic locality~\cite{CostelloGwilliam2017,CostelloLi2016,CostelloLi2020} are applied to the degree-$(-2)$ Gerstenhaber bracket on $\HH^\bullet(D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E)))$. The specialisation is factorisation homology over the K3-fibre $\Sigma_2 = K3$ restricted to the elliptic base $C = E$ \textup{(}Lurie~\cite{LurieHA}; Francis--Gaitsgory~\cite{FrancisGaitsgory2012}\textup{).}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$\Phi.3$}{Phi.3}: framed object-level \texorpdfstring{$d = 3$}{d = 3} locus]
\label{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}\textup{(under \textup{H1--H4}; toric witnesses supplied below; named compact branches require explicit framing and analytic-completion witnesses.)}

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Hypotheses.}
Let $\cC$ be a CY$_3$ category satisfying:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(H\arabic*)}]
 \item\label{hyp:cy3-smooth} \emph{Smoothness}: $\cC$ is a smooth (homologically smooth) dg category over $\C$; equivalently, the diagonal bimodule $\cC \in \cC\text{-}\mathrm{bimod}$ is compact.
 \item\label{hyp:proper} \emph{Properness}: $\cC$ is proper; equivalently, $\dim \Hom^\bullet_\cC(E, F) < \infty$ for all $E, F$.
 \item\label{hyp:cy3} \emph{$\CY_3$ structure}: $\cC$ is equipped with a negative-cyclic Calabi--Yau class $[\sigma_\cC]\in HC^-_3(\cC)$ whose Hochschild shadow is a non-degenerate Serre pairing $\langle -, - \rangle \colon \HH_\bullet(\cC) \otimes \HH_\bullet(\cC) \to \C[-3]$ of degree $-3$, inducing an antisymmetric Euler form $\chi(E, F) = -\chi(F, E)$.
 \item\label{hyp:framing} \emph{Chain-level $\bS^3$-framing}: there exists a chain-level $\bS^3$-framing trivialization compatible with the $\BV$ operator on the cyclic bar complex of $\cC$ and with the Costello TCFT homotopy witnessing $\{b,B^{(2)}\}=0$.
\end{enumerate}
\textup{(}Conditions \ref{hyp:cy3-smooth}--\ref{hyp:cy3} are standard; condition~\ref{hyp:framing} is the substantive hypothesis.\textup{)}

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Conclusion.}
Under \ref{hyp:cy3-smooth}--\ref{hyp:framing}, and after choosing a witnessed admissible specialisation datum $(\Sigma_2,C)$ in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:witnessed-admissible-specialisation-datum}, the two-stage construction yields the object-level assignment
\[
 \Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(\cC)
 :=
 \SpCh_{\Sigma_2,C}\bigl(\PhiFA_3(\cC)\bigr)
 =
 A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)}
 \in \Eone\text{-}\mathrm{ChirAlg}(C),
\]
with the following properties; write $A_\cC$ for this specialised output inside the theorem.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(C\arabic*)}]
 \item\label{concl:generators} \emph{Generators}: the generating fields of $A_\cC$ are in bijection with $\HH^{\bullet+1}(\cC)$, as for $d = 2$ \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}(i)}\textup{)}.
 \item\label{concl:bar} \emph{Bar identification}: $B^{\Eone}(A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)}) \simeq \mathrm{CC}_\bullet^{\Eone}(\cC)$ as $\Eone$-factorization coalgebras on the framed locus, extending Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}(ii)} only where the Stage-$1$ and Stage-$2$ constructions are both defined.
 \item\label{concl:e1} \emph{Native $\Eone$ structure}: $A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ is natively $\Eone$, not $\Etwo$. The $\Etwo$-braided structure on $\Rep(A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)})$ arises through the Drinfeld center $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)}))$, not from the algebra itself \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:e1-universality-cy3}} for toric input\textup{)}.
 \item\label{concl:kappa} \emph{Modular characteristic} (Stage-2 value per Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:beauville-kappa-formula-subscript-split}}).
 The chiral invariant $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_\cC)$ is the
 Heisenberg--Cartan rank of the Stage-2 shadow, additive under
 K\"unneth decompositions of $\cC$ by generator-rank counting on
 $\HH^{\bullet+1}$.
 The categorical Euler $\kcat(\cC)$ is the Hodge supertrace of
 Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}} and is
 K\"unneth-multiplicative on products.
 Concretely: for products $S \times E$, the Heisenberg ranks add,
 whereas the categorical Euler is the product
 $\chi(\cO_S)\cdot\chi(\cO_E)$.
 For compact CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$, the BCOV reading has value
 $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24$; for local surfaces $\mathrm{Tot}(K_S)$,
 the local reading has value $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$.
 The two invariants coincide on compact CY$_2$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$ and
 split for strict CY$_3$ with $\chi(\cO_X) = 0$.
 \item\label{concl:shadow} \emph{Shadow tower}: $A_\cC^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ carries the shadow obstruction tower in the cases where the Vol~I tower is defined and bar-hocolim commutation applies \textup{(}Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:bar-hocolim}}\textup{)}.
\end{enumerate}

\medskip\noindent\textbf{The braiding mechanism at $d = 3$.}
The $\bS^3$-framing gives an $E_3$-structure on $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$. The $E_3$-structure restricts to $\Etwo$ via Dunn additivity, but this restricted braiding is \emph{symmetric} at the topological level, since $\pi_1(\mathrm{Conf}_2(\R^3))$ is trivial. The quantum group (non-symmetric) braiding for $d = 3$ does NOT arise from the $E_3$-to-$\Etwo$ restriction; it arises through the \emph{Drinfeld center} of the $\Eone$-monoidal representation category (Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}).

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Comparison with $d = 2$.}
At $d = 2$ (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}}), the correspondence $\Phi_2$ produces an $\Etwo$-chiral algebra unconditionally: the $\bS^2$-framing provides native $\Etwo$ structure (the fundamental group $\pi_1(\mathrm{Conf}_2(\R^2)) = \Z$ gives the braiding parameter), and no Drinfeld center passage is needed. At $d = 3$, hypotheses \ref{hyp:cy3-smooth}--\ref{hyp:cy3} are parallel to $d = 2$, but the framing hypothesis~\ref{hyp:framing} is new: the topological obstruction in $\pi_3(B\Sp)$ vanishes on the symplectic-reduction locus (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}}), but the chain-level $\Ainf$-compatible trivialization is an additional datum.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Scope of the input category.}
The theorem applies to:
\begin{itemize}
 \item Smooth proper CY$_3$ categories satisfying \ref{hyp:cy3-smooth}--\ref{hyp:framing}; compact projective examples are included only when the chain-level framing witness and the analytic completion cited below are supplied.
 \item Toric CY$_3$ categories with $T^3$-equivariant $\Omega$-deformation ($\C^3$, resolved conifold, local $\bP^2$, local $\bP^1 \times \bP^1$); for these, the CoHA construction supplies the object-level output, and holomorphic Chern--Simons supplies the framing datum.
 \item Ginzburg dg algebras $\mathrm{Gin}(Q, W)$ for quivers with CY$_3$ potential when the chosen potential and completion data supply a framed quiver chart (Definition~\textup{\ref{def:quiver-chart}}).
 \item Generalized CY$_3$ inputs with torsion canonical bundle (Enriques $\times E$), provided the orbifold extension of $\Phi$ is available (Conjecture~\textup{\ref{conj:phi-enriques-z2-orbifold}}).
\end{itemize}
The theorem does NOT apply to singular CY$_3$ categories (smoothness is essential for the factorization-envelope step), to non-proper inputs (properness controls the finiteness of the generating space), to arbitrary CY$_3$ morphisms, or to a global quantum vertex group $G(\cC)$. The typed morphism statement available from the same data is Theorem~\ref{thm:phi3-witnessed-kernel-functoriality}, which requires a $\Phi_3$-admissible kernel datum and the obstruction criterion of Proposition~\ref{prop:phi3-arbitrary-morphism-obstruction-criterion}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}}]
The proof assembles three independent ingredients.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step~1: Derived framing obstruction vanishes on the connected locus (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}}).}
For a smooth proper CY$_3$ category $\cC$ whose Hochschild chains are connective and unit-connected, the primary obstruction to lifting the canonical $\Etwo$-algebra structure on $\HH_\bullet(\cC)$ to an $E_3$-algebra structure lies in $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(\HH_\bullet(\cC), \HH_\bullet(\cC))$, which vanishes by the bar-filtration argument of Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}. The same filtration kills the higher Goodwillie--Ching--Harper obstruction groups. The strict chain-level equality $[m_3, B^{(2)}]=0$ is not asserted; the nonzero commutator in non-formal models is absorbed by the Costello TCFT homotopy $\{b,B^{(2)}\}=0$.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step~2: Non-perturbative convergence (\v{C}ech-HTT + Borel summability).}
The transferred $\Ainf$-operations $\mu_k^{\mathrm{HTT}}$ satisfy the Catalan bound $\|\mu_k^{\mathrm{HTT}}\| \leq \mathrm{Cat}(k-1) \cdot \|s \circ \delta\|^{k-2}$ (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence}}), giving absolute convergence of the coefficient series for finite-Leray-cover cases. This is a coefficient-series theorem, not an OPE theorem. The Gevrey-$1$ OPE completion is Borel summable in the class~$\mathbf{M}$ regime with $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$ and $S_4 > 0$, because $\Delta(Q_L) = -256\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3 S_4 < 0$ places no singularity on the positive Borel ray (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:class-m-borel-summability}}); the remaining OPE regimes stay in the MC5/sewing programme.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Step~3: Object-level assembly.}
Steps~1--2 verify hypothesis~\ref{hyp:framing} only on their shared hypotheses: a connective, unit-connected model for the derived obstruction, finite Leray cover for the coefficient series, and the stated positivity regime for class~$\mathbf{M}$ OPE summability. On that framed locus, the chain cyclic $\Ainf \to$ Lie conformal algebra $\to$ factorization envelope $\to$ $\Sd$-enhanced quantization steps of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}} give the Stage-$1$ output, and the fixed datum $(\Sigma_2,C)$ gives the Stage-$2$ specialisation. The $\Eone$-structure of the output follows from the categorical $\Etwo$-obstruction (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:e1-obstruction-categorical}}): Serre duality forces antisymmetry of the Euler form, obstructing $\Etwo$-enhancement on the stated CY$_3$ scope. The $\Etwo$-braided representation category is recovered by the Drinfeld center on the constructed loci (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}} for $\C^3$); global recovery for arbitrary CY$_3$ inputs is a separate center-construction problem.

\smallskip\noindent\textbf{Scope.}
For toric CY$_3$ inputs, the CoHA route proves the object-level output and bypasses the \v{C}ech-HTT series. For compact finite-Leray-cover cases, Proposition~\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence} proves coefficient convergence; OPE convergence is proved only in the Borel-summable regimes named above and remains a sewing problem elsewhere. See Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:derived-framing-actual-obstructions}} for the residual obstruction scope.

\smallskip\noindent\textit{Verification}: Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction}} ($51$~tests), Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence}} ($64$~tests), Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:class-m-borel-summability}} ($54$~tests), stress test ($45$~tests).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Alternative \texorpdfstring{$\SpCh$}{SpCh}-specialisations on \texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E} and adjacent CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} inputs]
\label{rem:alternative-spch-cy3}
The Borcherds--$\Delta_5$ comparison target of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}} is the specialisation $\SpCh_{K3, E}(\PhiFA_3(D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E))))$ along the K3-fibre and elliptic base, followed by the Mukai--Heisenberg reduction and Borcherds pushforward. The same Stage-$1$ output $\PhiFA_3(\cC)$, evaluated through alternative constructed $(\Sigma_2, C)$-data or alternative CY$_3$ categories, produces distinct $\Eone$-chiral shadows on the reference curve (Remark~\textup{\ref{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations}}): the $T^4$-fibration on $A \times E$ yields the Igusa $\chi_{10}$ shadow on its hypotheses, and the Enriques $\times E$ fibration yields Gritsenko--Clery paramodular shadows after the orbifold comparison data are constructed. The Fake-Monster chiral algebra is not a $d = 3$ Stage-$2$ specialisation of $K3 \times E$; it belongs to the external rank-$26$ Borcherds comparison / $d = 5$ conditional lane of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:fake-monster-d5-bialgebra}}. Each constructed alternative is an $\Eone$-chiral algebra on $C$; its $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$-weight is the Borcherds weight $c(0)/2$ of the chosen automorphic input. None is obtained from $\Phi_3$ applied repeatedly to $D^b(\Coh(K3 \times E))$. The multiplicity of BKM images attached to $K3 \times E$ on the chiral side is the multiplicity of Stage-$2$ specialisations of the single Stage-$1$ output, not a multiplicity of $\Phi_3$-outputs.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Status summary}
\label{subsec:d3-status}

The $d = 3$ construction resolves into one Hall/representation-side toric verification, one framed object-level theorem, and three residual conceptual issues. The toric and framed pieces identify the constructed object-level values of $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ on their hypotheses; they do not establish arbitrary CY$_3$ morphism functoriality, the hCS-to-Hall comparison of Chapter~\ref{ch:cy3-chain-level-bridge}, or a global quantum vertex group.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Hall/representation verification (toric).} For $\cC = \MF(\C^3, 0)$ the Hall-side chain $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1) \to \cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}) \to \cW_{1+\infty}$ is verified on its stated hypotheses (Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-functor-chain}), with $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\C^3) = 1$ confirmed by five independent paths (Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-c3}). This is not a proof of the preceding hCS-to-critical-CoHA map. Toric chart gluing is conditional on the oriented comparison data named in Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-chart-gluing} and Chapter~\ref{ch:cy3-chain-level-bridge}.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{$\infty$-categorical resolution (compact).} Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction} gives $\HH^{-2}_{E_1}(\cC) = 0$ on the connective, unit-connected smooth proper CY$_3$ scope covered by its hypotheses, via the bar-filtration connectivity of Costello's operadic open-closed TCFT; the \v{C}ech-HTT coefficient series converges absolutely for finite Leray covers (Proposition~\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence}), and the OPE completion is Gevrey-$1$ Borel summable in the class~$\mathbf{M}$ regime $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} > 0$, $S_4 > 0$ by the discriminant identity $\Delta(Q_L) = -256\,\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^3\, S_4 < 0$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:class-m-borel-summability}). Other OPE-completion regimes remain in the analytic sewing programme.

\smallskip\noindent\emph{Residual conceptual issues} (cf.\ Remark~\ref{rem:derived-framing-actual-obstructions}):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
 \item $\Omega$-deformation parameter-space uniqueness on compact CY$_3$ with $h^{2,1} > 1$: the correspondence should factor through a choice within $H^1(T_X)$, and invariance under that choice is unverified.
 \item Center-hocolim non-commutation (Proposition~\ref{prop:center-hocolim}): the global $\Etwo$-braided category on multi-chart CY$_3$ is not the hocolim of local Drinfeld centres, so $\Etwo$ recovery is a global operation.
 \item Global quantum vertex chiral group $G(\cC)$: unconstructed in general. For $\C^3$, the toric Hall output is $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ and $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone})$ gives the braided category; the assembly of local Yangians $Y^+(Q_\alpha, W_\alpha)$ into a global quantum group via the Drinfeld double is obstructed by non-commutation of doubles with hocolims.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\small
\begin{tabular}{llp{6.5cm}}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Component} & \textbf{Status} & \textbf{Anchor} \\
 \midrule
 Framed $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ on CY$_3$ objects & Conditional theorem & Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}; Theorem~\ref{thm:derived-framing-obstruction} \\
 $\C^3$ functor chain & Verified & Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-functor-chain} \\
 $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\C^3) = 1$ & 5-path verified & Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-c3} \\
 $\Eone \to \Etwo$ via $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone})$ & Proved & Construction~\ref{constr:rmatrix-from-center} \\
	 Toric CY$_3$ chart gluing & Hall side proved; hCS comparison conditional & Theorem~\ref{thm:toric-chart-gluing} \\
 Compact finite-Leray coefficient convergence & Proved under hypotheses & Proposition~\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence} \\
 Class $\mathbf{M}$ OPE Borel summability & Proved under positivity & Proposition~\ref{prop:class-m-borel-summability} \\
 \midrule
 $\Omega$-parameter independence & Open & Item~(1) above \\
 Global $G(\cC)$ construction & Open & Item~(3) above; CY-C conjectural \\
 DT/shadow beyond genus $1$ & Motivic-only & Conjecture~\ref{conj:dt-hocolim-shadow} \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

At $d = 2$, $\Phi_2$ is unconditional (Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}): the $\bS^2$-framing supplies native $\Etwo$ structure. At $d = 3$, $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}$ is constructed on the verified object-level loci; the three residual items above concern parameter choice, global assembly, and the unconstructed $G(\cC)$, not the local obstruction-vanishing package itself.


% ============================================================
\section{Compatibility with Koszul duality}
\label{sec:cy-koszul-compat}
% ============================================================

The CY-to-chiral correspondence programme $\{\Phi_d\}$ must be
compatible with the bar construction of Vol~I. Mirror symmetry for CY
categories should correspond to chiral Koszul duality; the bar complex
of the chiral algebra $A_\cC$ should recover the cyclic bar complex of
$\cC$. The following results state the two compatibilities separately.

The CY-to-chiral correspondence interacts with the Koszul duality engine
of Vol.~I through the output algebra \(A_\cC=\Phi_d(\cC)\). Its bar
coalgebra is \(B(A_\cC)\); its bar cohomology coalgebra is
\(A_\cC^i:=H^*B(A_\cC)\); its chiral Koszul dual algebra is
\(A_\cC^!:=\mathbb D_{\Ran}(A_\cC^i)\). The bar-shadow comparison with
\(\mathrm{CC}_\bullet(\cC)\) is U1. The further identification
\(\Phi_d(\cC^\vee)\simeq A_\cC^!\) for a mirror CY category
\(\cC^\vee\) is Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy-koszul-mirror}.

\begin{conjecture}[CY mirror symmetry and chiral Koszul duality]
\label{conj:cy-koszul-mirror}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
For a mirror pair $(\cC, \cC^\vee)$ of CY$_d$ categories, the CY-to-chiral correspondence $\Phi_d$ intertwines mirror symmetry with chiral Koszul duality:
\[
 \Phi(\cC^\vee) \simeq \Phi(\cC)^!.
\]
Equivalently, the bar complex of the mirror chiral algebra is the Verdier dual of the bar complex:
\[
 B(\Phi(\cC^\vee)) \simeq D_{\Ran}(B(\Phi(\cC))).
\]
For $\C^3$ at the self-dual point ($h_1 = 1, h_2 = 0, h_3 = -1$), the mirror is $\C^3$ itself. The Koszul dual of the Heisenberg VOA $H_1$ is $H_1^! = \Sym^{\mathrm{ch}}(V^*)$. At the level of modular characteristics, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1) = 1$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1^!) = -1$, so $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_1^!) = 0$, consistent with the KM/free-field complementarity rule (Vol~I).
\end{conjecture}

The Koszul compatibility conjecture predicts that mirror symmetry intertwines with chiral Koszul duality at the level of the correspondence programme $\{\Phi_d\}$, and that complementarity $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A^!) = K$ (the family-dependent Koszul conductor of Vol~I, Theorem~C; $K = 0$ on the KM/free-field lane) holds. A prerequisite for testing these predictions is a precise determination of the modular characteristic itself. The next section confronts the fact that the naive candidate $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ fails for most CY$_3$ geometries, and identifies the categorical Euler characteristic as the correct invariant.

% ============================================================
\section{The derived chiral envelope}
\label{sec:derived-chiral-envelope}
% ============================================================

The factorization envelope $U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}_\cC)$ of Section~\ref{sec:cyclic-to-chiral} is a $1$-categorical construction: it produces a chiral algebra but does not control the higher homotopies of the $\Ainf$-input. The derived chiral envelope $U^{\mathrm{ch,der}}(\mathfrak{L})$ is the $\infty$-categorical refinement that retains the full $\Ainf$-data, connecting the bar--CE identification, the PBW filtration, and the $E_n$ hierarchy.

\subsection{The chiral envelope functor}
\label{subsec:chiral-envelope-functor}

\begin{construction}[Chiral envelope]
\label{constr:chiral-envelope}
Let $\mathfrak{L}$ be a Lie conformal algebra (a Lie algebra in the symmetric monoidal category of $\cD$-modules on a smooth curve~$C$). The \emph{chiral envelope} $U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$ is the universal enveloping vertex algebra of $\mathfrak{L}$: the unique vertex algebra equipped with a Lie conformal algebra homomorphism $\iota \colon \mathfrak{L} \to U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$ through which every vertex algebra map $\mathfrak{L} \to V$ factors. Equivalently, $U^{\mathrm{ch}}$ is the left adjoint to the forgetful functor from vertex algebras to Lie conformal algebras:
\[
 U^{\mathrm{ch}} \colon \mathrm{LieConfAlg} \rightleftarrows \mathrm{VertAlg} \;:\! \mathrm{forget}.
\]
The associated factorization algebra $\Fact_C(\mathfrak{L})$ on $C$ satisfies $\Fact_C(\mathfrak{L})|_D \simeq U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$ on every small disk $D \subset C$, with factorization structure
\[
 \Fact_C(\mathfrak{L})|_{U_1} \otimes \Fact_C(\mathfrak{L})|_{U_2}
 \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\;
 \Fact_C(\mathfrak{L})|_{U_1 \cup U_2}
\]
for disjoint opens $U_1, U_2 \subset C$ (Beilinson--Drinfeld~\cite{BeilinsonDrinfeld}; Costello--Gwilliam~\cite{CostelloGwilliam}).
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Chiral envelope of the four standard families]
\label{prop:chiral-envelope-four-families}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
The chiral envelope construction specializes to the four standard families as follows:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lllll}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Lie conformal $\mathfrak{L}$} & $U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$ & \textbf{Shadow class} & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ & \textbf{CY source} \\
 \midrule
 Heisenberg $\mathfrak{h}_k$ & Heisenberg VOA $\cH_k$ & G & $1$ & Elliptic $E$ ($d = 1$) \\
 $\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_2$ at level $k$ & $V_k(\mathfrak{sl}_2)$ & L & $3$ & 3d hol CS \\
 Positive Yangian current $\mathfrak{L}_{Y^+}$ & $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ & M & $1$ & $\C^3$ ($d = 3$) \\
 Mukai lattice $\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ & $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ & G & $2$ & K3 ($d = 2$) \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
For the $\C^3$ row, $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ is the direct positive-half output; the full $\cW_{1+\infty}$ appears only after Drinfeld double/centre passage and vacuum-module evaluation. For the K3 Mukai envelope, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_{\mathrm{K3}}) = 2$ (Proposition~\textup{\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}}), not the lattice rank~$24$; the CY modular characteristic is the \emph{signed} Hochschild Euler characteristic, not the generator count.

\smallskip
\noindent\emph{Verification}: 107 tests in \texttt{chiral\_envelope\_derived.py}, covering all four families, character computation through $q^{10}$, and cross-family comparison.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Each case follows from the explicit $\lambda$-bracket and the universal property of $U^{\mathrm{ch}}$.

\emph{Family 1: Heisenberg.} The Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{h}_k$ has a single generator $J$ with $\{J_\lambda\, J\} = k\lambda$. The zeroth product vanishes ($J_{(0)}J = 0$): the algebra is abelian. The factorization envelope of an abelian Lie conformal algebra is the \emph{free field theory} generated by $J$ with the OPE $J(z)J(w) \sim k/(z-w)^2$ (Frenkel--Ben-Zvi~\cite{FrenkelBenZvi}, Theorem~3.4.8). This is the Heisenberg VOA~$\cH_k$.

\emph{Family 2: Kac--Moody.} The Lie conformal algebra $\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_2$ at level $k$ has generators $e, f, h$ with $\{e_\lambda\, f\} = h + k\lambda$, $\{h_\lambda\, e\} = 2e$, $\{h_\lambda\, f\} = -2f$, $\{h_\lambda\, h\} = 2k\lambda$. The envelope is the affine vertex algebra $V_k(\mathfrak{sl}_2)$ (classical; Kac~\cite{Kac}).

\emph{Family 3: Positive Yangian.} The Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}_{Y^+}$ is the quantized input at Step~2 of the $\C^3$ functor chain (Section~\ref{sec:d3-functor-chain}). Its envelope is the positive half $Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$ as an $\Eone$-chiral algebra. At the self-dual point ($h_1 = 1$, $h_2 = 0$, $h_3 = -1$), the full Yangian/Drinfeld-centre representation on the vacuum module recovers $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$, which degenerates to the Heisenberg VOA $\cH_1$ (Prochazka--Rapc\'ak~\cite{ProchazkaRapcak}).

\emph{Family 4: K3 Mukai.} The Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ is the rank-$24$ Heisenberg with bilinear form $\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ of signature $(4, 20)$, constructed in Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-k3-explicit}. The envelope is $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}} = \Fact_X(\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{Muk}})$, the Heisenberg VOA of the Mukai lattice.
\end{proof}


\subsection{The bar--CE identification}
\label{subsec:bar-ce-envelope}

The bar complex of the chiral envelope identifies with the Chevalley--Eilenberg chain complex of the input Lie conformal algebra. This is the chiral analogue of the classical identification $B(U(\mathfrak{g})) \simeq \mathrm{CE}_*(\mathfrak{g})$.

\begin{proposition}[Bar--CE identification]
\label{prop:bar-ce-chiral}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For a Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bar-ce}
 B\bigl(U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})\bigr) \;\simeq\; \mathrm{CE}_*(\mathfrak{L})
\end{equation}
as chain complexes, where $\mathrm{CE}_*(\mathfrak{L}) = (\bigwedge^*(\mathfrak{L}),\, d_{\mathrm{CE}})$ is the Chevalley--Eilenberg complex with differential encoding the $\lambda$-bracket.

For an $L_\infty$-conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}$ (with higher structure maps $l_n$ for $n \geq 3$), the identification extends to:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bar-ce-linfty}
 B\bigl(U^{\mathrm{ch,der}}(\mathfrak{L})\bigr) \;\simeq\; \mathrm{CE}_*^{L_\infty}(\mathfrak{L}),
\end{equation}
where $d_{\mathrm{CE}}^{L_\infty} = \sum_{k \geq 2} d^{(k)}$ has contributions from each $l_k$.

The identification~\eqref{eq:bar-ce} connects to the shadow tower of Vol~I: the shadow invariant $S_k$ is the obstruction class from $l_k$ in the $L_\infty$ CE complex \textup{(}Vol~I, shadow/$A_\infty$-coproduct discussion\textup{).}

\noindent\emph{Verification}: cross-checked against \textup{\texttt{chiral\_ce\_complex.py}} (Heisenberg, sl$_2$, Virasoro, Yangian).
\end{proposition}


\subsection{The chiral PBW theorem}
\label{subsec:chiral-pbw}

\begin{theorem}[Chiral Poincar\'e--Birkhoff--Witt]
\label{thm:chiral-pbw}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For a Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}$, the PBW filtration on $U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$,
\[
 F_0 = k \;\subset\; F_1 = k \oplus \mathfrak{L} \;\subset\; F_p = \text{span of $\leq p$-fold normally-ordered products},
\]
has associated graded isomorphic to the chiral symmetric algebra:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:chiral-pbw}
 \mathrm{gr}\, U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}) \;\cong\; \mathrm{Sym}^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}).
\end{equation}

At the level of characters: for $\mathfrak{L}$ of rank $r$ with generators of conformal weight $\Delta_1, \ldots, \Delta_r$,
\[
 \chi\bigl(U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L});\, q\bigr)
 \;=\; \chi\bigl(\mathrm{Sym}^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L});\, q\bigr)
 \;=\; \prod_{i=1}^{r} \prod_{n \geq 1} \frac{1}{1 - q^{n + \Delta_i - 1}}.
\]
For all spin-$1$ generators (Heisenberg type): $\chi(q) = \prod_{n \geq 1} (1-q^n)^{-r}$.

For the derived chiral envelope $U^{\mathrm{ch,der}}$ of an $L_\infty$-conformal algebra, the PBW filtration acquires corrections from the higher structure maps:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:derived-pbw}
 \mathrm{gr}\, U^{\mathrm{ch,der}}(\mathfrak{L})
 \;=\; \mathrm{Sym}^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})
 \;+\; \sum_{n \geq 3} \mathrm{corr}_n(l_n),
\end{equation}
where $\mathrm{corr}_n = S_n / (n-1)!$ and $S_n$ is the $n$-th shadow invariant of Vol~I. The correction vanishes for strict Lie conformal algebras (classes~G and~L); it is nonzero and finite for class~C; and it is a divergent (Gevrey-$1$, Borel-summable) series for class~M.

\noindent\emph{Verification}: PBW character verified through $q^{10}$ for Heisenberg (rank $1$, $24$) and checked at $q^3$ against the OEIS coefficient $1/\eta^{24} \to 1, 24, 324, 3200$ for K3 Mukai.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The classical PBW theorem for vertex algebras (Frenkel--Ben-Zvi~\cite{FrenkelBenZvi}, Section~3.4) establishes~\eqref{eq:chiral-pbw} for strict Lie conformal algebras. The universal property of $U^{\mathrm{ch}}$ gives a surjection $\mathrm{Sym}^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L}) \twoheadrightarrow \mathrm{gr}\, U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$, and injectivity follows from the reconstruction theorem for vertex algebras (which shows that the normally-ordered monomials form a PBW basis).

The derived extension~\eqref{eq:derived-pbw} follows from the bar--CE identification~\eqref{eq:bar-ce-linfty}: the PBW filtration on $U^{\mathrm{ch,der}}$ is controlled by the degree filtration on $\mathrm{CE}_*^{L_\infty}$, and the $l_n$ contributions produce corrections at PBW degree~$n$. The identification $\mathrm{corr}_n = S_n/(n-1)!$ connects to the shadow tower through the Vol~I shadow / $A_\infty$-coproduct construction, where $S_n$ is the $n$-th shadow invariant attached to $l_n$.
\end{proof}


\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$E_n$}{E-n} interaction with the chiral envelope}
\label{subsec:en-chiral-envelope}

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$E_n$}{E-n} preservation by the chiral envelope]
\label{prop:en-envelope-preservation}
\ClaimStatusConditional{} \textup{(structural statement unconditional; application at $d = 2$ unconditional via CY-A$_2$; application at $d = 3$ conditional on the framed object-level locus of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}; application at $d \geq 4$ conditional on CY-A$_d$)}
The chiral envelope functor $U^{\mathrm{ch}}$ does not raise $E_n$ level: if $\mathfrak{L}$ carries an $E_n$-Lie conformal algebra structure, then $U^{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathfrak{L})$ carries at most an $E_n$-vertex algebra structure. Concretely:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item $E_\infty$ input (abelian $\mathfrak{L}$, $d = 1$) $\Rightarrow$ $E_\infty$ output (commutative factorization).
 \item $E_2$ input ($d = 2$, $\bS^2$-framing) $\Rightarrow$ $E_2$ output (braided factorization via Kontsevich--Vlassopoulos).
 \item $E_1$ input ($d = 3$, holomorphic-topological) $\Rightarrow$ $E_1$ output (ordered factorization; $E_2$ braiding via Drinfeld center $\cZ(\Rep^{E_1}(A))$, not from the envelope).
 \item For $d \geq 4$: $E_1$-stabilized (Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy}}).
\end{enumerate}

The averaging map $\mathrm{av} \colon B^{\mathrm{ord}}(A) \to B^{\Sigma}(A)$ satisfies $\mathrm{av}(r(z)) = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$: passing from $E_1$ to $E_\infty$ destroys the $R$-matrix data and reduces the coproduct to the scalar collision residue. The $E_n$ level is therefore an \emph{upper bound}: the quantum group content (Hopf structure, $R$-matrix, braiding) lives on the $E_1$ side and is invisible to the $E_\infty$ projection.

\noindent\emph{Verification}: $E_n$ levels verified for $d = 1, 2, 3, 4, 5$ in \texttt{chiral\_envelope\_derived.py}.
\end{proposition}


\subsection{Factorization on curves of higher genus}
\label{subsec:envelope-higher-genus}

The factorization algebra $\Fact_C(\mathfrak{L})$ depends on the curve~$C$, and the $E_n$ bar complex exhibits a sharp genus dependence classified by shadow class.

\begin{proposition}[Genus dependence of the \texorpdfstring{$E_n$}{E-n} bar complex]
\label{prop:en-bar-genus}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{} \textup{(all classes: G, L, C proved directly; M proved via K\"unneth, $\dim = 6^g$)}
For a Lie conformal algebra $\mathfrak{L}$ of rank~$r$ and a curve $C_g$ of genus~$g$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The $E_1$ bar Poincar\'e polynomial is $(1+t)^{rg}$, with total dimension $2^{rg}$.
 \item The $E_2$ bar Poincar\'e polynomial is $(1+t)^{2rg}$.
 \item The $E_3$ bar Poincar\'e polynomial is $(1+t)^{3rg}$ for classes~G, L, and~C. For class~M, the differential~$d_4$ (from the quartic shadow~$S_4$) survives, but the $E_3$ bar \emph{cohomology} is \emph{finite-dimensional}: $\dim H^*(B^{E_3}(A)) = 6^g$ (closed form via K\"unneth; $d_4$ kills $\Lambda^0$ and $\Lambda^3$ per handle, leaving Betti vector $[0, 3, 3, 0]$ at $g = 1$, total $6$ per handle). The chain-level complex $P(q)^{6g}$ is infinite, but the cohomology is $6^g$, not infinite.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The $E_n$ bar complex at genus~$g$ is $B_{E_n}(A)^{\otimes g}$ with the appropriate operadic tensor product. For abelian~$\mathfrak{L}$ (class~G), all differentials vanish and the cohomology is the exterior algebra $(1+t)^{nrg}$ at each $E_n$ level. For classes~L and~C, the tricomplex model $P(q)^{3g}$ at the chain level gives the same Poincar\'e polynomial because charge conservation kills~$d_4$ (cf.\ the bc system comparison, \texttt{e3\_bar\_bc.py}). For class~M, the differential~$d_4$ survives (it couples to the infinite shadow tower), but the cohomology is \emph{finite}: $\dim H^*(B^{E_3}(A)) = 6^g$ (closed form via K\"unneth; $d_4$ kills $\Lambda^0$ and $\Lambda^3$ per handle, leaving Betti vector $[0, 3, 3, 0]$ at $g = 1$). The chain-level complex $P(q)^{6g}$ is infinite, but the cohomology is $6^g$.
\end{proof}


% ============================================================
\section{The CY modular characteristic}
\label{sec:cy-modular-char}
% ============================================================

The modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ of a CY$_3$ chiral algebra is determined by the CY category, not by the Virasoro central charge alone. The distinction between $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is established below.

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24 \neq \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{chi-top/24 != kappa-ch} in general]
\label{thm:chi-neq-kappa}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
The topological Euler characteristic $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24$ does \emph{not} equal the modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X)$ in general. Explicit counterexamples:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.2}
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
 \toprule
 \textbf{CY variety} & $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ & \textbf{Match?} \\
 \midrule
 Elliptic curve $E$ ($\CY_1$) & $0$ & $1$ & No \\
 K3 surface ($\CY_2$) & $1$ & $2$\rlap{$^*$} & No \\
 $K3 \times E$ ($\CY_3$) & $0$ & $3^{\mathrm{Heis}}$\rlap{$^\dagger$} & No \\
 Resolved conifold & $1/12$ & $1$ & No \\
 Local $\bP^2$ & $1/8$ & $3/2$ & No \\
 \midrule
 Quintic ($\chi = -200$) & $-25/3$ & $-25/3$ & Yes \\
 $\bP^5[3,3]$ ($\chi = -144$) & $-6$ & $-6$ & Yes \\
 $\bP^5[2,4]$ ($\chi = -176$) & $-22/3$ & $-22/3$ & Yes \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
{\footnotesize $^*$The K3 entry records $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi(\cO_{\mathrm{K3}}) = 2$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}; verified via chiral de Rham, Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-times-e}). The distinct quantity $\dim \HH_*(D^b(\mathrm{K3}))/2 = 12$ arises from an alternative algebraization (the Monster orbifold); the full trichotomy ($\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \in \{2, 12, 24\}$) is developed in the K3 discussion of Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-times-e}.

$^\dagger$The $K3 \times E$ entry records the canonical Stage-2 Heisenberg value $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times E) = 3$ (Heisenberg--Cartan rank, Beauville-additive: Proposition~\ref{prop:categorical-euler}, $2 + 1$). The compact total-space supertrace has value $0$; the categorical Euler $\kcat(K3 \times E)$ and $\chi(\cO_{K3 \times E})$ have the same value by Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}. The Borcherds automorphic weight has value $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 5 = \mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5)$, a third distinct invariant.}

\smallskip

\noindent The BCOV formula $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ holds for compact CICYs with $h^{1,0} = 0$ (complete intersections in products of projective spaces) but fails systematically for K3-fibered CY$_3$ (which have $h^{1,0} \geq 1$) and for non-compact toric CY$_3$.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 119 tests in \texttt{cy3\_grand\_atlas.py}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The BCOV formula $F_1 = -\tfrac{1}{2} \log \det(\bar{\partial}^\dagger \bar{\partial})$ on a compact CY$_3$ $X$ gives $F_1 = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24$ when $h^{1,0}(X) = 0$. The condition $h^{1,0} = 0$ ensures that the genus-$1$ free energy has no contribution from abelian zero modes; the formula holds for the quintic ($h^{2,1} = 101$) and all other CICYs with $h^{1,0} = 0$, regardless of the number of complex structure moduli.

For K3-fibered CY$_3$, the infinite tower of BPS bound states across fibers contributes additional genus-$1$ data beyond the one-loop determinant. For $K3 \times E$: the $24$ free bosons from the K3 fiber give $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}} = 24$, but the sewing along $E$ via the DMVV formula introduces imaginary root contributions that produce $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = \mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5) = 5$ (the weight of the Igusa cusp form). The ``lost'' $19$ units ($24 - 5 = 19$) are absorbed by the Borcherds product structure.

For the resolved conifold: $\chi_{\mathrm{top}} = 2$ (the total space deformation retracts onto $\bP^1$) gives $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24 = 1/12$, but the DT computation gives $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 1$ (the conifold has a single BPS state contributing at genus~$1$).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}: Heisenberg-level \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$}{kappa-ch-Heis} and \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$}{kappa-BKM} are distinct]
\label{prop:categorical-euler}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
For $K3 \times E$ the Heisenberg-level chiral modular characteristic (the rank of the primitive generator lattice of the Heisenberg-level chiral algebra $\Omega^{\mathrm{ch}}(K3 \times E)$, which coincides with the generator-rank invariant $\rho^{R_1}$ of the route-$R_1$ stratum in Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-stratification-CY-C}) is
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times E) \;=\; \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(E) \;=\; 2 + 1 \;=\; 3 \;=\; \dim_\C(K3 \times E),
\]
while the Borcherds automorphic weight is the distinct invariant
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(K3 \times E) \;=\; 5 \;=\; \mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5).
\]
The two values track different structural features and do not coincide.

The BKM weight accounts for the full automorphic/BPS package encoded by the Borcherds denominator; it should not be identified with the Heisenberg-level chiral modular characteristic. For K3-fibered CY$_3$, the infinite tower of bound states across fibers contributes to $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ through the Borcherds product data, while the single-copy shadow scalar still records only $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$.

Five independent verifications of $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(K3 \times E) = 5$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item Weight of the Igusa cusp form: $\mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5) = 5$.
 \item DMVV exponent: the Borcherds lift weight formula gives $c(0)/2 = 10/2 = 5$.
 \item Bar Euler product: the genus-$1$ coefficient of $\log Z_{\DT}$ gives $F_1 = 5/24$.
 \item Relative DT: fiber-by-fiber computation via K3 fibers sewed along $E$.
 \item Anomaly cancellation: the worldsheet anomaly for the $K3 \times E$ sigma model.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 78 tests in \texttt{k3e\_relative\_shadow.py}; 119 tests in \texttt{cy3\_grand\_atlas.py}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Scope: Heisenberg-level additivity vs.\ Hodge-supertrace multiplicativity]
\label{rem:categorical-euler-scope}
Proposition~\ref{prop:categorical-euler} asserts Heisenberg-level additivity, \emph{not} additivity of the Hodge-supertrace invariant $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification}. The two quantities are distinct:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$ is the route-$R_1$ generator-rank invariant $\rho^{R_1}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-stratification-CY-C}(iv): the rank of the primitive generator lattice of the Heisenberg-level chiral algebra attached to $X$ by the chiral-de-Rham route. It is rank-additive under products: $\rho^{R_1}(S \times E) = \rho^{R_1}(S) + \rho^{R_1}(E)$, matching $\dim_\C$-additivity of the underlying CY factors. For $X = K3 \times E$ this gives $2 + 1 = 3$.
 \item The Hodge-supertrace invariant $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(X) = \Xi(X) = \sum_q (-1)^q h^{0,q}(X)$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:kappa-hodge-supertrace-identification} is \emph{K\"unneth-multiplicative}: $\Xi(X \times Y) = \Xi(X) \cdot \Xi(Y)$. For $X = K3 \times E$ with Hodge numbers $(h^{0,0}, h^{0,1}, h^{0,2}, h^{0,3}) = (1, 1, 1, 1)$ by K\"unneth, the alternating sum gives $\Xi(K3 \times E) = 1 - 1 + 1 - 1 = 0$; equivalently, $\Xi(K3) \cdot \Xi(E) = 2 \cdot 0 = 0$.
\end{itemize}
The two invariants coincide at $(d, h^{1,0}) = (2, 0)$ where the Hodge column collapses to $\chi(\cO)$ (this is the content of Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}: at $d = 2$, $h^{1,0} = 0$, the Heisenberg-level and Hodge-supertrace invariants both equal $\chi(\cO_X) = 2$ for K3). At $d = 3$ the two invariants diverge: the Heisenberg-level generator rank is additive and records $\dim_\C X$, while the Hodge-supertrace vanishes by Proposition~\ref{prop:chi-O-vanishes-odd-d}. No additive decomposition of $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ into $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$-data holds across the CHL family: the uniform formula is $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}), which gives $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_1) = 10/2 = 5$ for $K3 \times E$ directly from the Gritsenko $\Delta_5$ Fourier coefficient, not by adding $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$-terms. The N=1 numerical coincidence $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(E) + 2 = 5$ has no structural content and fails at $N \geq 2$.
\end{remark}

\section{Cross-volume bridges}
\label{sec:cross-volume}
% ============================================================

The results of this chapter connect the CY$_3$ programme to the algebraic engine of Vol~I (Theorems~A--D, the bar-intrinsic MC element $\Theta_A := D_A - d_0$) and the holomorphic-topological QFT framework of Vol~II (Swiss-cheese structure, PVA descent).

\subsection{Shadow tower identification}
\label{subsec:cross-vol-shadow}

The shadow obstruction tower $\Theta_A$ of Vol~I specializes as follows for CY$_3$ chiral algebras:

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{lll}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Vol~I object} & \textbf{CY$_3$ specialization} & \textbf{Identification} \\
 \midrule
 $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)$ & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\C^3) = 1$ & MacMahon genus-$1$ \\
 Cubic shadow $C$ & BKM lightlike roots & $c(0) = 10$ for $K3 \times E$ \\
 Quartic shadow $Q$ & BKM timelike roots & $c(D > 0)$ for $K3 \times E$ \\
 Shadow class G/L/C/M & Toric vertex bound & $r_{\mathrm{max}} \leq N$ \\
 Bar Euler product & Denominator identity & $1/M(q)$ for $\C^3$; $\Delta_5$ for $K3 \times E$ \\
 Shadow connection $\nabla^{\mathrm{sh}}$ & Picard--Fuchs & Modular family \\
 Complementarity $Q(A) + Q(A^!)$ & Koszul dual CY$_3$ & Mirror symmetry \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}


\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1}-chiral coalgebra and the derived center}
\label{subsec:cross-vol-sc}

The bar complex $B(A)$ is an $\Eone$ chiral coassociative coalgebra over $(\mathrm{ChirAss})^!$; the $\SC^{\mathrm{ch,top}}$ two-colour structure emerges on the derived center pair $(C^\bullet_{\mathrm{ch}}(A,A),\, A)$, not on the bar complex itself. Three bar constructions reflect three levels of operadic symmetry:
\begin{itemize}
 \item $B_{\Eone}(A)$: ordered tensors, deconcatenation coproduct ($\Eone$ coalgebra).
 \item $B_{\Etwo}(A)$: braided tensors, with $R$-matrix data ($\Etwo$ coalgebra via Drinfeld center).
 \item $B_{\Einf}(A)$: symmetric tensors, Vol~I shadow tower ($\Einf$ coalgebra).
\end{itemize}
The $\Eone \to \Etwo$ passage via Drinfeld center (Theorem~\ref{thm:c3-drinfeld-center}) corresponds to the open-to-bulk passage in Vol~II via the chiral derived center $\cZ^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)$. The identification is:
\[
 \cZ\bigl(\Rep^{\Eone}(A)\bigr) \simeq \Rep^{\Etwo}\bigl(Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)\bigr).
\]
%: The Drinfeld center = chiral derived center is the UNIVERSAL BULK.
% It is NOT bar-cobar inversion (which recovers A itself) nor Verdier
% duality (which produces B(A!)). See Vol I.

\subsection{The BCOV holomorphic anomaly equation as genus spectral sequence}
\label{subsec:bcov-genus-spectral}

The Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa holomorphic anomaly equation (HAE) for the topological B-model on a CY$_3$ $X$ has a natural interpretation in the bar-complex framework: it is the genus spectral sequence of the $\Eone$ bar complex.

\begin{theorem}[HAE \texorpdfstring{$=$}{=} MC genus spectral sequence, structural identification]
\label{thm:hae-mc-structural}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The BCOV holomorphic anomaly equation
\[
 \bar{\partial}_{\bar{\imath}} F_g = \tfrac{1}{2} \bar{C}^{jk}_{\bar{\imath}}
 \bigl(D_j D_k F_{g-1} + \textstyle\sum_{r=1}^{g-1} D_j F_r \cdot D_k F_{g-r}\bigr)
\]
is the genus-$g$ projection of the MC equation $D \cdot \Theta + \tfrac{1}{2}[\Theta, \Theta] = 0$ in the Costello--Li dgLa, where:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item $\bar{\partial}_{\bar{\imath}} F_g$ corresponds to the $d_1$ differential of the genus spectral sequence $E_1^{g,*} \to E_1^{g+1,*}$;
 \item $\bar{C}^{jk}_{\bar{\imath}}$ corresponds to the bar propagator $d\log E(z,w)$;
 \item $D_j D_k F_{g-1}$ corresponds to the handle-creation/sewing term;
 \item $\sum D_j F_r \cdot D_k F_{g-r}$ corresponds to $[\Theta^{(r)}, \Theta^{(g-r)}]$ (factorization).
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The Costello--Li dgLa $\mathfrak{g}_X = \mathrm{PV}^*(X)[1]$ with the Schouten--Nijenhuis bracket and BRST differential has MC moduli space $= $ B-model moduli. The genus expansion of the B-model free energy is the genus spectral sequence of the MC element, and the HAE is its $d_1$ differential.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 130 tests in \texttt{test\_bcov\_e1\_shadow\_engine.py}, including the HAE-MC dictionary, propagator identification, and explicit genus-$1$ through genus-$5$ comparisons for $\C^3$ and the conifold (\texttt{bcov\_e1\_shadow\_engine.py}).

\begin{remark}[BCOV holomorphic anomaly as \texorpdfstring{$\Eone$}{E1} shadow connection]
\label{rem:bcov-shadow-connection}
The Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa holomorphic anomaly equation for the genus-$g$ topological string free energy is the $\Eone$ shadow connection $\nabla^{\mathrm{sh}}$ of Volume~I (Theorem~\ref{thm:shadow-connection}), restricted to the complex structure moduli of the CY$_3$. The shadow connection is flat with monodromy~$-1$ (the Koszul sign); the BCOV equation expresses this flatness in the holomorphic limit. The genus spectral sequence (Theorem~\ref{thm:hae-mc-structural}) organises the holomorphic anomaly into a degeneration at each genus page.

Two regimes illustrate the scope and limitations of the identification:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item For CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$ (rigid CICYs): $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$, and the BCOV propagator is the genus-$1$ shadow. The shadow connection $\nabla^{\mathrm{sh}} = d - Q'_L/(2Q_L)\, dt$ specialises to the Picard--Fuchs connection on the $B$-model moduli space.
 \item For K3-fibered CY$_3$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \neq \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:chi-neq-kappa}), and the shadow connection carries fiber-global mixing data beyond the BCOV framework.
