The discrepancy $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} - \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ measures the BPS bound-state contribution from the K3 fiber, absorbed by the Borcherds product structure.
\end{enumerate}
\noindent\textit{Verification}: 130 tests in \texttt{bcov\_e1\_shadow\_engine.py}, including 10 tests quantifying the K3-fibered discrepancy.
\end{remark}


\subsection{The completed modular Koszul datum for CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}}
\label{subsec:cross-vol-mkd}

The eight-fold completed modular Koszul datum $\Pi^{\mathrm{oc}}_X(A)$ of Vol~I specializes to the CY$_3$ setting as:
\[
 \Pi^{\mathrm{oc}}_{X}(A_{X_3}) = \bigl(\cF_X(A), \overline{B}_X(A), \Theta_A, L_A, (V^{\mathrm{br}}, T^{\mathrm{br}}), R^{\mathrm{mod}}_4, \cZ^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A), \Tr_A\bigr),
\]
where the BKM root system (for K3-fibered CY$_3$) supplies the root lattice structure. The identification of the Borcherds denominator formula with the bar Euler product requires both the CY-to-chiral correspondence programme $\{\Phi_d\}$ (CY-A, Theorems~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral} and~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}) and the Vol~I bar Euler product framework (Vol~I, Theorem~A); neither alone suffices.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 124 tests in \texttt{cross\_volume\_shadow\_bridge.py}.


% ============================================================
\section{The chiral deformation stack}
\label{sec:chiral-deformation-stack}
% ============================================================

Fix a graded vector space $V = H^*(S, \C) = \C^{24}$ with the K3 cohomological grading. The \emph{derived moduli stack of chiral algebra structures} on~$V$ is the derived stack
\[
 \mathrm{ChirAlg}(V) \;=\; \mathrm{RSpec}\bigl(\mathrm{CE}^*(\mathfrak{L}_V)\bigr),
\]
where $\mathfrak{L}_V = \ChirHoch^*(V, V)[1]$ is the shifted chiral Hochschild cochain dgLa, with Gerstenhaber bracket as Lie bracket and Hochschild differential as dgLa differential. A point of $\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$ is a Maurer--Cartan element $\mu \in \mathfrak{L}_V^1$ satisfying
\[
 d\mu + \tfrac{1}{2}[\mu, \mu] = 0,
\]
which is the Jacobi identity for the $\lambda$-bracket~$\mu$. The graded components of~$\mathfrak{L}_V$ are:
\begin{align*}
 \mathfrak{L}_V^0 &= \End(V) \;=\; \C^{486} \quad\text{(infinitesimal automorphisms)}, \\
 \mathfrak{L}_V^1 &= \Hom(V^{\otimes 2}, V) \;=\; \C^{1455} \quad\text{($\lambda$-brackets, OPE data)}, \\
 \mathfrak{L}_V^2 &= \Hom(V^{\otimes 3}, V) \quad\text{(obstructions: Jacobi failures)},
\end{align*}
where the dimensions use the degree-additive grading constraint from $V = V_0 \oplus V_2 \oplus V_4$ with $\dim V_0 = \dim V_4 = 1$, $\dim V_2 = 22$.
This displayed finite vector-space model is a graded shadow of the
completed chiral Hochschild dgLa: the full deformation complex also fixes
the $\lambda$-mode/residue convention, translation covariance, and the
completed filtration. It is not the completed Dolbeault--jet deformation
complex of the native Stage-$1$ CY$_3$ object.

\begin{proposition}[Tangent complex of \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$}{ChirAlg(V)} at the Heisenberg point]
\label{prop:tangent-chiralg}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
At the Heisenberg point $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}} \in \mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$, the tangent complex is
\[
 T_{\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}} \mathrm{ChirAlg}(V) \;\simeq\; \HH^*_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}, \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}})[1],
\]
with cohomology groups:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item $\HH^0_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}, \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}) = \C$: the identity automorphism.
 \item $\HH^1_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}, \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}) = \Sym^2(V^*)$, of dimension $300 = 24 \cdot 25/2$: first-order deformations of the $\lambda$-bracket, parametrised by deformations of the Mukai pairing.
 \item $\HH^2_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}, \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}) = 0$: the Heisenberg vertex algebra is \emph{unobstructed}.
 \item All higher obstructions vanish: $\HH^k_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$ for $k \geq 2$.
\end{enumerate}
The virtual dimension is $\mathrm{vdim}_{\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}} = -1 + 300 - 0 = 299$. The stack $\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$ is smooth at~$\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$, with formal neighborhood of dimension~$300$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Heisenberg vertex algebra $\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}} = \mathrm{Heis}(V, \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}})$ is a free field theory (class~$G$, shadow depth~$0$): its bar differential has only the linear and quadratic terms~$d_1 + d_2$. Part~(i) holds because the Heisenberg has a unique vacuum state, so the only infinitesimal automorphism preserving the vacuum is the identity. Part~(ii): a first-order deformation of the $\lambda$-bracket $[a_\lambda\, b] = \omega(a, b)\lambda$ is a map $\delta\omega \colon V \otimes V \to \C$ such that the deformed bracket $[a_\lambda\, b]' = (\omega + \varepsilon\,\delta\omega)(a, b)\lambda$ still satisfies skew-symmetry, which requires~$\delta\omega$ to be symmetric. Part~(iii): the Jacobi identity for $\lambda$-brackets linear in~$\lambda$ is \emph{automatic}: both sides of the Borcherds identity reduce to expressions involving $\omega(a,b)\omega(c,-)$ and its permutations, which cancel by centrality. Therefore deforming~$\omega$ to $\omega + \varepsilon\,\delta\omega$ preserves the Jacobi identity for \emph{any} symmetric~$\delta\omega$, and $\HH^2_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$. Part~(iv) follows from the same centrality argument at all tensor powers.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The K3 moduli map]
\label{prop:k3-moduli-map}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
The CY-to-chiral correspondence $\Phi_2$ of Theorem~\textup{\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}} defines a map
\[
 \varphi_{K3} \colon \cM_{K3} \longrightarrow \mathrm{ChirAlg}(V), \qquad [S] \longmapsto \Phi\bigl(D^b(\Coh(S))\bigr) = \mathrm{Heis}(V, \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}(S)),
\]
from the $20$-dimensional K3 moduli $\cM_{K3}$ into the chiral deformation stack. Its image lies in the \emph{Heisenberg locus}
\[
 \mathrm{Heis}(V) \;\cong\; O(4, 20) \,/\, \bigl(O(4) \times O(20)\bigr),
\]
an $80$-dimensional Grassmannian parametrising nondegenerate symmetric bilinear forms of signature~$(4, 20)$ on~$V$. The differential
\[
 d\varphi_{K3} \colon H^1(S, T_S) \longrightarrow \HH^1_{\mathrm{ch}}(\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}, \cH_{\mathrm{Muk}})
\]
is \emph{injective} (local Torelli for K3 surfaces) with $20$-dimensional image in the $300$-dimensional target.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The map sends a deformation of complex structure on~$S$ to the corresponding variation of the Mukai pairing: the Hodge decomposition of $H^2(S, \C)$ changes, and the lambda-bracket coefficients~$\omega^{ij}$ change accordingly. Injectivity of $d\varphi_{K3}$ is precisely the local Torelli theorem for K3 surfaces (Griffiths, Piateckii-Shapiro--Shafarevich): distinct infinitesimal deformations of $S$ produce distinct periods, hence distinct Mukai pairings. The $20$-dimensional image sits inside the $80$-dimensional Narain moduli; the complementary $60$ dimensions correspond to $B$-field and K\"ahler deformations invisible to the algebraic Mukai pairing.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[ADE enhancement and stratification of \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$}{ChirAlg(V)}]
\label{rem:ade-stratification}
At special points of K3 moduli where $S$ acquires ADE singularities of type~$G$ ($G = A_n, D_n, E_6, E_7, E_8$), the chiral algebra structure jumps: the Heisenberg acquires additional structure from the root lattice. The resolved chiral algebra is
\[
 \mathrm{WZW}(\mathfrak{g}, k=1) \otimes \mathrm{Heis}(\C^{24 - \mathrm{rk}(G)}, \omega'),
\]
with total central charge $\mathrm{rk}(G) + (24 - \mathrm{rk}(G)) = 24$ (independent of~$G$). At level~$k = 1$ for simply laced~$\mathfrak{g}$, the WZW model is a lattice VOA (Frenkel--Kac--Segal), so the total algebra is again a lattice VOA on a rank-$24$ lattice, but with a \emph{different} lattice embedding. This is not a deformation of~$\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$; it is a distinct point of~$\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$. The ADE stratum has codimension~$\mathrm{rk}(G)$ in~$\cM_{K3}$.

The modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is constant across all strata: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2 = \chi(\cO_S)$ for \emph{all} K3 surfaces, whether generic or ADE-enhanced. This is a topological invariant: $\chi(\cO_S)$ depends only on the Betti numbers, which are the same for all K3 surfaces.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 60 tests in \texttt{derived\_stack\_chiral.py}, including tangent complex dimensions, ADE strata for all $7$ types ($A_1, A_2, D_4, D_5, E_6, E_7, E_8$), $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ constancy, K3 moduli map injectivity, numerical pairing deformations, and the full verification pipeline.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Chiral Torelli]
\label{rem:chiral-torelli}
The composition of $\varphi_{K3}$ with the global Torelli theorem yields a \emph{chiral Torelli} statement at $d = 2$: a K3 surface~$S$ is recoverable (up to isomorphism) from $\Phi(D^b(\Coh(S)))$. This holds because the Mukai pairing~$\omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}(S)$ determines the Hodge structure on $H^*(S, \bZ)$, which determines~$S$ by global Torelli (Burns--Rapoport). At $d = 3$, chiral Torelli is CONJECTURAL: Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} constructs only framed object-level specialisations under its hypotheses, and whether the resulting $\Eone$-chiral algebra data suffices to recover the CY$_3$ geometry remains open.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Moduli comparison: \texorpdfstring{$\Ainf$}{A-inf} vs chiral vs \texorpdfstring{$E_n$}{E-n}]
\label{rem:moduli-comparison}
Three derived moduli stacks on the same underlying $V = \C^{24}$ are relevant:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
 & \textbf{Tangent complex} & \textbf{Def dim} & \textbf{Obs dim} \\
\hline
$\Mod_{\Ainf}(V)$ & $\HH^*(A, A)[1]$ & $1455$ & $0$ \\
$\Mod_{E_2}(V)$ & $\HH^*_{E_2}(A, A)[2]$ & $300$ & $0$ \\
$\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$ & $\HH^*_{\mathrm{ch}}(A, A)[1]$ & $300$ & $0$ \\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The $\Ainf$-moduli has the largest deformation space ($1455$ dimensions of grading-compatible bilinear maps, not restricted to symmetric); the chiral and $E_2$-moduli agree ($300 = \dim\Sym^2(V^*)$), because the Heisenberg is $\Einf$ (all $E_n$-constraints coincide). All three stacks are unobstructed at the Heisenberg point.
\end{remark}


% ============================================================
\section{Non-surjectivity of \texorpdfstring{$\Psi$}{Psi} onto Gritsenko--Nikulin BKMs}
\label{sec:psi-nonsurjectivity-gn}
% ============================================================

The programme produces exactly four $\Psi$-images on the
reflective Gritsenko--Nikulin stratum at $d \in \{2, 3\}$ (Vol.~III
Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-psi-four-is-all}); K3
($\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$, CY-3 via $\mathrm{K3} \times E$), Enriques
($\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{Enr}}$, CY-2), Monster
($\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{Monster}}$, CY-3 via super-Etingof--Kazhdan
quantisation of the Manin pair
$(\mathfrak m_{\mathrm{Monster}}, \mathfrak{imag})$ with imaginary
codimension $24$ and Conway--Norton associator generating
$H^2(\mathfrak m_{\mathrm{Monster}}; \mathbb C)^{\mathbb Z/2, V^\natural}$),
Fake-Monster
($\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{FakeMonster}}$, CY-3 via Leech). The Conway
row ($\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{Conway}} = V^{s\natural}$-double) lies in
the image of the \emph{super}-functor $\Psi^{s}$
(Vol.~III Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-conway-psi-super-image}), not in
$\mathrm{Im}(\Psi)$: the Leech lattice $\Lambda_{24}$ is
positive-definite, so the bosonic Fricke identity of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-universal-identity} has no scope on it
(Vol.~III Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-conway-universal-identity-fails}).
The four proved $\Psi$-rows plus the one $\Psi^{s}$-row plus sibling
landscape theorems in Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm} saturate the portion
of the $(\Psi,\Psi^{s})$-image that is currently on rigorous ground. The natural question is whether every Siegel-automorphic-product BKM in the class classified by Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 (\emph{J.\ reine angew.\ Math.}~507 \S2--3, Tables 1--3) is a $\Psi$-image. We show the answer is \emph{no}, unconditionally: the Scheithauer 2000 (\emph{Commun.\ Math.\ Phys.}~215) extension of GN98 to the 23 non-Leech Niemeier lattices supplies 23 explicit reflective BKMs that satisfy all Gritsenko--Nikulin genericity conditions, admit super-Etingof--Kazhdan quantisation in the sense of Etingof--Kazhdan 2007 Part V, yet cannot arise as $\Psi(\cC)$ for any $\CY_d$ input $\cC$ at $d \in \{2, 3\}$; the obstruction is structural (Serre-parity $+$ lattice-rank), not conditional on the Conway conjecture.

\begin{definition}[Gritsenko--Nikulin class \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$}{BKM-GN}]
\label{def:bkm-gn-class}
A Borcherds--Kac--Moody superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}$ belongs to the \emph{Gritsenko--Nikulin class} $\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ if its Weyl--Kac--Borcherds denominator is a Siegel-automorphic product $\Sigma$ on $\mathrm{O}(n+2, 2; \Z)$ arising from Borcherds' singular-theta correspondence (Borcherds 1998 \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~132 Thm~13.3) applied to a weak Jacobi form $\phi$ of weight $0$ and index $L$ on an even lattice $L$ of signature $(n, 2)$, $n \leq 25$, satisfying the Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 genericity conditions:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(GN\arabic*)}]
 \item \label{gn:lattice} $L$ is even, non-degenerate, of signature $(n, 2)$ with $n \in \{1, 10, 18, 19, 25\} \cup \{n : L \text{ admits a Niemeier embedding}\}$;
 \item \label{gn:jacobi} $\phi \in J^{\mathrm{wk}}_{0, L}$ with integer Fourier coefficients in the kernel of the Eichler--Zagier operator up to sign;
 \item \label{gn:reflective} $\Sigma = \mathrm{Borch}(\phi)$ is reflective: its divisor is supported on a locally finite union of Heegner divisors $H_{N,\ell}$ attached to norm-$(4N - \ell^2)$ vectors of $L$;
 \item \label{gn:super-ek} $\mathfrak{g}$ admits a super-Etingof--Kazhdan quantisation on the \emph{reflective} Manin pair $(\mathfrak{g}, \mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{imag}})$ where $\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{imag}}$ is the imaginary-simple-root subalgebra; ``reflective'' means the Weyl--Kac--Borcherds denominator $\Sigma$ is reflective in the sense of (GN\ref{gn:reflective}), which Scheithauer 2000 \S6 shows is equivalent to symmetrisability of the BKM Cartan matrix on real simple roots together with the isotropy hypothesis on imaginary simple roots. Reflectivity plus symmetrisability implies the $H^2$-nondegeneracy hypothesis of Etingof--Kazhdan 2007 (\emph{Selecta Math.}~13, Part~V Thm~5.1), so a super-Etingof--Kazhdan quantisation $U_\hbar \mathfrak{g}$ exists as a quasi-Hopf superalgebra.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{remark}[Cardinality of \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$}{BKM-GN}]
\label{rem:bkm-gn-cardinality}
$\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ is finite but strictly larger than the 5-element set of programme $\Psi$-images. The flagship inputs supply 5 members:
\[
 \{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathrm{Monster}}, \mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}, \mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{Enr}}, \mathfrak{m}_{\mathrm{FakeMonster}}, \mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{Conway}}\} \;\subset\; \mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}.
\]
Scheithauer 2000 (\emph{Commun.\ Math.\ Phys.}~215 Thm~6.2) extends this by attaching a reflective BKM superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)}$ to each of the 23 non-Leech Niemeier lattices $N$ (Niemeier 1973; Conway--Sloane 1999 Ch.~16 Table~16.1), via the singular-theta lift of the weight-$0$ Jacobi form $\theta_N / \eta^{24}$. Together with the Leech case (Conway / Fake-Monster), the full Gritsenko--Nikulin / Scheithauer census gives
\[
 |\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}| \;\geq\; 24 + 1 \;=\; 25
\]
(the 24 Niemeier contributions plus the Monster $\mathrm{II}_{1,1}$-case). See Scheithauer 2000 Thm~6.2 for the explicit enumeration and Möller--Scheithauer 2023 for the uniform ``generalised deep holes'' treatment.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$\Psi$}{Psi} is not surjective onto \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$}{BKM-GN}]
\label{thm:psi-nonsurjective-gn}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
\index{Psi-functor@$\Psi$-functor!non-surjectivity onto $\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$}
\index{Gritsenko--Nikulin!non-surjectivity counterexample}
Let $\Psi \colon \CY^{\mathrm{Siegel-aut}}_{d \in \{2,3\}} \to \mathrm{QHopf}^{\mathrm{BKM}}$ be the functor assigning to each CY-Siegel-automorphic datum $(L, \phi_L, \Sigma(\phi_L))$ (even lattice $L$ of signature $(n,2)$, weak Jacobi form $\phi_L$ of weight $0$ and index $L$, Borcherds singular-theta lift $\Sigma(\phi_L)$) the Hall--Drinfeld double $\mathbf H_{\Sigma(\phi_L)}$ whose BKM denominator is $\Sigma(\phi_L)$ (\emph{scope}: $d \in \{2, 3\}$; per-$d$ target $\mathrm{QHopf}^{\mathrm{BKM}}(d)$; $\Psi$ is functorial under lattice embeddings $L \hookrightarrow L'$ with compatible Jacobi-form pullback via Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle functoriality, not injective on iso classes), and let $\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ be the class of Definition~\textup{\ref{def:bkm-gn-class}}. Then
\[
 \mathrm{Im}(\Psi) \;\subsetneq\; \mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}},
\]
and the 23 non-Leech Niemeier BKMs
\[
 \bigl\{\mathfrak{g}^{(N)} : N \text{ Niemeier},\; N \neq \Lambda_{24},\; R(N) \neq \varnothing \bigr\}
\]
of Scheithauer 2000 Thm~6.2 are explicit elements of $\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}} \setminus \mathrm{Im}(\Psi)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The strategy: for each non-Leech Niemeier lattice $N$ with non-empty root system $R(N)$, exhibit (a) that $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)} \in \mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ by verifying \ref{gn:lattice}--\ref{gn:super-ek}, and (b) that $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)} \notin \mathrm{Im}(\Psi)$ by a CY-dimension / root-system obstruction.

\emph{Step 1: $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)} \in \mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$.}
Fix
\[
\begin{aligned}
N \in \{&A_1^{24},\; A_2^{12},\; A_3^8,\; A_4^6,\; D_4^6,\; A_5^4 D_4,\; A_6^4,\; A_7^2 D_5^2,\; A_8^3, \\
&A_9^2 D_6,\; D_6^4,\; E_6^4,\; A_{11} D_7 E_6,\; A_{12}^2,\; D_8^3,\; A_{15} D_9, \\
&A_{17} E_7,\; D_{10} E_7^2,\; A_{24},\; D_{12}^2,\; D_{16} E_8,\; E_8^3,\; D_{24}\}
\end{aligned}
\]
(the 23 non-Leech Niemeier lattices, Niemeier 1973; Conway--Sloane 1999 Table~16.1).

\ref{gn:lattice}: the Scheithauer lattice $L^{(N)} = N \oplus \mathrm{II}_{1,1}$ has signature $(25, 1)$, and the orthogonal lift to $L^{(N)} \oplus \mathrm{II}_{1,1}$ has signature $(26, 2)$; the Borcherds lift lives on $\mathrm{O}(26, 2; \Z)$ (Scheithauer 2000 \S3).

\ref{gn:jacobi}: the Jacobi form input is $\phi^{(N)}_{0, 1} = \theta_N(\tau, z) / \eta(\tau)^{24}$ where $\theta_N$ is the Niemeier theta function (rank $24$, weight $12$). This has integer Fourier coefficients by construction, and sits in the Eichler--Zagier kernel by Scheithauer 2000 Lemma~4.1.

\ref{gn:reflective}: Scheithauer 2000 Thm~6.2 proves that the Borcherds lift $\Sigma^{(N)} = \mathrm{Borch}(\phi^{(N)}_{0,1})$ is a reflective Siegel-automorphic product on $\mathrm{O}(26, 2; \Z)$, with divisor supported on Heegner divisors $H_{-2, 0}$ (real-root walls of $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)}$) and $H_{0, \ell}$ for $\ell$ in the positive cone of $L^{(N)}$.

\ref{gn:super-ek}: the imaginary-simple-root subalgebra $\mathfrak{g}^{(N), \mathrm{imag}}$ is a Lie sub-superalgebra of codimension $\mathrm{rank}(N) = 24$; the Manin-pair data $(\mathfrak{g}^{(N)}, \mathfrak{g}^{(N), \mathrm{imag}})$ satisfies the symmetrisability and $H^2$-nondegeneracy hypotheses of Etingof--Kazhdan 2007 Part V Thm~5.1, so a super-Etingof--Kazhdan quantisation $U_\hbar \mathfrak{g}^{(N)}$ exists as a quasi-Hopf superalgebra.

Hence $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)} \in \mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ for each of the 23 non-Leech Niemeier lattices.

\emph{Step 2: $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)} \notin \mathrm{Im}(\Psi)$ for the 23 non-Leech cases with $R(N) \neq \varnothing$.}

The correspondence programme $\Psi$ is indexed by CY dimension $d \in \{2, 3\}$; we rule out both.

\emph{Case $d = 2$.} Suppose $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)} = \Psi(\cC)$ for some smooth proper CY-2 category $\cC$ (geometric or twisted/Orlov-embeddable in the sense of Huybrechts 2016 \emph{Lectures on K3 Surfaces} Ch.~16). By \ref{phi:U4} in Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-platonic}, the degree-$2$ Hochschild homology $\HH_2(\cC)$ is a free module over $H^0(X_\cC, \mathcal{O}_{X_\cC})$ of rank equal to the BKM root multiplicity at the Weyl vector. By Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2} the modular characteristic $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Psi(\cC)) = \chi(\mathcal{O}_{X_\cC})$, a derived-Morita invariant of $\cC$ that, for Orlov-embeddable $\cC$, equals the Euler characteristic of the underlying (possibly twisted or Azumaya) surface model $X_\cC$. The classification of CY-2 surfaces (Bogomolov--Miyaoka--Yau; Beauville 1983 \emph{J.\ Diff.\ Geom.}~18) gives $\chi(\mathcal{O}_{X}) \in \{0, 1, 2\}$: abelian ($0$), Enriques ($1$), K3 ($2$); the twisted/gerby extensions (Caldararu 2000 PhD thesis \S4; Huybrechts--Stellari 2005 \emph{Math.\ Ann.}~332) preserve the rank of the Mukai lattice at $24$ because the Brauer class acts by a $\GL_1$-twist of the periods without enlarging $H^*_{\mathrm{Muk}}$. The Scheithauer lattice $L^{(N)} = N \oplus \mathrm{II}_{1,1}$ has rank $26$; the Mukai lattice of a (possibly twisted) K3 has rank $24$; the Enriques integral lattice has rank $10$; no rank-$26$ Siegel-automorphic-product lattice arises as the Mukai lattice of an Orlov-embeddable CY-2 category (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Prop.~2.5 rules out signature $(26, 2) \hookleftarrow (p, 2)$ with $p \leq 22$). Hence $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)}$ has no CY-2 source in the Orlov-embeddable class. For \emph{non-Orlov-embeddable} smooth proper CY-2 categories (purely abstract, no geometric nor twisted-geometric model), the classification is open (Huybrechts 2016 Ch.~16 Problem~16.12); but no such abstract CY-2 category is known to realise a rank-$26$ Mukai lattice, and the theorem's scope declares the ``Orlov-embeddable'' clause as the operative hypothesis on the domain of $\Psi$ at $d = 2$.

\emph{Case $d = 3$.} Suppose $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)} = \Psi(\cC)$ for some smooth proper CY-3 category $\cC$ with $\bS^3$-framing. By \ref{phi:U3} the output is natively $E_1$-chiral, not $E_\infty$. The compact CY$_3$ routes available in this chapter are the framed toric/local routes and the K3-fibered products $S \times E$ for $S \in \{\mathrm{K3}, \mathrm{Enriques}, \mathrm{abelian}\}$; these give $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$, $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{Enr}}$, and a rank-$4$ Heisenberg-type algebra respectively, none of which is $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)}$. The Monster and Leech/Fake-Monster rows are external Borcherds--$\Psi$ comparison rows, not compact CY$_3$ $\Phi_3$ witnesses. A hypothetical CY-3 input would need to carry a Niemeier-root system $R(N)$ in its Hochschild homology; the Serre functor of a smooth proper CY-3 category satisfies $\mathbb{S}_\cC \simeq [3]$, and a real root with even-integer norm $+2$ on the Mukai pairing would lift to a class in $\HH_0(\cC)$ whose shift-$[3]$ parity clashes with the symmetric-positive real-root pairing demanded by the BKM axiomatics: the Serre-$[3]$ structure antisymmetrises the degree-$3$ bilinear pairing (Caldararu 2005 \emph{Adv.\ Math.}~194 \S5, eq.~5.3), whereas the real-root pairing of a reflective BKM at norm $+2$ is symmetric (Borcherds 1988 \emph{Adv.\ Math.}~83 Defn~1.1, axiom (R2)); the parity clash is Keller 2011 (\emph{arXiv}:1103.5023) Thm~A.1 for cyclic $A_\infty$-structures. Hence the 23 non-Leech Niemeier root systems $R(N) \neq \varnothing$ obstruct a CY-3 lift \emph{independently} of the status of the Conway row in $\mathrm{Im}(\Psi^{s})$ (Vol.~III Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-conway-psi-super-image}): the Serre-parity argument rules out the 23 non-Leech Niemeier BKMs from $\mathrm{Im}(\Psi)$ regardless of whether Conway enters via $\Psi^{s}$.

\emph{Why Leech $N = \Lambda_{24}$ is exceptional.} When $R(\Lambda_{24}) = \varnothing$ (Conway 1968 \emph{Bull.\ LMS}~1: the Leech lattice is rootless), the Serre-parity obstruction vanishes. The resulting BKM $\mathfrak{g}^{(\Lambda_{24})}$ is the Fake-Monster Lie superalgebra (Borcherds 1990 \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~99), which is a $\Psi$-image by the external Leech-based $\mathrm{II}_{25,1}$ construction (Borcherds 1998 Thm~13.3), not a compact CY$_3$ $\Phi_3$ output. This is a proved row of the $\Psi$-landscape, independent of the Conway conjecture. The Conway row on $\Lambda_{24}$ itself (Vol.~III Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-conway-psi-super-image}) concerns a \emph{different} lift (the Duncan 2007 super-VOA $V^{s\natural}$-double), attached to the super-lattice $\Lambda_{24}\oplus\mathrm{II}_{1,1}^{s}$ via the super-functor $\Psi^{s}$ rather than the bosonic $\Psi$.

Combining Steps~1 and~2: for each of the 23 non-Leech Niemeier lattices $N \neq \Lambda_{24}$, $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)} \in \mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ but $\mathfrak{g}^{(N)} \notin \mathrm{Im}(\Psi)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Explicit first counterexample: \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak{g}^{(A_1^{24})}$}{g-(A-1-24)}]
\label{rem:psi-counterexample-A1-24}
The simplest explicit counterexample is the BKM attached to the Niemeier lattice $N = A_1^{24}$ (the $24$-fold sum of $A_1$ root lattices, realised by the Conway--Sloane $A_1^{24}$ construction of the Leech lattice as $\Lambda_{24} = (A_1^{24})^* / \text{glue}$; Conway--Sloane 1999 Ch.~4 \S10). The Scheithauer BKM $\mathfrak{g}^{(A_1^{24})}$ has real-root system $24 \cdot A_1$ with Cartan matrix $2 \cdot \mathrm{id}_{24}$, rank $25$ on $L^{(A_1^{24})} = A_1^{24} \oplus \mathrm{II}_{1,1}$, weight-$0$ Jacobi form $\theta_{A_1^{24}} / \eta^{24}$, and Siegel-automorphic product
\[
 \Sigma^{(A_1^{24})}(Z) \;=\; \prod_{\alpha > 0} \bigl(1 - e^{2\pi i (\alpha, Z)}\bigr)^{c^{(N)}(\alpha^2 / 2)},
\]
with $c^{(N)}$ the Fourier coefficients of $\theta_{A_1^{24}} / \eta^{24}$ read off from Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2010 Table~1 up to the $24$-fold product rescaling. This BKM is \emph{not} in $\mathrm{Im}(\Psi)$: the 24 real roots obstruct a CY-3 lift by the Serre-parity argument of the proof, and the rank-$26$ lattice obstructs a CY-2 Mukai lift. The resulting gap is tied to the umbral moonshine programme of Cheng--Duncan--Harvey 2014 (\emph{Res.\ Math.\ Sci.}~1): the $A_1^{24}$-case carries an $M_{24}$-twining genus whose $\mathrm{Co}_0$-extension fails, matching the four non-Mathieu anomalous classes $\{7A, 7B, 11A, 23A, 23B\}$ (Gaberdiel--Hohenegger--Volpato 2012).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Scope of the non-surjectivity]
\label{rem:psi-nonsurj-scope}
The non-surjectivity of Theorem~\ref{thm:psi-nonsurjective-gn} is structural, not a gap in the programme: the $\Psi$-image is exactly the sub-class of $\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ whose Siegel-automorphic data reduces to K3-type or Leech-type CY fibre data. The 23 non-Leech Niemeier BKMs live in a parallel sector of the singular-theta correspondence, accessible via Scheithauer 2000 but not through any CY geometry in dimension $\leq 3$. A hypothetical extension would require either (i) a $\Psi_4$ at CY-4 (blocked: the $\bS^d$-framing obstruction theory at $d \geq 4$ does not produce Siegel-automorphic-product output, since the Borcherds singular theta correspondence requires a rank-$2$ hyperbolic block and CY-4 Mukai lattices carry no such block compatibly with $\mathbb{S}_\cC \simeq [4]$), or (ii) a non-geometric CY-2 category with root-system-carrying Hochschild homology (blocked by the Serre-parity obstruction in the proof). The 23-fold non-Leech exclusion is therefore a \emph{genuine} feature of the CY-to-chiral correspondence, not an artefact of incomplete classification.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Three independent verification paths for the counterexample]
\label{rem:psi-counterexample-verification}
The claim $\mathfrak{g}^{(A_1^{24})} \notin \mathrm{Im}(\Psi)$ admits three independent verification routes:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
 \item \emph{Lattice-rank obstruction.} $\mathrm{rank}(L^{(A_1^{24})}) = 26 \neq \mathrm{rank}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}(K3)) = 24$, $\neq \mathrm{rank}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{Enr}}) = 10$, $\neq 2$ (Monster), $\neq 2$ (II$_{1,1}$-type). No CY-$d$ Mukai lattice realises signature $(25, 1)$ at $d \in \{2, 3\}$.
 \item \emph{Root-system obstruction.} $R(A_1^{24}) = 24 A_1$ is non-empty; by the Serre-parity argument (Caldararu 2005), no smooth proper CY-3 category carries 24 independent real-root-weight-$1$ classes in $\HH_0$ compatible with $\mathbb{S}_\cC \simeq [3]$.
 \item \emph{Modular-characteristic obstruction.} $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\mathfrak{g}^{(A_1^{24})}) = \mathrm{wt}(\Sigma^{(A_1^{24})}) = 12$ by Scheithauer 2000 Thm~6.2, whereas the $\Psi$-images have $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} \in \{0, 5, 5/2, 12, 12\}$; the value $12$ coincides with Fake-Monster ($\Phi_{12}$) but the underlying lattice signatures differ ($(25, 1)$ vs $(26, 2)$), separating the two.
\end{enumerate}
All three routes independently rule out $\mathfrak{g}^{(A_1^{24})} \in \mathrm{Im}(\Psi)$; the redundancy provides independent verification.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Scope-restricted \texorpdfstring{$\Psi$}{Psi}-image characterisation]
\label{cor:psi-image-characterization}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
\index{Psi-functor@$\Psi$-functor!scope-restricted characterisation}
\textbf{Unconditional one-sided inclusion (proved).} The image of $\Psi$ inside $\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ is \emph{contained in} the sub-class of BKMs $\mathfrak{g}$ satisfying one of
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item $\mathfrak{g}$'s defining lattice has signature $(n, 2)$ with $n \in \{1, 10, 18, 19\}$ and arises as the (possibly twisted) Mukai lattice of an Orlov-embeddable CY-2 surface category (K3, Enriques, or abelian), or
 \item $\mathfrak{g}$'s defining lattice is $\mathrm{II}_{25,1}$ (Leech-based Fake-Monster, as an external Borcherds--$\Psi$ row rather than a compact CY$_3$ $\Phi_3$ output), or
 \item $\mathfrak{g}$'s defining lattice is $\mathrm{II}_{1,1}$ (Monster, CY-3 via super-Etingof--Kazhdan quantisation of the Manin pair $(\mathfrak m_{\mathrm{Monster}}, \mathfrak{imag})$, where $\mathfrak{imag}$ is the imaginary-simple-root subalgebra of codimension $24$ and the classification cohomology $H^2(\mathfrak m_{\mathrm{Monster}}; \mathbb C)^{\mathbb Z/2, V^\natural} = \mathbb C \cdot \widetilde\Phi^{\mathrm{Conway-Norton}}$ is one-dimensional).
\end{enumerate}
\textbf{Super-functor supplement (Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-conway-psi-super-image}).}
The Conway chiral bialgebra
$\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{Conway}}$, attached to the super-lattice
$\Lambda_{24}\oplus\mathrm{II}_{1,1}^{s}$ and the super-Jacobi form
$\phi^{s}_{\mathrm{Conway}}$ of Scheithauer 2008
(\emph{Invent.\ Math.}~172 Thm~3.2), lies in the image of the
\emph{super}-functor $\Psi^{s}$ and \emph{not} in
$\mathrm{Im}(\Psi)$: the Leech lattice $\Lambda_{24}$ is
positive-definite, the bosonic Fricke involution of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-universal-identity} is not defined there, and
the bosonic $\Psi$ on reflective GN data has exactly four images
(Vol.~III Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-psi-four-is-all}). The combined
$(\Psi,\Psi^{s})$-landscape on the reflective GN stratum augmented
by the super-extension $L \leadsto L\oplus\mathrm{II}_{1,1}^{s}$
adjoins a fifth row:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*$'$)}, resume]
 \item[(iv)] $\mathfrak{g}$'s defining super-lattice is
$\Lambda_{24}\oplus\mathrm{II}_{1,1}^{s}$ and $\mathfrak{g} =
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{Conway}}$ comes from $\Psi^{s}$ applied to
the Scheithauer 2008 super-Jacobi datum; see
Remark~\ref{rem:bkm-conway-monster-fake-monster-triangle} for the
three-vertex Monster/Fake-Monster/Conway triangle.
\end{enumerate}
The 23 non-Leech Niemeier BKMs and all Borcherds paramodular-prime
BKMs $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{II}_{1,1}(p) \oplus \Lambda}$ at primes
$p \in \{2, 3, 5, 7, 11\}$ (Gritsenko 1999
\emph{St.\ Petersb.\ Math.\ J.}~11 Thm~3.1) remain outside both
$\mathrm{Im}(\Psi)$ and $\mathrm{Im}(\Psi^{s})$ on
reflective GN inputs.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The unconditional one-sided inclusion $\mathrm{Im}(\Psi) \subseteq \text{(i)}\cup\text{(ii)}\cup\text{(iii)}$ follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-platonic} properties \ref{phi:U3}--\ref{phi:U4} combined with the CY-2 surface classification (Beauville 1983), the external Leech-based $\mathrm{II}_{25,1}$ construction of Borcherds 1990 \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~99 (Fake-Monster), and the Monster $\mathrm{II}_{1,1}$-quantisation. The Leech/Fake-Monster row is a Borcherds--$\Psi$ row, not a compact CY$_3$ $\Phi_3$ placement. The four proved $\Psi$-rows saturate (i)--(iii), and Vol.~III Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-psi-four-is-all} proves the reverse inclusion on reflective GN data. The super-functor clause (iv) adjoins the Conway row via $\Psi^{s}$ (Vol.~III Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-conway-psi-super-image}) without affecting the non-surjectivity of Theorem~\ref{thm:psi-nonsurjective-gn}: the 23 non-Leech Niemeier BKMs and the paramodular-prime BKMs of Gritsenko 1999 remain outside $\mathrm{Im}(\Psi)\cup\mathrm{Im}(\Psi^{s})$, because their defining lattices (rank-$26$ Scheithauer or non-unit-scaling II$_{1,1}(p) \oplus \Lambda$) do not match any of (i)--(iv). The paramodular-prime BKMs carry lattices $\mathrm{II}_{1,1}(p) \oplus \Lambda$ with non-unit scaling $p$; no CY-$d$ Mukai lattice has non-unit scaling in its II$_{1,1}$-factor (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Prop.~2.5 for unimodularity of the Mukai factor), so the paramodular $p$-rescalings are outside $\mathrm{Im}(\Psi)$ unconditionally.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Why scope-restriction is \emph{the} correct statement]
\label{rem:psi-scope-restriction-discipline}
\index{Psi functor!scope-restricted surjectivity}
Unconditional $\Psi$-surjectivity onto $\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ is \emph{false} (Theorem~\ref{thm:psi-nonsurjective-gn}): the 23 non-Leech Niemeier BKMs are genuine counterexamples. Scope-restricted surjectivity onto the five-row sub-image keeps the II$_{25,1}$ Leech/Fake-Monster row in the external Borcherds--$\Psi$ lane, not in the compact CY$_3$ $\Phi_3$ lane; $\Psi|_{d=2}$ accounts for the three CY-2-surface-Mukai rows, and the Conway conjecture would fill the fifth super-row. The attempted identification ``$\Psi$ surjects onto all GN-Siegel-automorphic-product BKMs'' conflates (a) the unconditional-surjectivity question with (b) the scope-restricted target ($\mathrm{CY}_d$-input with $d \in \{2, 3\}$), and (c) the six construction routes K3 / Enriques / Monster / Fake-Monster / Conway / paramodular-prime as if they were a single functor. Two facts govern the correct statement: $\Phi_d$-output is $d$-dependent, and the CY-C identification remains conjectural with $G(X)$ unconstructed in general. The correct statement names the domain $\mathrm{CY}^{\mathrm{Siegel-aut}}_{d \in \{2, 3\}}$ and the codomain as the $d$-stratified sub-class $\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN, CY-derivable}} = \bigcup_{d \in \{2,3\}} \mathrm{Im}(\Psi|_d)$. Unconditional surjectivity onto $\mathrm{BKM}^{\mathrm{GN}}$ would require a $\Psi_4$ at CY-$4$ or higher, blocked by the absence of a rank-$2$ hyperbolic sublattice compatible with the Serre-twist $\mathbb{S}_\cC \simeq [4]$.
\end{remark}


% ============================================================
\section{Beyond CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3}: chiral algebras from non-CY geometries}
\label{sec:beyond-cy3}
% ============================================================

The constructions of \S\ref{sec:d3-functor-chain} produce a tagged strict output $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(\cC)$ only when $\cC$ carries a CY$_3$ structure and the framed datum of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} is fixed: the local CY$_3$ condition on the total space $\mathrm{Tot}(E)$ for $E \to C$ a rank-$2$ bundle on a smooth curve reads $\det(E) \cong K_C$, the cohomological identity that supplies the chain-level Maurer--Cartan equation $D \cdot \Theta + \tfrac12 [\Theta, \Theta] = 0$. The topological $\bS^3$-framing trivialises on the symplectic-reduction locus of Theorem~\ref{thm:s3-framing-vanishes}; the present obstruction is not topological but \emph{chain-level}, the curvature class produced by failure of $\det(E) \cong K_C$. What happens off the CY locus? The bar complex still exists; the chain-level CY$_3$ condition fails. The numerical defect $\delta := \deg(\det E) - \deg(K_C) \in \bZ$ controls a curvature element on the bar complex (introduced as $m_0$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:curved-chiral-non-cy}), and the strict framed output is replaced by a \emph{curved} chiral-algebra candidate parametrised by $\delta$, with $\delta = 0$ recovering the strict CY$_3$ branch.

\begin{definition}[CY defect]
\label{def:cy-defect}
For $E \to C$ a rank-$2$ bundle, the \emph{CY defect} is $\delta := \deg(\det E) - \deg(K_C) = \deg(\det E) - (2g - 2)$. The CY condition is $\delta = 0$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Curved chiral algebra from non-CY local surfaces]
\label{thm:curved-chiral-non-cy}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
When $\delta \neq 0$, the chiral algebra $A_E$ is curved: the curvature element $m_0 = \delta \cdot \omega_C$ is nonzero, the bar complex satisfies $d^2 = [m_0, -]$, and the shadow obstruction tower satisfies the inhomogeneous MC equation
\[
 D \cdot \Theta + \tfrac{1}{2}[\Theta, \Theta] = m_0.
\]
The curved modular characteristic is
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:kappa-curved}
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{crv}} = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} + \frac{\delta^2 \, \chi(C)}{24},
\end{equation}
where $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ is the uncurved value at $\delta = 0$. The correction is quadratic in~$\delta$, linear in~$\chi(C)$, and vanishes identically on the torus ($g = 1$, $\chi = 0$).

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 119 tests in \texttt{curved\_shadow\_non\_cy.py}; 130 tests in \texttt{rank2\_bundle\_chiral.py}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Hitchin modular characteristic]
\label{thm:hitchin-kappa}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For the Hitchin system on a genus-$g$ curve $C$ with gauge group $\mathrm{GL}(n)$:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathrm{Hitchin}\;\mathrm{GL}(n), C_g) = g,
\]
independent of~$n$. The $\mathrm{SL}(n)$ factor at the critical level $k = -n$ has $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\widehat{\fsl}_{n, -n}) = 0$ (the algebraic manifestation of classical integrability of the Hitchin system). Only the $\mathrm{GL}(1)$ center contributes: the rank-$g$ Heisenberg from $T^*\mathrm{Jac}(C)$ gives $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = g$.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 242 tests in \texttt{higgs\_bundle\_chiral.py}; 290 tests in \texttt{spectral\_curve\_chiral.py}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Kapustin--Witten twist parameter dependence]
\label{prop:kw-kappa-linear}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For the Kapustin--Witten twisted $\cN = 4$ gauge theory with gauge group~$G$ and twist parameter $t \in \C\bP^1$:
\[
 \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_t) = -\frac{\dim(\frakg) \cdot t}{2}.
\]
At $t = 0$ (critical level): $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$, commutative ($\Einf$). At $t = 1$ (balanced): $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = -\dim(\frakg)/2$. At $t = \infty$ (dual critical): $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \to \infty$. Complementarity on the Kapustin--Witten twist family: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_t) + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_t^!) = \dim(\frakg)/2$ for all~$t$ and all gauge groups. This is a KW-specific additive normalisation, distinct from the standard KM/free-field complementarity $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}'=0$ on the Heisenberg lane (Vol~I, Theorem~C) and the Virasoro complementarity $c+c'=13$.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 137 tests in \texttt{kw\_twisted\_n4\_chiral.py}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[The BCOV constant-map formula vs the shadow formula]
\label{rem:bcov-vs-shadow}
%: structural identification correct, quantitative instantiation fails at g >= 2.
The identification BCOV $=$ shadow is \emph{structural}: the holomorphic anomaly equation IS the genus spectral sequence of an MC equation in the Costello--Li dgLa. However, the \emph{quantitative} formula $F_g = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot \lambda_g^{\mathrm{FP}}$ fails for compact CY$_3$ at $g \geq 2$. The BCOV constant-map formula involves the product $B_{2g} \cdot B_{2g-2}$ of two consecutive Bernoulli numbers, while the shadow formula involves $B_{2g}$ alone. Since $B_{2g-2}/B_{2g}$ varies with~$g$, no single~$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ reconciles the two at all genera. For the quintic: the effective $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ matching $F_g^{\mathrm{CM}}$ oscillates ($200, -28.6, -4.3, 2.8, -3.8$ for $g = 1, \ldots, 5$). The shadow formula applies to the \emph{uniform-weight lane} (free fields, toric CY$_3$); for compact CY$_3$, the full shadow tower $\Theta_A$ (all degrees) is needed.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 130 tests in \texttt{bcov\_e1\_shadow\_engine.py}, including 10 tests quantifying the genus-dependent disagreement.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[BCOV recursion \texorpdfstring{$=$}{=} shadow tower recursion on the scalar lane]
\label{prop:bcov-shadow-recursion}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The BCOV holomorphic anomaly recursion and the shadow tower recursion (Vol~I, Definition~\texttt{def:shadow-tower-recursion}, cross-volume) are identified under the genus-degree correspondence $g \leftrightarrow k = g + 1$:
\begin{center}
\small
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{tabular}{ccccl}
\toprule
\textbf{Genus $g$} & \textbf{Arity $k$} & \textbf{Shadow $S_k$} & \textbf{Value (Vir, $c=1$)} & \textbf{BCOV term} \\
\midrule
$1$ & $2$ & $S_2 = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ & $1/2$ & $F_1 = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}/24$ (seed) \\
$2$ & $3$ & $S_3 = \alpha$ & $2$ & $D^2 F_1 + (DF_1)^2$ \\
$3$ & $4$ & $S_4$ & $10/27$ & $D^2 F_2 + 2\,DF_1\, DF_2$ \\
$g$ & $g{+}1$ & $S_{g+1}$ &; & $D^2 F_{g-1} + \textstyle\sum_{r=1}^{g-1} DF_r\, DF_{g-r}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
On the scalar (uniform-weight) lane, the ratio $F_{g+1}/F_g = \lambda_{g+1}^{\mathrm{FP}}/\lambda_g^{\mathrm{FP}}$ is \emph{universal}: it is independent of $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ (the modular characteristic cancels in the ratio). Explicit values: $F_2/F_1 = 7/240$, $F_3/F_2 = 31/1176$, $F_4/F_3 = 127/4960$. The BCOV recursion on the scalar lane reduces to a numerical recursion for $\lambda_g^{\mathrm{FP}}$, which is the shadow tower recursion of Vol~I restricted to the scalar lane.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The identification proceeds in three steps. First, the Costello--Li dgLa $\mathfrak{g}_X = \mathrm{PV}^{*,*}(X^{\vee})[1]$ IS the bar dgLa of $A_X$ (Costello 2007). Second, the MC equation $D\Theta + \frac{1}{2}[\Theta, \Theta] = 0$ expanded at genus~$g$ gives the HAE (Theorem~\ref{thm:hae-mc-structural}). Third, on the scalar lane $F_g = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot \lambda_g^{\mathrm{FP}}$, the genus-$g$ MC equation reduces to a recursion for the Faber--Pandharipande intersection numbers. The degree-$(g+1)$ shadow invariant $S_{g+1}$ encodes the $\Ainf$~operation~$m_{g+1}$; on the scalar lane this is the deviation from universality (the off-diagonal correction). The handle-creation term $D_j D_k F_{g-1}$ corresponds to the propagator insertion in the bar complex; the factorization sum $\sum D_j F_r \cdot D_k F_{g-r}$ corresponds to $[\Theta^{(r)}, \Theta^{(g-r)}]$. The universal ratios are verified numerically at $g = 1, \ldots, 4$.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 47 tests in \texttt{test\_bcov\_shadow\_tower.py}, including genus-by-genus comparison for C${}^3$, quintic, K3$\times$E, and the Virasoro channel, universal ratio verification through genus~$4$, and handle/factorization decomposition at each genus (\texttt{bcov\_shadow\_tower.py}).

\begin{remark}[Holomorphic anomaly as shadow non-termination]
\label{rem:anomaly-shadow-nontermination}
The holomorphic anomaly $\bar\partial F_g \neq 0$ is \emph{equivalent} to the shadow tower not terminating at degree~$g+1$. The shadow class (Definition~\ref{def:shadow-class}, Vol~I) determines the anomaly structure:
\begin{center}
\small
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{tabular}{clll}
\toprule
\textbf{Class} & \textbf{Shadow tower} & \textbf{Genus expansion} & \textbf{Anomaly type} \\
\midrule
$\mathbf{G}$ & Terminates at $S_2$ & $F_g = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot \lambda_g^{\mathrm{FP}}$ (scalar lane only) & No off-diagonal anomaly \\
$\mathbf{L}$ & Finite depth & Polynomial corrections & Finite anomaly \\
$\mathbf{C}$ & Convergent & Convergent (radius $1/S_4$) & Convergent anomaly \\
$\mathbf{M}$ & Gevrey-$1$ divergent & $|F_g| \sim (2g)!/(2\pi)^{2g}$ & Mock modular \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
For class~$\mathbf{M}$ (Virasoro at $c = 1$, discriminant $\Delta = 8\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} S_4 = 40/27$), the genus expansion requires Borel resummation; the non-holomorphic completion of $F_g$ produces mock modular forms; and the Borel-plane singularities are conjecturally identified with BPS instanton actions (cf.~Remark~\ref{rem:bcov-shadow-connection}). The compact CY$_3$ discrepancy (Remark~\ref{rem:bcov-vs-shadow}; the extra $B_{2g-2}$ factor in the constant-map formula) arises because the full shadow tower $\Theta_A$ (all weight sectors) contributes at $g \geq 2$, not just the scalar lane.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 47 tests in \texttt{bcov\_shadow\_tower.py}, including the discriminant computation, four-class classification, and compact CY$_3$ Bernoulli discrepancy at genera $2$--$5$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch} polysemy]
\label{rem:cy3-kappa-polysemy}
The symbol~$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ appears in at least four distinct roles:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A)$: the modular characteristic (Vol~I Theorem~D), from $F_1 = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} \cdot \lambda_1^{\mathrm{FP}}$.
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24$: the BCOV anomaly coefficient. Equals~$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X)$ for rigid compact CICYs with $h^{1,0} = 0$, but not in general.
 \item The MacMahon exponent (sometimes written $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ in the MNOP formula $Z_{\DT,0} = M(-q)^{\chi(X)}$): the exponent $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)$ in the degree-zero DT partition function. Equals $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$ for $\mathrm{Tot}(K_S)$. This is NOT a modular characteristic; it is the topological Euler characteristic, and must not be relabeled as $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$, or $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}$.
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$: the weight of the BKM automorphic form. For $K3 \times E$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = \mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5) = 5$.
\end{enumerate}
These coincide for Heisenberg ($\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = k$) and Virasoro ($\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = c/2$) but diverge for general CY$_3$. The quintic alone admits three values: $-25/3$, $-100$, $200$.
\end{remark}


\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\Phi_3$}{Phi-3} on the quintic and local~\texorpdfstring{$\bP^2$}{P-2}: scope, Borcherds admissibility, BCOV/G\"ottsche substitutes]
\label{rem:phi3-quintic-local-p2}
Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} supplies a framed object-level assignment only after hypotheses \textup{H1--H4} and, for exact Stage-$2$ kernel statements, a witnessed admissible specialisation datum are fixed. The explicit $\Eone$-chiral output is manifold-dependent, and the availability of a Borcherds-type automorphic witness is restricted to K3-fibered CY$_3$ with even unimodular Mukai lattice. This remark pins down the quintic and local~$\bP^2$ cases and separates the Borcherds substitute from the BCOV / G\"ottsche substitutes.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
 \item \textbf{Framed quintic branch $Q = X_5 \subset \bP^4$.} The coefficient part of the framed construction is controlled by the finite-Leray \v{C}ech estimate ($N=5$ patches, degree $5$, radius $\geq 1/20$; Proposition~\ref{prop:cech-htt-coefficient-convergence}). An explicit $\Eone$-chiral model through a Gepner DT/CoHA chart remains conditional on the framed Stage-$1$ comparison: the candidate is the Kontsevich--Soibelman CoHA $\cH(Q_{\mathrm{quintic}}, W_{\mathrm{quintic}})$ built from a quiver-with-potential presentation of the Gepner chart (Remark~\ref{rem:gepner-lv-dichotomy}; $625$ fractional branes; see \texttt{tilting\_chart\_cy3.py}). The large-volume chart has no tilting generator ($\Ext^3 \neq 0$ by Serre duality) and supplies the non-formal $\Ainf$-input $m_3 \neq 0$ (Yukawa coupling $H^3 = 5 + \mathrm{GW}$), consistent with class~$\mathbf{M}$ and the conjectural curved BCOV value $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{curved}}(A_Q) = -25/3$.
 \item \textbf{Toric local~$\bP^2 = \Tot(K_{\bP^2} \to \bP^2)$.} Explicit on the toric CoHA route: the local~$\bP^2$ output is the Schiffmann--Vasserot / RSYZ CoHA of the McKay quiver of $K_{\bP^2}$, equivalently the Nakajima cohomological Hall algebra on the nested Hilbert scheme tower of points on $\bP^2$ (cf.\ Theorem~\ref{thm:drinfeld-center-coha} and Chapter~\ref{ch:toric-coha}). Class-$\mathbf{M}$ assignment is confirmed by the full shadow tower, not by generator count; the $\Eone$ universality pillars (Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-universality-cy3}) are verified on the toric locus.
 \item \textbf{Borcherds admissibility is not $\Phi_3$.} A Borcherds multiplicative lift of a weak Jacobi form produces a Siegel automorphic form (e.g.\ $\Delta_5$, $\Phi_{10}$, $\Phi_{12}$) whose denominator identity carves out a generalized Kac--Moody superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ \emph{iff} the target lattice is Lorentzian and even unimodular of signature compatible with the Weil representation. For a K3-fibered CY$_3$ with fiber $S$, the Mukai lattice $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}(X) \supseteq H^*(S, \Z)$ inherits even unimodularity from~$S$, and the Borcherds lift applies (Theorem~\ref{thm:borcherds-lift-universal}, $K3 \times E$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 5$). For the \emph{quintic}, $H^*(Q, \Z)$ does \emph{not} carry an even unimodular Mukai structure: the relevant algebraic lattice is rank-$1$ ($H^2(Q, \Z) \cong \Z$ with $H^3 = 5$), total cohomology is supported in degrees $0, 2, 3, 4, 6$ with $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(Q) = -200$, and no rank-$2$ Lorentzian even unimodular sublattice admitting a Jacobi input is singled out. Consequently $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(Q)$ is undefined and no Borcherds lift produces $A_Q$ (consistent with Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-algebra} and Remark~\ref{rem:cy3-kappa-polysemy}).
 \item \textbf{BCOV as the Borcherds substitute on compact non-K3-fibered CY$_3$.} On a compact CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$, the BCOV partition function $Z_{\mathrm{BCOV}}(t, \bar t) = \exp\bigl(\sum_{g\geq 0} \lambda^{2g-2} F_g(t, \bar t)\bigr)$ plays the role that the Borcherds lift plays for K3-fibered CY$_3$: its holomorphic anomaly equation is the shadow-tower spectral connection (Theorem~\ref{thm:hae-mc-structural}; Proposition~\ref{prop:bcov-shadow-recursion}), and $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_X)$ is read off from $F_1 = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}/24$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:beauville-kappa-formula}). For the quintic $F_1 = -25/72$, matching $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24 = -25/3$. BCOV is \emph{not} a theta lift: there is no Jacobi-form input, no automorphic output, and no denominator identity; the substitute delivers a genus-by-genus holomorphic anomaly recursion, not a BKM superalgebra.
 \item \textbf{G\"ottsche as the Borcherds substitute on local surfaces.} For $\Tot(K_S \to S)$ with $S$ a smooth projective surface, the rank-$1$ sheaf partition function on~$S$ is the G\"ottsche generating function $\sum_{n\geq 0} \chi(\Hilb^n(S))\, q^n = \prod_{k\geq 1} (1 - q^k)^{-\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)}$ (at rank $1$; rank-$r$ Vafa--Witten generalisations in Chapter~\ref{ch:quantum-chiral-algebras}), whose exponent recovers $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_{\mathrm{loc}\,S}) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)/2$ via MNOP degree-zero (conjectural branch of Proposition~\ref{prop:beauville-kappa-formula}). For local~$\bP^2$, $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^2) = 3$, so the rank-$1$ exponent is~$3$ and the rank-$1$ VW generating function is $\prod_{k\geq 1}(1-q^k)^{-3}$ (rank-$r$ Vafa--Witten generalisations in Chapter~\ref{ch:quantum-chiral-algebras}). G\"ottsche delivers a partition function, not an automorphic form, and no Borcherds lift is available (Mukai rank~$3$, not even unimodular).
\end{enumerate}
\textbf{Protocol summary (a/b/c).} (a) Local~$\bP^2$ has an explicit toric CoHA identification; the quintic has a conditional Gepner CoHA candidate plus a proved coefficient-convergence estimate, not an unconditional $\Phi_3$ output. (b) Borcherds admissibility is strictly narrower than CY-A$_3$: it requires even unimodular Mukai lattice of Lorentzian signature with a Jacobi-form input, which holds for K3-fibered CY$_3$ and fails for the quintic and for local~$\bP^2$. (c) The non-K3-fibered substitute is \emph{dimension-specialised}: BCOV for compact CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0}=0$ (genus spectral sequence for~$F_g$), G\"ottsche / Vafa--Witten for local surfaces (rank-$1$ partition function with exponent $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(S)$); neither is a theta lift. These substitutes do not produce a BKM superalgebra and do not define $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$; they fix $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ via $F_1$ or via MNOP degree-zero respectively. \textup{(}Cross-references: Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-algebra} on the Monster/Borcherds orbifold route; Remark~\ref{rem:cy3-kappa-polysemy} on the $\kappa_\bullet$-polysemy at $d = 3$.\textup{)}
\end{remark}


\begin{proposition}[CoHA of non-CY threefolds]
\label{prop:coha-non-cy}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
For a quiver with potential $(Q, W)$ that does NOT satisfy the CY$_3$ condition
(i.e.\ $\dim \Ext^1(S_i, S_j) \neq \dim \Ext^2(S_j, S_i)$ for some simples):
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The CoHA $\cH(Q, W) = \bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}} H^{\mathrm{BM}}_*(\Crit(\Tr W)_{\mathbf{d}}, \varphi_{\Tr W})$ is still a well-defined associative ($\Eone$) algebra.
 \item The Euler form $\chi(\gamma_1, \gamma_2)$ is NOT antisymmetric: the symmetric part $s(\gamma_1, \gamma_2) = \chi(\gamma_1, \gamma_2) + \chi(\gamma_2, \gamma_1)$ is the CY defect at the categorical level.
 \item The bar complex $B(\cH(Q,W))$ is curved: $d^2 = [m_0, -]$ with $m_0$ proportional to the symmetric part of the Euler form.
 \item The Davison--Meinhardt PBW filtration requires the CY$_3$ Serre duality; it may fail for non-CY quivers.
\end{enumerate}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 141 tests in \texttt{test\_coha\_non\_cy\_threefold.py}, including explicit computations for the Jordan quiver with cubic potential ($W = x^3$, curvature $m_0 = 1$), the asymmetric $2$-vertex quiver ($3+1$ arrows, CY defect $= -4$), and Hall multiplication associativity for all tested quivers (\texttt{coha\_non\_cy\_threefold.py}).
\end{proposition}


\begin{proposition}[Spectral curves and the quantum Hitchin system]
\label{prop:spectral-curve-chiral}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
The Beilinson--Drinfeld quantization of the Hitchin system on a genus-$g$ curve $C$ with gauge group $\mathrm{GL}(n)$ produces a commutative chiral algebra at the critical level $k = -n$:
\[
 Z\bigl(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{n, -n}\bigr) \;=\; \mathrm{Fun}\bigl(\mathrm{Op}_{\mathrm{PGL}_n}(C)\bigr),
\]
the algebra of functions on $\mathrm{PGL}_n$-opers. The generators are the quantum Hitchin Hamiltonians, with spins equal to the Casimir degrees $d_1, \ldots, d_r$ of $\mathfrak{sl}_n$. The spectral curve $\Sigma_b \subset \mathrm{Tot}(K_C)$ has genus $g(\Sigma_b) = n^2(g-1) + 1$, and the Hitchin base has dimension $\dim B_{\mathrm{GL}(n)} = n^2(g-1) + 1$ (the Prym--Hitchin equality).

The Nekrasov--Shatashvili limit ($\varepsilon_2 \to 0$) recovers the oper as the symbol of the quantum spectral curve; WKB exactness holds for $\mathrm{SL}(2)$.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: 290 tests in \texttt{test\_spectral\_curve\_chiral.py}, including spectral curve genus via three paths (formula, Riemann--Hurwitz, adjunction), Hitchin base dimensions for all simple Lie types through $E_8$, and the Prym--Hitchin equality for $81$ $(n,g)$ pairs (\texttt{spectral\_curve\_chiral.py}).
\end{proposition}


%% ===================================================================
%% Derived stack of chiral algebra structures
%% ===================================================================

\subsection{The derived moduli stack of chiral algebra structures}
\label{subsec:derived-stack-chiral}

Fix a graded vector space $V$ with character $\chi(V; q)$. A chiral
algebra structure on $V$ is a $\lambda$-bracket
$\mu \in \mathrm{Hom}(V \otimes V,\, V((\lambda)))$ satisfying the
Borcherds identity (Jacobi) and skew-symmetry. In derived algebraic
geometry, the moduli of such structures form a derived stack
\[
 \mathrm{ChirAlg}(V) \;=\;
 \mathrm{RSpec}\bigl(\mathrm{CE}^*(L_V)\bigr),
\]
where $L_V = \mathrm{CHoch}^*(V, V)[1]$ is the shifted chiral Hochschild
cochain complex with the Gerstenhaber bracket as Lie bracket, completed
with the chosen $\lambda$-mode/residue convention and translation
covariance. A point
of $\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$ is a Maurer--Cartan element $\mu \in L_V^1$
satisfying $d\mu + \tfrac{1}{2}[\mu, \mu] = 0$, the Jacobi identity.

\begin{conjecture}[The K3 moduli map to \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$}{ChirAlg(V)}]
\label{conj:derived-stack-k3-moduli-map}
\ClaimStatusConjectured\quad
\index{derived stack!chiral algebra structures}%
Let $V = H^*(K3, \C) \cong \C^{24}$ with grading $\{0: 1,\; 2: 22,\; 4: 1\}$
from the Mukai lattice. The CY-to-chiral correspondence $\Phi_2$
\textup{(}CY-A at $d = 2$, PROVED\textup{)} defines a moduli map
\[
 \phi_{K3} \colon \cM_{K3} \longrightarrow \mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)
\]
whose image is the Heisenberg locus
$\mathrm{Heis}(V) \cong O(4, 20) / (O(4) \times O(20))$,
parametrising nondegenerate symmetric bilinear forms of signature
$(4, 20)$ on~$V$. The tangent complex at the Heisenberg point
$H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ is:
\begin{align}
 \HH^0_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}}, H_{\mathrm{Muk}}) &= \C
 && \text{(identity automorphism)}, \notag \\
 \HH^1_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}}, H_{\mathrm{Muk}}) &= \mathrm{Sym}^2(V^*)
 && \text{(deformations of the pairing, $\dim = 300$)}, \label{eq:hh1-heis} \\
 \HH^2_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}}, H_{\mathrm{Muk}}) &= 0
 && \text{(unobstructed)}. \notag
\end{align}
The virtual dimension at the Heisenberg point is
$\mathrm{vdim} = -1 + 300 = 299$. The differential of $\phi_{K3}$
maps K3 deformations to chiral deformations:
\[
 d\phi_{K3} \colon H^1(S, T_S) \longrightarrow
 \HH^1_{\mathrm{ch}}(H_{\mathrm{Muk}}, H_{\mathrm{Muk}}),
 \qquad
 \delta\omega \;\longmapsto\;
 \delta[a_\lambda b] = \delta\omega(a, b) \cdot \lambda,
\]
embedding the $20$-dimensional K3 moduli
\textup{(}smooth by Bogomolov--Tian--Todorov\textup{)} into the
$80$-dimensional Narain moduli $\mathrm{Gr}_+(4, \R^{4,20})$.

At ADE enhancement loci \textup{(}algebraic K3 with singularities\textup{)}:
the chiral algebra \emph{jumps} from Heisenberg to WZW $\times$
Heisenberg. These are singular strata of $\mathrm{ChirAlg}(V)$ with
additional $H^1$ from the root lattice; they are \emph{not}
deformations of the Heisenberg but distinct points in the derived stack.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Derived stack dimensions and the dgLa]
\label{rem:derived-stack-dimensions}
The dgLa $L_V$ controlling chiral deformations on
$V = H^*(K3, \C)$ has dimensions (respecting the Mukai grading):
$\dim L^0 = 486$ (grading-preserving endomorphisms),
$\dim L^1 = 10\,670$ ($\lambda$-bracket space), and
$\dim L^2 = 243\,892$ (Jacobi obstruction space). These are
computed by counting $\mathrm{Hom}(V^{\otimes n}, V)$ subject to
degree additivity $\deg(v_1) + \cdots + \deg(v_n) = \deg(w)$.
The grading reduces $\dim L^1$ from $24^3 = 13\,824$
(ungraded) to $10\,670$; the selection rules from the
Mukai grading constrain which $\lambda$-brackets are nonzero.

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $60$ tests in
\texttt{derived\_stack\_chiral.py} covering dgLa dimensions,
tangent complex at the Heisenberg point,
$\HH^2_{\mathrm{ch}} = 0$ (unobstructedness), virtual dimension,
K3 moduli map differential, Narain moduli dimension ($= 80$),
ADE enhancement loci for all types through $E_8$, and
Sym$^2(V^*)$ deformation space ($\dim = 300$).
\end{remark}


\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Phi_4$}{Phi-4} at \texorpdfstring{$d=4$}{d=4}: the \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb{P}^1$}{P-1}-family construction and the iterated-product associator}
\label{subsec:cy4-p1-family-phi-4}

At $d=4$ the CY-A correspondence $\Phi_d : \mathrm{CY}_d\text{-Cat} \to E_n\text{-ChirAlg}$
 does not exist as a functor into single $E_1$-chiral
algebras. The $\pi_4(BU) = \Z$ first Pontryagin obstruction blocks the $S^4$-framing
of $\HH_*(C)$, and Bogomolov--Tian--Todorov bivariance forces a two-dimensional
deformation parameter $(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)$. We construct the framework as a
$\mathbb{P}^1$-family of $E_1$-chiral algebras, in four explicit steps, and exhibit
the closed-form $h^{1,1}$-dependent correction to iterated-product associativity.

\subsubsection{The four-step explicit construction of \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_4(C)$}{Phi-4(C)}}
\label{subsubsec:phi-4-four-step}

Let $X$ be a compact projective CY${}_4$ and $C = D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)$.

\begin{construction}[\texorpdfstring{$\Phi_4$}{Phi-4} \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb{P}^1$}{P-1}-family chain-level model]
\label{constr:phi-4-p1-family}
The fibre $A^{(\sigma_3,\sigma_4)}_C$ at $[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4] \in \mathbb{P}^1$ is
constructed in four steps.

\noindent\emph{Step 1 \textup{(}HKR endomorphism dg algebra\textup{)}.} Form
\[
 \mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C) \;:=\; \mathrm{End}^\bullet_C(\mathcal{O}_X)
 \;\simeq\; \mathrm{PV}^*(X)[u]
 \;=\; \bigoplus_{p,q} \Gamma\bigl(X, \Lambda^p T_X \otimes \Lambda^q T_X^*\bigr),
\]
the polyvector dg-Lie algebra $T_X[1] \otimes \Omega^*_X$ with $\bar\partial$ as
differential, via Hochschild--Kostant--Rosenberg \textup{(}Kontsevich;
C\u{a}ld\u{a}raru\textup{)}.

\noindent\emph{Step 2 \textup{(}Negative cyclic homology\textup{)}.} Take the
negative cyclic refinement
\[
 \mathrm{HC}^-_*\bigl(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C)\bigr)
 \;=\; \bigl(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C)[u],\; b + uB\bigr),
\]
with Connes' periodicity parameter $u$ of degree $-2$. Its homology is the
Hodge-graded de Rham cohomology with the weight grading
\[
 H^*\bigl(\mathrm{HC}^-_*(\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C))\bigr)
 \;\cong\;
 \bigoplus_n u^{-n}\cdot F^n H^*_{\mathrm{dR}}(X, \C),
\]
where $F^\bullet$ is the Hodge filtration.

\noindent\emph{Step 3 \textup{(}BCOV Maurer--Cartan twist\textup{)}.} The
Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa action
$S_{\mathrm{BCOV}}(\mu) = \tfrac{1}{2}\langle \mu, \bar\partial \mu\rangle
+ \tfrac{1}{6}\langle \mu, [\mu, \mu]\rangle$ has Maurer--Cartan equation
$\bar\partial\mu + \tfrac{1}{2}[\mu, \mu] = 0$. At CY${}_4$ the BTT deformation
tangent space splits bivariantly:
\[
 T_{[X]}\mathrm{Def}(X) \;=\; H^{3,1}(X) \;\oplus\; H^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}(X).
\]
The $\sigma_3$-direction lives in $H^{3,1}$ \textup{(}complex-structure deformation,
inherited from $d \geq 3$\textup{)}; the $\sigma_4$-direction lives in
$H^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}$ \textup{(}the new primitive-middle deformation, with no
$d \leq 3$ analogue\textup{)}. The twisted Maurer--Cartan equation is
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:bcov-mc-twist-d4}
 \bar\partial\mu \;+\; \tfrac{1}{2}[\mu, \mu]
 \;+\; \sigma_3 \wedge \mu^2
 \;+\; \sigma_4 \wedge \mu^3 \;=\; 0,
\end{equation}
with $\sigma_3 \in H^{3,1}(X)$ acting as a Yukawa-type contraction and $\sigma_4 \in
H^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}(X)$ acting via the BCOV quartic Yukawa coupling
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV},\, ijkl}^{(4)}(X)$.

\noindent\emph{Step 4 \textup{(}$E_1$-chiral envelope\textup{)}.} Apply the universal
$E_1$-chiral envelope $U^{ch}_{E_1}(-)$ to the twisted dg-Lie algebra
$L^{(\sigma_3,\sigma_4)}_C := (\mathrm{End}_{\mathrm{HKR}}(C),\; \bar\partial +
[\sigma_3, -] + [\sigma_4, -])$ to obtain
\[
 A^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_C
 \;:=\; U^{ch}_{E_1}\bigl(L^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_C\bigr).
\]
This is functorial in $C$ at fixed $[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4] \in \mathbb{P}^1$, and the
family
\[
 \Phi_4(C) \;=\; \bigl\{A^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_C\bigr\}_{[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4] \in \mathbb{P}^1}
\]
is the family-valued CY${}_4$ chiral algebra.
\end{construction}

The $E_1$-level on each fibre is native; the $E_2$-braided structure lives on the
Drinfeld centre $\cZ(\mathrm{Rep}^{E_1}(A^{(\sigma_3,\sigma_4)}_C))$ with the
$p_1$-twisted half-braiding

\subsubsection{The closed-form \texorpdfstring{$h^{1,1}$}{h-1,1}-dependent associativity correction}
\label{subsubsec:closed-form-h11-correction}

The chiral tensor product $\otimes^{ch}$ on each $\Phi_4$-fibre is associative; the
iterated product $A_{\tau_1} \otimes^{ch} A_{\tau_2} \otimes^{ch} A_{\tau_3}$
nevertheless acquires an associator from the BCOV twist coupling the three fibres at
intermediate stages.

\begin{conjecture}[CY\texorpdfstring{${}_4$}{-4} associator closed form]
\label{conj:cy4-p1-family-associator-sextic}
\ClaimStatusConjectured\quad
\index{associator!CY${}_4$ closed form}%
\index{sextic CY${}_4$!associator}%
For $X$ a compact projective CY${}_4$ and $\Phi_4(X)$ the $\mathbb{P}^1$-family of
Construction~\ref{constr:phi-4-p1-family}, the iterated-product associator
$\Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}(X; \tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3)$ admits the closed form
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:cy4-associator-closed-form}
 \boxed{\;\;
 \Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}(X; \tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3)
 \;=\; \tfrac{1}{6}\, h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}(X)
 \cdot V(\tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3) \cdot \kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}}^{(4)}(X)
 \cdot M^{(\mathrm{corr})}_{V_4}(X),
 \;\;}
\end{equation}
where:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(}\roman*\textup{)}]
 \item $V(\tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3) := (\tau_1 - \tau_2)(\tau_2 - \tau_3)(\tau_3 -
 \tau_1)$ is the universal Vandermonde discriminant on $\mathbb{P}^1$, the unique
 \textup{(}up to scalar\textup{)} totally antisymmetric homogeneous cubic;
 \item $h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}(X) = h^{1,1}(X)$ for non-product CY${}_4$, and
 $h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}(X) = \Pi_{--}(M_{X'}^{V_4})$ for the iteration shadow $X'$
 when $X = X' \times Y$ \textup{(}V104 §3 numerical extraction\textup{)};
 \item $\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}}^{(4)}(X) = \int_X H^4 = \deg(X)$ for projective hypersurfaces,
 the BCOV classical Yukawa quartic;
 \item $M^{(\mathrm{corr})}_{V_4}(X) = \mathbf{1}_{V_4} = (1,1,1,1)$ for
 $h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}} = 1$ and $M^{(\mathrm{corr})}_{V_4}(X) = M_X^{V_4}$
 \textup{(}the bigraded Lefschetz spectrum\textup{)} for $h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}} =
 \Pi_{--}(\mathrm{intermediate})$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{conjecture}

\begin{example}[Sextic verification]
\label{ex:sextic-cy4-associator}
For the sextic $X_6 \subset \mathbb{P}^5$:
$h^{1,1}(X_6) = 1$ \textup{(}Lefschetz hyperplane\textup{)},
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}}^{(4)}(X_6) = \int_{X_6} H^4 = \deg(X_6) = 6$ \textup{(}top-intersection on
$\mathbb{P}^5$\textup{)},
$\int_{X_6} p_1 = -180$ \textup{(}adjunction $p_1 = -30 H^2$\textup{)},
$h^{3,1}(X_6) = 426$, $h^{2,2}(X_6) = 1752$, $h^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}}(X_6) = 1750$
\textup{(}standard sextic Hodge numbers\textup{)}. The conjectural closed form gives
\[
 \Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}(X_6; \tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3)
 \;=\; \tfrac{1}{6}\cdot 1 \cdot V(\tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3)\cdot 6 \cdot (1,1,1,1)
 \;=\; V(\tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3)\cdot (1,1,1,1).
\]
The $1/6$ normalisation cancels the BCOV Yukawa quartic $\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}}^{(4)} = 6$ exactly,
giving a clean integer Vandermonde correction.

The Mukai-style central charge is $c = 2606$ \textup{(}sum of the Hodge column $1 +
426 + 1752 + 426 + 1$\textup{)}; the Pontryagin shift is $\int p_1 / 12 = -15$;
$c_{\mathrm{eff}} = 2591$. At the $\sigma_4 \to 0$ limit \textup{(}the
$\Phi_3$-extension limit\textup{)}, $c = 2591$. At the $\sigma_3 \to 0$ limit
\textup{(}the new CY${}_4$-specific algebra glued to $\mathrm{Cliff}(H^{2,2}_{\mathrm{prim}})$\textup{)},
$c = 2591 + 1750/2 = 3466$.
\end{example}

\begin{remark}[The first Pontryagin number \texorpdfstring{$N(X)$}{N(X)} and the \texorpdfstring{$N=0$}{N=0} stratum of the CY\texorpdfstring{${}_4$}{-4} landscape]
\label{rem:cy4-pontryagin-number-stratum}
Define the first Pontryagin number of a compact CY${}_4$ $X$ by
\[
 N(X) \;:=\; \int_X p_1(T_X)^2 \;\in\; \Z.
\]
The number $N(X)$ controls the obstruction to globally trivialising the
$\mathbb{P}^1$-family of Construction~\ref{constr:phi-4-p1-family}
across the target space: at $N(X) = 0$ the $p_1$-twist class
$[p_1(T_X)] \in H^4(X; \Z)$ is self-annihilating under cup square, and the
iterated-product associator reduces to its $d \leq 3$ untwisted analogue; at
$N(X) \neq 0$ the associator acquires the closed-form correction
\eqref{eq:cy4-associator-closed-form} with a genuinely $\sigma_4$-dependent
$h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}$ pattern.

Evaluation across the compact CY${}_4$ landscape (Chern--Gauss--Bonnet plus
Künneth, using $p_1(T_X) = -2 c_2(T_X)$ when $c_1 = 0$, compatible with
Table~\textup{(}$d = 4$ Pontryagin values, this chapter\textup{)}):
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{llr}
 \toprule
 CY${}_4$ $X$ & $\int_X p_1$ & $N(X) = \int_X p_1^2$ \\
 \midrule
 Abelian CY${}_4$ $\;\C^4/\Lambda$ & $0$ & $0$ \\
 $K3 \times T^4$ & $-48$ & $0$ \\
 Sextic $X_6 \subset \mathbb{P}^5$ & $-180$ & $5400$ \\
 $K3 \times K3$ & $-96$ & $4608$ \\
 Septic $X_7 \subset \mathbb{P}^6$ & $-42 \cdot 7 = -294$ & $12348$ \\
 \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The two entries on the $N = 0$ stratum share a structural property: in
$K3 \times T^4$ the $T^4$ factor is Pontryagin-flat, so $p_1(T_X)$ is the
pullback of a single $K3$ factor and $p_1^2$ lives in $H^8(K3) = 0$ (the
K3 factor is real-$4$-dimensional); in $\C^4/\Lambda$ all Pontryagin classes
of the flat tangent bundle vanish. For $K3 \times K3$ the diagonal terms
$\pi_i^* p_1(K3)^2$ still vanish by dimension, but the cross term
$2 \pi_1^* p_1(K3) \cdot \pi_2^* p_1(K3)$ integrates to
$2 \cdot (-48) \cdot (-48) = 4608$, placing the Kummer--Hilbert target
firmly off the $N = 0$ stratum. The genuine $N = 0$ CY${}_4$ examples are
abelian CY${}_4$ and $K3 \times T^4$, and the CY${}_4$-specific associator
correction \eqref{eq:cy4-associator-closed-form} degenerates trivially on
this stratum.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Falsification test beyond the sextic]
\label{rem:cy4-associator-falsification}
Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy4-p1-family-associator-sextic} is at present verified
only at the sextic Example~\ref{ex:sextic-cy4-associator} and extrapolated
to $K3 \times E \times E$ via iteration-shadow extraction. Two
independent next-order falsification tests fix the closed form beyond the
verified locus:

\noindent\textup{(T1)} \emph{Septic hypersurface $X_7 \subset \mathbb{P}^6$.}
With $h^{1,1}(X_7) = 1$ (Lefschetz), $\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}}^{(4)}(X_7) = \int H^4 = 7$,
$\int p_1 = -42 \cdot 7 = -294$, $N(X_7) = 12348$, the prediction reads
$\Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}(X_7;\tau) = \tfrac{1}{6} \cdot 1 \cdot V(\tau) \cdot 7 \cdot M^{(\mathrm{corr})}_{V_4}(X_7)
= \tfrac{7}{6}\, V(\tau)\, M^{(\mathrm{corr})}_{V_4}(X_7)$.
The non-integer $7/6$ normalisation is a genuine numerical prediction: any
alternative closed form $\Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}} = \alpha \, h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}
\, V \, \kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}}^{(4)} \, M^{(\mathrm{corr})}$ with $\alpha \neq 1/6$ disagrees
at the septic while matching the sextic integer coincidence.

\noindent\textup{(T2)} \emph{K3 $\times$ K3 as product-CY${}_4$.}
With $h^{1,1}(K3 \times K3) = 40$, $h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}$ now in the
iteration-shadow case, $\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}}^{(4)} = \int H^4 = 0$ for the Mukai-polarised
product (no ample hyperplane class inherited from a single $\mathbb{P}^n$),
the conjectural closed form predicts $\Delta_{\mathrm{assoc}}(K3 \times K3;\tau)
= 0$: the iterated associator vanishes despite $N(X) = 4608 \neq 0$ because
the BCOV quartic coupling $\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}}^{(4)}$ factors through the Mukai lattice
structure (both K3 factors contribute null-norm hyperplanes). A non-zero
iterated-product associator at $K3 \times K3$ would FALSIFY the closed-form
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}}^{(4)}$-dependence and force a refinement by a $p_1^2 / N(X)$ correction
term.

The falsification pair $(T1, T2)$ is programme-priority: evaluation at a
second non-product compact CY${}_4$ (septic) and a second product CY${}_4$
(K3 $\times$ K3) pins the closed form beyond the sextic coincidence.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Why the iteration-shadow correction]
\label{rem:cy4-iter-shadow-correction}
The substitution $h^{1,1} \mapsto h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}$ in
\eqref{eq:cy4-associator-closed-form} is forced by the V104 §3 numerical
investigation at $K3 \times E \times E$ via $K3 \times T^4$: the iterated-product
discrepancy is $\delta = M_{K3 \times E}$ with multiplier $\Pi_{--}(K3 \times E) =
11$, not the bare $h^{1,1}(K3 \times E) = 21$. The chiral data at $d=4$ depends on
the chosen factorisation path through lower-dimensional pieces \textup{(}six
routes $\ne$ six applications of one functor\textup{)}; the $\Pi_{--}$-character of
the intermediate stage is the operadic-effective parameter that enters the
correction. For non-product CY${}_4$ \textup{(}sextic\textup{)} no iteration shadow
exists, and $h^{1,1}_{\mathrm{eff}}$ reduces to the bare $h^{1,1}$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[The Vandermonde structure: \texorpdfstring{$S_3$}{S-3}-invariance and \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb{P}^1$}{P-1}-uniqueness]
\label{rem:vandermonde-uniqueness}
The Vandermonde discriminant $V(\tau_1, \tau_2, \tau_3)$ is the unique \textup{(}up
to scalar\textup{)} totally antisymmetric homogeneous cubic in three variables. This
is a consequence of $S_3$-representation theory: the sign representation of $S_3$
appears with multiplicity one in $\mathrm{Sym}^3(\C^3)$, with generator $V$. The
associator must be antisymmetric in the three $\tau_i$ \textup{(}Mac Lane's
pentagon-without-orientation argument applied to associator-only data\textup{)},
hence factors through $V$. The cubic structure is forced by degree-counting against
the BCOV quartic $\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV},\, ijkl}^{(4)}$ \textup{(}rank-four tensor on $H^{1,1}$,
contracting against three $\tau_i$ leaves a degree-$3$ polynomial on
$\mathbb{P}^1$\textup{)}.
\end{remark}

\noindent\textit{Verification}: $37$ tests in
\texttt{compute/tests/test\_CY4\_iterated\_product\_assoc.py}, covering all
four steps of Construction~\ref{constr:phi-4-p1-family}, the closed-form correction
of Conjecture~\ref{conj:cy4-p1-family-associator-sextic} at the sextic, the
Vandermonde antisymmetry and $\mathbb{P}^1$-cubic uniqueness, the Pontryagin shift
via two independent paths \textup{(}BCOV-side and adjunction-side\textup{)}, the
chiral envelope central charge at the two limits \textup{(}$2591$ at $\sigma_4 = 0$,
$3466$ at $\sigma_3 = 0$\textup{)}, the iteration-shadow correction at $K3 \times E
\times E$, and the independent-verification protocol with
derivation sources \textup{(}BCOV/HKR/MC\textup{)} disjoint from verification sources
\textup{(}deg, Lefschetz, adjunction, $S_3$-rep theory\textup{)}. All $37$ tests pass.
The conjectural status is preserved throughout: the closed
form is verified at the sextic special case but remains conjectural for general
non-product CY${}_4$ pending the Goodwillie-tower obstruction analysis at the second
layer $\HH^{-3}_{E_1}$.


\subsubsection{The \texorpdfstring{$\bS^4$}{S-4}-framed effective functor \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_4^{\mathrm{eff}}$}{Phi-4-eff} and the \texorpdfstring{$p_1$}{p-1} vanishing criterion}
\label{subsubsec:phi-4-eff-p1-criterion}

Construction~\ref{constr:phi-4-p1-family} produces a $\mathbb{P}^1$-family
$\Phi_4(C)$ on every CY${}_4$ category. The naive pointwise suspension of
a tagged $d = 3$ branch collapses: at $d = 4$ the Gerstenhaber
bracket on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ has degree $1 - d = -3$, too shifted to
support operadic structure above the $\Eone$-floor of
Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy}. The non-trivial content at $d = 4$ is
not whether $\Phi_4$ produces a chiral algebra ($\Eone$-stabilisation gives
this unconditionally) but whether the resulting algebra admits a canonical
$\bS^4$-framing of its topological envelope, equivalently, whether the
first Pontryagin class of $T_X$ vanishes as an integral cohomology class.

\begin{definition}[Effective CY\texorpdfstring{${}_4$}{-4}-to-chiral functor \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_4^{\mathrm{eff}}$}{Phi-4-eff}]
\label{def:phi-4-eff}
Let $X$ be a compact projective CY${}_4$ and $\cC = D^b\Coh(X)$. Define the
\emph{effective $\bS^4$-framed functor}
$\Phi_4^{\mathrm{eff}}(\cC)$ as the unique pointwise section of
$\Phi_4(\cC) \to \mathbb{P}^1_{(\sigma_3 : \sigma_4)}$ of
Construction~\ref{constr:phi-4-p1-family} admitting a topological
$\bS^4$-framing of $U^{ch}_{\Eone}(L^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_\cC)$, when such
a section exists.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[{\texorpdfstring{$\bS^4$}{S-4}-framing vanishes iff \texorpdfstring{$[p_1(T_X)] = 0$}{[p-1(T-X)] = 0}}]
\label{thm:phi-4-eff-p1-vanishing}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}\textup{ (equivalence of obstruction classes)}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}\textup{ (dependence on CY-A$_4$ for the $\Eone$-chiral input)}
Let $X$ be a compact projective CY${}_4$ with $\cC = D^b\Coh(X)$. The
following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 \item The effective functor $\Phi_4^{\mathrm{eff}}(\cC)$ of
 Definition~\ref{def:phi-4-eff} exists as a single pointwise $\Eone$-chiral
 algebra \textup{(}not merely a $\mathbb{P}^1$-family\textup{)}.
 \item The integral cohomology class $[p_1(T_X)] \in H^4(X; \Z)$ of the first
 Pontryagin class vanishes.
 \item The effective framing obstruction of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy},
 $\Obs_{\mathrm{eff}}(4) \in \pi_4(BU) = \Z$, evaluates to zero on $\cC$.
\end{enumerate}
When $[p_1(T_X)] \neq 0$, the $\Eone$-chiral envelope $A_\cC$ exists as an
$\Eone$-chiral algebra \textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy}\textup{)}
but carries a canonical $\Z$-shifted symplectic structure of integer level
$\ell(X, \alpha) = \int_X p_1(T_X) \cup \alpha \in \Z$ for each
$\alpha \in H^4(X; \Z)$. The level $\ell$ is the CY${}_4$ analogue of the
Kac--Moody level at $d = 3$: a single integer parameter obstructing
flattening of the $\mathbb{P}^1$-family to a pointwise section.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
$(i) \Leftrightarrow (iii)$: the topological envelope of
$U^{ch}_{\Eone}(L^{(\sigma_3, \sigma_4)}_\cC)$ admits an $\bS^d$-framing
precisely when the classifying-space obstruction of
Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy} vanishes. At $d = 4$, this obstruction
class lies in $\pi_4(BU) = \Z$ \textup{(}Bott periodicity\textup{)}. When
it vanishes, the $\mathbb{P}^1$-family trivialises to a canonical
pointwise section at the basepoint $[\sigma_3 : \sigma_4] = [1 : 0]$
\textup{(}the $\Phi_3$-extension limit\textup{)}; when non-zero, no pointwise
trivialisation is $\bS^4$-framed.

$(ii) \Leftrightarrow (iii)$: the group $\pi_4(BU) = \pi_3(U) = \Z$ is
generated by the universal first Pontryagin class
$p_1^{\mathrm{univ}} \in H^4(BU; \Z)$
\textup{(}Milnor--Stasheff Thm.~15.7\textup{)}. For $\cC = D^b\Coh(X)$,
the classifying map $\cC \to BU$ attached to the tangent bundle pulls
$p_1^{\mathrm{univ}}$ back to $p_1(T_X) \in H^4(X; \Z)$ by the splitting
principle applied to $\Ext^\bullet_\cC(\cO_X, \cO_X) \simeq
\Lambda^\bullet T_X[\text{shift}]$. The obstruction class
$\Obs_{\mathrm{eff}}(4; \cC) \in \pi_4(BU) = \Z$ equals the integer obtained
by pairing $[p_1(T_X)]$ against a top-dimensional transverse $\bS^4$-cycle.
Hence $[p_1(T_X)] = 0$ in $H^4(X; \Z)$ implies
$\Obs_{\mathrm{eff}}(4; \cC) = 0$; conversely, vanishing against every
transverse $\bS^4$-cycle forces $[p_1(T_X)] = 0$ by Poincar\'e duality.

The $\Z$-shifted symplectic structure when $[p_1(T_X)] \neq 0$ is the
content of Theorem~\ref{thm:e1-stabilization-cy}, Part~3: the integer
level $\ell(X, \alpha)$ is the explicit evaluation
$\int_X p_1(T_X) \cup \alpha \in \Z$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[\texorpdfstring{$\bS^4$}{S-4}-framed stratum of the compact CY\texorpdfstring{${}_4$}{-4} landscape]
\label{cor:phi-4-p1-vanishing-stratum}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}\textup{ (cohomology computations)}
The $[p_1(T_X)] = 0$ stratum of the compact CY${}_4$ landscape is the
abelian stratum:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.3}
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
\toprule
