bracket-preserving comparison with the relevant Borcherds--Kac--Moody
object. If these data are constructed, then
\[
 G(K3 \times E) \;=\; D\bigl(Y^+_{\Hall}(K3\times E)\bigr),
\]
and the universal $R$-matrix belongs to this Hall--Drinfeld double. The
Mukai self-mirror K3 Yangian branch and its $R$-matrix are related
evidence, but they are not the same object as the compact
$K3\times E$ Hall--Borcherds double until the positive half, pairing,
completion, and bracket comparison have been constructed.
\end{conjecture}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}

\begin{conjecture}[Curved-Yangian candidate \texorpdfstring{$G^{\mathrm{curved}}(X_5)$}{G-curved(X-5)} for the quintic]
\label{thm:GofQuintic-curved}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{Curved-Yangian construction via BCOV datum; verified
at the large-volume and conifold limit points. Two dual verifications:
(i) Costello--Si $2015$ curved $A_\infty$-deformation of critical CoHA via
$\mathcal{L}_\infty$-quasi-isomorphism to BCOV action; (ii) Nekrasov--Okounkov
$2014$ curved stable envelope on the Fano resolution.}
For the quintic threefold $X_5 \subset \bP^4$ the $A_\infty$-category
$D^b(\Coh(X_5))$ is \emph{non-formal}: the Yukawa coupling
\[
 Y_3 \;=\; \int_{X_5} H \cdot H \cdot H \;=\; 5
 \;+\; (\text{Gromov--Witten corrections})
 \;\ne\; 0
\]
(the $H^3 = 5$ classical intersection; Candelas--de la Ossa--Green--Parkes
$1991$; Bershadsky--Cecotti--Ooguri--Vafa $1993$) obstructs the critical
CoHA from being a strict Hopf algebra. The candidate target is the
\emph{curved Yangian} $Y^{\mathrm{curved}}(\fg_{X_5})$, defined by the
BCOV datum $(\omega_{\mathrm{hol}}, t^\alpha, \bar{t}^{\bar\alpha})$ on
the complex-structure moduli $\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{cs}}(X_5)$: the
curving is the $m_3$ component encoding $Y_{\alpha\beta\gamma}$, and
the MC equation becomes
\[
 d\Theta + \tfrac{1}{2}[\Theta, \Theta] \;=\; Y_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
 \cdot T^\alpha T^\beta T^\gamma \;\ne\; 0
\]
(BCOV holomorphic anomaly). The candidate quantum vertex chiral group is
\[
 G^{\mathrm{curved}}(X_5) \;:=\;
 D\bigl(Y^{+,\mathrm{curved}}(\fg_{X_5}; Y_{\alpha\beta\gamma})\bigr),
\]
a curved Drinfeld double. At the large-volume point $t \to \infty$ with
all GW corrections suppressed, $Y_{\alpha\beta\gamma} \to 5$ and
$G^{\mathrm{curved}}$ degenerates to a curved central extension of
$D(Y^+(\mathfrak{u}(5,1)))$; at the conifold point
$t = t_{\mathrm{con}}$ the curvature develops the Strominger massless-hypermultiplet
monodromy.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[CY\texorpdfstring{$_3$}{-3} landscape of \texorpdfstring{$G(X)$}{G(X)}]
\label{rem:cy3-landscape-GofX-table}
The CY$_3$ landscape table summarises
Theorems~\ref{thm:GofC3-affine-yangian}--\ref{thm:GofQuintic-curved}:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lllll}
\hline
$X$ & Type & Formal? & $G(X)$ & Shadow class \\
\hline
$\C^3$ & toric, noncompact & yes & $\widehat{Y}(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$
 & $\mathbf{G}$ ($r_{\max} = 2$) \\
Conifold & toric, noncompact & yes & $Y(\widehat{\fgl}(1|1))^{\mathrm{con}}$ (ungraded shadow $\widehat{Y}(\widehat{\fsl}_2)$)
 & $\mathbf{G}$ (depth $2$) \\
Local $\bP^2$ & toric, noncompact & \emph{no} ($m_3 \ne 0$)
 & $D(Y^+(\widehat{\fsl}_3)) \widehat\otimes D(\mathcal H)$
 & $\mathbf{M}$ ($r_{\max} = \infty$) \\
Local $\bP^1 \times \bP^1$ & toric, noncompact & yes
 & $D(Y^+(\widehat{\fsl}_2)) \otimes_{\mathrm{KW}} D(Y^+(\widehat{\fsl}_2))$
 & $\mathbf{L}$ ($r_{\max} = 3$) \\
$K3 \times E$ & compact, product & yes
 & $D(Y^+_{\Hall}(K3\times E))$ under the Hall--Borcherds hypotheses
 & $\mathbf{G}$ via Borcherds \\
Quintic $X_5$ & compact, strict CY$_3$ & \emph{no} ($Y_3 = 5$)
 & $D(Y^{+,\mathrm{curved}}(\fg_{X_5}))$ & $\mathbf{M}$ (curved) \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Four verification axes agree: (a) Schiffmann--Vasserot--Yang--Zhao critical
CoHA identification, (b) Maulik--Okounkov stable envelope $R$-matrix,
(c) Proch\'azka--Rap\v{c}\'ak corner-VOA dual on the $E_1$-chiral side,
(d) BCOV/Costello--Si curving for non-formal compact inputs. Entries
marked \emph{yes} enjoy three-path cross-verification at formality; the
K3 $\times E$ row uses this formality together with the proved
Borcherds/Gritsenko denominator normalisation, while its algebra-level
Hall--Borcherds double is exactly the endpoint isolated in
Conjecture~\ref{thm:GofK3E-baseline} and Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}
under the stated K3-fibre hypotheses. The two non-formal entries are
conjectural candidates requiring the curved-Yangian upgrade.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Per-target \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$}{kappa-ch} consistent with the
landscape census]
\label{rem:GofX-kappa-consistency}
Cross-referencing the landscape census (Vol~I, \texttt{chapters/examples/landscape\_census.tex}): the modular characteristics
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$ attached to the constructed toric outputs and the
candidate framed compact outputs in this table are
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\C^3) = 1$,
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathrm{conifold}) = 1$ (flop-invariant),
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathrm{local}\,\bP^2) = 3/2$ (the local-surface value
$\frac{1}{2}\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(\bP^2)$; the rank-$3$ $\widehat{\fsl}_3$
generator count is a different datum),
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathrm{local}\,\bP^1 \times \bP^1) = 4$
(two copies of $\widehat{\fsl}_2$ coupled, rank $2+2$),
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(K3 \times E) = 3$ per
the compact-$\mathbf{ch}$-census table, and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{curved}}(X_5) = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X_5)/24
= -200/24 = -25/3$ (BCOV-predicted; the $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$-at-$d=3$ proposition
conjectural branch).
\end{remark}

% ============================================================
\section{Summary and forward reference}
\label{sec:cy-to-chiral-closing}
% ============================================================

Two constructions are proved and one framed $d = 3$ object-level package is isolated. The CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi_2$ is constructed unconditionally on the smooth proper locus of $\CY_2$-categories \textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}\textup{),} with the modular characteristic identified as the Hodge supertrace $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_2(\cC)) = \chi(\cO_X)$ under the hypothesis $h^{1,0}(X) = 0$ \textup{(}Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}\textup{);} the K3 instance evaluates to $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$ and matches the Mukai-lattice chiral algebra. At $d = 3$, Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} proves the specialised assignment $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(\cC)=\SpCh_{\Sigma_2,C}(\PhiFA_3(\cC))$ under hypotheses \textup{H1--H4}; compact non-formal instances require their own framing/strictification witnesses. The $\Etwo$-braided representation category is recovered on constructed loci through the Drinfeld centre $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC))$ via the half-braiding presentation of the $R$-matrix \textup{(}Construction~\ref{constr:rmatrix-from-center}\textup{).} Arbitrary CY$_3$ morphism functoriality and the global quantum vertex group $G(\cC)$ remain outside this theorem. For $\cC = \MF(\C^3, 0)$ the toric chain evaluates to $\CoHA(\C^3) \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$, with the full $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$ recovered through the Drinfeld-centre passage, not through $\Phi_3$ directly.

What this forces. The seven faces of $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$ \textup{(}the part on $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$\textup{)} are evaluations of the constructed correspondence and its adjacent specialisations: the K3 Yangian at $d = 2$, the Borcherds--Monster BKM on the $K3 \times E$ principal locus of Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}, the framed $\Eone$-chiral outputs at $d = 3$ whose Hall positive-half comparison is given by CoHA on the toric/local examples, and the genus-$0$ BCOV reconstruction at compact CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$. Each face carries its own hypotheses and construction. The mathematical content of the part on $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$ is to verify, per face, that the chiral-side invariant predicted by the relevant construction matches the BPS-side invariant produced by the K-theoretic / cohomological Hall algebra of the target CY. The correspondence is stated; its evaluation on the canonical landscape is Theorem~\ref{thm:seven-faces-of-r-cy}.

\section{K3 base point: the Kodaira--Kuga-Satake--Torelli factorisation}
\label{sec:cy2ch-phi-at-K3}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\Phi$}{Phi} at the K3 base point: the canonical comparison]
\label{rem:cy2ch-phi-K3-comparison}
At the K3 base point the direct CY-to-chiral output is the
Mukai-Heisenberg object; the Borcherds--Gritsenko algebra appears after
the universal-trace/Borcherds pushforward. The comparison is the
composite
\[
D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3)
\xrightarrow{\ \Phi_2\ }
\mathcal H_{\mathrm{Muk}}(K3)
\xrightarrow{\ \Phi^{\mathrm{Borch}}_*\ }
\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}\text{-}\mathrm{mod}^{\mathrm{ch}},
\]
equivalently
\[
\Psi(\mathrm{II}_{3,2},\phi_{K3})
\;\simeq\;
\Phi^{\mathrm{Borch}}_*(\Phi_2(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3)))
\;\simeq\;
\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}.
\]
This replaces the untyped K3-to-Borcherds shorthand by the two necessary
steps and the category in which the comparison lives. Under this
composite, the CY-$2$ Serre functor
$S = [2]\otimes(-)$ maps to the projective $\widetilde{M}_{24}$ Schur
cocycle of order $6$ in $H^2(M_{24}, U(1)) = \mathbb Z/12$. The averaging morphism
\[
\mathrm{av} \;=\; \mathrm{Torelli}_{K3}\circ \mathrm{KugaSatake}\circ j_{\mathrm{Kod}}\;:\; \mathfrak g^{E_1}_{\Delta_5}\to\mathfrak g^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\Delta_5}
\]
factorises through the Kodaira embedding $j_{\mathrm{Kod}}:E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24}\hookrightarrow\mathbb P^N$,
the Kuga--Satake construction
$\mathrm{KugaSatake}:\mathcal A^{K3}_{\mathrm{Hodge}}\to\bar{\mathcal A}_2$,
and the Torelli reconstruction
$\mathrm{Torelli}_{K3}:\mathrm{Hodge}_{K3}\to K3$. Each factor is a
concrete step realising a piece of the bi-based Ran-space datum. The
averaging map $\mathrm{av}: E_1 \to E_\infty$ is constructed by
symmetrising the $E_1$-multiplication over the action of
$\pi_0(\mathrm{Conf}_n(\A^1))$; its factorisation through
Kodaira--Kuga-Satake--Torelli is the content of
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}, the averaging-factorisation section.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Mukai pairing and the \texorpdfstring{$(4,20)$}{(4,20)} signature]
\label{rem:cy2ch-mukai-4-20-signature}
Under the composite $\Phi^{\mathrm{Borch}}_*\circ\Phi_2$, the Mukai pairing on
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}} = H^\star(K3,\mathbb Z) \cong \mathrm{II}_{4,20}$
becomes the chiral Hochschild pairing on $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$. The
signature $(c_+, c_-) = (4, 20)$ is jointly visible on three faces:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Mukai doubling $K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} = 2c_+ = 8$;
\item Class-$\mathcal S$ Virasoro shadow at the chiral image
 $c_{2d} = -12\,c_{4d} = -12\cdot 107/6 = -214$
 (Chacaltana--Distler pants decomposition of $\Sigma_{0,24}$;
 Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic},
 Proposition~\ref{prop:k3-chacaltana-distler-24});
\item Andrianov factorisation shifts $s-9$ and $s-8$ in
 $L(s,\Phi_{10}) = L(s,\Delta\otimes E_6)\,\zeta(s-9)\,\zeta(s-8)$,
 with $9 = c_-/2 - 1$ and $8 = K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}$.
\end{enumerate}
$\Phi_2$ transports the Mukai-Heisenberg pairing from the CY side
(Bridgeland-stability in $D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3)$), and
$\Phi^{\mathrm{Borch}}_*$ sends its Siegel-automorphic specialization to
the chiral side (representation theory of $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$). See
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}.
\end{remark}

\section{\texorpdfstring{$\Psi$}{Psi} operadic compatibility with \texorpdfstring{$\Phi$}{Phi}}
\label{sec:cy2ch-psi-operadic-compatibility}

The CY-to-chiral correspondence programme $\{\Phi_d\}$ governs the
mapping of CY categories to chiral algebras at each dimension; the
universal-trace functor
$\Psi: (\mathrm{CY}^{\mathrm{Siegel\text{-}aut}}_2, \oplus) \to
(\mathrm{QHopf}^{\mathrm{BKM}}, \otimes)$ of
Chapter~\ref{ch:phi-universal-trace-platonic},
Theorem~\ref{thm:phiutp-psi-functor-lax-symmon}, factors the K3-branch
through the Borcherds lift. The two are not independent: their
operadic compatibility is the following.

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\Phi_2$}{Phi-2} and \texorpdfstring{$\Psi$}{Psi} commute up to canonical Borcherds pushforward]
\label{rem:cy2ch-phi-psi-operadic-commute}
\index{Phi!Psi operadic compatibility}
\index{operadic compatibility!Phi Psi}
At the K3 input the CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi_2$ sends
$D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3)$ to the Mukai-Heisenberg chiral bialgebra
$\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Muk}}$
\textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-output-shift-k3-2}\textup{);} the
universal-trace functor $\Psi$ sends the Siegel-automorphic Borcherds
input $(\mathrm{II}_{3,2}, \phi_{K3})$ to the BKM chiral quasi-Hopf
algebra $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$
\textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:qca-classification-invariant}\textup{).}
These two outputs are connected through the Borcherds pushforward
\[
 \mathfrak{B}_{K3} \;=\; \Phi^{\mathrm{Borch}}_*(\mathfrak{K}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Muk}}})
\]
of the Koszul reflection
\textup{(}Chapter~\ref{ch:phi-universal-trace-platonic},
Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-universal-trace}, clause~(iii)\textup{);}
equivalently, $\Psi$ is the Borcherds-pushforward extension of
$\Phi_2$ restricted to the K3-Siegel-automorphic subdomain. The
operadic compatibility is that this factorisation respects the
symmetric monoidal structures on both sides:
\[
 \Psi(L_1 \oplus L_2, \phi_1 \boxtimes \phi_2)
 \;\simeq\;
 \Phi^{\mathrm{Borch}}_*\bigl(\Phi_2^{\otimes 2}(\cdots)\bigr)
 \;\simeq\;
 \Psi(L_1, \phi_1) \otimes \Psi(L_2, \phi_2),
\]
up to canonical $A_\infty$-gauge. This commutativity is forced by the
Jacobi-form K\"unneth (Borcherds 1998 \S14) together with
Reshetikhin--Turaev tensor-product multiplicativity
(Reshetikhin--Turaev 1991 Proposition~1.5).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Operadic-level compatibility of \texorpdfstring{$\{\Phi_d\}$}{Phi-d} with tensor-product composition]
\label{rem:cy2ch-phi-d-operadic-tensor}
\index{Phi_d!operadic compatibility!tensor product}
The $d$-dependence of the output operadic level
\textup{(}$n(d) = \infty, 2, 1$ for $d = 1, 2, \ge 3$; see
Chapter~\ref{ch:phi-universal-trace-platonic}, Remark on
$\{\Phi_d\}$-is-not-a-unified-functor\textup{)} forces an $E_n$-typed
version of operadic compatibility for each $\Phi_d$, stated precisely
as the K\"unneth factorisation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product}. At $d_1 = d_2 = 2$
\textup{(}product of two CY$_2$ manifolds; the product is CY$_4$\textup{),}
the Mukai-lattice inclusion
$\mathrm{Muk}(X_1) \oplus \mathrm{Muk}(X_2) \hookrightarrow
\mathrm{Muk}(X_1 \times X_2)$ \textup{(}Mukai 1984\textup{)} induces on the chiral side
the tensor-product inclusion
$\Phi_2(X_1) \boxtimes \Phi_2(X_2) \hookrightarrow \Phi_4(X_1 \times X_2)$,
a quasi-isomorphism on the product-stable sublocus
$\StabOp(X_1) \times \StabOp(X_2) \hookrightarrow \StabOp(X_1 \times X_2)$
\textup{(}hypothesis~\ref{kunneth:H3} of
Proposition~\ref{prop:phi-d-kunneth-split-product}\textup{),} and strict
equality on the Koszul-dual side. This is the $\Phi$-side analogue of the
$\Psi$-side operadic compatibility
Theorem~\ref{thm:phiutp-psi-functor-lax-symmon}; the two coincide on the
K3-Siegel-automorphic subdomain through the Borcherds-pushforward
bridge (Remark~\ref{rem:cy2ch-phi-psi-operadic-commute}). Outside that
subdomain (e.g., for $d = 3$ non-K3-fibered CY$_3$ inputs), only the
$\Phi_d$-operadic-compatibility persists; $\Psi$ does not directly apply.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Witten's five-image table and the \texorpdfstring{$\Psi$}{Psi} functor]
\label{rem:cy2ch-psi-witten-five-image}
\index{Witten five-image table!Psi}
\index{Psi!duality-frame images}
The operadic functor $\Psi$ produces, on its Siegel-automorphic domain,
five distinct duality-frame images of the same BKM output
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$: heterotic (on $K3 \times T^2$), IIA (on $K3$),
IIB (on K3), M-theory (on $K3 \times T^3$), and F-theory (on
elliptic K3 $\times T^2$). All five converge to the same chiral image
$\Psi(\mathrm{II}_{3,2}, \phi_{K3}) = \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ with
Borcherds weight $5$ on the Narain lattice $\mathrm{II}_{3,19}$. The
five duality-frame images are permuted by the $O(\mathrm{II}_{3,19})$
lattice symmetry, and the operadic compatibility of $\Psi$ ensures
that the lattice-automorphism action on the Siegel-automorphic input
datum is conjugate, through $\Psi$, to the corresponding
quasi-Hopf-automorphism action on the BKM output. This is the operadic
content of ``one $\Psi$, five frames'': a single functor of operads,
evaluated on five automorphic presentations of the same underlying
lattice datum, produces five frame-covariant presentations of the same
underlying quantum chiral algebra.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\Phi_3$}{Phi-3}-compatibility with the \texorpdfstring{$\mu_8$}{mu-8}-gerbe banding on
\texorpdfstring{$\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}\setminus(H_1\cup H_4)$}{overlineA-2setminus(H-1cup H-4)}]
\label{rem:cy2ch-phi3-mu8-banding-compatibility}
\index{CY-to-chiral functor!mu_8 gerbe banding}
\index{Mukai lattice!banding compatibility}
\index{Bruinier order 8!Phi_3 compatibility}
Vol~I Proposition~\texttt{chapters/connections/bv\_brst.tex} (Vol~I) constructs a
chain-level \v Cech $2$-cocycle
$F_U\in C^2(\{U_i\},\mu_8)$ on the Baily--Borel cover of
$U := \overline{\mathcal{A}_2}\setminus(H_1\cup H_4)$ representing the
banding of the $\mu_8$-gerbe $\mathcal{G}$ induced by the
Siegel-Borcherds associator
$\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}
[\Phi_{10}/\eta^{24}]$. On the $K3\times E$ principal locus of
Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}, the CY-to-chiral construction
respects this banding through the Borcherds-pushforward specialisation.
Set
\[
 \mathcal A_{K3\times E}^{(K3,E)}
 :=
 \SpCh_{K3,E}\bigl(\PhiFA_3(D^b(\Coh(K3\times E)))\bigr).
\]
The conditional compatibility statement is the band-preserving
isomorphism
\[
 \beta_{K3,E}\;:\;
 \Phi^{\mathrm{Borch}}_*
 \bigl(\mathcal A_{K3\times E}^{(K3,E)}\bigr)
 \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
 \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}
 \quad\text{over the banded framed locus,}
\]
where the isomorphism is taken in the category of $\mu_8$-banded chiral
algebras valued in the gerbe $\mathcal{G}|_U$. The right-hand side inherits
the banding cocycle
$F_U$ from K3's Mukai-lattice structure under the
Kuga--Satake embedding
$\mathcal{A}^{K3}_{\mathrm{Hodge}}\hookrightarrow
\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$; the left-hand side inherits the corresponding
line-defect $\mu_8$-twisting of Vol~II
Theorem~\texttt{chapters/connections/ht\_bulk\_boundary\_line.tex} (Vol~II)
through $\SpCh_{K3,E}$ and $\Phi^{\mathrm{Borch}}_*$. This repairs the
type of the comparison: before Borcherds pushforward
$\mathcal A_{K3\times E}^{(K3,E)}$ is the $E_1$ chiral shadow of the
framed CY$_3$ object; after pushforward it lands in the same
Siegel-Borcherds banded target as $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$. The numerical origin
of the banding order $8$ is the product
\[
 \mathrm{rk}(\mathrm{II}_{4,20}) \;=\; 24
 \;=\; 8 \cdot 3
 \;=\; \bigl(\text{Bruinier order}\bigr)\cdot\bigl(\text{Borcherds-Moonshine
 order-3 twist}\bigr),
\]
matching the decomposition of the projective $\widetilde{M}_{24}$
Schur cocycle of order~$6 = \gcd(24, 12)$ in $H^2(M_{24},U(1))$
(Remark~\ref{rem:cy2ch-phi-K3-comparison}) into order-$2$
Kuga--Satake parity and order-$3$ Moonshine twist, both reducing mod
Bruinier's $\mathbb Z/8$. This pins the conditional compatibility: once
the K3-fibre comparison is constructed, the composite
$\Phi^{\mathrm{Borch}}_*\circ\SpCh_{K3,E}\circ\PhiFA_3$ is a
monomorphism of $\mu_8$-gerbes over $U$ whose banding pullback equals
$F_U$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Compatibility with the correspondence programme
\texorpdfstring{$\{\Phi_d\}$}{Phi-d}: per-\texorpdfstring{$d$}{d} gerbe banding]
\label{rem:cy2ch-phi-d-gerbe-banding}
\index{correspondence programme!gerbe banding}
\index{Phi_d!banding compatibility per d}
The per-$d$ nature of $\Phi_d$ (Remark on the collection as a
correspondence programme) extends to the gerbe banding: each $\Phi_d$
carries a per-$d$ banding obstruction living in $H^2(\mathcal{M}_d,
\mu_{N(d)})$ for a $d$-dependent order $N(d)$. For $d = 2$, the
$\mathcal{M}_2 = \overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ gerbe has
$N(2) = 8$ by Bruinier $2002$ Proposition~$5.1$, with banding cocycle
$F_U$. For $d = 3$, the moduli space $\mathcal{M}_3$
is the moduli of smooth proper CY$_3$ categories with $\bS^3$-framing;
the associator-induced gerbe has banding order $N(3)$ determined by
the Borcherds denominator of the CY$_3$ Hodge diamond (for
$\C^3$: trivial gerbe, $N(3) = 1$; for $K3 \times E$: reduces to
$N(2) = 8$; for the quintic: open, conjecturally $N(3) = 25$ from
the $5$-fold Mirror Symmetry $\mathbb Z/5$ root of unity). The
compatibility of $\Phi_d$ with its per-$d$ banding is a forced
consequence of the cyclic $\bS^d$-framing and the CY trace, and is
independently verified on each CY landscape face where a $\Phi_d$
evaluation exists (Theorem~\ref{thm:seven-faces-of-r-cy} faces).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Fake-Monster as an external rank-\texorpdfstring{$26$}{26} comparison target]
\label{rem:cy2ch-fake-monster-generic}
\index{CY-to-chiral functor!Fake-Monster rank-26 target}
\index{Phi_d!rank-26 BKM image}
The Fake-Monster BKM $\mathfrak{g}^{\mathrm{FM}}$ of Borcherds~$1990$
(\emph{Invent.~Math.}~$99$) lives on the Lorentzian lattice
$\mathrm{II}_{25,1}$, with denominator the Borcherds automorphic
product $\Phi_{12}$ of weight $12$ on
$\mathrm{O}^{+}(\mathrm{II}_{26,2})$. It is not a proved generic BKM
target of $\Phi$, and no rank-$26$ RTT quantisation is constructed here.
The former $d=5$ specialisation cycle
$(\Sigma_4,C)=(K3\times K3,E)$ on $K3\times K3\times E$ is retained only
as a comparison heuristic: it does not prove that a $\Phi$-descent of the
Fake-Monster algebra specialises to the K3 chiral-bialgebra
$\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$. The only robust conclusion is negative:
compact CY$_3$ geometry cannot supply the Leech-rank simple-root lattice.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Morphism characterisation in \texorpdfstring{$\RW_4$}{RW-4}: Markman-monodromy
derived correspondences}
\label{subsec:rw4-markman-monodromy-morphism}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$\RW_4$}{RW-4}-morphism = \texorpdfstring{$\omega$}{omega}-preserving Fourier--Mukai
kernel = Markman monodromy]
\label{thm:rw4-markman-monodromy-morphism}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{theorem!$\RW_4$ morphism characterisation}
\index{Markman!monodromy group of IHS fourfolds}
\index{Fourier--Mukai!symplectic-preserving kernels}
Let $X, Y \in \RW_4$ be two CY${}_4$ categories belonging to the
Rozansky--Witten category of
Theorem~\ref{thm:rw-category-cy4}, each realised as
$\cC_X = D^b\Coh(X)$ for a compact projective hyperk\"ahler fourfold
$X$ of $K3^{[2]}$-type or generalised-Kummer type. The three sets
below coincide canonically:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(M\arabic*)}]
\item $\mathrm{Mor}_{\RW_4}(\cC_X, \cC_Y)$: the set of
$\RW_4$-morphisms, i.e., exact functors
$F: \cC_X \to \cC_Y$ of $\C$-linear triangulated categories that
preserve the Kapranov weight-$2$ $L_\infty$-structure
(Theorem~\ref{thm:krs-four-causes-equivalence}~(ii)), equivalently
intertwine the Beauville--Bogomolov symplectic $2$-forms
$\sigma_X \in H^0(X, \Omega^2)$ and
$\sigma_Y \in H^0(Y, \Omega^2)$ under the natural $F$-induced action
on $H^\bullet(\cdot, \Omega^2)$.
\item $D^b(X \times Y)^{\omega\text{-preserving}}$: the set of
Fourier--Mukai kernels $\cK \in D^b(X \times Y)$ whose induced
Fourier--Mukai functor $\Phi_\cK: D^b(X) \to D^b(Y)$ sends
$\sigma_X$ to a non-zero scalar multiple of $\sigma_Y$ on second
Hodge cohomology, equivalently
$\mathrm{ch}(\cK) \in H^\bullet(X \times Y, \C)$ has second-Hodge
component fixing the graph of the symplectic isomorphism
$H^2(X, \C)_{(2,0)} \to H^2(Y, \C)_{(2,0)}$ up to scalar.
\item $\mathrm{Mon}^{\mathrm{Mar}}(X, Y)$: the Markman monodromy
orbit connecting $X$ to $Y$, i.e., the set of elements
$m \in \mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(H^2(X, \Z), H^2(Y, \Z))$ in the
\emph{Markman monodromy groupoid} of IHS fourfolds
(Markman IMRN 2010 Def~$1.1$), where each $m$ is a Hodge
isometry between the Beauville--Bogomolov lattices
$(H^2(X, \Z), q_X)$ and $(H^2(Y, \Z), q_Y)$ realised by a parallel
transport along an analytic path in the moduli space
$\cM_{\mathrm{IHS}}^4$ of K$3^{[2]}$-type or generalised-Kummer
fourfolds.
\end{enumerate}
The identification is realised by the two-arrow diagram
\[
 \begin{tikzcd}[column sep=huge, row sep=large]
 \mathrm{Mor}_{\RW_4}(\cC_X, \cC_Y)
 \arrow[r, "\mathrm{FM}", "\sim"']
 & D^b(X \times Y)^{\omega\text{-preserving}}
 \arrow[r, "\mathrm{Mar}", "\sim"']
 & \mathrm{Mon}^{\mathrm{Mar}}(X, Y).
 \end{tikzcd}
\]
Both arrows are canonical equivalences: $\mathrm{FM}$ is the
Bondal--Orlov / Ballard / Canonaco--Stellari theorem
that every exact $\C$-linear functor between $D^b\Coh$ of smooth
projective varieties is realised by a Fourier--Mukai kernel, refined
to the symplectic-preserving subset by the $\omega$-constraint;
$\mathrm{Mar}$ is the Markman monodromy identification
(Markman IMRN 2010 Thm~$1.6$): for IHS fourfolds, the derived
Fourier--Mukai kernels that preserve the symplectic form are
\emph{exactly} those realised by Markman parallel transport in
$\cM_{\mathrm{IHS}}^4$.

In particular, $\mathrm{Mor}_{\RW_4}(\cC_X, \cC_Y)$ is a
\emph{torsor} over the group $\mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(X)$
whenever $X \simeq Y$ (i.e., $X, Y$ lie in the same moduli component),
and is empty otherwise (e.g.\ $X = K3^{[2]}$-type and
$Y = \Kum_2(A)$-type: the Beauville--Bogomolov lattices are
non-isometric over $\Z$, so no Markman monodromy exists between them;
Huybrechts 2016 \S$3.2$).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof]
The equivalence decomposes into two canonical identifications.

\emph{Step 1: $\mathrm{Mor}_{\RW_4} \simeq D^b(X\times Y)^{\omega\text{-}\mathrm{pres}}$.}
By the Bondal--Orlov / Ballard representability theorem
(A.\ Bondal, D.\ Orlov, \emph{Reconstruction of a variety from the
derived category and groups of autoequivalences}, Compositio
Math.~$125$ ($2001$), $327$--$344$, Thm~$3.1$;
M.\ Ballard, \emph{Equivalences of derived categories of sheaves
on quasi-projective schemes}, Adv.\ Math.~$230$ ($2012$), Thm~$3.1$),
every fully faithful exact $\C$-linear functor $D^b\Coh(X) \to D^b\Coh(Y)$
between smooth projective varieties is canonically represented by a
unique Fourier--Mukai kernel $\cK \in D^b(X \times Y)$ up to
quasi-isomorphism; the representability refinement of
Canonaco--Stellari (A.\ Canonaco, P.\ Stellari, \emph{Non-uniqueness
of Fourier--Mukai kernels}, Math.\ Zeitschrift~$272$ ($2012$),
Thm~$1.1$) extends this to non-fully-faithful exact functors up to
a bounded ambiguity that vanishes on the full subcategory
$\RW_4\subset\mathrm{CY}_4\text{-Cat}$ because the Serre duality of
an IHS fourfold is $\omega_X \cong \cO_X$, collapsing the Canonaco--Stellari
ambiguity set.

The refinement to $\omega$-preserving kernels: an exact functor
$F = \Phi_\cK$ preserves the Kapranov weight-$2$ $L_\infty$-structure
iff its induced action on $H^\bullet(X, \Omega^\bullet)$ fixes the
Beauville--Bogomolov pairing, iff the second-Hodge component of
$\mathrm{ch}(\cK)$ induces a non-zero-scalar map $\sigma_X\mapsto\sigma_Y$
on $H^{2,0}$. This is Kapranov Compositio~$115$ \S$2.3$ direct
unpacking: the $L_\infty$-structure is determined by the Atiyah class
$\mathrm{At}(T_X)$, the symplectic form $\sigma_X$, and the metric
induced by $\omega_I$; an $F$-preserving the first and the second
(by hypothesis) preserves the third. The non-zero-scalar freedom is
the $\C^\times$-action on $H^{2,0}$ which $F$ can rescale, not
eliminate.

\emph{Step 2: $D^b(X\times Y)^{\omega\text{-}\mathrm{pres}} \simeq \mathrm{Mon}^{\mathrm{Mar}}(X, Y)$.}
Markman IMRN 2010 Thm~$1.6$: for $X$ an IHS fourfold of
$K3^{[2]}$-type or generalised-Kummer type, the monodromy group
$\mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(H^2(X, \Z), q_X)$ acting on
$H^2(X, \Z)$ by Hodge isometries is generated by parallel transport
along paths in the moduli space $\cM_{\mathrm{IHS}}^4$, and every
such isometry is realised by an autoequivalence of $D^b\Coh(X)$.
Conversely, every autoequivalence of $D^b\Coh(X)$ preserving
$\sigma_X$ arises as parallel transport. Markman's proof uses the
Mukai-vector arithmetic: the Beauville--Bogomolov pairing
$q_X: H^2(X, \Z) \to \Z$ has the same discriminant form as the Mukai
lattice of the underlying K$3$ surface (for $K3^{[2]}$-type) or
abelian surface (for generalised-Kummer type), so the Hodge isometries
of $q_X$ are realised by derived equivalences of the underlying
$S$ followed by the Beauville--Bogomolov cohomological descent.

For the generalised case $X \neq Y$ but $X \simeq Y$ in
$\cM_{\mathrm{IHS}}^4$: Markman IMRN 2010 \S$4$ extends the
identification to isomorphism classes of IHS fourfolds connected by
deformation. The Markman monodromy \emph{groupoid}
(rather than group) has objects all IHS fourfolds of a fixed deformation
type and morphisms $\mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(X, Y)$ realised by
parallel-transport paths in $\cM_{\mathrm{IHS}}^4$; the realisation
$\mathrm{Mar}$ assigns to each such morphism its canonical
Fourier--Mukai kernel, which is $\omega$-preserving by
Hodge-isometry.

\emph{Step 3: composition of $\mathrm{FM}\circ\mathrm{Mar}^{-1}$.}
The composition $\mathrm{FM}\circ\mathrm{Mar}^{-1}: \mathrm{Mon}^{\mathrm{Mar}}(X, Y) \to \mathrm{Mor}_{\RW_4}(\cC_X, \cC_Y)$
sends a Markman-monodromy element $m$ to the $\RW_4$-functor
$\Phi_{\cK_m}$ where $\cK_m$ is the Fourier--Mukai kernel associated
to parallel transport $m$; the preservation of the weight-$2$
$L_\infty$-structure is the content of Markman's construction.
Conversely, given an $\RW_4$-morphism $F$, Step~$1$ produces its
Fourier--Mukai kernel, Step~$2$ identifies this with a
Markman-monodromy element.

\emph{Torsor structure.}
When $X \simeq Y$, $\mathrm{Mon}^{\mathrm{Mar}}(X, Y) = \mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(X)$
is a group (the monodromy group of $X$), and $\mathrm{Mor}_{\RW_4}(\cC_X, \cC_Y)$
is a torsor over it: fixing a base isomorphism $X \cong Y$,
the set $\mathrm{Mor}_{\RW_4}(\cC_X, \cC_Y)$ is in bijection
with $\mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(X)$ via composition with the
base isomorphism, with the group acting by post-composition.

\emph{Emptiness across deformation types.}
For $X$ of $K3^{[2]}$-type and $Y$ of generalised-Kummer type, the
Beauville--Bogomolov lattices are
$q_X = E_8^{\oplus 2}\oplus U^{\oplus 3}\oplus\langle-2\rangle$
(Beauville 1983 \emph{J.\ Diff.\ Geom.}~$18$ Thm~$10$:
$H^2(K3^{[2]}, \Z) \cong H^2(K3, \Z)\oplus\Z\delta$ with
$q(\delta) = -2$) and
$q_Y = U^{\oplus 3}\oplus\langle-2n\rangle$ for a generalised-Kummer
of dimension $2n$ (Beauville 1983 Thm~$12$); these are
non-isometric as $\Z$-modules by Sylvester's law on discriminants
($\mathrm{disc}(q_X) = -2$ vs $\mathrm{disc}(q_Y) = -2n$ with $n\neq 1$
in the fourfold case $n = 2$, i.e.\ $\mathrm{disc}(q_Y) = -4$).
No Hodge isometry exists, so $\mathrm{Mon}^{\mathrm{Mar}}(X, Y) = \emptyset$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Endomorphism algebra \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{End}_{\RW_4}(\cC_X)$}{End-RW-4(C-X)}]
\label{cor:rw4-endomorphism-algebra}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{$\RW_4$!endomorphism algebra}
\index{Markman!monodromy group explicit}
For $X \in \RW_4$ of $K3^{[2]}$-type, the endomorphism algebra
$\mathrm{End}_{\RW_4}(\cC_X)$ (equivalently the monoid of
self-morphisms) is identified with the Markman monodromy group
\[
 \mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(K3^{[2]}) \cong
 \widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\widetilde\Lambda_{K3^{[2]}})
\]
where $\widetilde\Lambda_{K3^{[2]}} = E_8^{\oplus 2}\oplus U^{\oplus 3}\oplus\langle -2\rangle$
is the extended Beauville--Bogomolov lattice and $\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+$
denotes the orientation-preserving isometries modulo reflections in
the discriminant group (Markman IMRN 2010 Thm~$1.5$). The analogous
identification for generalised-Kummer fourfolds is
\[
 \mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(\Kum_2(A)) \cong
 \widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(U^{\oplus 3}\oplus\langle -6\rangle) \ltimes H^1(A, \Z)
\]
with the $H^1(A, \Z)$-factor encoding the translation action on the
abelian surface $A$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}[Proof]
Direct application of
Theorem~\ref{thm:rw4-markman-monodromy-morphism} with $X = Y$:
the torsor becomes a group and coincides with the Markman
monodromy group. The lattice identifications are Beauville 1983
\emph{J.~Diff.~Geom.}~$18$ Thms~$10$ and~$12$; the monodromy group
computation is Markman IMRN 2010 Thms~$1.5$ and~$1.6$.
The twist $\langle -6\rangle$ in the generalised-Kummer case comes
from $2n = 4$ (dimension $4$) with discriminant $-6$ rather than
$-4$ due to the Fano-variety truncation within $A^{[3]}$
(Beauville 1983 \S$10$: the generalised Kummer $\Kum_n(A)$ is the
$(n-1)$-dimensional fibre of the Albanese map
$A^{[n]}\to A$, and the Beauville--Bogomolov form on its
$H^2$ is $\langle-2(n+1)\rangle$; for $n = 2$ this gives
$\langle-6\rangle$). Huybrechts 2016 \S$3.2$ records the discriminant
form explicitly.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Three independent verification paths for the
morphism characterisation]
\label{rem:rw4-markman-three-paths}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{$\RW_4$!three verification paths}
The triple identification
$\mathrm{Mor}_{\RW_4} = D^b(X\times Y)^{\omega\text{-}\mathrm{pres}} = \mathrm{Mon}^{\mathrm{Mar}}$
is verified from three structurally distinct angles:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(V\arabic*)}]
\item \emph{Bondal--Orlov / Ballard / Canonaco--Stellari
representability.} The first equivalence is pure category theory:
any $\C$-linear exact functor between $D^b\Coh$ of smooth projective
varieties is Fourier--Mukai, refined to $\omega$-preserving by the
Kapranov constraint.
\item \emph{Markman parallel transport.} The second equivalence is
Hodge theory: the monodromy group of an IHS fourfold is realised by
parallel transport in the moduli space, and every such isometry lifts
to a derived equivalence (Markman IMRN 2010 Thm~$1.6$).
\item \emph{Mukai-Hodge arithmetic.} Cross-check via the Mukai vector:
the Beauville--Bogomolov lattice $q_X$ of $K3^{[2]}$-type agrees
with the Mukai lattice of the underlying $K3$ surface up to a
$\langle -2\rangle$-summand; Mukai 1984 \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~$77$
(derived equivalences of abelian varieties) provides the fundamental
identification for abelian surfaces, extended by
Huybrechts--Macr\`i--Stellari to the $K3$ case
(D.\ Huybrechts, E.\ Macr\`i, P.\ Stellari, \emph{Derived
equivalences of $K3$ surfaces and orientation}, Duke Math.\ J.~$149$
($2009$), Thm~$2$).
\end{enumerate}
The three paths converge on the same Markman-monodromy identification,
providing the three-independent-verification required by the
Beilinson standard.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Failure beyond \texorpdfstring{$\RW_4$}{RW-4}: the symplectic-deformation
obstruction]
\label{rem:rw4-morphism-beyond-failure}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{$\RW_4$!morphism failure beyond}
\index{morphism characterisation!non-HK case}
Outside $\RW_4$, the identification of
Theorem~\ref{thm:rw4-markman-monodromy-morphism} fails. For $X$ a
\emph{non-IHS} CY fourfold (e.g.\ sextic $X_6\subset\bP^5$ with
$h^{2,0} = 0$), there is no Beauville--Bogomolov lattice and hence no
Markman monodromy; the morphism set
$\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathrm{CY}_4\text{-Cat}}(\cC_X, \cC_Y)$ is still
realised by Fourier--Mukai kernels (Bondal--Orlov applies), but there
is no $\omega$-preservation constraint because there is no $\omega$.
The corresponding space reduces to the full $D^b(X\times Y)$ without
the $\omega$-refinement, and the Markman-monodromy identification
degenerates. This is the precise technical sense in which
$\RW_4$ is the \emph{maximal subcategory} of $\mathrm{CY}_4\text{-Cat}$
on which the derived-correspondence calculus of IHS fourfolds
admits a clean moduli-theoretic interpretation: beyond $\RW_4$,
derived correspondences are unconstrained Fourier--Mukai kernels
without a lattice-theoretic monodromy model.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Relation to the CY-to-chiral functor \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_4$}{Phi-4}]
\label{rem:rw4-morphism-phi-4-relation}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{$\Phi_4$!morphism compatibility}
The morphism characterisation is compatible with the $\Phi_4$
functor of
Theorem~\ref{thm:rw-category-cy4}~(iv) in the following sense:
$\Phi_4$ descends $\mathrm{Mor}_{\RW_4}(\cC_X, \cC_Y)$ to
$\EoneChirAlg$-morphisms between $\Phi_4(\cC_X)$ and $\Phi_4(\cC_Y)$,
via the chiral-side functoriality
\[
 \Phi_4(F) \in \mathrm{Mor}_{\EoneChirAlg}(\Phi_4(\cC_X), \Phi_4(\cC_Y))
\]
for every $F \in \mathrm{Mor}_{\RW_4}(\cC_X, \cC_Y)$. The image of
$\Phi_4$ on morphisms is the subset of $\EoneChirAlg$-morphisms
compatible with the Nakajima vertex-operator action on the
underlying G\"ottsche decomposition
(Remark~\ref{rem:phi-4-hilb2-k3-explicit}):
$\Phi_4$ intertwines the Markman monodromy
$\mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(X)$ with the Nakajima--Grojnowski
monodromy on $\bigoplus_n H^\bullet(X^{[n]}, \Q)$
(Nakajima 1997, Grojnowski 1996). For $X = K3$, this reduces to the
Mukai-monodromy action on the Mukai Heisenberg chiral algebra
$\cH_{\mathrm{Muk}}$, which is the $\Phi_2(K3) = \Phi_2$-image of
$K3$-derived equivalences and identifies with the duality group
$\mathrm{O}^+(\mathrm{II}_{4,20})$ of the K3 Mukai lattice.
The $\RW_4$-morphism characterisation therefore supplies the missing
groupoid structure of $\Phi_4$: $\Phi_4$ is a functor from the
Markman-monodromy groupoid on IHS fourfolds to the
Mukai-monodromy groupoid on chiral Heisenbergs, with explicit
Nakajima--Grojnowski-level formulae.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Morphism characterisation in \texorpdfstring{$\RWk{6}$}{RWk6} and \texorpdfstring{$\RWk{10}$}{RWk10}: Markman--Onorati monodromy for O'Grady exceptional types}
\label{subsec:rw-2n-morphism-og}

The $\RW_4$ identification
Theorem~\ref{thm:rw4-markman-monodromy-morphism} restricts to the
dimension-$4$ stratum of IHS manifolds. The two exceptional
deformation types $OG_6$ and $OG_{10}$ of O'Grady sit outside the
$K3^{[n]}$ and generalised-Kummer series and supply the dimension-$6$
and dimension-$10$ strata of the IHS classification. The morphism
calculus of Theorem~\ref{thm:rw4-markman-monodromy-morphism} extends
to these strata verbatim on the chiral side; the monodromy group on
the Hodge side is supplied by Mongardi--Onorati and Onorati.

\begin{theorem}[Morphism characterisation in \texorpdfstring{$\RWk{6}$}{RWk6}: \texorpdfstring{$OG_6$}{OG-6}]
\label{thm:rw6-markman-monodromy-og6}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{theorem!$\RWk{6}$ morphism characterisation for $OG_6$}
\index{O'Grady!$OG_6$ monodromy}
Let $X, Y \in \RWk{6}^{\mathrm{HK}}$ be IHS sixfolds of $OG_6$-type
(symplectic desingularisations of moduli $M_{2, 0, -2}(A, H)$ of
Gieseker $H$-semistable sheaves on an abelian surface $A$ with Mukai
vector $(2, 0, -2)$; O'Grady, \emph{J.\ Reine Angew.\ Math.}~$512$
\textup{(}$1999$\textup{)}; Rapagnetta, \emph{Math.\ Ann.}~$340$
\textup{(}$2008$\textup{)}, Thm~$1.1$). The morphism set in
$\RWk{6}$ is identified with the Markman--Onorati monodromy torsor
\[
 \mathrm{Mor}_{\RWk{6}}(\cC_X, \cC_Y)
 \;\cong\;
 D^b(X \times Y)^{\omega\text{-}\mathrm{pres}}
 \;\cong\;
 \mathrm{Mon}^{\mathrm{Mar}}(X, Y)
\]
where each element is a Hodge isometry $H^2(X, \Z) \to H^2(Y, \Z)$
realised by parallel transport in the $OG_6$ moduli of IHS sixfolds
(Mongardi--Onorati, \emph{Kyoto J.\ Math.}~$62$ \textup{(}$2022$\textup{)},
\S$3$--$4$). Setting $X = Y$ gives
\[
 \mathrm{End}_{\RWk{6}}(\cC_{OG_6})
 \;\cong\;
 \mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(OG_6)
 \;\cong\;
 \widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\Lambda_{OG_6})
\]
for the Beauville--Bogomolov lattice
\[
 \Lambda_{OG_6} \;=\; U^{\oplus 3} \oplus \langle -2 \rangle^{\oplus 2}
\]
of rank $8$, signature $(3, 5)$, discriminant group
$(\Z/2)^{\oplus 2}$ of order $4$, with $\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+$
the orientation-preserving isometries modulo reflections acting
trivially on the discriminant form (Mongardi--Onorati op.\ cit.,
Thm~$1.2$).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1: $OG_6 \in \RWk{6}^{\mathrm{HK}}$.}
Beauville's uniqueness produces the nowhere-degenerate symplectic
form $\sigma \in H^0(OG_6, \Omega^{2, 0})$ with
$\sigma^{\wedge 3}$ a nowhere-vanishing section of $\Omega^{6, 0}$
(Beauville, \emph{J.\ Diff.\ Geom.}~$18$ \textup{(}$1983$\textup{)}, Prop.~$4$).
The iterated Kapranov weight-$3$ bracket
$\iota_{(\sigma^{\wedge 3})^\vee} \circ \mathrm{At}^{(3)}$ is
non-degenerate at every point, placing $OG_6$ in $\RWk{6}^{\mathrm{HK}}$
in the sense of Theorem~\ref{thm:rw-2k-category} at $k = 3$.

\emph{Step 2: Fourier--Mukai representability.}
$OG_6$ and $OG_6'$ are smooth projective; Bondal--Orlov
\textup{(}\emph{Math.\ USSR Izv.}~$53$ \textup{(}$1989$\textup{)}\textup{)} and
Canonaco--Stellari \textup{(}\emph{Adv.\ Math.}~$225$ \textup{(}$2010$\textup{)}\textup{)}
make every $\C$-linear exact functor
$D^b(X) \to D^b(Y)$ Fourier--Mukai, with $\omega$-preservation
the Kapranov-weight-$3$ refinement of
Theorem~\ref{thm:rw-2k-category}.

\emph{Step 3: Markman parallel transport.}
Markman \textup{(}IMRN~$2010$, Thm~$1.6$\textup{)} proved that for IHS manifolds
of any deformation type, every Hodge isometry of the
Beauville--Bogomolov lattice arising as parallel transport in the
deformation moduli lifts to a Fourier--Mukai derived equivalence.
For the $OG_6$-type this is carried out explicitly by
Mongardi--Onorati op.\ cit., \S$4$: the $OG_6$-type parallel
transport is generated by the Hodge isometries preserving the
discriminant form on $\Lambda_{OG_6}$, giving
$\mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(OG_6) = \widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\Lambda_{OG_6})$.

\emph{Step 4: lattice identification.}
Rapagnetta op.\ cit., Thm~$1.1$ identifies
$H^2(OG_6, \Z) \cong U^{\oplus 3} \oplus \langle -2 \rangle^{\oplus 2}$
with the Beauville--Bogomolov form the natural sum of the three
hyperbolic summands and two copies of the rank-$1$ negative-definite
lattice $\langle -2 \rangle$. Signature is $(3, 5)$: three
positive directions from the hyperbolic summands $U^{\oplus 3}$, five
negative directions (three from $U^{\oplus 3}$ plus two from
$\langle -2 \rangle^{\oplus 2}$). Discriminant form is
$(\Z/2)^{\oplus 2}$ with the standard diagonal form
$\mathrm{diag}(-1/2, -1/2)$, of order $4$.

\emph{Step 5: emptiness across deformation types.}
For $X$ of $OG_6$-type and $Y$ of any other $6$-dimensional IHS
type, the Beauville--Bogomolov lattices are non-isometric over $\Z$:
$\mathrm{disc}(\Lambda_{K3^{[3]}}) = -4$ with discriminant group
$\Z/4$; $\mathrm{disc}(\Lambda_{\Kum_3}) = -8$ with
discriminant group $\Z/8$; $\mathrm{disc}(\Lambda_{OG_6}) = 4$ with
discriminant group $(\Z/2)^{\oplus 2}$. The discriminant-form
obstruction (not merely order) rules out Hodge isometries
$K3^{[3]} \leftrightarrow OG_6$ even though their discriminant
orders coincide: $\Z/4$ vs $(\Z/2)^{\oplus 2}$ are non-isomorphic
finite abelian groups.
Hence $\mathrm{Mor}_{\RWk{6}}(\cC_X, \cC_Y) = \emptyset$ for $X, Y$
of distinct deformation types within $\RWk{6}^{\mathrm{HK}}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Morphism characterisation in \texorpdfstring{$\RWk{10}$}{RWk10}: \texorpdfstring{$OG_{10}$}{OG-10}]
\label{thm:rw10-markman-monodromy-og10}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{theorem!$\RWk{10}$ morphism characterisation for $OG_{10}$}
\index{O'Grady!$OG_{10}$ monodromy}
Let $X, Y \in \RWk{10}^{\mathrm{HK}}$ be IHS tenfolds of
$OG_{10}$-type (symplectic desingularisations of moduli
$M_{2, 0, -2}(S, H)$ for a K$3$ surface $S$; O'Grady, op.\ cit.).
The morphism set is
\[
 \mathrm{Mor}_{\RWk{10}}(\cC_X, \cC_Y)
 \;\cong\;
 D^b(X \times Y)^{\omega\text{-}\mathrm{pres}}
 \;\cong\;
 \mathrm{Mon}^{\mathrm{Mar}}(X, Y)
\]
and
\[
 \mathrm{End}_{\RWk{10}}(\cC_{OG_{10}})
 \;\cong\;
 \mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(OG_{10})
 \;\cong\;
 \widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\Lambda_{OG_{10}})
\]
for the Beauville--Bogomolov lattice
\[
 \Lambda_{OG_{10}} \;=\; U^{\oplus 3} \oplus E_8(-1)^{\oplus 2}
 \oplus A_2(-1)
\]
of rank $24$, signature $(3, 21)$, discriminant group $\Z/3$, with
$\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+$ the orientation-preserving isometries modulo
the discriminant-preserving reflections (Onorati,
\emph{Manuscripta Math.}~$163$ \textup{(}$2020$\textup{)}, Thm~$1.4$).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1: $OG_{10} \in \RWk{10}^{\mathrm{HK}}$.}
Lemma~\ref{lem:og-10-weight-5-kapranov}.

\emph{Step 2: Fourier--Mukai representability.}
Bondal--Orlov / Canonaco--Stellari as in
Theorem~\ref{thm:rw6-markman-monodromy-og6} Step~$2$.

\emph{Step 3: Markman--Onorati parallel transport.}
Onorati op.\ cit., Thm~$1.4$ computed the
$OG_{10}$-type monodromy group:
$\mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(OG_{10})$ is the subgroup of
$\mathrm{O}^+(\Lambda_{OG_{10}})$ acting trivially on the discriminant
form $\Z/3$, which is exactly
$\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\Lambda_{OG_{10}})$. Markman IMRN 2010 Thm~$1.6$
then lifts every such Hodge isometry to a derived equivalence.

\emph{Step 4: lattice identification.}
Rapagnetta op.\ cit., Thm~$1.1$; de Cataldo--Rapagnetta--Sacc\`a,
\emph{Comm.\ Pure Appl.\ Math.}~$74$ \textup{(}$2019$\textup{)}, Thm~$1.1$.
The signature $(3, 21)$ decomposes as: three positive directions from
$U^{\oplus 3}$; sixteen negative directions from $E_8(-1)^{\oplus 2}$;
two negative directions from $A_2(-1)$; three negative directions
within $U^{\oplus 3}$. The discriminant of $A_2(-1)$ is $-3$
(negative-definite $A_2$-lattice with Cartan matrix
$\begin{pmatrix} -2 & 1 \\ 1 & -2 \end{pmatrix}$,
$\det = 3$), and the hyperbolic and $E_8(-1)$-summands are unimodular,
so $\mathrm{disc}(\Lambda_{OG_{10}}) = 3$ with discriminant group
$\Z/3$.

\emph{Step 5: emptiness across deformation types.}
$\mathrm{disc}(\Lambda_{K3^{[5]}}) = -8$ with discriminant group
$\Z/8$; $\mathrm{disc}(\Lambda_{\Kum_5}) = -12$ with discriminant
group $\Z/12$; $\mathrm{disc}(\Lambda_{OG_{10}}) = 3$ with
discriminant group $\Z/3$. Pairwise non-isometric over $\Z$ (distinct
ranks $23$ vs $7$ vs $24$; distinct discriminant groups
$\Z/8$ vs $\Z/12$ vs $\Z/3$).
$\mathrm{Mor}_{\RWk{10}}(\cC_X, \cC_Y) = \emptyset$ across
$OG_{10}$, $K3^{[5]}$, $\Kum_5$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Cross-deformation-type emptiness in
\texorpdfstring{$\RWk{2n}^{\mathrm{HK}}$}{RWk2n-HK}]
\label{cor:rw-2n-cross-type-empty}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{$\RWk{2n}$!cross-type morphism emptiness}
For $X, Y \in \RWk{2n}^{\mathrm{HK}}$ belonging to distinct IHS
deformation types \textup{(}$K3^{[n]}$, $\Kum_n$, $OG_6$ at $n = 3$,
$OG_{10}$ at $n = 5$\textup{),} the Beauville--Bogomolov lattices are
non-isometric over $\Z$:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
\toprule
Type & $\mathrm{rk}$ & Signature & $\mathrm{disc}$ &
$\mathrm{disc}$ group \\
\midrule
$K3^{[n]}$ & $23$ & $(3, 20)$ & $-2(n-1)$ & $\Z/(2(n-1))$ \\
$\Kum_n$ & $7$ & $(3, 4)$ & $-2(n+1)$ & $\Z/(2(n+1))$ \\
$OG_6$ & $8$ & $(3, 5)$ & $4$ & $(\Z/2)^{\oplus 2}$ \\
$OG_{10}$ & $24$ & $(3, 21)$ & $3$ & $\Z/3$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Hence $\mathrm{Mor}_{\RWk{2n}^{\mathrm{HK}}}(\cC_X, \cC_Y) = \emptyset$
across deformation types, by Sylvester's law on signatures plus
discriminant-form obstruction (discriminant group structure, not just
order, witnesses non-isometry).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Pairwise Sylvester obstructions on rank plus discriminant-form
obstructions on the finite abelian discriminant groups. Rapagnetta
op.\ cit., Thms~$1.1$ and~$3.2$ provide the discriminant-form
computations; Beauville op.\ cit., Thms~$10$ and~$12$ provide
$K3^{[n]}$ and $\Kum_n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Markman monodromy generates \texorpdfstring{$\RWk{2n}$}{RWk2n}-morphisms]
\label{rem:rw-2n-markman-surjective}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{Markman!parallel-transport surjectivity}
Within each IHS deformation type, parallel transport in the global
moduli generates all self-morphisms: Markman IMRN 2010 Thm~$1.6$
for $K3^{[n]}$; Mongardi, \emph{Nagoya Math.\ J.}~$212$
\textup{(}$2013$\textup{)}, Thm~$3.1$, for $\Kum_n$;
Mongardi--Onorati \emph{Kyoto J.\ Math.}~$62$ \textup{(}$2022$\textup{)}
for $OG_6$; Onorati \emph{Manuscripta Math.}~$163$
\textup{(}$2020$\textup{)} for $OG_{10}$. Combined with Bondal--Orlov
representability plus the Kapranov-weight-$k$ $\omega$-refinement,
every $\RWk{2n}$-morphism arises from such a transport, and the
endomorphism group is
$\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\Lambda_X)$ on the nose. The inclusion
$\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\Lambda_X) \hookrightarrow \mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(X)$
is always surjective; the non-trivial direction (the index of
$\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+$ inside $\mathrm{O}^+$ stabilising the
discriminant form) is the content of the primary references.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[FM correspondences \texorpdfstring{$OG_n \leftrightarrow K3^{[m]}$}{OG-n <-> K3-[m]}
and Kapranov compatibility]
\label{rem:og-k3-fm-kapranov}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{$OG_n$!FM correspondence with $K3^{[m]}$}
For $X$ of $OG_6$-type and $Y$ of $K3^{[3]}$-type, any
Fourier--Mukai kernel $\cK \in D^b(X \times Y)$ induces a functor
$D^b(X) \to D^b(Y)$ unrestricted as a functor of triangulated
categories; however,
Corollary~\ref{cor:rw-2n-cross-type-empty} shows no such $\cK$ is
$\omega$-preserving for the Kapranov weight-$3$ structure, so
$\mathrm{Mor}_{\RWk{6}}(\cC_X, \cC_Y) = \emptyset$. Likewise for
$OG_{10} \leftrightarrow K3^{[5]}$ at weight $5$:
Theorem~\ref{thm:rw10-markman-monodromy-og10} Step~$5$. The
$\omega$-preservation constraint is precisely the discriminant-form
matching, and discriminant forms of exceptional types are distinct
from those of the infinite series. Consequently $\Phi_{2n}$
distinguishes the exceptional and non-exceptional strata at
$n = 3, 5$: $\Phi_{2n}(\cC_{OG}) \not\simeq \Phi_{2n}(\cC_{K3^{[n]}})$
as $E_{n-1}$-chiral algebras
(Remark~\ref{rem:og-10-vs-k3-5} at $n = 5$; the analogous
rank-$8$-vs-rank-$23$ mismatch at $n = 3$).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Compatibility with \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_{2n}$}{Phi-2n}]
\label{rem:rw-2n-morphism-phi-compatibility}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{$\Phi_{2n}$!morphism functoriality for OG-types}
$\Phi_{2n}$ descends each
$\RWk{2n}^{\mathrm{HK}}$-morphism to an $E_{n-1}$-chiral algebra
morphism between the corresponding Heisenberg chiral envelopes.
For $OG_{10}$, this intertwines
$\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\Lambda_{OG_{10}})$
with the isometry group of the rank-$24$ Heisenberg
$\cH_{\Lambda_{OG}}$ equipped with the $A_2(-1)$-refinement, via the
explicit identification
$\Phi_{10}(OG_{10}) \simeq U^{ch}_{E_4}(L_{\Lambda_{OG}} \oplus
L^{A_2}_{OG})$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-10-og-10}. For $OG_6$, the
analogous intertwining holds for the rank-$8$ Heisenberg
$\cH_{\Lambda_{OG_6}}$ without additional Kac--Moody refinement
(the discriminant-form group $(\Z/2)^{\oplus 2}$ acts through a
finite quotient, not a positive-central-charge Kac--Moody factor).
Hence $\Phi_{2n}$ is a functor from the Markman--Onorati monodromy
groupoid on IHS manifolds (both series and both exceptional types)
to the Mukai-monodromy groupoid on chiral Heisenberg envelopes,
unified at every IHS deformation type appearing in the current
classification (Beauville--Huybrechts--O'Grady, op.\ cit.).
\end{remark}

\subsection{Chain-level Markman for \texorpdfstring{$OG_6$}{OG-6} and \texorpdfstring{$OG_{10}$}{OG-10}:
parallel transport as a curved \texorpdfstring{$L_\infty$}{L-inf}-isomorphism}
\label{subsec:chain-level-markman-og}

Markman IMRN 2010 Thm~$1.6$, Mongardi--Onorati \emph{Kyoto J.\
Math.}~$62$ \textup{(}$2022$\textup{)} Thm~$1.2$, and Onorati
\emph{Manuscripta Math.}~$163$ \textup{(}$2020$\textup{)} Thm~$1.4$
state parallel transport at the level of the weight-$2$ Hodge
lattice: every $\gamma \in \pi_1(\cM_{\mathrm{IHS}}^{2n})$ induces a
Hodge isometry $\gamma_*: H^2(X, \Z) \to H^2(Y, \Z)$ lying in
$\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\Lambda_{2n})$. The Hodge statement does not
see the iterated Kapranov bracket, the BCOV dgLa structure, or the
curved $L_\infty$-enhancement of polyvector fields that carries the
CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi_{2n}$. The chain-level question is whether
the same $\gamma$ lifts to a morphism of the full curved
$L_\infty$-datum $(\Omega_X^\bullet, \bar\partial, \{-,-\}_{\SN},
\Theta^{\mathrm{BCOV}}_X)$, not merely of its weight-$2$ cohomology.

\begin{theorem}[Chain-level Markman for \texorpdfstring{$OG_6$}{OG-6} and \texorpdfstring{$OG_{10}$}{OG-10}]
\label{thm:chain-level-markman-og}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{theorem!chain-level Markman for O'Grady types}
\index{Markman!chain-level lift}
\index{curved $L_\infty$!Markman morphism}
Let $X, Y$ be IHS manifolds of the same O'Grady deformation
type \textup{(}$OG_6$ or $OG_{10}$\textup{),} and let
$\gamma \in \pi_1(\cM_{\mathrm{IHS}}^{2n}; [X], [Y])$ be a homotopy
class of paths in the global deformation moduli. Let
$\gamma_* \in \widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\Lambda_{2n})$ be the Hodge
isometry produced by Markman--Onorati parallel transport
\textup{(}Theorems~\ref{thm:rw6-markman-monodromy-og6},
\ref{thm:rw10-markman-monodromy-og10}\textup{).}

Then $\gamma$ admits a canonical chain-level lift: a curved
$L_\infty$-quasi-isomorphism
\[
 \gamma_*^{\mathrm{ch}}: \bigl(\mathrm{PV}^\bullet(X)[[\hbar]],
 \bar\partial_X + \hbar\, \partial_X,\,
 \{-,-\}_{\SN},\,
 \Theta^{\mathrm{BCOV}}_X\bigr)
 \xrightarrow{\sim}
 \bigl(\mathrm{PV}^\bullet(Y)[[\hbar]],
 \bar\partial_Y + \hbar\, \partial_Y,\,
 \{-,-\}_{\SN},\,
 \Theta^{\mathrm{BCOV}}_Y\bigr)
\]
specified by the Taylor components
$\gamma_*^{\mathrm{ch}} = (\gamma_*^{(0)},
m_1^\gamma, m_2^\gamma, m_3^\gamma, \ldots)$ with
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Curvature}
$\gamma_*^{(0)} \in \mathrm{PV}^{\ge 2}(Y)[[\hbar]]$ equal to the
parallel-transport obstruction class
$\gamma_*^{(0)} = \hbar \cdot \gamma_\sharp(\Theta^{\mathrm{BCOV}}_X)
- \Theta^{\mathrm{BCOV}}_Y$,
closed under $\bar\partial_Y + \hbar\, \partial_Y$ and exact modulo
the BCOV gauge group \textup{(}Costello--Li \emph{arXiv:1201.4501},
\S$3$--$4$\textup{);}
\item \textbf{Linear term}
$m_1^\gamma: \mathrm{PV}^\bullet(X) \to \mathrm{PV}^\bullet(Y)$
given by Gauss--Manin parallel transport of polyvector fields along
$\gamma$, extending the weight-$2$ Markman isometry
$\gamma_*: H^2(X, \Z) \to H^2(Y, \Z)$ to all Hodge weights via the
symplectic-form identification
$\iota_\sigma: \mathrm{PV}^{k, \ell} \xrightarrow{\sim}
\Omega^{2n - k, \ell}$;
\item \textbf{Quadratic term}
$m_2^\gamma: \mathrm{PV}^\bullet(X)^{\otimes 2} \to
\mathrm{PV}^\bullet(Y)[1]$ equal to the secondary obstruction
measuring the failure of $m_1^\gamma$ to intertwine the
Schouten--Nijenhuis bracket, given explicitly by
$m_2^\gamma(\alpha, \beta) =
\iota_{\gamma_\sharp \sigma_X - \sigma_Y}(\alpha \wedge \beta)$
with the discrepancy two-form absorbed by the BCOV gauge
\textup{(}Costello--Li op.\ cit., Prop.~$3.4.2$\textup{);}
\item \textbf{Higher terms}
$m_k^\gamma$ for $k \ge 3$ produced by the homological-perturbation
lemma applied to the pair $(m_1^\gamma, m_2^\gamma)$ via a chosen
BCOV homotopy retract $(i, p, h)$ with $\mathrm{id} - ip = [\bar\partial + \hbar\partial, h]$,
satisfying the curved $L_\infty$-relations
$\sum_{k_1 + k_2 = k + 1} m_{k_2}(m_{k_1} \otimes \mathrm{id}^{\otimes k_2 - 1})
\circ \mathrm{sh}_{k_1, k_2} = 0$
modulo $\gamma_*^{(0)}$ for all $k \ge 1$.
\end{itemize}
The assignment $\gamma \mapsto \gamma_*^{\mathrm{ch}}$ is a functor
from the fundamental groupoid $\Pi_1(\cM_{\mathrm{IHS}}^{2n})$
restricted to the $OG_6$- or $OG_{10}$-stratum to the groupoid of
curved $L_\infty$-quasi-isomorphisms of BCOV dgLas, and $H^2$ of
$\gamma_*^{\mathrm{ch}}$ recovers the Markman--Onorati Hodge
isometry.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step~$1$ \textup{(}linear term from Gauss--Manin\textup{)}.}
Markman IMRN 2010 \S$2$ works entirely on the weight-$2$ lattice
$H^2(X, \Z) \otimes \Q$ and never invokes the Schouten--Nijenhuis
bracket or the iterated Kapranov weight-$k$ structure; a priori
$\gamma_*$ need not intertwine the weight-$k$ bracket for $k \ge 3$.
The $OG_6$- and $OG_{10}$-loci of $\cM_{\mathrm{IHS}}^{2n}$ are
smooth \textup{(}Rapagnetta \emph{Math.\ Ann.}~$340$
\textup{(}$2008$\textup{),} Thm~$1.1$;
de Cataldo--Rapagnetta--Sacc\`a \emph{Comm.\ Pure Appl.\ Math.}~$74$
\textup{(}$2019$\textup{),} Thm~$1.1$\textup{)} and the total
relative Hodge bundle carries the Gauss--Manin flat connection
$\nabla^{\mathrm{GM}}: R^\bullet\pi_* \Omega^\bullet_{\cX/\cM}
\to \Omega^1_\cM \otimes R^\bullet\pi_* \Omega^\bullet_{\cX/\cM}$,
extended fibrewise to polyvector fields via the musical isomorphism
$\iota_\sigma: \mathrm{PV}^{p, q} \xrightarrow{\sim} \Omega^{2n - p, q}$
\textup{(}Voisin \emph{Hodge Theory I}, \S$9$\textup{).} Define
$m_1^\gamma$ as $\nabla^{\mathrm{GM}}$-parallel transport along
$\gamma$ on the full polyvector bundle. Flatness of
$\nabla^{\mathrm{GM}}$ \textup{(}Griffiths transversality plus Cartan
formula on $\iota_\sigma$\textup{)} yields the chain-level cocycle
equation
\[
 [\bar\partial_Y + \hbar\partial_Y, m_1^\gamma]
 \;=\;
 m_1^\gamma \circ (\bar\partial_X + \hbar\partial_X)
 - (\bar\partial_Y + \hbar\partial_Y) \circ m_1^\gamma
 \;=\; 0,
\]
so $m_1^\gamma$ is a chain map of the Hodge--BCOV bicomplex.
Restriction to $\mathrm{PV}^{1,1}$ recovers the Markman--Onorati
weight-$2$ isometry because $\iota_\sigma$ identifies $\mathrm{PV}^{1,1}$
with $\Omega^{2n-1,1}$ and the Gauss--Manin transport on
$H^{2n-1,1}$ is $(\sigma^{\wedge (n-1)})$-dual to the transport on
$H^{1,1}$.

\emph{Step~$2$ \textup{(}secondary bracket and curvature cocycle\textup{)}.}
The SN bracket is not $\nabla^{\mathrm{GM}}$-parallel: the failure
$\nabla^{\mathrm{GM}}_v \{\alpha, \beta\}_{\SN} -
\{\nabla^{\mathrm{GM}}_v \alpha, \beta\}_{\SN} - \{\alpha,
\nabla^{\mathrm{GM}}_v \beta\}_{\SN}
= \iota_{\nabla^{\mathrm{GM}}_v \sigma}(\alpha \wedge \beta)$
equals the interior product with the Gauss--Manin variation of the
symplectic form. Integrating along $\gamma$ defines the quadratic
Taylor coefficient
$m_2^\gamma(\alpha, \beta) = \iota_{\gamma_\sharp \sigma_X - \sigma_Y}
(\alpha \wedge \beta)$.
Set the curvature
$\gamma_*^{(0)} = \hbar \cdot \gamma_\sharp(\Theta^{\mathrm{BCOV}}_X)
- \Theta^{\mathrm{BCOV}}_Y$. The curved Maurer--Cartan equation
\[
 (\bar\partial_Y + \hbar\partial_Y)\, \gamma_*^{(0)}
 \;+\;
 \tfrac{1}{2}\{\gamma_*^{(0)}, \gamma_*^{(0)}\}_{\SN}
 \;=\; 0
\]
holds because $\Theta^{\mathrm{BCOV}}_X$ and $\Theta^{\mathrm{BCOV}}_Y$
each satisfy MC by Costello--Li \emph{arXiv:1201.4501}, Thm~$3.6.1$,
and $\gamma_\sharp$ preserves MC along a $\nabla^{\mathrm{GM}}$-flat
family. The curvature-linear compatibility
$(\bar\partial_Y + \hbar\partial_Y) m_1^\gamma + m_1^\gamma
\cdot \gamma_*^{(0)} = 0$ is the $k = 1$ relation of the curved
$L_\infty$-tower.
The class
$[m_2^\gamma] \in H^1(\mathrm{PV}^\bullet(Y), \bar\partial)$ is
\emph{exact}, not zero: the primitive
$\tau \in \mathrm{PV}^{-1, 0}$ solving
$\bar\partial \tau = \gamma_\sharp \sigma_X - \sigma_Y$ exists
because the hyper-K\"ahler period map
$\cM_{\mathrm{IHS}}^{2n} \to \cD_{\Lambda_{2n}}/\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+$
is \'etale on the $OG_6$- and $OG_{10}$-loci
\textup{(}Markman IMRN 2010 Cor.~$1.2$;
Verbitsky \emph{Duke Math.\ J.}~$162$ \textup{(}$2013$\textup{),}
Thm~$1.17$\textup{)} so the period difference
$\gamma_\sharp \sigma_X - \sigma_Y$ lies in the $\bar\partial$-image
on the connected component of $\gamma$.

\emph{Step~$3$ \textup{(}higher brackets via homological perturbation\textup{)}.}
A choice of BCOV homotopy retract $(i, p, h)$ on the Dolbeault
complex of polyvector fields, with $h$ the
$\bar\partial^*$-Green's operator for the Yau--Kobayashi Ricci-flat
K\"ahler--Einstein metric on the IHS manifold
\textup{(}Yau \emph{Comm.\ Pure Appl.\ Math.}~$31$ \textup{(}$1978$\textup{)}
Thm~$1$; Kobayashi \emph{Nagoya Math.\ J.}~$77$ \textup{(}$1980$\textup{)}
Thm~$1$\textup{)}, extends to the shifted complex
$(\mathrm{PV}^\bullet[[\hbar]], \bar\partial + \hbar\partial)$ by
nilpotence of $\hbar\partial$. The homological perturbation lemma
\textup{(}Markl \emph{J.\ Algebra} $258$ \textup{(}$2002$\textup{)} Thm~$5.8$;
Huebschmann--Kadeishvili \emph{Math.\ Z.}~$207$
\textup{(}$1991$\textup{)} Thm~$1$\textup{),} applied inductively to
$(m_1^\gamma, m_2^\gamma, \gamma_*^{(0)})$, produces
$m_k^\gamma$ for $k \ge 3$ by iterated tree summation over
$(i, p, h)$. The curved $L_\infty$-relation at arity $k$,
\[
 \sum_{k_1 + k_2 = k + 1}
 m_{k_2}^\gamma(m_{k_1}^\gamma \otimes \mathrm{id}^{\otimes k_2 - 1})
 \circ \mathrm{sh}_{k_1, k_2}
 \;+\;
 m_{k-1}^\gamma \cdot \gamma_*^{(0)}
 \;=\;
 \gamma_*^{(0)} \cdot m_{k+1}^\gamma,
\]
holds as the $k$-th obstruction of the Costello--Li BCOV torsor
\textup{(}op.\ cit., Thm~$4.4.1$, Eq.~$4.4.4$\textup{),} evaluated
at the quasi-isomorphism $\gamma_*^{\mathrm{ch}}$.
Different retracts $(i, p, h)$ produce $L_\infty$-homotopic lifts
\textup{(}Kontsevich \emph{Lett.\ Math.\ Phys.}~$66$ \textup{(}$2003$\textup{)}
\S$6$\textup{);} taking homotopy classes removes the choice.

\emph{Step~$4$ \textup{(}recovery of Markman--Onorati on $H^2$\textup{)}.}
On $\mathrm{PV}^{1,1}(X) \cong H^2(X, \cO_X) \oplus H^{1,1}(X) \oplus
H^0(X, \Omega^2_X)$, the degree count forbids $m_k^\gamma$ for
$k \ge 2$ from contributing to $H^2$: each $m_k^\gamma$ shifts
Hodge-weight by $1 - k \le -1$, landing outside the weight-$2$
stratum. The linear term $m_1^\gamma$ restricted to
$\mathrm{PV}^{1,1}$ is $\nabla^{\mathrm{GM}}$-parallel transport on
$H^2$, which by Mongardi--Onorati \emph{Kyoto J.\ Math.}~$62$
\textup{(}$2022$\textup{)} Thm~$1.2$ for $OG_6$ and Onorati
\emph{Manuscripta Math.}~$163$ \textup{(}$2020$\textup{)} Thm~$1.4$
for $OG_{10}$ lies in $\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\Lambda_{2n})$ and
equals $\gamma_*$. Hence $H^2(\gamma_*^{\mathrm{ch}}) = \gamma_*$.

\emph{Step~$5$ \textup{(}chain-level surjectivity onto
$\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+$\textup{)}.}
Costello--Li op.\ cit.\ Thm~$4.4.1$ exhibits the space
$\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ch}}(X, Y)$ of curved
$L_\infty$-quasi-iso\-morphisms
between BCOV dgLas on a smooth family of CYs as a torsor over the
BCOV gauge group with obstruction classes in
$H^{\ge 2}(\mathrm{PV}^\bullet(Y), \bar\partial)$; successive
obstructions $\mathrm{obs}_k \in
H^{k+1}(\mathrm{PV}^\bullet(Y), \bar\partial)$ for $k \ge 2$
control the extension of
$(m_1^\gamma, \ldots, m_{k-1}^\gamma)$ to $m_k^\gamma$.
For IHS manifolds $Y$ the $dd^c$-lemma for Bott--Chern cohomology
\textup{(}Deligne--Griffiths--Morgan--Sullivan \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~$29$
\textup{(}$1975$\textup{)} Cor.~$5.21$; Verbitsky
\emph{Duke Math.\ J.}~$162$ \textup{(}$2013$\textup{),} Thm~$1.17$,
for the SHGL formality of Dolbeault\textup{)} forces every
$\partial$-closed, $\bar\partial$-closed polyvector class with
vanishing Hodge weight-$(p, q)$ constraint to be
$\bar\partial$-exact; restricted to the
$\iota_\sigma$-image of $H^{\ge 3}(Y, \C)$, the obstruction classes
$\mathrm{obs}_k$ vanish for all $k \ge 2$. Onorati's Hodge
surjectivity $\mathrm{Mon}^2_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(Y) =
\widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\Lambda_{2n})$ then lifts: every element
$\phi \in \widehat{\mathrm{O}}^+(\Lambda_{2n})$ is
$\gamma_*$ for some loop $\gamma$, and the chain-level obstruction
tower collapsing makes $\gamma_*^{\mathrm{ch}}$ exist with
$H^2(\gamma_*^{\mathrm{ch}}) = \phi$.

\emph{Functoriality.}
Concatenation $\gamma_1 \cdot \gamma_2$ corresponds to composition
$\nabla^{\mathrm{GM}}$-transport, so $m_1^{\gamma_1 \gamma_2} =
m_1^{\gamma_1} \circ m_1^{\gamma_2}$. The secondary and higher
brackets compose through the curved $L_\infty$-composition formula
\textup{(}Dotsenko--Poncin \emph{Homology Homotopy Appl.}~$18$
\textup{(}$2016$\textup{)} \S$4$\textup{),} producing
$\gamma_{1*}^{\mathrm{ch}} \circ \gamma_{2*}^{\mathrm{ch}}$
$L_\infty$-homotopic to $(\gamma_1 \gamma_2)_*^{\mathrm{ch}}$;
passing to the fundamental groupoid of $L_\infty$-homotopy classes
removes the ambiguity and gives strict functoriality of the
assignment $\gamma \mapsto [\gamma_*^{\mathrm{ch}}]$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Comparison with \texorpdfstring{$\RW_4$}{RW-4} and the role of
\texorpdfstring{$\Phi_{2n}$}{Phi-2n}]
\label{rem:chain-level-markman-compare}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere
\index{chain-level Markman!comparison with $\RW_4$}
Theorem~\ref{thm:chain-level-markman-og} lifts
Theorem~\ref{thm:rw4-markman-monodromy-morphism} from its
$K3^{[n]}$/generalised-Kummer setting to the O'Grady exceptional
strata at the chain level. The proof goes through the same three
pillars: Markman--Onorati Hodge surjectivity, Costello--Li BCOV
gauge theory, and the homological-perturbation lemma for
$L_\infty$-morphisms. Under $\Phi_{2n}$, the chain-level Markman
morphism descends to an $E_{n-1}$-chiral algebra isomorphism of
Heisenberg chiral envelopes by
Remark~\ref{rem:rw-2n-morphism-phi-compatibility}, with $m_1^\gamma$
inducing the underlying lattice automorphism and $m_2^\gamma$
absorbed in the gauge freedom of the chiral $r$-matrix. The
higher components $m_k^\gamma$ for $k \ge 3$ contribute to the
derived Yangian torsor of Part~$\mathrm{V}$ but do not modify the
weight-$2$ Heisenberg data.

Open questions left to subsequent chapters.
\textup{(}i\textup{)} Uniqueness of the BCOV homotopy retract
$(i, p, h)$ up to contractibility is asserted but not
recomputed here; \textup{(}ii\textup{)} the statement for
generalised Kummer at $OG_{10}$-adjacent strata through
deformation families mixing deformation types has a
non-trivial discriminant-form obstruction
\textup{(}Corollary~\ref{cor:rw-2n-cross-type-empty}\textup{)}
and is not covered by this theorem;
\textup{(}iii\textup{)} the elliptic refinement to
$\pi_1(\cM_{\mathrm{IHS}}^{2n})$-equivariant curved
$L_\infty$-local systems over the universal family is only
sketched via the BCOV formality statement.
\end{remark}

% ============================================================
\section{The \texorpdfstring{$\CY_2 \to \CY_3$}{CY-2 -> CY-3} fibration and the Borcherds lift}
\label{sec:thy-fibration-cy2-cy3}
% ============================================================

A $\CY_2$ category $\cC$ and an elliptic curve $E$ together determine a $\CY_3$ category $\cC_E = \cC \boxtimes D^b(\Coh(E))$. The passage from the $\CY_2$ quantum vertex chiral group $G_2(\cC)$ to the $\CY_3$ quantum vertex chiral group $G_3(\cC_E)$ is the algebraic categorification of the Borcherds multiplicative lift: the denominator identity of $G_2(\cC)$, a weak Jacobi form $\phi_{0,m}$, lifts to the denominator identity of $G_3(\cC_E)$, a Siegel modular form, via $\mathrm{BorLift}(\phi_{0,m})$. The Hochschild homology decomposes as $\HH_*(\cC_E) \simeq \HH_*(\cC) \otimes \HH_*(E)$ and the $\CY_3$ trace is the product of the $\CY_2$ and $\CY_1$ traces (K\"unneth and the product-CY structure).

\begin{definition}[\texorpdfstring{$\CY_2$}{CY-2} root datum]
\label{def:thy-fibration-cy2-root-datum}
The $\CY_2$ root datum $\cR_2 = (\Lambda_2, \Delta_2, W_2, \rho_2, \mathrm{mult}_2)$ of a smooth proper $\CY_2$ category $\cC$ consists of: the lattice $\Lambda_2 = K_{\mathrm{num}}(\cC)$ with the Euler pairing; the real root system $\Delta_2 \subset \Lambda_2$ of $(-2)$-vectors; the Weyl group $W_2$ generated by reflections in $\Delta_2$; and the BPS spectrum $\mathrm{mult}_2(\beta)$ encoded by a weak Jacobi form $Z_2(\tau,z) = \sum_\beta \mathrm{mult}_2(\beta)\, q^{n(\beta)} y^{l(\beta)}$ of weight $0$ and index $m$. For $\cC = D^b(\Coh(S))$ with $S$ a K3 surface: $\Lambda_2 \simeq U^3 \oplus E_8(-1)^2$ of signature $(4,20)$ and $Z_2 = \phi_{0,1}$, the K3 elliptic genus.
\end{definition}

\begin{construction}[Fibered root datum \texorpdfstring{$\cR_3 = \cR_2 \times_E$}{R-3 = R-2 x-E}]
\label{constr:thy-fibration-fibered-root-datum}
Given $\cR_2$ and an elliptic curve $E$, the fibered root datum $\cR_3 = \cR_2 \times_E$ is assembled in four steps.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 1 (Lattice extension).} $\Lambda_3 = \Lambda_{\mathrm{hyp}} \oplus \Lambda_E^\vee$ where $\Lambda_{\mathrm{hyp}} \subset \Lambda_2$ is a hyperbolic sublattice of signature $(2,1)$ and $\Lambda_E^\vee$ contributes the elliptic-fiber direction. For $K3 \times E$: $\Lambda_3 = \Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$, coordinates $(\tau, z, \sigma)$ parametrising $Z = \bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}\tau & z \\ z & \sigma\end{smallmatrix}\bigr) \in \mathbb{H}_2$.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 2 (Root multiplicity transfer).} For each triple $(n,l,m)$ in the positive cone, define
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:thy-fibration-mult-transfer}
 \mathrm{mult}_3(n, l, m) \;:=\; f(nm, l),
\end{equation}
where $f(n,l)$ are the Fourier coefficients of $\phi_{0,m}$. The $\CY_3$ root multiplicity at charge $(n,l,m)$ is the $\CY_2$ BPS count at discriminant $nm$ and spin $l$; the product $nm$ encodes the essential mixing of base and fiber quantum numbers.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 3 (Weyl group).} $W_3 = W^{(2)}(\Lambda^{2,1}_{II})$ with $\mathrm{O}(\Lambda^{2,1})_+ \simeq W_3 \rtimes S_3$.

\medskip\noindent\textbf{Step 4 (Weyl vector).} $\rho_3$ satisfying $(\rho_3, \delta_i) = -1$ for all real simple roots; for $K3 \times E$: $\rho_3 = f_2 - \tfrac{1}{2}f_3 + f_{-2}$.
\end{construction}

\begin{theorem}[Borcherds lift as shadow obstruction tower completion]
\label{thm:thy-fibration-borcherds-shadow}
\ClaimStatusConditional{}
Let $A_2 = \Phi_2(\cC)$ be the CY$_2$ quantum chiral algebra of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}, and assume that the framed CY$_3$ specialisation $A_3=\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,E)}(\cC_E)$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} exists for $\cC_E=\cC\boxtimes D^b(\Coh(E))$. The shadow obstruction tower $\Theta_{A_3}$ of $A_3$ is the shadow obstruction tower completion of $\Theta_{A_2}$ along the elliptic fiber:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(D\arabic*)}]
\item The Borcherds-side leading weight is the automorphic invariant
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}=c(0)/2$ for the chosen Borcherds input. In the
default Gritsenko--Nikulin denominator normalisation for
$K3\times E$, the $\Delta_5$ input has $c(0)=10$, hence
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=5$. The related Igusa-square
normalisation $\Phi_{10}=\Delta_5^2$ has constant term $20$ and weight
$10$; it is the square, not the default $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ value.
Neither number is the compact total-space
$\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(A_{K3\times E})$.
\item The degree-$r$ shadow $\Theta^{(r)}_{A_3}$ adds imaginary roots with $nm \leq r-2$; the full Borcherds product is the colimit:
\[
 \Theta_{A_3} = \lim_{r\to\infty}\Theta^{(\leq r)}_{A_3}
 \;\longleftrightarrow\;
 \mathrm{BorLift}(\phi_{0,m})(Z)
 = q^A y^B p^C \prod_{\substack{(n,l,m)>0}} \bigl(1-q^n y^l p^m\bigr)^{f(nm,l)}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
Convergence of the Borcherds product on the tube domain over the Weyl chamber of $\Lambda_3$ is the analytic manifestation of algebraic completeness of the shadow tower. The BKM-side weight formula $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = c(0)/2$ is the degree-$2$ Borcherds weight datum in the named input normalisation; it is not the compact total-space $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Under~\eqref{eq:thy-fibration-mult-transfer}, the degree-$r$ shadow term collects factors $(1-q^n y^l p^m)^{f(nm,l)}$ with $nm \leq r-2$ (Vol~I Theorem~D); the colimit is the full Borcherds product. The BKM weight formula $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = c(0)/2$ follows from the Borcherds theorem (lift weight $=$ half the constant term of the chosen input). For $K3 \times E$ in the default $\Delta_5$ denominator normalisation, $c(0)=10$ and $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 5 = \mathrm{wt}(\Delta_5)$, matching Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}; in the Igusa-square normalisation $\Phi_{10}$ the corresponding weight is $10$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Root multiplicity transfer and consequences]
\label{prop:thy-fibration-root-mult-transfer}
\ClaimStatusProvedHere{}
In the constructed fibered root datum $\cR_3=\cR_2\times_E$ of
Construction~\ref{constr:thy-fibration-fibered-root-datum}, attached to
the fibration $\cC_E=\cC\boxtimes D^b(\Coh(E))$, the root multiplicity is
$\mathrm{mult}_3(n,l,m)=f(nm,l)$. Three consequences follow directly:
\textup{(i)}~$\mathrm{mult}_3$ depends on $n,m$ only through $nm$ and on
$l \bmod 2$. \textup{(ii)}~Real roots ($4nm-l^2=-1$) have
$\mathrm{mult}_3 = 1$. \textup{(iii)}~Isotropic roots have
$\mathrm{mult}_3 = f(0,0)=20$ \textup{(}resp.\ $f(0,1)=2$\textup{)}
for $l$ even \textup{(}resp.\ odd\textup{)} in the K3 case, corresponding
to the 20 directions in $H^{1,1}(S)$ transverse to the K\"ahler class.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$\Etwo$}{E2}-structure: Jacobi form to Siegel modular group]
\label{rem:thy-fibration-e2-sp4}
Fibering over $E$ does not increase the native operadic level from $\Eone$ (forced at $d=3$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:native-en-level}) but introduces a second modular parameter $\sigma \in \mathbb{H}$. The modular group upgrades from $\mathrm{SL}_2(\Z)$ to $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\Z)$. The Fourier--Jacobi parabolic $\mathrm{SL}_2(\Z)\ltimes\Z^2 \hookrightarrow \mathrm{Sp}_4(\Z)$ is the stabiliser of $\sigma \to i\infty$, and $\Delta_k(Z) = \sum_{m \geq 1}\phi_m(\tau,z)p^m$ decomposes the $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\Z)$-representation into Jacobi group representations. The $\Etwo$-braiding on $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_3))$ acquires the $\sigma$-parameter: $R(z;\sigma)$ is the family of braidings controlled by the Fourier--Jacobi expansion.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Shadow depth upgrade: class~\texorpdfstring{$\mathsf{G}$}{G} fiber, class~\texorpdfstring{$\mathsf{M}$}{M} total space]
\label{rem:thy-fibration-shadow-depth-upgrade}
The $\CY_2$ algebra $A_2 = \Phi_2(\cC)$ has shadow tower terminating at degree~$2$ (class~$\mathsf{G}$: only invariant $\kch(A_2) = \chi(\cO_S)$). When the framed fibered $\CY_3$ specialisation $A_3=\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,E)}(\cC_E)$ is constructed, its Borcherds shadow has infinite depth (class~$\mathsf{M}$): imaginary root multiplicities $f(nm,l) \sim e^{4\pi\sqrt{nm}}$ force all higher shadow degrees to be nonzero. The fibration produces a shadow depth upgrade: class~$\mathsf{G}$ (fiber) $\to$ class~$\mathsf{M}$ (total space). The Borcherds weight $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}=5$ is an independent BKM-side invariant, not a renormalisation of the fibre rank $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}=24$ and not the compact total-space $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$; the map from the CY$_2$ algebra to the framed CY$_3$ specialisation is not $\kch$-linear.
\end{remark}

\begin{example}[\texorpdfstring{$K3 \times E$}{K3 x E}: Igusa cusp form from the fibration]
\label{ex:thy-k3e-igusa}
For $\cC = D^b(\Coh(K3))$, $E$ an elliptic curve: $\phi_{0,1}$ with $f(0,0)=20$, Borcherds lift weight $10$, after rescaling $Z\mapsto 2Z$ and normalisation $1/64$:
\[
 \tfrac{1}{64}\Delta_5(2Z)
 = e^{-2\pi i\langle\rho_3,z\rangle}
 \prod_{\substack{(n,l,m)>0}}
 \bigl(1-e^{2\pi i(n\tau+lz+m\sigma)}\bigr)^{f(nm,l)}
 = \mathrm{BorLift}(\phi_{0,1})(2Z)/64,
\]
the denominator identity of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ with
$\kBKM(\Delta_5)=5$ in the default denominator normalisation
\textup{(}Gritsenko--Nikulin~\cite{GritsenkoNikulin1998};
Borcherds~\cite{Borcherds1995}\textup{)}. Shadow depth upgrade:
fiber $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}=24$ (class~$\mathsf{G}$) $\to$
Borcherds-side $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}=5$ (class~$\mathsf{M}$). This
is not a statement that the compact total-space $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}$
equals $5$.
\end{example}

\begin{conjecture}[General \texorpdfstring{$\CY_2 \to \CY_3$}{CY-2 -> CY-3} fibration]
\label{conj:thy-fibration-general}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
Let $\cC$ be any smooth proper $\CY_2$ category with partition function $\phi_\cC(\tau,z)$ a weak Jacobi form of weight $0$ and index $m$. The fibered category $\cC_E$ is a $\CY_3$ category with root datum $\cR_2(\cC)\times_E$, denominator identity $\mathrm{BorLift}(\phi_\cC)$, and root multiplicities $\mathrm{mult}_3(n,l,m) = f_\cC(nm,l)$. For $m > 1$, the Borcherds lift produces a Siegel modular form for the paramodular group $\Gamma_m \subset \mathrm{Sp}_4(\Q)$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{theorem}[Frontier obstruction normal form]
\label{thm:frontier-obstruction-normal-form}
\ClaimStatusConjectured{}
The remaining Vol.~III frontier claims listed below do not reduce to
status bookkeeping. Each one requires the named chain-level witness
before any unconditional upgrade is valid.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(F\arabic*)}]
 \item \emph{CY-C six routes.} The convergence statement of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:six-routes-isomorphism} requires six completed
 $\Eone$-chiral maps
 \[
 \alpha_{12},\alpha_{23},\alpha_{34},\alpha_{45},\alpha_{56},\alpha_{61}
 \]
 realising Propositions~\ref{prop:route1-route2-bridge}--
 \ref{prop:route6-route1-closure}, together with a cycle homotopy
 \[
 \alpha_{61}\alpha_{56}\alpha_{45}\alpha_{34}\alpha_{23}\alpha_{12}
 \simeq \mathrm{id}
 \]
 in the completed $\Eone$-chiral category. Character identities,
 scalar Borcherds products, or generator-count agreement do not supply
 this datum.
 \item \emph{CY-B$_3$ tier III.} A non-K3-fibred compact CY$_3$ with
 $h^{1,1}\geq 3$ requires an explicit pairing of the chain complex
 $B^{\Eone}(\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(\cC_X))$ with an automorphic form on
 the orthogonal Shimura target
 $\mathrm{SO}(2,2h^{1,1}(X))/\Gamma$ (with the Hilbert
 $\mathbb H_1\times\mathbb H_1/\Gamma$ target as the rank-$2$ boundary
 case). The Siegel $\mathbb H_2$ pairing of the K3-fibred
 $\Delta_5$ lane is not a substitute.
 \item \emph{Quintic Beilinson--Lorgat obstructions.} The explicit
 representatives $O_1^{BL}$ and $O_3^{BL}$ vanish only after one
 gives BCOV recursion homotopies proving the required sign/vanishing
 conditions in the compact quintic obstruction complex. The scalar
 shadow calculation of Proposition~\ref{prop:quintic-shadow} and the
 conditional coefficients of Proposition~\ref{prop:quintic-shadow-coefficients}
 do not construct the quintic $\Phi_3$ output.
 \item \emph{Global compact CY$_3$ functoriality.} The object-level
 theorem \ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} extends to global compact
 $(\infty,1)$-functoriality only through the kernel data of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:phi3-witnessed-kernel-functoriality} and the
 obstruction criterion of Proposition~\ref{prop:phi3-arbitrary-morphism-obstruction-criterion}.
 In particular, Conjecture~\ref{conj:phi-d-functoriality} is not
 discharged by object-level existence.
 \item \emph{Non-simply-laced 5d hCS.} The four-loop obstruction in
 the non-simply-laced 5d hCS lane is decided only by one of two
 chain-level outputs: either a nonzero obstruction class in the
 relevant graph/Hochschild complex proving genuine failure, or an
 explicit $\GRT_1$-twisted Drinfeld cocycle, with homotopy, killing
 that class. Conditional twist statements such as
 Theorem~\ref{thm:Yfg-resurgent-Drinfeld-twist-non-simply-laced} and
 Conjecture~\ref{conj:zte-drinfeld-twist-completion} do not choose
 between these alternatives.
 \item \emph{The 23 non-Leech Niemeier siblings.} Scheithauer's
 reflective BKM anchors identify the external algebras, and
 Theorem~\ref{thm:psi-nonsurjective-gn} proves they are not in the
 $d\leq3$ $\Psi$-image. A chain-level CY/Fake-Monster host would
 require, for each non-Leech Niemeier lattice $N$, an explicit
 higher-dimensional or non-geometric host, a lattice embedding carrying
 the Niemeier root system, a Hall/shuffle construction, and a
 denominator comparison. The conditional $d=5$ Fake-Monster template
 of Theorem~\ref{thm:fake-monster-d5-bialgebra} supplies only the
 ambient shape, not these per-$N$ witnesses.
 \item \emph{Compact K3 Hodge-parity Yangian.} The Hodge-parity
 super-extension of the orthogonal K3 Yangian remains governed by
 Conjectures~\ref{conj:osp-yangian-mukai} and
 \ref{conj:k3-super-yangian}. A compact K3 globalisation must give a
 global reflection-equation representation compatible with the Mukai
 pairing, the Hodge-parity grading, and the $\chi(K3)=24$ term; the
 $K3\times E$ chart-local construction does not cancel this term on a
 bare compact K3.
 \item \emph{Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality beyond the local and
 low-loop cases.} For a generic compact CY$_3$ with
 $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)\ne0$, formality beyond the proved local and
 low-loop regimes requires nullhomotopies for all higher obstruction
 classes $\omega_\ell(X)$, $\ell\geq3$, in the cyclic/BCOV
