 obstruction tower. The $E_3$ formality torsor of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:cfg-form3-grt1-torsor} is a background torsor, not
 a proof that those compact obstruction classes vanish.
 \item \emph{Unified $\PhiFA_d$ for $d\geq4$.} The single theorem
 promised by the higher-dimensional programme must construct
 $\PhiFA_d$ for every $d\geq4$, specify the Bott/framing obstruction
 datum, prove specialisation compatibility, and prove coherence in
 $d$. The $d=4$ and $d=5$ fragments remain conditional surfaces
 until those witnesses are assembled into one functorial theorem.
 \item \emph{Programme-scope compatibility.} The cross-volume status
 theorem for A/B/C/D/H, the Vol.~II K3 mock-modular pushforward to the
 $d=3$ lane, the ZTE $T$-matrix interpretation through $\Phi$, and the
 chain origin of the shadow tower each require an explicit comparison
 map between the Vol.~I/II object and the corresponding Vol.~III
 $\PhiFA_d$ or $\SpCh_{\Sigma,C}$ object. Numerical or matrix
 concordance without that map is evidence, not a proof.
\end{enumerate}
Consequently the only monotone manuscript move compatible with these
dependencies is to keep the cited surfaces conditional or conjectural
until the corresponding witness package is constructed.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Each item is a direct unpacking of the definitions of the cited
conditional statements. For (F1), Theorem~\ref{thm:six-routes-isomorphism}
is a colimit statement in an $\Eone$-chiral category; such a colimit is
not determined by equality of characters, and it needs the six arrows
and the closing homotopy. For (F2), the non-K3-fibred tier-III target
is orthogonal/Hilbert rather than Siegel, so the $\Delta_5$ pairing
does not even have the correct domain. For (F3), a vanishing theorem
for $O_i^{BL}$ is exactly a chain homotopy killing the representative
in the obstruction complex; scalar BCOV data identify coefficients but
do not exhibit that homotopy.

For (F4), Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} constructs objects under
H1--H4, while Theorem~\ref{thm:phi3-witnessed-kernel-functoriality}
and Proposition~\ref{prop:phi3-arbitrary-morphism-obstruction-criterion}
list the additional morphism data. Hence object existence cannot imply
global functoriality. For (F5), the obstruction class and the possible
$\GRT_1$ correction live in the same deformation complex; deciding the
case requires either a nonzero class modulo indeterminacy or an
explicit nullhomotopy after twisting. For (F6), Scheithauer supplies
external BKM algebras, whereas a CY/Fake-Monster host is additional
geometric and Hall-theoretic data; Theorem~\ref{thm:psi-nonsurjective-gn}
already rules out the $d\leq3$ shortcut.

For (F7), the conjectural K3 Hodge-parity Yangian names the needed
reflection-equation and parity-compatible globalisation data. For
(F8), the Kontsevich--Tamarkin torsor gives the space of formality
choices; it does not set all compact BCOV obstruction classes to zero.
For (F9), the higher-dimensional fragments construct or compute only
parts of the desired functorial system, so coherence in $d$ is a
separate datum. For (F10), a cross-volume compatibility theorem is by
definition a comparison map preserving the relevant structures; shared
constants and matching matrices are insufficient without that map.
Thus every attempted unconditional upgrade factors through the
corresponding witness package listed above.
\end{proof}

% ============================================================
\section{Summary and forward reference (extended)}
\label{sec:cy-to-chiral-closing-extended}
% ============================================================

Two constructions are proved and one framed $d = 3$ object-level package is isolated. The CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi_2$ is constructed unconditionally on the smooth proper locus of $\CY_2$-categories \textup{(}Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}\textup{),} with the modular characteristic identified as the Hodge supertrace $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\Phi_2(\cC)) = \chi(\cO_X)$ under the hypothesis $h^{1,0}(X) = 0$ \textup{(}Proposition~\ref{prop:cy-kappa-d2}\textup{);} the K3 instance evaluates to $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = 2$ and matches the Mukai-lattice chiral algebra. At $d = 3$, Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3} proves the specialised assignment $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2,C)}(\cC)=\SpCh_{\Sigma_2,C}(\PhiFA_3(\cC))$ under hypotheses \textup{H1--H4}; compact non-formal instances require their own framing/strictification witnesses. The $\Etwo$-braided representation category is recovered on constructed loci through the Drinfeld centre $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(A_\cC))$ via the half-braiding presentation of the $R$-matrix \textup{(}Construction~\ref{constr:rmatrix-from-center}\textup{).} Arbitrary CY$_3$ morphism functoriality and the global quantum vertex group $G(\cC)$ remain outside this theorem. For $\cC = \MF(\C^3, 0)$ the toric chain evaluates to $\CoHA(\C^3) \simeq Y^+(\widehat{\fgl}_1)$, with the full $\cW_{1+\infty}$ at $c = 1$ recovered through the Drinfeld-centre passage, not through $\Phi_3$ directly.

What this forces. The seven faces of $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$ \textup{(}the part on $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$\textup{)} are specific evaluations of $\Phi_d$ at the canonical inputs singled out by property~\textup{(U4)} together with adjacent constructions: the K3 Yangian at $d = 2$ from $\Phi_2(D^b\Coh(\mathrm{K3}))$; the framed $\Eone$-chiral outputs at $d = 3$ whose Hall positive-half comparison is supplied by toric CoHA on $\C^3$, the conifold, $K3 \times E$, and local $\bP^2$; the genus-$0$ BCOV reconstruction at compact CY$_3$ with $h^{1,0} = 0$. On the principal locus of Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-is-sp-k3}, the Borcherds--Monster BKM on $K3 \times E$ is a $d = 3$ object obtained by a K3-fibre Borcherds lift, one of six independent constructions producing $G(K3 \times E)$ \textup{(}Remark~\ref{rem:alternative-spch-cy3}; Remark~\ref{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations}\textup{)}. Each face is a specific consequence of its own construction. The mathematical content of the part on $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$ is to verify, per face, that the chiral-side invariant predicted by the relevant construction matches the BPS-side invariant produced by the K-theoretic / cohomological Hall algebra of the target CY.
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lllp{4.2cm}}
 \toprule
 \textbf{Dim.} & \textbf{$\Phi_d$-output scope} & \textbf{Canonical $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}$} & \textbf{Source} \\
 \midrule
 $d = 1$ & $E_\infty$-chiral (lattice VOA) & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(E) = 1$ & Borcherds~1986; Kac~1998 \\
 $d = 2$ & $\Etwo$-chiral (Mukai--Heisenberg) & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathrm{K3}) = 2$ & Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral}; $\bS^2$-framing \\
 $d = 3$ & framed $\Eone$-chiral; $\Etwo$ via $\cZ(\Rep^{\Eone})$ on constructed loci & $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(\C^3) = 1$, (conifold)$= 1$, local $\bP^2 = 3/2$, $K3\times E = 3$ & Theorem~\ref{thm:cy-to-chiral-d3}; \mbox{Construction~\ref{constr:rmatrix-from-center}} \\
 $d \geq 4$ & $E_1$-stabilised (conjectural) & BCOV Yukawa $\kappa_{\mathrm{BCOV}}^{(d)}(X_d) = \deg(X_d)$ & Section~\ref{sec:beyond-cy3} \\

\noindent The correspondence programme is stated; its evaluation on the canonical landscape is Theorem~\ref{thm:seven-faces-of-r-cy}.
